# Session log — 2026-09

> Newest first. This is the project's engineering changelog: what changed, why,
> what was verified, and what was deliberately not done. Durable rules extracted
> from these entries live in CLAUDE.md; this file is the detail behind them.

**Session summary (2026-09-10, session 275 — a recap that a later settlement invalidated is restated automatically on both surfaces; the nflverse fetch retries a transient 403):**

- mike: *"Do the outstanding tasks."* Two.
- **Automatic restatement.** Every settle pass (the 6am run and each refresh
  pass) now calls `discord_notifier.restate_published_recaps()`: for each
  daily recap snapshot in the last 7 days, count picks in the recap's OWN
  universe (`_SETTLED_SQL`) with `settled_at > published_at`. Any → re-post
  Discord (title "· restated", note: "N picks settled after this recap was
  first posted (M settled then, K now). Same picks, same results…") then X
  ("📊 Sep 9 results (restated): …"), each under its own ledger kind
  (`discord_results_restate`, `x_results_restate`) keyed on the published_at
  being corrected, so one late batch fires once and a second batch fires
  again. The Discord post overwrites the snapshot's published_at, which is
  what closes the loop. The 2026-08-29 hard-coded date list and its note are
  gone; the manual `publish_results` job stays for hand re-posts.
- **Why the trigger is late settlement and not a count difference, measured:**
  every daily snapshot 09-01 → 09-08 disagrees with what the recap query
  returns today (09-01: 35 published, 23 now; 09-04: 56 → 33; 09-05: 63 → 67)
  because the query applies the CURRENT action cuts and those moved (the live
  cut re-sweep of 09-09, pauses). A count trigger would have restated eight
  days for nothing. This is also a finding on its own: **the published daily
  record is recomputed under today's cuts**, so a recap re-rendered later can
  differ for reasons other than late settlement. Not changed here.
- **Forward-looking:** `RESULTS_RESTATE_FROM = "2026-09-10"`. Recaps published
  before it (09-04: 2 late NCAAF; 09-05: 4 NCAAF + 2 UFC) are not restated
  automatically; that stays mike's call via the manual job, because a restated
  09-04 would also show 56 → 33 from cut drift and read as something it is not.
- **nflverse retry** (`nfl_player_stats_ingestor._fetch_season_csv`, shared by
  Step 0h's two ingestors): 3 attempts, 5s then 15s, on a network error or
  403/429/5xx; 404 returns at once; a persistent failure still raises.
- Tests: `tests/test_recap_restatement.py` (14 cases: the scan, its bounds,
  the universe-plus-late query, the key, the note, both notifiers, the settle
  wiring, the retry). The two 08-29-specific restate tests in
  `test_discord_notifier.py` replaced.

**Session summary (2026-09-10, session 274 — NFL prop CLV was never captured: the 2026-08-29 prop-CLV map had no NFL entries and its guard test excluded them; the market-relative rule now closes at its own book):**

- mike, on the re-posted 09-09 recap: *"Where is the CLV stats on nfl?"* The
  NFL line read `2-1 · +0.85u · +29.2% ROI` with no beat-the-close figure.
- **Why, measured:** all three NFL picks had `clv_captured_at` NULL. The
  2026-08-29 change that extended CLV to props (`_PROP_MARKET_FOR_MODEL`)
  listed MLB, WNBA and NBA models only, and `test_every_prop_model_maps_to_a_dk_market`
  was written with `if not m.startswith(("nfl_prop", "golf_"))` — the guard
  excluded the gap it existed to catch. With no entry, `_capture_clv` treated
  an NFL prop as game-level, looked for a close in `odds` (no NFL rows),
  found nothing and `continue`d without stamping the pick, so `_backfill_clv`
  re-walked it on every settle and never filled it. No NFL prop had settled
  before 09-09, so there was no symptom. Meanwhile `player_prop_odds` held
  256,076 quotes for NE @ SEA (58 players, 23 markets) with a DraftKings
  close for all three picks at 23:25Z, 55 minutes before kickoff.
- **Fix (`tracking/paper_tracker.py`):** the twelve distributional NFL prop
  models added to the map with the market key the scorer prices them on
  (`models/scorer._NFL_PROP_CONFIG`, pinned by an ast parity test); the
  market-relative rules `nfl_prop_market` and `wnba_prop_market` map to the
  `FROM_PROP_MARKET` sentinel and take the market from `picks.prop_market`,
  which `_capture_clv` now fetches.
- **The market-relative close is read at the pick's own book.** The card
  writes the SOFT book's price into `dk_odds` and names the book only in the
  label suffix (`… (FD)`), so a DK close against an FD open is two books.
  `_closing_prop_odds` gained `bookmaker` (default DraftKings) and
  `_book_from_label` resolves the suffix through `_LABEL_BOOK`, the inverse of
  both cards' `_BOOK` maps (pinned), accepting the raw key a card writes for
  an unmapped book (`(fliff)`) and defaulting to DraftKings with no suffix.
  Checked before writing: FanDuel spells "Jadarian Price" and BetMGM "Sam
  Darnold" exactly as the labels do, with pre-kickoff rows (FD 1.5 +124/-166;
  MGM 20.5 +100/-135 at 23:25Z). Every NFL prop BET on the board (09-09 to
  09-14, 34 rows) has a `games` row, so the `JOIN games` in `_capture_clv`
  loses none of them.
- **Backfill is automatic:** `_backfill_clv` runs on every settle and gates on
  `clv_captured_at IS NULL`, so the first refresh-pass settle after deploy
  fills the three 09-09 picks. Expected from the closes above: Stevenson's
  DK line held at 88.5 (price CLV applies, close -114 vs -115); Price's FD
  line held at 1.5 (close +124 vs +124, 0.00pp); Darnold's MGM line moved
  19.5 → 20.5 on an under (-1.0 line CLV: the number moved against the
  under). **Measured after deploy, captured 09:24 ET by the 09:17 refresh
  pass:** Stevenson close -114 at 88.5, clv_pct -0.22pp; Price FD close
  +124 at 1.5, 0.00pp; Darnold MGM close -135 at 20.5, line_clv_pts -1.0.
  None of the three beat the close.
- Tests: `tests/test_nfl_prop_clv.py` (new, 12 cases) and the exclusion
  removed from `test_clv_props.py`, watched failing with the twelve names.
- **Third post of 09-09** (mike: *"Report a third time"*): job 54132 via
  `publish_results` at 11:55 ET. Discord posted (NFL `0% beat close (0/2)`,
  day `38% beat close (3/8)`). **X refused it — HTTP 403 "duplicate
  content"**: the tweet format carried no CLV, so its text was byte-identical
  to the 08:50 tweet still standing. `render_results` now carries the day's
  `clv_line` string (the embed's own), budgeted inside the 280, so the two
  surfaces change together; re-posted to X through `publish_x_results` once
  deployed. The X ledger row for 09-09 was cleared by job 54132 and stays
  clear until that post lands.

**Session summary (2026-09-10, session 273 — the 2026-09-09 recap went out without the NFL: box scores were ingested after settle, so the three NFL BETs settled an hour after the recap was ledgered):**

- mike: *"I did not see nfl results posted in the discord or X. nFL results were
  missed."* He is deleting the two partial posts by hand; the full day re-posts
  from the worker.
- **What happened, from the worker log and `push_sent` / `picks.settled_at`:**
  the 6am run settled 2026-09-09 at 06:02 ET and posted the recap to Discord
  and X at 06:02:22 / 06:02:23 — MLB 3-4, 7 settled, nothing else. The three
  NFL prop BETs from NE @ SEA (Stevenson U88.5 Ru+Re LOSS, Price O1.5 Rec WIN,
  Darnold U19.5 Comp WIN) settle off `nfl_player_game_log`, and the steps that
  fill it ran AFTER settle: Step 4b at 06:06 (failed — a transient 403 from the
  nflverse GitHub release URL) and Step 4c at 06:11, which wrote the 57
  box-score rows (`created_at` 10:11:19Z). The 07:17 refresh pass logged
  "Found 3 unsettled prop picks for 2026-09-09" and settled them at 07:24. The
  recap was already ledgered, so nothing re-posted.
- **Fix, same PR:** Steps 4b (player stats) and 4c (prop modelling data) are
  now **Step 0h**, before Step 0 settle — the same rule 0d/0e/0f/0g already
  apply to MLB, WNBA, NFL finals and NCAAF finals. Pinned by
  `tests/test_recap_waits_for_nfl_box_scores.py` (source-order inside
  `run_daily_pipeline`, watched failing before the move).
- **Recovery, on the worker:** new job type **`publish_results`** in
  `tracking/job_queue.py` — clears the `discord_results` and `x_results`
  ledger rows for one date and calls BOTH ordinary notifiers, Discord first,
  seconds apart, off the same query. Neither day-is-over guard is bypassed.
  The `results_snapshots` rows for the date are overwritten by the re-post.
  The X-only `publish_x_results` job stays.
- **The figures the re-post publishes**, computed locally from the production
  rows through `_settled_rows` / `_tally` before the job ran:
  daily **5-5 · -0.54u · -4.8% ROI · 10 settled** (MLB 3-4 · -1.39u; NFL 2-1
  · +0.85u); all-time since 2026-09-01 **131-120 · -4.39u · -1.5% ROI · 251
  settled**. The tweet: `📊 Sep 9 results: 5-5, -0.54u, -4.8% ROI / MLB 3-4 ·
  NFL 2-1`. The original partial tweet was id `2097989050489766174`; its
  ledger row is gone with the re-post, mike deletes the tweet itself.
- **This is the fourth date in a week with the same shape**, measured as
  action-cut BETs with `settled_at` after the date's `results_snapshots.published_at`:
  2026-09-04 (2 NCAAF, settled 09-07 18:35), 2026-09-05 (4 NCAAF settled
  09-07 18:35; 2 UFC settled 09-06 14:23), 2026-09-09 (3 NFL, 07:24). The
  reorder fixes the NFL cause only. The general fix is an automatic
  restatement — in `step_settle`, any date whose current settled count exceeds
  its snapshot's re-posts both surfaces labelled "restated" — which generalises
  the 2026-08-29 machinery already in `notify_discord_results`. Not built this
  session; the 09-04 / 09-05 restatements are mike's call (Decisions).
- **Also seen, not fixed:** Step 4b's 403 from
  `github.com/nflverse/nflverse-data/releases/download/stats_player/stats_player_week_2026.csv`
  at 06:06:54; 4c fetched the same asset successfully at 06:11. Transient, one
  occurrence, no retry in the ingestor.

**Session summary (2026-09-09, session 268 — the Stats board was drawing an arbitrary first 1,000 rows, and on the NFL slate that was an empty board):**

- Matt, on the night of the 2026 NFL opener (NE @ SEA, 8:20pm ET): *"The NFL
  player stats are not showing for tonight's game. Prop and game lines should be
  available on the stats tab."* Two separate findings.

- **The Stats board's leaderboard reads were never paged, and every one of them
  is over the row cap.** `lib/paging.ts` has documented since 2026-09-04 that
  Supabase caps every PostgREST response at max-rows (1,000 here) whatever
  `.limit()` asks for, silently — that fix covered the four all-books LINE
  reads and stopped there. Measured against production today:

  | read | rows |
  |---|---|
  | `player_recent_games_ncaaf(2025, 10)` | 54,687 |
  | `player_recent_games_nfl(2025, 10)` | 12,850 (5.7 MB) |
  | `player_window_totals_ncaaf(2025, 10)` | 12,204 |
  | `player_recent_games_mlb(2026, batter, 10)` | 6,216 |
  | `player_recent_games_nba(2026, 10)` | 5,447 |
  | `player_recent_games_wnba(2026, 10)` | 2,074 |
  | `player_season_stat_values_nfl(2025, ...)` | 1,798 |
  | `v_player_season_totals_mlb (2026)` | 1,568 |
  | `player_window_totals_nfl(2025, 10)` | 1,123 |

  So every sport's board has been drawing the first 1,000 rows of its read. It
  surfaced on the NFL board because that one OPENS filtered to the slate
  (`defaultTonightOnly`): the 1,000 rows are the 136 lowest `player_id`s league
  wide, of which SIX play tonight — Hooper, Diggs, Lawrence, Reed, Myers,
  Leonard Williams — and none is a quarterback, so the default Pass Yards board
  filtered to an empty list. Un-capped, the same board is four rows: Drake Maye
  6/10 over 224.5, Sam Darnold 5/10, Dobbs and Lock.

- **Paging alone was not the fix.** Draining 54,687 rows onto a phone to render
  one game's players is its own bug, so a board already filtered to a slate now
  asks the SERVER for that slate: `statsBoard.slateTeams()` → `.in('team', …)`
  on the RPC, which PostgREST filters and pages exactly like a view. NFL tonight
  goes from 12,850 rows (13 pages) to ~730 (one). NULL for UFC, whose slate keys
  are fighter NAMES and whose rows carry no team. The board now waits for the
  slate query to settle (`slateReady`) before its first read, or the first read
  is the whole league and is thrown away a moment later.

- **THE FIRST NARROWING WAS WRONG, AND THE UX REVIEW CAUGHT IT.** `.in('team', …)`
  on the request is exact for `player_window_totals_*` and
  `player_season_stat_values_*` — both emit one row per player carrying
  `team = (array_agg(team ORDER BY game_date DESC))[1]`, which IS the team the
  board groups to. `player_recent_games_*` emits one row per GAME, each carrying
  the team the player played THAT GAME for, so the same filter filters GAMES
  where the board filters PLAYERS. Measured on tonight's slate: **5 of the 110
  players would have had their last-10 window silently cut to only the games
  played for tonight's team, and 5 more who have LEFT SEA/NE would have appeared
  on the board as though they were still on them** — the row prints "3 of 5"
  under a chip that says L10, so neither is visible.

  Fixed at the source: `data/migrations/player_recent_games_slate_teams.sql`
  adds `p_teams text[] DEFAULT NULL` to all five functions, filtering inside the
  ranked CTE on `rn = 1`. DROP-then-CREATE inside one DO block, because an added
  parameter makes an OVERLOAD and a call carrying exactly `{p_season, p_window}`
  then matches both — PostgREST answers **300 Multiple Choices**, which would
  break every app build already in the field. Guarded on its own property so it
  runs once (every DDL statement fires `pgrst_ddl_watch` and this file executes
  on all ~54 passes a day), and it re-issues the grants DROP takes with it.
  Applied to production the same evening and verified there: **110 players
  either way, zero difference in either direction**, and the legacy 2-arg call
  still returns all 12,850 rows.

  The guard that missed it is the lesson: `verify_leaderboard_reads.ts` already
  asserted "the narrowing must agree with the client-side filter it replaces"
  and PASSED, because every row in its fixture carried one team. §7's rule about
  a test inheriting its author's blind spot, again.

- **Four more from the same review, all fixed.** The slate gate was released
  only by `.finally()`, and supabase-js is built with no fetch timeout — a hung
  request never settles, so the tab parked on a spinner with no Retry (`error`
  stays null) and no way back; bounded at 4s, failing OPEN to the whole-league
  read. `load()` had no request-generation guard and the change made the race
  window 10-50x longer (one request became up to 55), so NCAAF rows could be
  painted under NFL stat labels; every load is stamped now and a stale response
  writes nothing. Rows that answer a question the user has since changed are
  replaced by row-shaped skeletons rather than left up as the answer, and the
  slate chip is disabled while its read is in flight with a busy VoiceOver
  label. `fetchSlateGames` was an unordered `.limit(500)` that now decides which
  players exist on the board at all — ordered and paged (widest 7-day window
  measured today: 118 NCAAF games).

- **Two more things Matt asked for, and one the review caught in them.** NFL
  GAME LINES: `odds_ingestor.SPORT_KEYS` had no NFL entry, so the table held DK
  spreads and nothing else. Adding the entry is one line and a trap —
  `_build_game_id` mints `NFL_2026-09-09_NE_SEA` where the schedule's row is
  `NFL_2026_01_NE_SEA`, so every slate would have grown sixteen phantom games.
  `_process_events` now takes an optional resolver; the NFL is read-only against
  `games`, emits no game row, and drops an unresolvable event whole. The
  resolver is the NFL prop ingestor's, already in production. ~162 credits/day
  against 3,068,382 remaining, approved with the number stated.

  TOUGHNESS: graded off the opponent's defence for NFL / NCAAF / NBA, anchors
  measured (NFL points allowed n=32 median 23.09; NCAAF EPA/play allowed n=136
  median 0.155; NBA def rating n=30 median 112.40). The join was measured too,
  because a mismatch between `games.home_team` and `team_stats_board.team`
  returns the column silently to dashes: **NFL 32/32, NBA 30/30, NCAAF
  133/211** — the 78 misses are FCS visitors with no rating, an honest dash
  rather than a join bug. NHL is left ungraded: `team_stats_board('NHL', 2026)`
  has no rows and an anchor for an empty table is a number nobody measured.

  And the review's blocker on that work: moving the football season fallback
  INTO `fetchTeamStats` broke the Teams board's header, which loops the
  candidates itself and labels the rows with what it ASKED for — so 2025 numbers
  would have rendered under "TEAM · 2026". The read now returns the season it
  used. Also fixed from the same review: the MATCHUP tooltip described only the
  two baseball answers and asserted "this season"; the three new labels were
  abbreviations going straight into VoiceOver and contradicted the Teams board's
  wording for the same number; the grade colour ramp painted ~110 rows one red
  on tonight's two-team slate (`gradeColorDiscriminates`, the same rule
  `colorful` applies to hit rate); the slate gate was a boolean read stale on a
  sport switch, so the first read after one was narrowed by the OUTGOING sport's
  teams (now an identity — `slateFor !== sport`); and `disabled={loading}` put
  tertiary text on the tint fill of an active chip and dimmed for loads it did
  not cause (now a `busy` prop that announces without dimming).

- **A read reached through a `view` VARIABLE is invisible to the read-surface
  tripwire.** `tests/test_anon_readable.py` parses literal `.from('…')` out of
  `mobile/src`, and `fetchSeasonTotals` picked the two football views through a
  ternary — so `v_player_season_totals_nfl` / `_ncaaf` were never in
  `data/anon_readable.py` and would have lost SELECT the day the default grant
  is revoked, silently, with an empty screen and nothing in any log near the
  cause. Both call sites are now literal and both views are declared. They
  already hold anon SELECT, so the grant apply is a no-op; declaring them is
  what KEEPS it.

- **The second finding is NOT a UI bug: NFL game lines barely exist.** For the
  2026-09-09 → 09-14 window, `odds` holds, per sport:

  | sport | markets | books |
  |---|---|---|
  | MLB | h2h, spreads, totals (+F5) | 14 |
  | NCAAF | h2h, spreads, totals | 14 |
  | UFC | h2h, totals | 11 |
  | **NFL** | **spreads only** | **1 (draftkings)** |

  `data/ingestors/odds_ingestor.SPORT_KEYS` has no NFL entry and never has —
  every NFL row in `odds` comes from `nfl/data_ingest/line_snapshots.py` via
  `publish_line_snapshots`, which dumps DK totals/spreads only for games a wind
  or opener card has published. So the Teams board's LINE column has one book's
  spread for NFL and no moneyline at all. NOT FIXED, deliberately: it is a spend
  and a settlement decision, put to Matt (~162 credits/day, 3 markets × 1 region
  × ~54 refresh passes, against 3,068,382 remaining measured today; and adding
  DK totals would start filling CLV for `nfl_wind_totals` picks, which changes a
  measured series).

- **Verified.** `python -m pytest -q tests/` 3,277 passed / 16 skipped / 0
  failed. `npx tsc --noEmit` error SET compared to an `origin/master` worktree:
  identical, minus four pre-existing `SeasonTotalsRow` cast errors the rewrite
  removed; zero new. All 40 `mobile/scripts/verify_*.ts` run on both trees —
  the same seven pre-existing failures on each, none new. New
  `mobile/scripts/verify_leaderboard_reads.ts` watched to FAIL on master (23
  checks) before passing here.
**Session summary (2026-09-09, session 272 — the Picks market filter offered baseball markets on the NFL board, and thirteen NFL models had no name in the app):**

- Matt, from a screenshot of the NFL board: *"This filter that is on shows
  baseball and it's under nfl. Please fix and consider for all sports."* The
  active pill read **"Pitcher, Batter, Player"** over a board of football
  props, and the card behind it showed **`nfl_prop_rush_rec_yards`** — the raw
  model id — as its market chip. Two independent defects in one screenshot.

- **Defect 1 — the Market cut was never scoped to the sport.**
  `PicksHomeScreen` computed `availableModelIds` only for the Signals/Live
  views (`view === 'today' ? undefined : [...]`), and `PickFilters` read
  `undefined` as "offer all four categories". `pitcher_prop` and `batter_prop`
  are MLB-only markets, so every non-MLB board carried them: the sheet's Market
  section, the Props quick chip (which wrote all three prop categories into the
  state) and the pill that named them back. The mirror case was as bad and
  quieter — NCAAF and UFC boards are 100% game models today, so Game/Props were
  controls that filtered nothing.

  Fixed by deriving the offered set from the picks on screen
  (`presentCategoriesFor`), on EVERY view, and making `availableModelIds` a
  REQUIRED prop — the permissive default is what produced the bug. Every
  category comparison is now relative to what the board holds rather than to
  the four-category universe: the Filters badge, the pills, the quick-chip
  active states and the sheet summary. A board with fewer than two categories
  present hides the cut entirely, unless the state is already narrowed, so the
  control that produced a filter is always reachable to undo it (the state
  survives a Today→Signals switch and those segments hold different markets).

- **Defect 2 — thirteen live NFL models had no `MODEL_META` entry.** The twelve
  per-stat NFL prop models were unpaused server-side by sessions 265-267
  (`model_action_thresholds.paused = false` for all thirteen NFL prop ids,
  measured 2026-09-09) while the app had never been given labels for them, so
  `modelShort()` fell through to the raw id on the card. `nfl_live_prop` — LIVE
  since 2026-09-05 per CLAUDE.md §2 — had the same gap and has simply never
  written a pick, which is the only reason nobody saw it.

  The second half of that defect was invisible: `applyFilter` cut on
  `if (meta && !state.categories.has(meta.type))`, so a model with no metadata
  **skipped the category cut entirely** and survived every filter — including
  "Game", which a receiving-yards prop is not. A missing label was also a
  filtering bug. `modelCategory()` now derives a category from the model id
  when the registry has no entry, and `applyFilter` calls it unconditionally.

- **Verification, both halves watched failing.** The pure state moved to
  `mobile/src/lib/pickFilterState.ts` so it can be EXECUTED rather than
  grepped: `mobile/scripts/verify_pick_categories.ts` (21 checks) runs the
  category maths on the real production boards. With the registry entries
  removed it fails 2; with `presentCategoriesFor` and the `meta &&` escape
  restored it fails 8. `tests/test_mobile_pick_categories.py` pins the
  cross-file invariant — every id in `config.ACTION_THRESHOLDS` can be named by
  the app — and runs the tsx script when node modules are present.

- **The test found its own §7 blind spot.** The first version of
  `_config_scorable_ids()` anchored its RETIRED_MODELS block on the bare name,
  whose first occurrence in `config.py` is a COMMENT 300 lines above the
  assignment — so it read 9 KB of `ACTION_THRESHOLDS` as the retired set,
  subtracted every model from itself, and **passed against the broken code**.
  Anchoring on the declaration (`RETIRED_MODELS: frozenset = frozenset({`) made
  it fail correctly and immediately surfaced a 13th missing model,
  `nfl_live_prop`, that the wrong regex had hidden. The corrected version
  asserts the retired set is smaller than half the registry, so the same drift
  cannot go green again.

- **Verified:** mobile `tsc` error set **byte-identical to `origin/master`**
  (27 pre-existing, 0 in touched files, compared via
  `git worktree add --detach origin/master`); every `mobile/scripts/verify_*.ts`
  output byte-identical to master (five carry pre-existing failures, unchanged);
  `python -m pytest -q tests/` 3283 passed, 16 skipped.

- **UX review (mandatory, `.claude/rules/frontend.md`): "ship with fixes", no
  blockers, `ux_scan --changed` 0 new findings in touched lines.** Seven
  findings, six taken:
  - The quick-chip row is POSITIONAL and shared with SORT, so hiding Game/Props
    slid every sort chip left under a thumb already on the row — a tap meaning
    "Game" would silently re-sort the board. Split the two surfaces: the bar
    always renders the chips and DISABLES them (`FilterChip` has carried a
    `disabled` prop for exactly this; `SportToggle` one row above already mutes
    rather than removes), the sheet keeps hiding its Market section because a
    vertical list moves nothing anyone is aiming at.
  - The Today→Signals empty-intersection case: pill copy is now "No markets on
    this board", and each Market chip carries its pick COUNT, so an empty result
    is attributable to the tap that caused it before it is committed.
    `availableModelIds` is passed undeduplicated for this.
  - `PickCard.metaRow` now wraps. It is a non-wrapping row and this change
    introduced the longest model chip in the app; at an accessibility text size
    the SharpScorePill went off the right edge unreachable.
  - Label family corrected: "Comp" -> "Pass Comp", "Any TD" -> "Anytime TD"
    (DK's own market name, which is what the betslip hand-off is searched for),
    "Rec" -> "Recs", "LIVE Pass Att" -> "LIVE Att", and the two `statLabel: 'Att'`
    collisions split into "Pass Att" / "Rush Att".
  - The Props quick chip now writes the PRESENT props, not all three. No visible
    difference today because every read intersects with `presentCategories` —
    which is the bug masked rather than absent.
  - `presentCategoriesFor([])` no longer falls back to all four. The fallback
    re-created the reported bug exactly and was unreachable only because
    `PicksHomeScreen` happens to gate the filter bar on a non-empty board.
  - The twelve new markets reach the custom-model builder with no graded
    history, where the footer reads "Backtest 0 / Record — / Flat ROI —" and
    looks broken. Added one caption at the footer (not the picker, which has no
    settled-count data without a new query): "New market — no settled picks
    yet". Deliberately NOT the go-live-gate wording, which is banned from the
    app as copy AND as a constant.
  - Fixed in passing: `ModelEditScreen`'s icon-only sheet-close button had no
    `accessibilityLabel` and was silent for VoiceOver (pre-existing, a5079b5).
    `ux_scan` reports it as a BLOCKER on any change touching the file, so
    leaving it would have shipped a blocker in this PR's own scan output.

- **Deliberately NOT done, and why.** (a) `mobile/src/lib/thresholds.ts` still
  lists all twelve NFL prop models in the bundled `PAUSED_MODELS` and carries no
  `ACTION_THRESHOLDS` row for `nfl_live_prop`. That map is the OFFLINE /
  first-launch fallback (the server store is authoritative), so a cold-started
  app hides NFL prop BETs until `model_action_thresholds` is fetched. Syncing it
  is a model-state edit carrying `Updated-By:` semantics — Matt's call, not a
  side effect of a filter fix. (b) Discord and push were checked and are
  unaffected: they render `picks.pick_label`, never the model id, and the Market
  filter is app-only UI, not a publishing gate. (c) Two §4 UNITS violations the
  review found in passing — `DailyResultsModal.tsx:451` renders money through
  `formatCurrencySigned`, and `ModelEditScreen`'s Flat ROI stat is captioned
  "$100/bet". Both are real ("results are always in units, never dollars"), both
  pre-existing, and neither is on this change's path. Fixing one caption while
  the modal beside it still prints dollars is the scoped-by-rule-not-by-symptom
  trap `.claude/rules/frontend.md` names; they want one pass together.

**Session summary (2026-09-09, session 271 — the betslip's hold note is one line, and the book tiles are the bet button):**

- Matt, on a screenshot of a 2-leg negative-EV slip: *"Make the red text - tooltip
  so it doesn't take up so much space"* and *"why does it say bet DK at the bottom
  when we give the user the ability to bet with multiple sportsbooks remove that
  bottom one and move the save slip somewhere else that's fits better to
  condense"*. Both are the same complaint — the card had grown more explanation
  and chrome than slip.

- **The negative-EV note is one line + "Details".** It was five lines of red
  between the book row and the legs, so on a small screen the legs it was warning
  about could be below the fold. The claim stays on the card ("Negative EV —
  straight bets are better value"); the reasoning, the ~15–25% hold figure and
  the DraftKings pricing sentence move into the tooltip that already existed on
  this screen. `InfoTooltip` grew an optional inline `label` and a `warn` tone.
  **The rule written into the code comment:** the summary is never a teaser — a
  reader who never taps has still been told not to place the slip.

- **The green "Bet on DraftKings" button is gone; the "Open with" tiles are the
  bet control**, and the row is renamed "Place this bet at". The button named ONE
  book (the member's stored preference, or DraftKings when theirs could not take
  the slip — Matt's 2026-09-04 call) 40pt under a row that already priced the
  slip at every bettable book and ranked them by payout. Two controls for one
  action, and the button was the one showing no prices. A tile now opens the same
  leg-by-leg `ParlayDkHandoff` sheet the button opened, at that book.

- **`handoffAtBook` has no DraftKings fallback, deliberately.** `handoffBookFor`
  needs one because the button had to choose a book unaided and "Bet on FanDuel"
  must never open a slip FanDuel cannot take. A tapped tile is not a guess: it
  already shows that book's odds, or `N/M legs`, so opening anything else answers
  a question the member did not ask. Pinned in `verify_betslip.ts`, and all three
  new checks were watched failing against a deliberately re-added fallback.

- **Save moved out of the card** into the actions block below it, which became a
  two-up grid — Save parlay / Find players / Custom leg / Clear slip, four
  buttons in the height two used to take. The card ends on the legs.

- **The UX review returned 1 blocker and 8 should-fix; 7 landed.** The blocker
  was the new one-line warning: capped at two lines over a fixed 16pt box at 12pt
  caption, and the sentence already wraps to two lines at 100% type on a 375pt
  device, so every step above default truncated the only warning a
  non-tapping reader gets. Also landed: the negative-EV note moved ABOVE the
  book row (below it, a member could read the payout, tap a tile and reach the
  hand-off sheet — which says nothing about EV — with the warning never on
  screen); the warn tone became a tinted panel in `gradeBad` because
  `colors.avoid` is 3.55:1 on the card, under AA; Clear left the grid for its own
  row behind an Alert, since it destroys hand-typed legs with no undo; grid cells
  got `minHeight: 44`, wrapping labels and a single column past fontScale 1.3;
  and the tooltip modal got ParlayDkHandoff's VoiceOver containment.

- **The hand-off sheet was listing DraftKings' prices under another book's
  name** — a FanDuel tile showing +648 opened a sheet whose legs multiplied out
  to DK's payout. Structurally pre-existing (master's `ParlayActions` did the
  same for the preferred book); the tile row is what made it visible.
  `handoffFrom` already called `legPriceAtBook` per leg for `posted` and threw
  the number away, so `Handoff` now carries `prices`. Display only — edge, EV,
  stake and CLV are still DK's (§6) — and the sheet says whose prices they are.

- **TWO DECISIONS MATT MADE ON THE REVIEW'S REMAINING FINDINGS (2026-09-08).**
  Both were put to him because both are product direction, not defects:
  - **The tiles stay the only bet affordance.** The reviewer's preferred fix was
    a full-width filled commit button following a selected tile; Matt declined —
    that is the button he had just removed. Nothing on the populated betslip is
    styled as a primary action, and that is the accepted trade. The scroll
    indicator is back on so the 7 off-screen tiles are discoverable.
  - **THE PREFERRED-BOOK CONCEPT RETIRES FROM THE BETSLIP.** Matt's 2026-09-04
    call — "the parlay button … should change to match the Sportsbook the user
    selects as their preferred" — was implemented by the button this change
    deletes. Asked whether to mark or re-sort the member's own books in the tile
    row, he chose neither: the row stays payout-only, because the TAP is now the
    choice. `usePreferredBooks` still drives the Stats board pills, TeamsBoard
    and the saved-parlay button; it no longer has a surface on the builder.

- **What did NOT change:** the slip is still priced and modelled at DraftKings
  wherever it is placed (§6), and the saved-parlay card keeps its own
  "Bet on <book>" button, since it has no book row to replace it with — so the
  app now has two different bet affordances for the same object. Flagged, not
  redesigned.

- Verified: `verify_betslip.ts` ALL PASS, `verify_live_record_start.ts` ALL PASS
  (it caught a real regression first — the word "paper" in one of my own code
  comments, which is exactly the ban working), `verify_preferred_book` /
  `verify_book_lines` failure sets identical to master (pre-existing book-count
  pins), `pytest -q tests/` 3194 passed / 16 skipped, tsc error set
  byte-identical to `origin/master` with 0 in touched files, `ux_scan` on the
  three touched UI files 12 findings -> 9 (all pre-existing).


**Session summary (2026-09-09, session 271 — first live NFL game: the prop poll asked the book for ESPN's event id, and the lane never saw a quote):**

- NE @ SEA kicked off 00:20Z. The in-play worker picked it up on its 00:30Z
  relaunch: `live=1 states=1`, anchor polled every tick at the designed 5s,
  in-play prices logged (180 rows by 00:37Z), credits ~36/min. And every
  prop poll, once a minute, answered `422 INVALID_EVENT_ID`: `_poll_for`
  passed ESPN's event id to The Odds API's per-event endpoint. `dec=0` for
  the whole first quarter. The fakes ignored the id argument, which is why
  the suite passed throughout (§7: a test written from the same
  understanding as the code).

- Fix: `GamedayWorker._book_event_id` resolves the book's id from the slate
  anchor on (home, away) abbreviations, the way `_anchor_value` resolves a
  line -- `Quote.game_id` is the book's event id. No match means no poll and
  a `no_book_event_id` skip on the tick line. Three tests, watched failing.

- Also this session, earlier: the declared backfill job failed 3/3 on an
  `opening_signals` UNIQUE collision (#633 made it skip and report the seven
  duplicate pairs; the re-run landed the one live-key ledger row).

**Session summary (2026-09-09, session 270 — the NFL board did not line up with Discord: one lock_key was serving a whole game's props, and six voided picks were still green in the app):**

- Matt: *"Make sure the NFL bets line up with discord I think the wind bets were
  moved to 3 days out. Check for anything else."* Audited the live NFL set
  against the ledger rather than reasoning from the code (§00). Detail:
  `docs/discord.md` → "One board is not enough".

- **The wind answer is 4 days, not 3.** `wind_totals.MAX_FIRE_LEAD = 4.0`
  (mike, 2026-09-06, choosing 4 over 7 on the persistence measurement —
  `docs/nfl_wind_lead_evidence.md`). Deployed: the worker runs master 09565b2 as
  of 22:11Z, which carries #517. Nothing changed; 4 → 3 is a model update
  needing an `Updated-By:` stamp and is Matt's call.

- **THE BUG: `nfl_prop_market` had ONE publishing key per GAME, not per pick.**
  The synthesised `push_sent.lock_key` was `game_id:model_id[:player_id]`, which
  is unique per pick for every model in the repo except this one — it writes
  `player_key` + `prop_market` and leaves `player_id` NULL. `(lock_key, kind)`
  is UNIQUE, so the second prop in a game was not delayed, it was gone.
  Measured: **8 eligible BETs, 7 ledgered keys, 7 shadow rows.** `Sam Darnold
  Under 19.5 Comp (MGM)`, written 09-09 08:26 ET for that night's NE @ SEA
  kickoff, was on the app's board and could never reach Discord, a phone, or
  `opening_signals`. The 24 h prop lead ceiling (#610) makes it worse, not
  better: a ceiling clusters a game's props onto game day, which is when they
  collide.

- **The key now lives in `tracking/publish_keys.py`**, carrying `player_key` and
  `prop_market` when present. A row with only `player_id` — every other model,
  every sport, every row ledgered before today — gets a BYTE-IDENTICAL key.
  Verified against production before applying: of every ledgered key,
  `nfl_prop_market`'s were the only ones that moved (16 rows, one model).
  `pick_side` stays out (§1c). All four producers plus capture and the restate
  delete path now mint it from that one helper, and every DISTINCT ON moved with
  it — a partition coarser than the key drops the row before the ledger is ever
  consulted.

- **Migration ran BEFORE the code, deliberately.** `scripts/backfill_publish_keys.py`
  re-ledgered the seven already-posted picks under their new keys (14 push_sent
  rows across `discord_signal` and `new_bet`; 7 `opening_signals` rows re-keyed
  rather than duplicated). In that order the window is inert; the other way
  round every posted pick republishes into a paid channel. The owner of an old
  key is not inferred — it is `DISTINCT ON (old_key) ORDER BY created_at`, the
  same expression that did the posting. Verified after: 7 ledgered, Sam Darnold
  alone unledgered. Running the fixed producer's exact SQL against production
  returns **one row, across all sports**: that pick.

- **Six VOIDED wind picks were still drawing as green BETs in the app.** They
  were voided 09-07 and mike deleted them from Discord by hand; nothing carried
  that to the app, which had no concept of `condition_status` at all. Now
  excluded on both sides — `condition_status <> 'VOID'` in the publishers'
  SQL, `passesActionFilter` in the app. Only `'VOID'`; NCAAF's `'OK'` / `'GONE'`
  are live states on real picks.

- **`_dropped_signals` joined on `player_id`**, which is not a match at all when
  both sides are NULL: every `nfl_prop_market` pick in a game matched every
  shadow row for that game, so one flipped prop could announce a different one
  as dropped. Now joined on the lock_key.

- **The app's NFL look-ahead was 8 days; the publishers have no horizon.**
  Nothing was beyond it today (0 rows), so this closes the gap before it costs a
  pick rather than after — the last time these two windows differed, two wind
  picks 9 days out reached the app and nothing else. `NFL_AHEAD_DAYS` 8 → 11 =
  the 10-day poll/prop horizon plus ET/UTC margin.

- **Verified.** Full suite **3288 passed, 16 skipped** (two pre-existing tests
  pinned the old key expression as a string literal and were updated to the
  helper; one sliced on `conn.execute("""` and now matches the `f` prefix
  loosely). `tests/test_publish_key_identity.py` is new — **nine mutations, each
  watched failing** (§1b), including the two that matter most: reverting
  `KEY_PARTS` and smuggling `pick_side` into it. tsc against a detached
  `origin/master` worktree: **error set byte-identical, 26 = 26, zero
  regressions, zero new errors in any touched file.**

- **The UX review caught the fix being HALF a fix, and a regression my own
  check missed.** `passesActionFilter` is called by five app surfaces and not by
  the others, so with only that filter changed the Today segment, both model
  screens, the Stats odds pill and the betslip still drew all six voided picks
  as green, stakeable BET cards — while the header count above them excluded
  them. That is the retired-model bug of 2026-09-02 again, and `useTodayPicks`
  already carried its fix and the comment explaining it one line away. The
  exclusion now sits at the SOURCE filter, with `passesActionFilter` kept as the
  belt for the settled/server paths, and `PickDetailScreen` (deep-linkable from
  a push already sent) shows "Withdrawn — this pick was published in error"
  instead of a book hand-off. `tests/test_publish_key_identity.py` pins the
  source filter as a SEPARATE test from the helper one, because the helper test
  passed the entire time the board was wrong.

- **A correction to my own verification.** My first tsc error-set diff reported
  "26 = 26, zero regressions" — it grepped `^src/` and so could not see three
  NEW errors under `mobile/scripts/`, where making `condition_status` required
  broke inline `Pick` fixtures. Re-measured over the WHOLE set: **34 = 34, zero
  regressions**, fixtures fixed. A filtered baseline is not a baseline.

- **And a correction to the code comments.** I wrote "NCAAF's 'OK' / 'GONE'" in
  four places. NCAAF does not write `condition_status` at all — a downgraded
  NCAAF row carries `downgrade_reason` (`models/scorer.py`). The column is the
  NFL locked-pick health signal, written by `scripts/nfl_pick_monitor.py` as
  `OK` / `DEGRADED` / `GONE` on real, STANDING picks. Corrected everywhere, and
  the test now asserts none of the three is ever filtered on.

- **Also fixed, from the review**: the Live board had no VOID exclusion on
  either side (`scripts/void_picks.py` takes any `--model`, so the first voided
  live pick would drop from Discord and stay in the app); the NFL picks read was
  capped at 200 ordered by `created_at` alone, which the 8 → 11 widening made
  worse — an opener pick has the OLDEST `created_at` in the window by
  construction, so it was first off the end of the cap, exactly the pick the
  widening was meant to save (now `signal_type` first, cap 5000, matching
  NCAAF); and the `v_latest_dk_odds` read was bounded on date only, pulling 11
  days of every sport through one unpaged read that truncates SILENTLY (now
  scoped `NFL_%`). `SETTLED_PICK_COLUMNS` gained the column so the type could be
  required, with the settled cache key bumped v3 → v4.

- **THE SAME BUG IS LIVE IN THE IN-PLAY LANE, NOT FIXED HERE.** #629 landed
  while this branch was in review and its key is `live:game_id:model_id:pick_side`
  — no player component at all. Measured: **92 live prop BETs can never
  announce**, all carrying `player_id`: `mlb_prop_batter_hits` 35 of 116,
  `mlb_prop_batter_walks` 23 of 67, `mlb_prop_batter_rbi` 13 of 68,
  `wnba_prop_player_rebounds` 8 of 34 (worst single key shares 5), and four
  more. The game-level live models (`mlb_live_total_runs`, `ncaaf_live_total`,
  `mlb_live_win_prob`, `mlb_live_runline`) are unaffected — one pick per game.
  Left out of this PR deliberately: it needs its own ledger migration and its
  own verification, and holding a verified, time-critical pre-game fix behind it
  would have cost tonight's pick. Next session's first job.

- **Deliberately not done.** The wind gate stayed at 4. `capture_opening_signals`
  still captures a pick that is voided later — it is measurement, never
  published from, and the void normally lands long after the first cross.
  `nfl_prop_market` still writes no `player_id`; fixing that at the source is
  the cleaner shape but would move the key again for every future row, and the
  key is now correct either way.
**Session summary (2026-09-09, session 270 — the live lock_key carries the player: two players' props in one live game are two announcements):**

- mike: *"Fix it now"*, on the collision #630 measured and left: the in-play
  producers keyed a pick as `live:game:model:side` with no player component,
  so a second quarterback's pass-attempt over (or a second batter's hits over)
  in the same game could never reach Discord or push. Kickoff for NE @ SEA
  was 80 minutes away; the fix is the same shape as #630's pre-game one.

- **The key.** `tracking/publish_keys.live_lock_key_sql` / `live_lock_key`:
  the old expression plus the same `KEY_PARTS` tail (`player_id`,
  `player_key`, `prop_market`, each COALESCEd). A row with none of the three
  produces a byte-identical key, which every game-level live model is. Both
  `_new_live_signals` (Discord, push) project the key from SQL and check the
  ledger on the same expression; the dict carries the projected column rather
  than an f-string rebuilt from three columns, which is how the player was
  lost. `first_signal_repair` clears the ledger through the same helper.

- **Measured before the change** (push_sent joined to picks on the old key):
  172 discord_live rows, none for a pick with a player; 609 live_signal rows,
  ONE for a pick with a player (Kyle Schwarber Over 0.5 Walks, 08-09,
  settled WIN); zero old keys owning more than one ledgered pick. So the
  deploy can republish nothing: the one row whose key moves belongs to a
  settled pick, and both producers filter `result IS NULL`.

- **The backfill could not run from the session**: the Supabase MCP refused
  the INSERT ("cannot execute INSERT in a read-only transaction"). Added a
  `backfill_publish_keys` job kind and a declared job so the worker runs
  `scripts/backfill_publish_keys.py --apply`, which now covers the live key
  too; the job re-ledgers that one row. Order is inert either way here.

- **Verified.** Seven new tests in `tests/test_publish_key_identity.py` and
  one in `tests/test_job_queue.py`; the key tests run against a detached
  `origin/master` worktree fail there. Fixtures in `test_discord_live_field`
  and `test_discord_notifier` carry the projected key as the last column.

**Session summary (2026-09-09, session 269 — live picks to their sport's live Discord channel; the NFL in-play lane announces for the first time):**

- mike: *"New global rule: Push picks to discord in live games to their live
  channels: NFL-Live, MLB-Live, NCAAF-Live"*, with the three webhook URLs.
  Promoted into CLAUDE.md §1b beside the parity rule. The URLs are
  credentials and went into Railway variables only (`DISCORD_WEBHOOK_LIVE_NFL`
  / `_MLB` / `_NCAAF`, on `worker` AND `pollers`, deploy skipped so the merge
  deploy carries them); nothing in the repo holds them.

- **Routing.** `config.DISCORD_WEBHOOKS_LIVE` is built from
  `DISCORD_WEBHOOK_LIVE_{SPORT}` over `DISCORD_SPORTS`, the same shape as the
  pre-game map. `discord_notifier._live_webhook_for_sport`: the sport's live
  channel, else the shared `DISCORD_WEBHOOK_LIVE`, else the sport's pre-game
  channel. `_configured()` counts the live map. The channel probe job reports
  each as `live:{SPORT}`.

- **The NFL in-play worker never announced.** `nfl/live_model/pick_writer`
  wrote `is_live` BETs to `picks` since 2026-09-05 and nothing called
  `notify_discord_live` or `notify_live_signals` for the lane: the MLB and
  NCAAF loops announce at the end of a pass, and this worker has no pass. The
  lane reached the app's Live tab and nowhere else. `PicksRecorder` now calls
  `announce_live_picks(game_date)` after each committed BET, after the
  connection closes, in its own try, with the row's own `game_date` (the
  decision's UTC date: a Sunday-night bet is Monday's row) rather than a clock
  date. Per BET, not per game or tick, so the O(games) notify lesson in
  `docs/discord.md` does not recur. Not measured: whether the lane has written
  a BET in production yet (the JSONL log on the worker volume and `picks WHERE
  model_id='nfl_live_prop'` would say; session 268 recorded zero in-play NFL
  prop rows).

- **Verified.** 13 new tests across `tests/test_discord_notifier.py`,
  `tests/test_nfl_live_pick_writer.py`, `tests/test_discord_channel_probe.py`,
  all watched failing against the unpatched source (stash), then the suite
  green. Not verified end to end: no live game today to post; the first real
  post shows up as `push_sent.kind='discord_live'` rows and a card in the
  sport's live channel.

- **Deliberately not done.** The Railway `worker` variable listing was denied by
  the sandbox permission classifier, so the pre-change worker variable set was
  not read; the set call succeeded on both services. `DISCORD_WEBHOOK_LIVE`
  (shared) was not set on `pollers` before this and stays unset. CLAUDE.md is
  40.9 KB against its own ~30 KB target, pre-existing.
**Session summary (2026-09-09, session 268 — #623 and #624 merged, tackles unpaused; the second pass over every DraftKings prop data set):**

- mike: *"merge 623 and unpause tackles and the ten other prop models
  actually do have plenty of draftkings data - did you look hard enough what
  is missing?"* #623 merged as `5ea6b891`, #624 (`Updated-By: mike`, the
  unpause, mirror table synced the same minute) as `f088461f`. Twelve NFL prop
  models live, none paused.

- **The second pass** (`docs/nfl_prop_profitability_search.md` §7): the t72 /
  t48 / t24 / t1 DraftKings snapshots (no market with a positive interval at
  any of them; t1 holds 2,928 DK rows and 115 bets across the ten); the projection's power to predict DK's own line move (real and
  stable: receptions corr +0.66 all three seasons, 93% of "model above" lines
  are raised — and worth less than the vig: following wins 51.8%, fading
  48.2%, at a 53.5% breakeven); DK's alternate ladders (574,525 rows, 21,153
  propositions, pulled into the cache this session — the models' far tail is
  too fat on every market, and the one market that looked profitable, pass
  yards, is a 2024 DK ladder error that the naive projection also collected
  and that 2025 no longer shows once bets are one per proposition); a
  boosted market-anchored classifier (0-1 trees on eight of ten markets,
  memorises on the rest). In-play NFL prop rows: zero, in production and
  cache.

- **Scripts:** `nfl_prop_dk_alt_ladder`, `nfl_prop_dk_boost`; the backtest
  dump now carries each row's fitted tail (`art`) so any strike can be priced.

- **Unchanged conclusion for the ten:** DraftKings is efficient against these
  models at every snapshot we hold and on its ladder. The one distributional
  model with information is tackles, and it is live.

**Session summary (2026-09-09, session 267 — the search for a profitable NFL prop model: tackles was grading the wrong stat, and DraftKings prices flat):**

- mike: *"don't come back until you find the features / data / approach to
  making the eleven distributional models winning in backtest, a projected
  profitable model."* Full record: `docs/nfl_prop_profitability_search.md`.

- **What won: `nfl_prop_tackles_assists` on the corrected stat.** The book
  grades the box-score TOTAL = nflverse solo + with_assist + assists (matches
  ESPN's TOT on 94.8% of 193 player-games; 100.0% / 99.2% on two full game
  days after the fix). The game log never ingested `def_tackles_with_assist`
  although the feed carries it. Ingested 2015-2025 (167k rows), the feature
  engine rolls it and builds the target from it, refusing to build if empty.
  Backtest at the live cut on the retrained artifact's hyperparameters:
  **+53.03u over 340 bets, +15.6%, CI (+6.4, +24.8), 2023 +5.4% / 2024 +29.0%
  / 2025 +6.9%** (pooled interval excludes zero; only 2024 does on its own).
  On the previous hyperparameters +50.24u over 363. Placebo (rolling-8 of all
  three components) +0.6% CI spanning zero, beaten in every season; blind unders −9.8% / +1.2% / +5.1%.
  Information test: coefficient clears zero in both fitting seasons, blend
  beats the book on Brier both test seasons, blend-as-bet positive with CI
  excluding zero at 3-4% in both. Still paused; artifact still the old-stat one.

- **What did not: anything at DraftKings on the other ten.** DK prices its
  standard lines flat (−115/−115 on 5,218 rows; 72-78% of pass/rush-rec yardage
  rows in 2023-24), so its information is in the line. Sharp lines vs DK,
  retail consensus, DK's own t72/t48 movement, line-vs-rolling-8, the injury
  report (bounded at the snapshot), and NGS receiving were each graded per
  season on a fixed grid (`scripts/nfl_prop_dk_signal_sweep`,
  `nfl_prop_injury_signal`, `nfl_prop_market_stack`): no cell positive with an
  interval excluding zero in all three seasons. An offset-logistic stack on the
  same features never beats the book's Brier where it matters.

- **NGS looked like +12-45% ROI on every feature, both quartiles — a leak.**
  The weekly file only has a row for a player who saw enough targets THAT
  week; merging on the current week reveals the outcome. As-of joined to the
  prior week: nothing. Written up so the next person asks why both quartiles
  won.

- **The lead: pass attempts at the soft books.** The stack with the sharp
  references learned rather than thresholded reads +16.5% (109) / +16.4% (150)
  in 2024 / 2025 with intervals excluding zero, +3.1% on 2023 read backwards,
  and a fanduel placebo of +9.7% / +15.8% on a third of the volume with
  intervals spanning zero. Two seasons of three; not shipped.

- **The second stage was broken twice before it was right**: a hand-rolled
  Newton fit diverged on near-collinear presence flags (Brier 0.274 against a
  0.25 coin flip), then clipping at ±3 sd cut the price feature on exactly the
  rows where the price carries information. Final form: the row's own
  logit(fair) as an offset with coefficient one, no presence flags, features
  standardised and clipped at ±4, training Brier printed beside the book's.

- **Found while checking gate 2: `models/saved/nfl_prop_params.json` was
  stale for all twelve models.** The backtest reads hyperparameters from that
  file, not the active artifact, and nothing rewrote it at the 09-07 retrains
  — so every prop backtest since then (the −40.53u table, the information
  test) graded the 08-23 parameters. Refreshed from the active artifacts and
  pinned by `tests/test_nfl_prop_params_match_artifacts.py` (watched failing
  on the stale file, 12 of 12). The eleven re-graded on the DEPLOYED
  parameters, 2023-25, at their live cuts: −14.13u over 2,084 bets (was
  −40.53u over 2,133), every interval still spanning zero; receptions +3.6%
  (585), rush_rec_yards +4.0% (256), pass_yards −10.6% (134), rec_yards −4.0%
  (349). The verdict on the ten does not change; the number does.

- **Data:** `def_tackles_with_assist` added to the ingestor column map, the

  feature engine's SELECT / roll list / target, and the local cache

  2026 game log has no rows yet.

- **Deploy-order note:** the worker keeps the old column map until this

- **Deliberately not done:** unpausing tackles (model update, mike's call);

**Session summary (2026-09-09, session 266 — the eleven NFL prop models hold no information the DraftKings line lacks):**

- mike: *"how do we get the models profitable, you keep saying -40 units so it
  seems like you've done absolutely nothing with new data."* True of the
  eleven: the Kalshi ladders, the alternate backfill and the sharp restore
  timed `nfl_prop_market` and graded two failed constructions; none touched
  the distributional models. This session measured whether anything can.

- **The 87%-unders lane was a calibration symptom.** `nfl_prop_backtest` now
  records the model's own mean P(over) beside the actual and book over-rates
  (`model_over_pct`, `model_gap_pp`, `mean_pred`, `mean_actual`). Ten of eleven
  sit 0.5-5.3pp below the empirical over-rate, and on most markets the
  projected mean is below the actual mean of the quoted players — mean bias
  from training on every player-game while the book quotes only the productive
  ones.

- **The information test, out of sample** (`scripts/nfl_prop_information_test`,
  fitted 2024, read 2025): the book beats the raw model on Brier on all eleven;
  calibrating the model reaches the book's Brier and no further; the residual
  coefficient `b` is never positive with an interval excluding zero in both
  seasons and flips sign on five markets; the blend produces ZERO bets at a 2%
  cut on every yardage market, and the only intervals excluding zero are
  negative (pass_completions −13 to −15%). Written up in
  `docs/nfl_prop_information_test.md`.

- **Second out-of-sample pair (fit 2023, read 2024): same picture.** No
  coefficient clears zero; nothing survives both pairs. The pass_attempts cell
  that read +10.6% has a negative fitted `b` in both pairs — it fades the
  model. `tackles_assists` is the only market with a coefficient clearing zero
  in both pairs, and it is the definitional-mismatch model (actual over 41.1%
  vs book 50.2%): reconciling that stat and re-running is the one lead.

- **The −40.53u figure reproduces to the unit** (2,133 bets, per model
  identical to `docs/nfl_prop_offset_evidence.md`) on the current cache for

- **Backtest gained `--dump DIR`** (every quoted row per model as CSV) and

- **Not done, deliberately:** no pause, no threshold change, no retrain. A pause
