**Session summary (2026-09-08, session 264 — the live model was memorising its training games):**

- mike: *"Do it."* — build the retrain swapping the six season-to-date stats
  for the pre-game total line, with `scripts/live_feature_sensitivity.py` as
  the pass/fail gate. The swap was built and it is good. It is **not** what was
  wrong with the model, and chasing why produced the actual cause of
  "claims ~73%, delivers ~54%". Full detail: `docs/mlb_volume_efficiency.md`
  §§15-17.

- **`pregame_total_line`, the anchor the model never had** (#5a99db15). CLAUDE.md
  §1b says a live total is priced RELATIVE TO THE STARTING LINE; the model
  carried no such feature. `total_line` already existed and was empty on both
  live paths for the same reason — it is filled from whichever market the
  caller passes as `odds_row`, and both live paths pass `h2h`. A dedicated key
  sourced from the TOTALS market, passed by both MLB builders and supplied by
  both live paths. Coverage 87-100% by training season, **100% at serve on
  every completed day since 08-24**; the 2021-23 gap deletes no rows (the live
  trainer never `dropna`s on features). Parity: **60 completed 2026 games built
  down both paths, 60 identical.**

- **A leak fixed on the way** — `_pregame_cutoff` (shipped in #606) bounded the
  pre-game read at `commence_time`, the SCHEDULED start, which over 415 games
  lands a mean 18.7 minutes AFTER the game begins. Permissive, on the decision
  path, while training used the actual first pitch. Now on the shared
  `pregame_cutoff_sql`. Blast radius: totals 0 changed, h2h 3, spreads 3, 0
  prices lost.

- **THE FINDING: the live CV was a lookup, not a cross-validation** (#a5f1ad92).
  One game is ~64 play rows sharing one game-level label, and the pre-game
  context columns are constant within a game and continuous to four decimals —
  a fingerprint. `_poisson_objective` used `KFold(shuffle=True)`, so a game's
  own plays sat on both sides of every fold and 25 Optuna trials optimised
  toward whatever memorised hardest.

      Optuna's best CV NLL   1.9200      what the tuner believed
      2025 holdout NLL       2.7422      what it actually was

  Found by elimination, not by reading the code: the feature swap moved
  calibration 0.0985 -> 0.0976, so the error was not in the features; the tail
  then looked overdispersed (holdout var/mean 2.5-2.7 vs Poisson's 1.0), but
  fitting dispersion on TRAIN collapsed to the Poisson boundary because
  in-sample the ratio was **0.63-0.76, BELOW Poisson** — a model more certain
  about its training rows than the count distribution allows. `_time_ordered_cv`
  fixed this same class of bug for the PRE-GAME models on 2026-09-03; the live
  path was never brought across. **Production has been fit this way since
  2026-06-14.**

- **`_poisson_objective` now takes `groups`**; given them, rows are sorted by
  game and split with `TimeSeriesSplit`. Ungrouped callers (props, game models)
  default to `None` and keep `KFold`. The test asserts the leak in the RIGHT
  direction — on a fixture built with **no signal at all**, shuffled scores
  NLL 1.52 and honest scores 4.89.

- **Six matched fits, three leaky and three honest**, all under `--no-register`.
  Honest B13 (the approved swap) is the best model on every count metric with
  five fewer features: RMSE 3.3252, MAE 2.4771, CV/holdout gap **+0.041**
  (was 0.82). `pregame_total_line` is its 4th most important feature.

- **The third defect, measured but NOT implemented: the Poisson tail is
  genuinely too tight** once the leak is gone (var/mean 2.15 in-sample, 2.41
  holdout — consistent, so real). NB1 fits properly this time (alpha 1.2208,
  train NLL 2.3193 vs Poisson 2.5030, no boundary collapse). B13's holdout gap
  at the >=0.70 band: **+0.1112 leaky -> +0.0586 honest -> -0.0095 with NB1**,
  calibrated in every band. Not built because `_poisson_over_prob` is shared
  with the K-prop models and this is a third change beyond the approved swap.

- **The volume answer, with no threshold moved.** States clearing the same 0.70
  floor: 73,702 today -> 65,457 honest CV -> **51,661 with NB1 (-30%)**. An
  overconfident tail is what pushes states over 0.70; correcting it removes the
  ones that never deserved to qualify.

- **Deliberately not done:** no artifact promoted (all six fits went to
  `models/saved/_baseline/`); the NB tail; the historical `sbr_consensus`
  date-only tie that makes `open`/`close` ordering arbitrary; the retired binary
  live branch's shuffled folds; `_oof_predictions`' shuffled folds for prop
  dispersion; the `half_innings_left` extra-innings mean bias. All in
  `docs/followups.md` with what to measure first.

- **Open for mike:** promote B13 as one swap with the CV fix and (if he wants
  it) the NB tail, rather than shipping the feature swap alone under a
  description it does not earn. And the 0.70/0.14/0.32 cut was swept on the
  leaked probabilities — it needs re-sweeping, which ~70 settled BETs cannot do.

**Session summary (2026-09-08, session 263 — the live model moves 12.8 points on a rounding error):**

- mike: *"yes do all 4 if they have not been done yet."* Item 4. §13.2 reported a
  19-point probability swing from ONE game; this is the distribution behind it,
  packaged as `scripts/live_feature_sensitivity.py` so it can be re-run and
  pointed at other models.

- **Across 60 live BETs, holding the live state, the DK line and the price
  fixed and changing only which day's team-stats snapshot feeds the feature
  row: median swing in `p_over` = 0.1281**, mean 0.1714, p90 0.3375, max 0.4864.
  92% of games swing more than 5 points, 68% more than 10, 37% more than 20.

- **What the inputs did, over 720 team-days since 08-15: season-to-date ERA
  moves a mean of 0.0164 per DAY** (max 0.12) and `runs_last_10` by 0.295. So
  the model's probability moves a median of 12.8 points in response to inputs
  moving by hundredths. That is not information arriving; it is noise being
  amplified.

- **Why it outranks the inning gate.** The model decides on `min_prob 0.70` and
  `min_edge 0.14`, so a 12.8pp median swing is comparable to the ENTIRE decision
  margin. Whether a game becomes a BET is substantially determined by which
  stats snapshot `_pregame_features` happened to freeze in its in-process cache.
  A gate reorders which unstable probability fires first; it does not make it
  stable. This is also the most plausible mechanical account of claims ~73% /
  delivers ~54%.

- **Deliberately NOT concluded:** that the other MLB models share it (they use
  the same features and the probe should be pointed at them — §1b's assess-
  against-all, measurement half only); what the cause is (features, target, or
  fit); and whether to pause. The model's record on its current cut is −1.26u
  over 70 at z −0.12, which is what a noise-driven model looks like but is not
  on its own grounds to stop it. mike's call.
**Session summary (2026-09-08, session 262 — recording what the live model saw, so a live decision can be reproduced):**

- mike: *"yes do all 4 if they have not been done yet."* Item 2, landed after
  item 3 because that one was a decision-path change and this is additive
  plumbing with no behaviour change.

- **`live_pick_features`** — one row per `(game_id, model_id)`, written when
  that lane's FIRST live BET is written, holding the exact feature row fed to
  the model, its lambda, the state snapshot it came from and the resulting
  probability. Table added to `SCHEMA_SQL` and applied to production by hand
  (`data/migrations/live_pick_features_2026_09_08.sql`), with `anon` and
  `authenticated` revoked BY NAME and RLS enabled — verified empty afterwards.

- **Why it had to exist:** `_pregame_features` memoises per
  `(game_date, game_id)` in process, so a running loop freezes one pre-game row
  per game at a moment nothing records. Same state, same DK line, same price
  give p_over 0.5263 on that day's stats and 0.7199 two days older, against
  production's 0.7268.

- **Two properties that are not "it writes a row".** ONE ROW PER LANE, THE
  FIRST ONE — `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` mirrors the first-signal lock, because a
  later pass must not overwrite the evidence for a pick it did not make (§1c
  applied to the audit trail). And A RECORDER MAY NEVER COST A PICK — the
  connection is not autocommit, so an unguarded failure would poison the
  transaction and roll back the picks inserted moments earlier. Each write takes
  its own SAVEPOINT. Both watched failing.

- **`tests/test_db_setup.py::EXPECTED_TABLES` caught the new table**, which is
  the registry guard working rather than a problem — added there too. Volume is
  ~9 rows/day, one per lane per game, not one per 5-second pass.


**Session summary (2026-09-08, session 261 — the live loop's "pre-game" moneyline was a decided game):**

- mike: *"yes do all 4 if they have not been done yet."* This is item 3, landed
  on its own because it is a DECISION-PATH change and the other two are not.

- **`scorer._get_dk_odds` filtered `snapshot_type != 'in_play'` and nothing
  else, which CLAUDE.md §6 already says is insufficient** — the evening refresh
  keeps writing `open` rows after first pitch. On `MLB_2026-09-07_LAA_BOS` those
  rows are stamped **19:41Z against a 17:36Z first pitch, home −10000 / away
  +1380**: a decided game read back as its pre-game moneyline, into
  `live_scorer._pregame_features`.

- **Blast radius measured BEFORE the fix**, every BET since 08-24: pre-game
  **265 bets, 0 affected** (they are written before their games start, so the
  newest `open` row is genuinely pre-game); live **175 bets, 121 were reading a
  post-first-pitch row**. No §1c problem — no locked pre-game pick was priced
  off a post-first-pitch line.

- **What it costs: 9 of 164 live-bet games have no genuine pre-game DK or sbr
  price at all** and now produce no live pick, roughly 5% fewer. Intended, not a
  regression — a pre-game line we never captured is not something to substitute
  a mid-game one for.

- **The prefix comparison was checked, not assumed.** Across 4,564,568
  non-in_play `odds` rows and 39,607 `games`, every timestamp is ISO,
  T-separated and UTC (`Z` or `+00:00`) — no other offset, no space separator —
  so cutting at 19 chars drops the offset and makes them comparable. The only
  other shape is date-only historical `cfbd_bovada` rows, which sort before any
  same-day timestamp and so are included. A game with NULL `commence_time` is
  not bounded at all: a guard that deletes every price on a missing timestamp
  is the WNBA leak run backwards.

- Verified end to end: the same call now returns **−181 / +149** for that game.
  Four tests in `tests/test_multi_book_odds.py`, watched failing. Full suite
  3,178 passed, 16 skipped.


**Session summary (2026-09-08, session 259 - the calibration check was grading a number the decision path stopped using):**

- mike: *"fix the model calibration issue"*

**The check accused four models; three of the four were artefacts of the check.**
`model_calibration` reported `wnba_prop_player_points` +14.5pp, `mlb_prop_pitcher_walks`
+12.1pp, `mlb_prop_pitcher_er` +10.7pp and `wnba_prop_player_rebounds` +10.1pp.
Two faults, which compounded into a verdict that could never come good:

- **It averaged the RAW probability.** `DECIDE_ON_CALIBRATED_PROB` has been on
  since Phase 2 (mike, 2026-08-31): the BET/AVOID call runs on the CALIBRATED
  number, and `docs/probability_calibration.md` says the raw column stays raw ON
  PURPOSE so old sweeps stay readable. The check was grading a number the
  decision path no longer uses — **no amount of calibration work could ever have
  moved it.**
- **Its window opened at the model VERSION's date**, which predates every
  promoted map. Measured: all seven promoted maps landed on **2026-09-07**, and
  `graded_since_promotion` was **0 for every model** — 100% of the sample was
  written under the old regime.

**And the population was frozen.** Two of the four accused are WNBA models whose
last graded pick is **2026-08-30**. The season is over until May, so the check
would have gone CRIT every morning for **eight months** about picks nobody can
change — the permanently-red-and-unactionable failure the skip-budget work was
about, arriving through a different door.

**The fix.** The check now grades
`COALESCE(picks.model_probability_cal, model_probability)` — the scorer stamps
that column from the PROMOTED map, so it IS the decision number and equals the
raw one where no map is promoted — over a window opening at the LATER of the
version date and the promotion date. `mv_scored_pick_outcomes` does not carry
the calibrated column, so it joins `picks` on `pick_id`. The probability floor
applies to the decision number too, or the population measured is not the
population bet.

**Result, against production:** four accused becomes **one**, and it is real —
`mlb_prop_pitcher_er`, **+10.0pp over 397 in-regime picks**. That is the one
model whose map mike deliberately did NOT promote on 09-07 because it *"helps
but does not close"*. The other two measurable models are fine:
`mlb_prop_batter_hits` +0.1pp/1903 and `mlb_prop_batter_tb` +1.5pp/395.

**What was NOT changed, deliberately.** `mlb_prop_pitcher_er`'s non-promotion is
a decision, not a bug — `promote()` requires `helps AND transfers`, and its own
fit note says *"publish it; do not build a threshold on it yet"*. Pausing it,
re-cutting it or retraining it is mike's call, and the check now surfaces it
alone instead of burying it among three artefacts.

**A skip budget of 45 days** was added: a retrain or a newly promoted map resets
the sample to zero, and 150 graded picks take weeks to accrue — the default 14
days would have called that a stuck gate.

Seven mutations watched failing, including the two that matter most: reverting
to `AVG(o.model_probability)`, and dropping the promotion date from the window.

---
**Session summary (2026-09-08, session 260 — the inning-gate replay: 14 missed games, one cause, and a model that changes its mind by 19 points overnight):**

- mike: *"why are live over under mlb picks firing in the second inning? I thought
  we made a rule"* -> *"fix the replay defect and re-run."* The rule was approved
  as *"replay first, then ship"*; the replay said do not ship, so no gate exists.
  Innings 1-3 are −10.82u over 60 bets and negative in both halves, so the
  instinct behind the question is right even though the gate was not the fix.

- **The 14 missed games are NOT a coverage problem.** Every one has state rows,
  in-play DK prices and a final score, and they pair 48-1308 snapshots each. All
  14 carry the identical diagnosis: paired fine, no signal cleared the cut. The
  replay and production disagree about the MODEL PROBABILITY.

- **THE FINDING THAT MATTERS MORE THAN THE GATE.** On `MLB_2026-09-07_LAA_BOS`,
  holding the state, the DK line (7.5) and the price (−118) fixed and varying
  only the stats snapshot: as-of 09-07 gives lam 7.853 / p_over **0.5263** (no
  bet); as-of 09-05 gives lam 9.391 / p_over **0.7199** (BET); production
  recorded **0.7268**. Only six features differ — team ERA 3.60 vs 3.65 and 4.21
  vs 4.25, runs_last_10 by 0.1-0.2. **That moves expected remaining runs by 1.5
  and the over probability by 19 points.** `mlb_live_total_runs` is
  pathologically sensitive to inputs that drift daily by noise, which is the
  most plausible mechanical account yet of claims ~73% / delivers ~54%.

- **The replay CANNOT be fixed by bounding on the pick timestamp, and it was not
  fixed.** `_pregame_features` memoises on `(game_date, game_id)` in process, so
  a running loop freezes one feature row per game at a moment recorded nowhere;
  `DECISION_LOG_DIR` is NFL-only and no MLB live pick stores its feature vector
  or lambda. The newest snapshot available at the 17:55 UTC pick gives 0.5263
  against production's 0.7268 — the information is not in the database.
  **Recording lambda and the feature row on every live pick is the prerequisite.**

- **A SECOND DEFECT, IN PRODUCTION.** `_pregame_features` passes
  `_get_dk_odds(conn, game_id, "h2h")` into `build_mlb_game_features`; for that
  game it returns `snapshot_type='in_play'`, −10000/+1380, stamped 19:44 UTC —
  two hours AFTER the pick, read as a pre-game price. CLAUDE.md §6's
  pre-game/in-play separation, broken in the live scoring path. It does not feed
  this model's 18 features, so it is not the bug above. Found, not fixed.

- **The control now gates the tables.** On 2026-09-07 the check printed BESIDE
  the gate tables, 13 games were missing, and the verdict was acted on anyway.
  The run now exits 2 and prints nothing without `--force`, and every drop is
  named rather than silently absent. `_refuse_tables` is extracted so it is
  testable, and its test was watched failing.

**Session summary (2026-09-08, session 259 - the edge is hours before kickoff, not days, and a routine cache refresh nearly deleted the proof):**

- mike: *"sure test relative to ncaaf props"*, *"merge 598 and buy the ncaaf
  season"*, then *"keep going, find more edge"*.

**NCAAF: tested, and the answer is no.** The 2025 prop season landed (541
events, 72,848 rows, **38,979 credits**) and the market-relative rule was graded
on it. **The placebo fails**: at the 5pp cut betonlineag ranks 2nd of 6
references (+8.31%, 52 bets) behind `hardrockbet`, a retail book (+13.16%, 114
bets); at 2pp it ranks 4th of 7. Nothing distinguishes the sharp reference.
Structurally the college board cannot support the test either - Pinnacle covers
59 of 483 games, and DraftKings posts **no two-way college quotes at all**, so
it can be neither de-vigged nor bet. One season yields ~50 bets at the cut,
where the SE on ROI is ~9pp; resolving a +8% edge needs ~1,000 bets, far more
seasons than the endpoint sells. **Do not buy 2023-24.** The credits bought a
clean negative, which is what they were spent to get.

**The finding: the rule decays with lead time.** Paired on the 433 games
carrying both boards - same seasons, same cut, offset the only difference -
`open` returns **+9.42% (379 bets, CI +1.3 to +17.4)** and `t48` returns
**-2.89% (996 bets)**. `open` is not the opening line; it is the production
polling series, **median 7.5h before kickoff**, and `PREGAME_SNAPSHOT_TYPES =
("open",)` so it is exactly what production reads. Held to the analysis bar: a
monotone plateau across cuts (2.46 -> 4.31 -> 9.83 -> 12.15%), positive in all
three seasons, and a placebo that no retail reference pair reproduces. Evidence:
`docs/nfl_prop_offset_evidence.md`.

**A correction I had to make to my own finding.** The T-72h backfill landed
late in the session and broke the clean story: paired on the 649 games carrying
it, `open` is +10.75% (CI +4.2 to +17.3), t24 +5.01%, **t48 +0.65%, t72 +4.54%**
- and pinnacle-only at t72 is +6.35% with a CI excluding zero. **T-72h beats
T-48h, so the decay is NOT monotone.** I had reported a smooth gradient and
argued from it that a 240h board must be worse still; that argument does not
hold, 48h is a trough rather than a floor, and the doc now says so. What
survives is narrower and still worth acting on: `open` is the only offset
clearing every bar.

**The window change was deliberately NOT acted on.** `NFL_PROP_WINDOW_HOURS`
went 30 -> 240 yesterday. Measured, not assumed: **all 21 NFL prop BETs written
in the 21 days to 09-08 were taken past 48h**, `nfl_prop_market`'s three at
137.6-179.8h, and under the §1c lock those are permanent at a lead time with no
positive evidence either way. Flagged for mike's decision, not reverted.

**The near miss, and the reason this session has two bug fixes in it.**
Refreshing `data/local/nfl_prop_odds.parquet` from Supabase replaced 916,965
rows with a board holding **no 2025 sharp quote at all**, and the rule promptly
re-graded at **-2.81%**. That number was an artefact of a deletion an hour
earlier. `player_prop_odds` is pruned and holds **no NFL sharp prop row older
than 2026-04-01**; 117,048 snapshots spanning 2023-2025 - the entire history the
rule is validated on - existed only in that tracked parquet. Restored as a union
so the T-48h backfill is kept too: **2,181,832 rows**.

- **The pruner never learned about the second reference.**
  `config.SHARP_BOOKMAKERS` is `"pinnacle"` alone, so when `betonlineag` became
  a de-vig reference the protection did not follow it - two hand-kept lists, one
  updated. `prune_odds` now derives the protected set from the models. The NCAAF
  backfill's 13,821 `betonlineag` rows were unprotected the same way.
- **`pull_local_cache` is additive**, and reads the parquet directly: routing it
  through `read_table` would have made it a silent no-op, since that returns
  `None` unless the cache is `activate()`d.

**A guard the dead code satisfied.** The first test of the merge exercised the
helper, not the call site, and **passed with the fix deleted from `_pull_odds`**.
`test_the_pull_ITSELF_is_additive` drives the real entry point and was watched
failing without it.

**Recorded so nobody rebuilds it.** The equal-line requirement discards 63,676
NFL propositions where sharp and soft quote two-way at different numbers.
Monotonicity recovers them model-free and conservatively - and yields **12 bets
at 5pp out of 63,676**, because the bound ignores the line gap rather than
harvesting it and so needs a double mispricing. Not built. The next rung, not
attempted, is an empirical per-market dP/dL slope from the sharp book's own
(line, fair-probability) pairs.

Verified: `3,170 passed, 16 skipped`. PR #603. #601 (CLV) merged.

**Session summary (2026-09-08, session 258 - the "tons of skipped and stale" was my own run, the NCAAF residue is gone, and a health row now says WHY in one word):**

- mike: *"go, do the residue cleanup also still tons of skipped and stale on the
  health checks? what ar we doing here"*, then *"add a column for refresh
  frequency and status reason, for exmaple if skipped, the reason wjyu"*.

**The health board first, because the answer was "that was me".** All twelve
non-OK rows on `run_date` 2026-09-08 were written at **09-07 20:29 ET by my own
prop-probe verification run** - one `checked_at`, 31 rows - under a **UTC date,
for a day that had not started** (it was 09-07 21:40 ET when he asked). Four were
STALE purely because "yesterday" had shifted a day forward; `signal_delivery` was
CRIT SKIPPED because prop-probe carries no `DISCORD_WEBHOOK_*`. Deleted.

The real last run is **09-07 21:15 ET** - the worker on merged master, correctly
ET-dated, which also confirms the `today_et()` fix landed in production. **30
rows, 5 non-OK**: three honest SKIPs (NBA offseason, the WNBA World Cup break
twice) and two real STALEs, `final_scores` and `model_calibration`. Not tons.
**The lesson is mine to carry: a verification run that writes to a production
monitoring table is not free.** I said the 6am pass would overwrite it; 6am was
ten hours away, and in the meantime the board I had just spent a session
cleaning read worse than before I touched it.

**The NCAAF residue, cleared.** Forward slate went from **36 split slots to zero
reported**:

- **5 phantom matchups deleted** (`florida_miami`, `alabama_troy`, `texas_utep`,
  `lsu_ole-miss`, `south-alabama_arkansas-state`) with their 4 NONE rows and 77
  odds rows. The script **aborts outright if any carries a BET**; none did.
- **27 childless mascot-variant rows deleted**, keeping the row whose away team
  is a registry school, guarded so a slot can never be emptied.
- **The last five held PAID odds on the wrong id, so they were MERGED, not
  dropped**: **1,211 odds rows repointed** onto the canonical id. `odds` has
  exactly one unique index and it is the surrogate PK, so a repoint cannot
  collide. Deleting them would have thrown away credits already spent.
- The one survivor is Bluffton, *"Madonna"* vs *"Madonna University (Mich.)"*,
  where **neither** name is in the registry - **the merge script refused to guess
  rather than picking one**. Division III, nothing prices it, so the check is now
  bounded to an FBS home team: the set the models score is the set where a split
  costs something.

**The identity test was lying, and only the mutation harness knew.**
`tests/test_ncaaf_game_identity` **reimplemented the check's SQL inline**, so
dropping the FBS bound and unbounding the window both went **UNCAUGHT**. It now
lifts the ACTUAL query out of the module and runs it against the sqlite fixture;
all four mutations are caught. Second instance this session of the §7 blind spot
- the first was the health-check fixture that had pinned the UTC clock.

**Then: why is this one skipped?** `detail` was already the reason, but it is a
SENTENCE, and every table that renders it clamps it to two lines - so the answer
sat inside a truncated cell. Two new columns on `system_health_checks`:

- **`reason`** - the one-word category: `gate shut`, `gate stuck`, `data behind`,
  `table empty`, `query failed`, `fresh`. A stuck gate is deliberately NOT "data
  behind": nothing was read, so the data is not the subject, and sending the
  reader to the feed is sending them to the one place the answer is not.
- **`cadence`** - the freshness bar the check applies (`daily`, `same day`,
  `every 6h`, `every 3 days`, `each run`), **DERIVED from each check's own
  argument** - `ts_check`'s `max_age_hours`, or the gap between `date_check`'s
  `min_date` and the run date. A hand-maintained frequency table drifts from the
  bar actually enforced the first time someone tunes a threshold, and then the
  column lies, which is worse than not having it.

**A bug my own test caught before it shipped.** `_cadence_from_dates` compared
`min_date >= run_date` BEFORE parsing - a STRING compare, and `"not-a-date"`
sorts after any ISO date, so an unparseable value reported **"same day"** with
total confidence. That is `.claude/rules/data-integrity.md`'s "parse timestamps
before comparing them" in the one place a wrong answer looks most plausible.
Parse first, then compare.

**And a guard its own import could satisfy.** The "the column DDL is gated" test
asserted `"schema_is_current" in body` - which the `from data.ddl_guard import
schema_is_current` line satisfies even with the guard call deleted. It went
MISSED under mutation until it was changed to assert on the CALL,
`schema_is_current(conn`. §7 again, third time this session.

**Deliberately not done:** the historical NCAAF weather backfill (now unblocked -
identity is settled), `final_scores` (NCAAF/UFC 09-05 finals still missing), and
`model_calibration`'s four overconfident models, which is a decision rather than
a fix.

**Session summary (2026-09-08, session 257 — I recommended live CLV, measured it, and withdrew it):**

- mike asked for further recommendations, agreed the order of operations, and
  said *"yes on n=75 critera."* Two of the three items did not survive contact
  with the data, which is the point of doing #1 first.

- **THE REVERSAL. I recommended instrumenting live CLV; it is not buildable, and
  the exclusion already in `paper_tracker.py` was right.** The data is entirely
  there — 101 of 101 settled live BETs since 08-24 have a later in-play DK
  snapshot, 100 at +10 minutes. But a live full-game total moves because RUNS
  ARE SCORED, so post-bet movement is a partial readout of the result: **OVER +
  line rose = 84.0% (n=25), UNDER + line rose = 0.0% (n=5)**. A CLV column filled
  this way would read strongly positive on a winning stretch for reasons
  unconnected to price. Recorded as a rule in `docs/live_betting.md` so it is not
  re-proposed, and the guard test now covers `_backfill_clv` as well as
  `_capture_clv` — the old one checked a single path, the §7 "scoped to the
  symptom already found" pattern.

- **The mis-scaled `k`/`hits` cut resolves to the refit, not a re-sweep.** A
  re-sweep on raw numbers would replace a cut swept on 95 calibrated bets with
  one swept on 35 and 13 raw ones — worse evidence, not better. #572's cap
  already bounds the damage to 2/day each, which is what makes waiting for the
  n≥150 refit (~09-12 to 09-15) the correct path rather than a dodge.

- **Dated review criteria written before the data arrives** (`docs/thresholds.md`):
  `k` and `outs` pause at n=75 on their current artifacts if still below
  breakeven; `mlb_live_total_runs` re-sweeps at n=150 since 08-31. Breakeven is
  the mean DK implied probability of the bets actually taken, not a fixed 52.4% —
  these models bet behind −140 floors. Deliberately NOT enforced in code: a dict
  nothing reads is a guard dead code satisfies.

- **The platform number needed an era split before it meant anything.** A naive
  30-day query says −32.5u over 461 bets; on current artifacts and current cuts
  only it is **−16.99u over 263, −6.5%, z = −1.16** — losing, not significantly.
  The difference is `mlb_over_under` (−11.78u, already paused) and `k`'s
  PRE-retrain artifact (−13.49u at z −2.36, replaced 09-04). `k`'s current
  version is z = −0.86. Across ~12 models you expect ~0.6 hits at p<0.05 by
  chance, so no model is significant in either direction.

- **Push demoted, not fixed.** 0 `device_push_tokens` against a 2,231-row
  `push_sent` ledger looks like a broken notifier, but there are **0 auth users
  and 0 subscriptions** — nothing is being missed. It is an untested path, to be
  exercised before the first real user rather than tonight.

- **`wnba_prop_player_assists` checked and cleared.** −4.67u over 10 looked like
  evidence against the map promoted earlier that day; calibration has never been
  applied to any of its bets (0 rows) and its last pick was 08-30, so those
  losses are what the map exists to correct.

**Session summary (2026-09-08, session 257 - golf retired properly, the health clock was UTC, and NCAAF is writing games nobody will play):**

- mike: *"retire golf for now, drop the check"*, then *"do all the tasks"*.

**Golf, retired with its reason attached.** The 09-03 pause asked why golf never
fires; the answer is that the feed was never switched on. **`DATAGOLF_API_KEY` is
not set on the Railway worker**, and `run_pipeline._golf_enabled()` no-ops every
golf step without it - so GOLF held **zero** rows in `games`, `golf_odds` and
`picks`, ever. Five models to `RETIRED_MODELS`, out of every registry and
threshold dict, out of `FEATURE_MAP`, out of the mobile mirror, and the
`golf_odds` health check gone with them. `docs/sports/golf.md` is now the
revival runbook; nothing was deleted.

**Retiring the models is not retiring the sport, and only the UX review caught
the difference.** GOLF was still a tappable chip on Picks, Stats and Models
leading to three dead ends - an empty board promising *"Lines refresh hourly
6am-6pm ET"*, *"leaderboards are on the way"*, and a models list calling golf
PAUSED. The daily recap kept a chip that could only answer "nothing cleared the
bar", and `useTodayPicks` still fetched the golf field on every load, where a
timeout printed *"Couldn't load the upcoming golf field"* on EVERY sport's
board. **Nothing rendered blank; everything rendered a wrong explanation**,
which is the worse failure and the one a diff review cannot see.

**The health clock was the container's, not ET.** `run_system_health` derived
`run_date` from `datetime.now()`, which is UTC anywhere `TZ` is unset. `worker`
sets it; `prop-probe` does not - so a verification run there wrote a 2026-09-08
row set at 20:29 ET on 09-07 and called `umpires` STALE for a day that had not
finished. Found by tripping it. Now `config.today_et()`.

**And the test fixture had PINNED the bug.** An earlier fix made the fixture use
the same naive `datetime.now()` as the check, which only worked because the
machine running the suite happened to be ET. That is §7's blind-spot rule in a
new costume: the fixture was written from the same understanding as the code, so
it agreed with it instead of testing it.

**`umpires` is not failing - I said it was last session and that was wrong.**
Every STALE is from a resolved outage (the table was frozen 2026-07-12 to
08-15); coverage has been 15-of-15 games daily since, and every real pipeline
run since 2026-08-22 is OK. The one recent STALE was my own wrong-dated run.

**The NCAAF finding, which is the big one.** Chasing "4 NCAAF games missing
weather" (also wrong - it is ~55% of past dates and 7 of 123 on the forward
slate) turned up **76 duplicated (date, home_team) slots across 155 `games`
rows**, 08-29 to 11-21. Two mechanisms, one signature:

- **Split identity.** Two feeds build NCAAF `game_id`s from resolved school
  names. CFBD writes its canonical school; the odds ingestor writes whatever
  `resolve_odds_api_school` makes of The Odds API's mascot-appended string. When
  they disagree the same real game gets two rows, and they do not split evenly -
  **the odds-side row collects every odds snapshot and every pick, the CFBD-side
  row collects the FINAL**. That is exactly why six live BETs sat unsettled on
  09-04/05 (session 253). Nothing was lost; the halves were on different ids.
- **Phantom opponents.** The longest-prefix rule could substitute a DIFFERENT
  school: `NCAAF_2026-09-10_florida_miami` (real: Florida A&M @ Miami),
  `..._alabama_troy` (Alabama State), `..._texas_utep` (Texas Southern),
  `..._lsu_ole-miss` (Wofford). These are matchups nobody will play, **and they
  are still being scored** - the Miami row was written 09-04 and picked up four
  fresh NONE rows at 01:10 on 09-08.

**The root cause is already fixed at source, and the timestamp proves it.**
`ncaaf_teams` was re-ingested at **2026-09-07 23:25:06** and now holds 683
schools (138 FBS + FCS), every one with a mascot - so the exact-school and
"school mascot" rules catch these before the prefix rule is ever reached. Rows
written at 22:30 that day are still mascot-appended; rows written at 23:30 are
correct. No NEW phantom can form. What remains is residue.

**Verified no money was double-counted.** All 11 BETs touching a duplicated slot
sit on exactly ONE id each - the duplication splits odds and weather, it never
doubled a wager.

**What was built for it:** `ncaaf_game_identity`, a CRIT health check bounded to
the forward slate (a duplicate on a played game is history; one on Saturday's
card splits this week's odds). One query catches BOTH mechanisms, deliberately,
so a new flavour of mis-resolution cannot slip past a signature-specific test.
Ungated, so no feed outage can silence it.

**Deliberately not done: the residue cleanup.** Deleting or merging `games` rows
on a LIVE forward slate is a production mutation with picks pointing at it, and
doing it in the tail of a long session is how §1c gets broken by accident. The
four phantom rows carry NONE rows only - no money at risk - and the new CRIT
check now surfaces the condition every morning. Handed over with the measured
list.

**Also left, and why:** `my_access()` / `has_app_access()` confirmed absent from
`pg_proc` (dormant - all three feature flags default false); the
`supabase_admin` default-ACL entry still grants `anon=arwdDxtm` on new objects
and is **not fixable from this project** (the entry is owned by `supabase_admin`,
which we cannot assume); the CVD palette check still needs the resolved
`--chart-1..5` values; and an inert `HEALTH_VERIFY_RUN` variable is left on
prop-probe because the Railway MCP has no delete-variable call.

**Session summary (2026-09-08, session 256 - three of the four skipped health checks were honest, one gate had never opened, and a fifth check was being thrown away):**

- mike: *"I see health checks routinely skipped? why? lets fix this"*

**Measured before anything was touched.** Four checks were SKIPPED on the
2026-09-07 run. Over 60 days:

| Check | Runs | SKIPPED | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| `golf_odds` | 59 | **59** | **Never once produced a verdict**, from its first row on 2026-07-04. GOLF holds **zero** rows in `games`, `golf_odds` and `picks`, and all five golf models are paused. |
| `nba_game_log` | 59 | 59 | Honest. The next scheduled NBA `games` row is **2026-10-20**, so this is a real offseason and it will un-skip on its own. |
| `wnba_game_log` | 59 | 8 (since 09-03) | Honest. |
| `espn_wnba_api` | 30 | 5 (since 09-03) | Honest. |

**The WNBA pair looked like the bug and was not, which is the whole reason to
measure.** `games` jumps from 2026-08-30 straight to 2026-09-17 - an 18-day hole
in the middle of a season that runs to late September. The 2026 season pauses
for the **FIBA Women's World Cup (Sept 4-13)** and resumes **Sept 17**, which is
exactly where the rows resume. Two independent confirmations: the odds feed
(which is what CREATES WNBA `games` rows) is being polled daily and returns only
the Sept 17/18 fixtures, and `wnba_player_game_log` stops at 08-30 in step. Had
this been called a broken schedule ingest and "fixed", the fix would have been
inventing games that were not played.

**So three of four skips were correct and one was a gate that had never opened -
and nothing in the report told them apart.** `.claude/rules/operations.md`
already forbids gating a check on the thing it detects, which fixed the
CIRCULAR gates; it says nothing about a gate that is simply shut forever. One
SKIPPED line looks exactly like the next.

**A fifth finding, found while reading the persist path.** `model_calibration`
was computed, logged, and then **dropped**: the INSERT loop ran BEFORE that
block, so `system_health_checks` held **zero** `model_calibration` rows, ever,
while the only surfaces that read the table - Claude mobile, the ops dashboard -
were told nothing. Its very first stored row, once fixed, is a real finding:
**four models 8pp+ overconfident on the live record** - `wnba_prop_player_points`
+14.5pp/206, `mlb_prop_pitcher_walks` +12.1pp/204, `mlb_prop_pitcher_er`
+10.7pp/423, `wnba_prop_player_rebounds` +10.1pp/382.

**What was built.** `SKIP_BUDGET_DAYS` plus `_skip_run_days` /
`_apply_skip_budgets`: every SKIPPED result now carries how many **calendar**
days its streak spans - days, not rows, because a day the pipeline never ran
leaves no row and counting rows would under-report exactly the outage that
stopped it. Past a per-check budget, derived from that sport's real dark window
in `games` rather than recalled, the status becomes STALE and names the gate as
the suspect. The streak reads `system_health_checks`, deliberately the one table
no feed can silence, and fails open. The persist moved after every check, with a
rollback on the calibration query so an aborted transaction cannot discard a run.
`golf_odds`' gate note now distinguishes "no tournament this week" from "this
sport has never ingested anything".

**Verified on the running system, not from the tests** (CLAUDE.md 7 - a guard is
not evidence). Ran on the Railway worker against production:

- `golf_odds` -> **STALE**, *"gate shut 67 days running, past this check's
  30-day budget -- suspect the GATE, not the feed. Gate said: games holds no
  GOLF row at all"*
- `nba_game_log` -> SKIPPED, *"(skipped 67 days running)"* - correctly still quiet
- the WNBA pair -> SKIPPED, *"(skipped 6 days running)"* - correctly still quiet
- `model_calibration` -> its first row in the table, ever

**A mutation harness that was itself lying.** Eight guards were checked by
mutating the source and watching each fail. The first run reported one MISS -
and the run could not be trusted at all, because rewriting a module several
times a second left a `.pyc` whose mtime still looked current, so pytest
imported the PREVIOUS mutation. With `__pycache__` cleared between iterations
the MISS was real and worth having: the "every budgeted check is a real check
name" test grepped the whole source, found the typo'd key **inside its own
declaration**, and passed - §7's *"a guard that dead code can satisfy is not a
guard"* in a new costume. The declaration is now cut out before the search.

**Deliberately not done.** The golf question is left to Matt: GOLF is listed as
a live sport in CLAUDE.md 8 with five registered models, and has zero data and
zero picks. The check now says so daily at WARN rather than being silently
retired, because retiring a sport is not Claude's call. `umpires` was noticed in
passing - 29 of 53 runs STALE/EMPTY over 60 days - and NOT investigated here.

**Session summary (2026-09-07, session 255 - the roster called seven working models broken, and "rules based" was only half the answer):**

- mike: *"some models still say no artifact when I want them to say rules based"*

**What the roster was doing.** `getModelRoster.ts` judged every model by its
`model_registry` row: `CASE WHEN r.version IS NULL THEN 'NO ARTIFACT'`, rendered
red, with a banner reading *"N models are registered and un-paused but have no
trained artifact - they cannot score."* Sixteen models were in that state.

**What is actually true, measured rather than inferred.** The obvious list to
reach for was `tracking/system_health.KNOWN_UNTRAINED`, and it is the wrong one -
its own comment says these models *"intentionally have no trained artifact
**yet**"*, which is awaiting-training, not rules-based. The real split came from
reading each model's scoring path:

| Models | How they score | Registry row |
|---|---|---|
| `nfl_wind_totals`, `nfl_opener_spread`, `nfl_live_prop`, `nfl_prop_market`, `wnba_prop_market` | A **frozen measured rule** - a `CALIBRATED_UNDER_RATE` lookup, a soft-vs-Pinnacle deviation, a frozen bias constant, a de-vigged sharp price. No ML artifact exists anywhere. | none, correctly |
| `ncaaf_live_win_prob`, `ncaaf_live_total` | A **trained two-stage LightGBM**, loaded by `ncaaf_live/serve.py` from `ncaaf_live/data/artifacts/` - off-registry because that package runs on Matt's machine on gamedays. | none, by design |
| `nba_over_under`, `nba_spread`, `nhl_over_under`, `nhl_puckline` | Nothing. Un-paused, expecting an artifact, haven't got one. | **missing - a real fault** |
| `golf_*` (5) | Nothing, and they are PAUSED. | missing, but paused |

So "rules based" was right for five and **wrong for two**. Labelling a LightGBM
booster a rule would have been a second wrong label shipped to fix the first, so
the engine pair got its own state.

**The trap that nearly landed.** The tempting derivation is
`set(ACTION_THRESHOLDS) - (set(MODELS) | set(PROP_MODELS))` - the models that do
not go through the generic scorer. That set has **eight** members, and the eighth
is `mlb_live_total_runs`, which **does** carry a registry artifact
(`models/saved/mlb_live_total_runs_20260614_075301.pkl`, `is_active=1`, loaded by
`models/live_scorer.py`). Shipping the derivation directly would have declared it
rules-based and hidden any future outage of it behind a green label. It is
declared `"artifact"` explicitly instead.

**What was built.** `config.SCORING_METHODS` declares the answer
(`"artifact" | "rule" | "engine"`), `config.scoring_method()` reads it, and
`data/threshold_sync.py` mirrors it into a new
`model_action_thresholds.scoring_method` column so the dashboard reads config
truth rather than a list copied into a SQL string. The `ALTER TABLE` is gated on
`data.ddl_guard.schema_is_current` - threshold_sync runs on every daily pass, and
ungated write-time DDL is what cost 11.6h of database time and ~3,600 forced
PostgREST cache reloads (`.claude/rules/operations.md`).

**Why the test is shaped the way it is.** This map rots silently: a new lane
lands in `ACTION_THRESHOLDS`, nobody classifies it, it inherits the `"artifact"`
default and shows up red. So `tests/test_scoring_methods.py` asserts coverage in
**both directions** against the derivation rather than against a copied list, and
sweeps every feature map so a `"rule"` model cannot quietly acquire a generic
scoring path. Per CLAUDE.md 1b, all five guards were **watched failing** under
mutation (drop a classification / add a stale one / a bad method value / flip the
default to `"rule"` / mislabel `mlb_live_total_runs`) before being kept.

**The dashboard.** States are now LIVE 40, RULES BASED 5, ENGINE 2, PAUSED 19,
NO ARTIFACT 4. **PAUSED moved to the front of the `CASE`**, which fixes a
pre-existing mislabel of its own: the five paused golf models had been reading
NO ARTIFACT, so the red banner's *"registered and un-paused"* was false about
five of the sixteen it counted. With the reorder the banner counts exactly the
four genuinely broken models, and says so for the first time.

**Deliberately not done.** No threshold, pause, feature list or artifact changed,
so CLAUDE.md 1b did not require an `Updated-By` trailer - it scopes the stamp to
model updates, and this is reporting plumbing. Raised as a judgement call;
**mike's answer was to stamp it anyway** (*"stamp it and merge to master"*), so
the landing commit carries `Updated-By: mike`. Worth keeping as the precedent:
the roster's state column is how a model that cannot score gets noticed, and the
stamp costs nothing when the call is ambiguous.
`tracking/system_health.KNOWN_UNTRAINED` was left alone - its
`expected` set is built from `MODELS | PROP_MODELS`, which never contained the
rule or engine models, so the health check was never wrong about them. The mobile
app selects an explicit column list from `model_action_thresholds`
(`mobile/src/lib/queries.ts:1628`), so the new column is invisible to it and no
app change was needed.

---

**Session summary (2026-09-07, session 254 — "highest conviction plays only": the filter does not exist, and on the live lane it is inverted):**

- mike: *"20 per day still seems way too high, run the analysis, want the highest
  conviction plays only."* The full measurement is `docs/mlb_volume_efficiency.md` §11.

- **The request assumes the board can be sorted by conviction. It cannot.** Graded
  by claimed-probability band (08-24 → 09-06, priced BETs), the pre-game top band
  is the WORST: 0.65-0.70 is +6.52u/42 and the only positive band, while 0.70-0.75
  is −1.35u/28 and 0.75+ is −0.58u/9. The overclaim GROWS with the claim —
  −5.4pp at 0.65-0.70 against −20.9pp at 0.75+.

- **On the live lane the EV sort is inverted, in both halves.** Ranking each day's
  `mlb_live_total_runs` BETs by claimed EV: top-2/day is −4.34u then −0.91u
  (−5.25u/26, −20.2% ROI); rank 5+ is +5.58u then +4.21u (+9.79u/49, +20.0%). A
  live cap that kept the highest-EV picks would keep precisely the losing ones.
  The interpretable form is price: −110-or-longer wins 42.9% in BOTH halves
  (−2.26u/14), −111..−160 is positive in both (+4.29u/80).

- **Volume, measured rather than projected.** Last 10 days: live 9.1/day, `k` 6.7,
  `hits` 4.6, `outs` 2.8, `f5` 0.9. Replaying the merged dedupe + cap over
  09-01 → 09-07 takes pitcher props from **17.6 → 8.1/day** (5-12 range), so the
  board lands near 18/day and pre-game stops being the larger half. The worker
  deployed that code at 21:58Z today; every 09-07 pitcher-prop BET was written
  01:54-15:20Z, so **09-08 is the first board it governs**.

- **Found, not fixed — a live threshold defect.** `k` (0.58/0.08) and `hits`
  (0.54/0.08) carry cuts their own config comments describe as swept on CALIBRATED
  probabilities, and `model_calibration.method` is now NULL for both, so they
  decide on raw numbers against a calibrated bar. Over 08-31 → 09-04 `hits` spanned
  raw 0.321-0.776 against calibrated 0.423-0.577 — a 0.54 cut means a sliver on one
  number and half the board on the other. This is a CONSEQUENCE of the
  endorsed-only migration approved this morning: both were promoted, went inert on
  09-05 via the §2 defect, and the migration demoted them as `unfitted, 26/150` and
  `47/150` — too few graded since the retrain, not a failed `transfers`. The
  migration was right; the mis-scaled cut it leaves standing is a decision for mike.

- **Corrected mid-session, on the advisor's challenge, three claims that were
  inference rather than measurement:** "not deployed yet" (Railway says deployed —
  the board simply predates it); "never promoted" (they were, then demoted today,
  for a different reason than I first wrote); and the live record, which is
  **−0.26u/69, −0.4%** on the current 0.70 cut, not the +1.88u/96 an 08-24 window
  reports by including pre-cut bets.

- **Then mike reaffirmed:** *"I just want the strongest ev picks for highest
  overall profitability."* Caveat given once and overridden, so it is his call —
  swept it properly (§12). An EV FLOOR is not supported: differencing the
  cumulative sweep gives marginal buckets that alternate sign ([0.24,0.26)
  −6.24u, [0.26,0.28) +3.83u, [0.28,0.30) −2.01u), so the +12.7% at 0.26 is a
  peak between two troughs. A DEPTH is supported, and it is the mechanism
  already shipped: top-N by claimed EV per model per day. **Top 2 is the maximum
  on BOTH units and ROI** — +6.96u / +16.2% / 3.1 per day, against +2.12u at
  top 1 and +4.00u at top 3.

- **Shipped:** `PROP_MAX_SIGNALS_PER_DAY` 3 → **2**, and `mlb_prop_pitcher_outs`
  added — it had been firing uncapped at up to 6/day while the config comment
  said the cap was complete. The coverage test that missed it asserted a written
  list; it is now derived from `PROP_ONE_BET_PER_PLAYER` minus `PAUSED_MODELS`,
  so unpausing `er` or `walks` fails it. Both new tests watched failing against
  the old config first.

- **Declined the per-model optimum on mike's behalf and said so.** hits 2 / outs
  uncapped / k paused scores +13.58u against the uniform 2's +6.96u, but it is
  three parameters fitted to n=14/23/44 in one window. The uniform rule gives up
  ~2u of in-sample profit to avoid the overfit.

- **Live stays unfiltered, and the reason is now a number.** On its current cut
  (n=69) claimed EV spans only 0.321-0.385; split at the median the top half is
  −4.99u and the bottom +4.72u. A floor cannot bite and a top-N selects the
  losing half — the inversion found a third way, on the current-cut population.

- **Worktree hygiene.** The docs commit was made while this shared tree sat on
  another session's `nfl-prop-soft-books` branch and would have ridden into
  their PR. Moved to `mlb-ev-top2` off `origin/master` via a separate
  `git worktree`, leaving their tree untouched. Local `master` was 8 commits
  behind `origin/master` at the time.

- **Deliberately not done.** No prob/edge threshold, and no pause. The `k`/`hits`
  mis-scaled-cut decision from §11.6 is still open, and `k` losing at every EV
  depth is evidence for that conversation, not a pause taken here.
**Session summary (2026-09-08, session 252, fifth part — "surprising you cannot find edge. go harder": two new levers on the 13-book backfill, both null):**

- Matt: *"any other ncaaf picks for this week? have we run everything?
  surpising you cannot find no edge. go harder"*

**This week: one pick** (Kennesaw State vs Georgia State Over 54.5), and
everything that exists ran: the totals rule on every DK-priced game, the
spread rule declined on every one (its opener preconditions fail on this
feed), moneyline paused, live lanes fire Saturday. The one lever "all books"
most obviously means — Pinnacle de-vigged vs DK at a shared instant — was run
tonight by another session on this backfill (PR #581): NCAAF negative on h2h
and spreads, flat on totals.

**Two shapes nobody had run, both built and run tonight
(`scripts/ncaaf_search/steam_lag.py`, `consensus_gate.py`; rows in the
search table):**

1. *Steam lag* — not "DK sits off Pinnacle" but "Pinnacle MOVED and DK's
   stored line is older". The backfill carries it by construction (each row's
   `snapshot_at` is that book's last_update). Totals at k=1 point: 407 bets,
   52.1% [0.472, 0.569]; spreads 48-53% at every k; the "DK followed" control
   sits at 50%. DK's close does drift toward Pinnacle after a lag (39% toward
   vs 22% away, +0.29 points on totals), so the lag is real and worth a third
   of a point of CLV — which does not clear -110.
2. *Consensus anchor and dispersion* — gate the totals rule on the 13-book
   median (or Pinnacle) instead of DK: 56.9% / 55.4% vs DK's 56.4%, the same
   bets, because DK sits a mean 0.16 points from the median. Books disagree
   by 1.5+ points in 17% of games and it changes nothing.

**What that says.** On this feed the NCAAF game-line market is one number
across thirteen books. There is no cross-book edge to find in NCAAF totals or
spreads; what edge exists is model-versus-market (the totals rule), it is
marginal, and its record is early-season (58.7-59.9% first half, 50.8-52.7%
second half, on every anchor). Where edge could still be: NCAAF player props
(ungradeable until the stats backfill queued in #581 lands), the live lanes,
and a better fundamentals model — the search that closed on 08-27 found none.

**Session summary (2026-09-08, session 252, fourth part — "why do you just stop work when database drops connection": reconnect in the wrapper, one game per transaction, and the ops log gets its reasons):**

- Matt: *"why do you just stop work when database drops connection, globally
  we need to add auto retry to pick up where we left off, I also want to know
  about the failures in the ops log (discord and dashboard) and the plan to
  rectify"*

**What happened at 01:30:57Z, measured.** Two processes lost their database
connection within the same second: my local dry run (`server closed the
connection unexpectedly` on Duke–Miami, then `connection already closed` on
every statement after) and a worker scoring pass that had started at
01:16:41Z (`dispatch:scoring` error at 01:31:00Z, 859s). The worker pass
left NO row of its own in `pipeline_log`: its handler does `rollback()` then
writes the error row on the same connection, and the connection was dead.
The 01:30 pass on the NEXT deployment completed ("Scoring complete: 3 BETs |
73 AVOIDs | 228.9s", 969 games in the window, 774 rows). Cause of the drop
not established: the Supabase log query returned "Backend error" twice.

**Why one drop cost a whole pass.** `run_scorer` was ONE transaction from the
housekeeping DELETE to the final commit (two `conn.commit()` calls, both
after the loop). So a drop or a redeploy at game 60 of 70 rolled back sixty
games' work, and the pass could not resume because nothing had landed.

**The ops log, three days, from `pipeline_runs` / `pipeline_log` /
`system_health_checks`:**

| Failure | Count | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| `aborted` passes | 23 | the worker died mid-pass: 20 redeploys between 23:32Z and 01:49Z alone, one per merge |
| `health-check` | 18 | the step returns False whenever a CRIT exists; ONE CRIT in the window (`model_artifacts_load`, 10 NFL prop artifacts, 09-07 03:35) — the other 17 reasons are not recorded anywhere queryable |
| `lineups` | 14 | reason not recorded (the step logs it to stdout and returns False) |
| `scoring`: statement timeout "while deleting tuple in relation picks" | 35 in 7 days, mostly at :20 | the housekeeping DELETE waiting on row locks held by another scorer's pass-long transaction, or scanning: EXPLAIN shows a Seq Scan over 137,630 open non-BET rows per pass |
| `scoring`: `OperationalError connection to server…` per model | 7 in 7 days | connection drops mid-pass, each costing the pass |
| `player-news-refresh`, `public-betting`, `odds`, `prop-odds`, `nba-prop-odds` | 3–8 each | reasons not recorded; the 09-06 cluster was EMAXCONNSESSION, fixed by the transaction-pooler switch that day |

The common thread: the table that alerts (`pipeline_log`) said "step returned
False (see the step's own log for the reason)", and the step's own log is a
container that had been redeployed by the time anyone looked.

**What changed (PR: reconnect, per-game commit, reasons).**

- `data/db.py`: `DBConnection` keeps its URL and a dirty flag. A lost
  connection (SQLSTATE 08xxx / 57P01-03, the driver's connection classes,
  `InterfaceError`, or `conn.closed`) with NO uncommitted writes reconnects
  (3 attempts, backoff) and re-runs the one statement; with uncommitted
  writes it reconnects and raises `ConnectionLost` so the caller re-runs its
  unit of work. `rollback()` on a dead connection reconnects instead of
  raising — that is what lets an error handler write its record. A
  `statement_timeout` (57014) is NOT a lost connection and is never retried:
  re-issuing a timed-out 120s DELETE on a fresh connection is the wrong
  reflex. `tests/test_db_reconnect.py`, watched failing on the old wrapper
  (4 failed, 6 errors).
- `models/scorer.py`: ONE GAME = ONE TRANSACTION. Each game clears its own
  non-BET rows (`WHERE game_id = %s`, an index scan on `idx_picks_game`),
  scores every model, commits. `ConnectionLost` re-runs that game once, then
  records it as a failure; the pass continues. The housekeeping DELETE became
  a SWEEP after the loop for games the loop did not re-score (`NOT IN
  rescored`), in its own transaction with `SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '10s'`;
  if it cannot lock, it yields and the next pass sweeps. Same window bound,
  same started-game and live-row guards.
  `tests/test_scorer_commits_per_game.py`, watched failing on origin/master
  (5 of 6).
- `run_pipeline._timed_step`: a loguru sink captures the LAST error line the
  step logs and stores it in `pipeline_log.error_msg` in place of "see the
  step's own log". `tests/test_pipeline_log_reason.py`, watched failing (3
  of 4).
- Separate PR #591 (plumbing, Matt merges): Railway `watchPatterns` so a
  docs-, app- or session-log-only merge no longer restarts the worker.

**§1b, assessed against the other scorers.** The prop scorers
(`run_prop_scorer`, `run_wnba_prop_scorer`, `run_nba_prop_scorer`) and the
NFL prop scorer share `data/db.py`, so they get the reconnect for free; their
commit cadence was not changed in this PR and is the next thing to look at
with the same question (how much work does one drop cost?).

**Index added the same night (Matt: "add the index").** `idx_picks_open_nonbet`
on `picks (game_id) WHERE result IS NULL AND signal_type <> 'BET' AND is_live
IS NOT TRUE`, created CONCURRENTLY on production in 0.8s (1 MB) and recorded
as `data/migrations/picks_open_nonbet_index_2026_09_08.sql`. EXPLAIN after:
the sweep is a nested loop over the ~700 window games with an index scan per
game, in place of the Seq Scan over 137,630 open non-BET rows. PR #591 (watch
paths) merged.

**Not done.** The 17 health-check and 14 lineups reasons are unknowable
until the reason capture has run for a day; the DELETE's Seq Scan over the
open non-BET rows is a candidate for a partial index once the per-game
change has shown whether the timeouts were locks or scans.

**Session summary (2026-09-07, session 252, third part — "it should be whenever lines are released": the NCAAF lead limit removed and the window widened to the season):**

- Matt, on the 5-day limit shipped an hour earlier: *"it should not be an
  arbitrary 5 days, where are you getting that shit? similar to other models
  it should be whenever lines are released. speed speed speed is what matters
  to get a good line"* — and *"need to look at all books, not just DK man,
  come on we've discussed this"*.

**Where the 5 came from, owned.** It was the longest lead the backfill could
grade with a full slate — a property of the sample, presented as a limit. The
measurement said timing does not move the win rate, so the limit should follow
the direction, and the direction is "at release".

**What changed (`Updated-By: matt`).** `NCAAF_TOTALS_MAX_LEAD_DAYS` 5 → inf:
the guard stays in the scorer for an env-bounded experiment, never triggers in
production. `NCAAF_SCORE_AHEAD_DAYS` 7 → 150, with the app mirror
`NCAAF_AHEAD_DAYS` in `useTodayPicks.ts` moved with it (§1b: the surfaces are
identical). Measured before choosing: the live feed held DK totals on 107 NCAAF
games, 44 more than 14 days out (marquee games listed to 12-12) — the 7-day
window was clipping every one; the scorer's DK-price prefilter is what admits a
game, so the wide window costs nothing on unpriced games.
`test_score_when_lines_open` re-pinned to 150 with the reason.

**"All books", measured on the backfill (13 books, 2023-2025, 2,651 games):**
every book's first NCAAF total lands a median 5.4-5.9 days before kickoff
(William Hill 725 firsts, FanDuel 620, DK 540); the gap between the earliest
book and DK is a median 0.0 days, 90th percentile 0.62, and only 205 games
(7.7%) had any book a full day ahead of DK. On SPEED, all books buys nothing:
the lines are released together, and firing at release — this change — is
the whole of it. On PRICE, `picks.best_*` already records the best line across
the 13 books for the betslip. Deciding on a non-DK line is a change to the §6
invariant (every threshold was swept on DK-implied edge) and is Matt's call,
not a rider on this PR; noted in Decisions.

**Live confirmation of the earlier change.** The worker's first pass after
the 5-day deploy wrote Kennesaw State vs Georgia State Over 54.5 at −105 (P
0.655) at 01:10:49Z, the same pick the dry run showed; §1c locks it there.

**What fired once #583 was live (worker pass 01:30:49Z, 107 DK-priced games
in the window, 70 scored past the FBS gate):** three more totals BETs, all
on marquee games DK had listed since 09-01 that the 7-day window had never
let the scorer see — Alabama vs Texas A&M Under 54.5 at −112 (10-24, P
0.719), Ole Miss vs Mississippi State Over 57.5 at −105 (11-27, P 0.667),
Navy vs Army Over 37.5 at −108 (12-12, P 0.671). All four published to
Discord and push at 21:35 ET (`push_sent`, kinds new_bet + discord_signal),
locked at those lines. The UX review of the app mirror (PR #584, merged,
OTA build succeeded) found the card's `games` read unpaged at 924 rows
against the 1,000-row cap and its DK-odds read unscoped by sport; both fixed
before the season window could truncate them. Unmeasured and stated: a
line released ten weeks out has no walk-forward behind it — the backfill
held 84 such games — so these three are the first test of that lead.

**Session summary (2026-09-07, session 252 continued — "shouldn't we capitalize on lines early? Friday is too late": the totals rule measured at 0-5 days out):**

- Matt: *"shouldnt we capitalize on lines early? i feel like friday is too late"*

**The question was unmeasured, and the backfill that landed tonight is what
measures it.** `ncaaf_over_under` fires only inside `NCAAF_TOTALS_MAX_LEAD_DAYS`
= 1 because the rule was validated against the archive's close and the doc
said, honestly, "earlier may well be better — it is simply not measured".
`scripts/ncaaf_search/totals_lead.py` measures it, house rules (definitions
in the docstring before any number, per season, Wilson vs 0.5238, time split):

- Prediction: the shipped walk-forward (fit on every earlier frame season,
  predict the held-out one; 2023 on 5,259 rows, 2024 on 6,023, 2025 on 6,798).
  The prediction is fundamentals-only, so only DK's line changes with lead.
- Lead d: DK's total as of 14:00Z d days before the kickoff's ET date, from the
  `source='odds_api_historical'` backfill (34,071 pre-kick DK totals rows,
  2,640 games). Close = DK's last pre-kick row (a 14:00Z or 23:00Z pull, so up to ~10
  hours early for a noon game). Archive = the frame's
  `_total_line`, the number the published 55.9% was measured on.
- Join: frame (CFBD id) to odds (ingestor id) on season + both teams within
  ±1 day, because a night game has two `games` rows. 2,194 of 2,309 frame
  games matched (502 of them only through the ±1-day alias); 115 unmatched.

**Result: flat.** At the shipped ±8 gate, pooled 2023-2025:

| lead | bets | win% | Wilson 95% | close−lead toward pick |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0d | 307 | 56.4% | [0.508, 0.618] | −0.03 |
| 1d | 308 | 55.2% | [0.496, 0.607] | −0.06 |
| 2d | 301 | 53.8% | [0.482, 0.594] | +0.07 |
| 3d | 289 | 54.0% | [0.482, 0.596] | +0.01 |
| 4d | 264 | 55.3% | [0.493, 0.612] | −0.12 |
| 5d | 232 | 55.6% | [0.492, 0.619] | −0.06 |
| DK close | 314 | 56.4% | [0.508, 0.617] | 0 |
| archive close | 309 | 57.3% | [0.517, 0.627] | +0.18 |

Every interval overlaps every other; none clears 0.5238 at 95%. The movement
column is the CLV evidence and it is zero at every lead: the market does not
drift toward the model between Tuesday and kickoff (mean |close − 5d line| is
1.44 points, but with no sign relative to the pick). Fewer bets at longer
leads is mostly fewer games LISTED (1,910 at 5d vs 2,194); per-game
qualification drifts from 14.0% at 0d to 12.2% at 5d.

Simulating what shipping a limit of L actually does — bet the first 14:00Z
snapshot that clears ±8, locked under §1c (a proxy: production passes run
more often): L=0 321 bets 57.0% [0.515, 0.623]; L=5 384
bets 55.7% [0.507, 0.606]. Twenty percent more bets, 1.3pp lower, inside the
noise. Per season at L=5: 2023 52.4%, 2024 61.5%, 2025 55.4%. Time halves at
fixed leads run 52-58% early and 52-60% late with no sign flip. Gate sweep
6-10 is flat at every lead (52-60%, no plateau that beats 8).

**Verdict.** Earlier is not measurably worse and not measurably better, and
there is no CLV to capture: the lead limit is a timing choice, not an edge
choice. So Matt's preference decides it. Proposed in its own PR with his stamp:
`NCAAF_TOTALS_MAX_LEAD_DAYS` 1 → 5, which puts the same picks on the board
Monday/Tuesday instead of Friday. The honest caveat travels with it: the rule
as a whole is marginal out of sample (57.0% [0.515, 0.623] on game day), and a
5-day lead does not change that either way.

**Not done, deliberately.** The sharp-versus-soft harness (Pinnacle de-vigged
vs DK at a shared timestamp) is the next piece and the one that could add
edge rather than move timing; the backfill now holds 13 books at two snapshots
a day for it.

**Session summary (2026-09-07, session 253 - sixteen unsettled BETs, four fixed windows, and an FCS visitor that became an FBS school):**

- mike: *"why are there unsettled picks across the board? they should be
  settled, most recent the 6 ncaaf ones over the past few days? look at all
  unsettled and settle them, what causes unsettled picks"*

**What "unsettled" is, measured.** 58 BET rows carry `result IS NULL`. 42 are
the current slate (09-07 MLB in progress, NFL week 1 from 09-09) and are
correctly pending. **16 have a game_date before today**, and those are the
question. `NONE` and `AVOID` rows never carry a `result` by design - they are
graded by `mv_scored_pick_outcomes`, not by `settle_picks` - so "unsettled
across the board" in the app is those 16 plus the day's slate.

**The four causes.** Each is a FIXED window that a pick fell outside of, and
none of them could self-heal:

| Picks | What | Why it never settled |
|---|---|---|
| 6 NCAAF `ncaaf_live_total`, 09-04/05 | The odds ingestor resolved the FCS visitor to the FBS school its name starts with: "Indiana State Sycamores" → **Indiana**, "Utah Tech" → **Utah**, "Houston Christian" → **Houston**, "North Carolina A&T" → **North Carolina**, "Tennessee State" → **Tennessee**, "Northwestern State" → **Northwestern** (and "Arkansas-Pine Bluff" → **Arkansas** on 09-03, NONE rows only). `ncaaf_teams` is `/teams/fbs` - 139 schools - so the resolver's "longest school that prefixes the input" rule had nothing longer to prefer. | The games row says Indiana @ Purdue; CFBD's final sits on `indiana-state_purdue`; the alias mirror requires the same slug pair. The live loop priced the REAL game (it reads pregame spread/total plus live state - no team-identity feature, `ncaaf_live/serve.py`), so the bets are bets of record on the right game under the wrong label. |
| 5 MLB props, 07-12 | **All 15 games that day have no final and no box score.** | `settle_picks` pulls finals for the trailing 5 days only; a day the fetch missed aged out. Why 07-12 was missed is not recoverable - the worker's July logs are gone. |
| 1 MLB F5 ML + 2 props, SF@ATL 06-16 | The final landed on **2026-09-01** (`games.updated_at`), eleven weeks late. | The game-level settle window is a fixed 14 days, so no pass ever revisited June; the props have no `player_game_log` rows for that game (14 of 15 games logged that day) and the daily game-log step ingests yesterday only. The prop path had a self-healing probe; the game path never did. |
| 2 MLB, NYM@LAD 04-16 and CLE@CIN 07-27 | No final, while every other game that day scored (10/11, 11/12); `commence_time` is NULL on the April row. | Consistent with a postponement, which nothing ever settles. NOT verified: `statsapi.mlb.com` is blocked from both the sandbox and WebFetch. The heal below reports which it was. |

Blast radius beyond the picks: **48 `data_source='live'` NCAAF rows since
08-28 name a different opponent than CFBD's row for the same home team and
day** - 8 are the prefix bug, 40 are unresolved FCS names that kept their
mascot ("Abilene Christian Wildcats"). 58 live rows in that window have no
score; 8 carry picks.

**What changed.**

- `resolve_odds_api_school`: a prefix match now requires the remainder to be
  that school's mascot (exact, or the same last word). "Indiana Hoosiers" still
  resolves; "Indiana State Sycamores" passes through unresolved, which is the
  documented safe outcome - an unresolved name builds an id nothing matches,
  a wrong school builds a matchup that never happened.
- `alias_score_updates`: a second pass anchors on one side matching exactly and
  lets the other side be an EXTENDED slug (`indiana` / `indiana-state`,
  `abilene-christian-wildcats` / `abilene-christian`). Exact beats loose; two
  different finals for one row still refuse. This is what grades the six.
- `mirror_stored_finals`: the same mirror fed from finals already in `games`,
  called from `settle_picks` - the CFBD pull runs once at 6am, settle runs
  every hourly pass, and the fix should not wait for tomorrow's pull.
- `paper_tracker`: the game-level window heals like the prop window
  (`_game_settle_window_days`, capped at 365); old unscored MLB dates that still
  carry a BET are re-fetched (`_unscored_mlb_dates_with_pending_bets`, 20 per
  pass); a game the Stats API lists as Postponed/Cancelled two or more days on
  settles **NO_ACTION** with a note (DK voids; a make-up is a new game_id
  here); scored games whose prop BETs have no box score at all get one fetched
  (`_scored_mlb_dates_missing_game_log`).
- **Found in passing and fixed:** `_settle_game_picks` excluded
  `mlb_/wnba_/nba_prop_%` but not `nfl_prop_%`. Eighteen NFL prop BETs are
  queued for 09-13; on the first Sunday with finals the game path would have
  run first, mapped each to `h2h`, stamped NO_ACTION, and the prop settler
  (result IS NULL only) would never have seen them.

**What the six will grade to**, from the CFBD finals already stored (the
same totals math `_compute_result` runs):

| Pick | Actual game | Final | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
