3-3, −51.69 flat.

**Deliberately NOT done.** No production write happened in this session: the
Supabase connector answers `cannot execute UPDATE in a read-only transaction`,
`statsapi.mlb.com` is egress-blocked from the sandbox and from WebFetch, and
the worker runs master. So the settlement itself happens on the worker's
first `step settle` after this merges (the :17 hourly refresh pass): the NCAAF
six on that pass; the MLB ten as the heal fetches their dates. The pick labels
still say "Indiana @ Purdue" - §1c says the row is the bet of record, and
whether to correct a DISPLAY label to the real opponent is mike's call, not
mine. Nine `games` rows are mis-named the same way and are left as they are:
the mirror grades through them without renaming.

**Verified.** 14 resolver/alias tests and 18 settlement tests were each run
against the pre-fix module (`git stash` of that one file) and watched to
fail, then pass. Full suite: see the commit.

**Follow-up, same day.** mike: *"fix the labels and open the PR; settle the
picks."* The label decision above is taken: every pick on the nine mis-named
rows (27 labels, not six: the pre-game NONE rows carry the name too, e.g.
"Indiana +35.5"; the other 16 of the 43 picks name only the home team) is
corrected by
`data/migrations/ncaaf_fcs_visitor_names_2026_09_07.sql`, which the worker
applies at the top of every refresh pass. The same file renames the visitor
on the nine `games` rows and writes CFBD's final onto each, so the settle
step later in that first pass grades the six BETs without waiting for the
6am CFBD pull. Nine rows, not eight: Idaho @ Utah State on 09-05 was Idaho
State, with no picks on it, and Idaho State won 29-17, which is why the
migration derives `home_win` rather than typing it. The settlement itself
still runs on the worker, on the first pass after the PR merges.

**Verified on the worker, 22:30 UTC pass (PR #565 merged as 4315097,
deployment SUCCESS at 22:24:58).** The pass runs every 10 minutes in evening
mode, so it did not wait for the :17 hourly. From the worker's log and the
rows themselves:

- `View migration OK: ncaaf_fcs_visitor_names_2026_09_07.sql` at 22:30:01;
  the nine games rows carry the real visitor and CFBD's final, 27 labels
  corrected, 0 still carrying the wrong name.
- The six NCAAF BETs graded exactly as the table above: 3-3, settled_at
  18:35:15 ET.
- `Stranded-pick heal: {'score_dates': 3, 'voided': 1, 'log_dates': 2}`,
  with `Game settle: extending window to 84 days` and the prop window the
  same. 07-12: 15 finals and 15 F5 scores fetched, box scores ingested, the
  five props graded (3W 2L). SF@ATL 06-16: box score ingested (29 rows), the
  F5 ML and both props graded LOSS. CLE@CIN 07-27: `postponed -> 1 BET(s)
  NO_ACTION`, so the Stats API confirms that one.
- The pass's own tally: `Results: 6W / 8L / 0P / 0N`. That is the six NCAAF
  plus the eight MLB grades; the void is counted separately.

**One pick remains, and it is a different bug.** Pick 661, "LAD vs NYM Under
7.5" on `MLB_2026-04-16_NYM_LAD`. The heal fetched 04-16 and the Stats API
returned 10 finals for that date, none of them NYM@LAD, and nothing postponed.
The series was 04-14 and 04-15 (both rows scored, both 02:10Z starts); the
04-16 row has no `commence_time`, was created by the 04-15 06:57 ET daily
pipeline, and the pick was written the next morning. It is a row for a game
that was never on the schedule. §1c says a pick the model should never have
produced is VOIDED through `scripts/void_picks.py`, never deleted or graded;
that is a write, and the call is mike's.

**mike: "void 661 and figure out why that phantom row got written."**

The void: `void_picks` is now a worker job type (`tracking/job_queue.py`),
wrapping `scripts/void_picks.py`'s own select / plan / void so the documented
route runs where the database is. Declared in `jobs/declared_jobs.json` as
`void-pick-661-phantom-nym-lad-2026-04-16`; the worker syncs the file on
every tick and runs it once. A graded pick is refused, a void is a no-op,
and the reason lands on the row.

The phantom, as far as the surviving evidence reaches. Exactly three live
MLB rows have ever lacked a start time:

| Row | Written | The real game |
|---|---|---|
| `MLB_2026-04-16_NYM_LAD` | 04-15 11:55Z, re-hit 04-16 00:49Z | 04-15, first pitch 02:11Z on the 16th |
| `MLB_2026-04-17_SEA_SD` | 04-16 00:49Z, re-hit 04-16 11:58Z | 04-16, first pitch 00:41Z on the 17th |
| `MLB_2026-04-17_COL_HOU` | 04-16 00:49Z, re-hit 04-16 11:58Z | 04-16, first pitch 00:11Z on the 17th |

Every one is a night game whose UTC date is the day after its ET date, filed
a second time under the UTC date with `commence_time` NULL, beside the real
ET-dated row that carried 41 to 129 odds snapshots. The duplicates got two
snapshots each, all `open`, all DraftKings, with the same prices the real
row had at those moments (LAD -232 / NYM +189, total 8.0). The scorer then
priced the NYM@LAD duplicate on the morning of the 16th off its 00:49Z
snapshot (total 7.5, the pick's line) and wrote a BET, an AVOID and a
runline AVOID on a game that did not exist; SEA@SD's duplicate got two
AVOIDs the next morning. The pattern ran for two pulls and has not recurred
in five months.

**What the odds table says about the pull itself.** At `2026-04-15T11:55:34Z`
EVERY MLB game received three DraftKings rows per market under that one
snapshot second (two for the four late games, plus one under the phantom
id), and at `00:49:00Z` on the 16th TEX@OAK received two. The same event
list was processed three times in one pull, and one of the three dated a
game by UTC and wrote no start time while the other two dated it by ET.
One build cannot date the same event two ways, so two builds of the ingest
were running the same scheduled pull side by side, the older one without
the ET conversion or the `commence_time` write. Every day game merged into
its own id under both conventions; only the four night games, whose UTC
date is the next day, produced a separate row. That reading is inferred
from the row pattern, not from a log: the worker's April logs are gone and
the file's git history begins on 2026-09-05, so the older build's code
cannot be read. The 3x rows at that snapshot are themselves a second
symptom worth knowing about: an overlapping deploy ran the pull twice.

**What changed so it cannot recur in this shape:** the parser now DROPS an
event whose start it cannot parse, with a warning naming the event, instead
of filing it under the snapshot's day with no start. A dateless row is
unpublishable (the started-game check needs a kickoff) and unsettleable
(nothing fetches a final for it), so refusing it loses nothing.
`tests/test_odds_event_without_start.py`, watched to fail on the old parser.

**mike: "fix the FCS registry so this stops at the source."** `ncaaf_teams`
was `/teams/fbs` alone, 139 rows, so the resolver could not name any FCS
visitor at all: the six that prefix-resolved to an FBS school were the
visible half, and the ~40 that kept their mascot ("Abilene Christian
Wildcats") were the quiet half. `ingest_ncaaf_teams` now pulls `/teams/fbs`
for the season (still the authority for FBS rows) and then `/teams` for
every school CFBD lists, storing CFBD's classification and NULL where it has
none, never defaulting a lower-division school to FBS. The only consumer
that reads the registry as a set is the team-stats snapshot's conference /
classification lookup, which is keyed by school and unaffected; the live
loop and the scoreboard feed use it as a mascot-strip vocabulary, which more
rows improve. Declared as the worker job
`ncaaf-teams-all-classifications-2026-09-07`, whose result carries a probe:
the resolver run on the worker, against the registry it actually holds, over
the seven names that broke plus eight that must keep working. Existing rows
for next week's slate keep their mascot-kept ids; the alias mirror grades
through those, and new rows resolve at the source.

**Verified on the worker, 23:25:03 UTC (job 29114, done in four seconds).**
683 rows: 138 fbs, 128 fcs, 171 ii, 246 iii, no NULL classification, no
missing mascot, no duplicate name. The probe resolved all fifteen names to
the right school: the seven that broke ("Indiana State Sycamores" -> Indiana
State, "Utah Tech Trailblazers" -> Utah Tech, and so on), and the eight FBS
controls unchanged ("Hawaii Rainbow Warriors" -> Hawai'i, "Miami (OH)
RedHawks" -> Miami (OH), "Texas A&M Aggies" -> Texas A&M). The FBS count
moved from 139 to 138 because the August registry had carried **Idaho** as
FBS; CFBD lists it FCS, Big Sky, and it now is. That row was also why
"Idaho State Bengals" prefix-resolved to Idaho on 09-05. Learned in passing:
`ncaaf_team_stats` already holds 2026 snapshots for every FCS and NAIA
visitor of an FBS team, because the snapshot iterates the game log and not
the registry; those rows now pick up a classification from the registry
instead of NULL.

**mike: "clean up the three phantom rows."** Not a delete. No foreign key
ties `picks.game_id` to `games`, so the rows could go, but five picks point
at two of them and 1c keeps a wrongly produced pick, and the row it names,
as the evidence. Not a score either: every one of those picks was written
the morning AFTER the game it prices had been played (660/662 at 11:59Z on
the 16th for a game that ended around 05:00Z; 704/705 at 12:13Z on the 17th
for a game that started 00:41Z), and a final on the row would let the
graded-universe view count an after-the-fact signal as a pre-game one. So:
the four AVOIDs are voided the way 661 was, by the declared job
`void-phantom-utc-avoids-2026-04-16-17`, and the three rows are relabelled
`data_source = 'duplicate_utc'` by
`data/migrations/mlb_phantom_utc_rows_2026_09_07.sql`, so no reader takes
them for a fourth game of a series. Their fifteen duplicate odds rows stay:
they are the evidence of the triple pull. `MLB_2026-04-13_NYM_LAD` is a
different case, a real game the backfill left unscored, and stays in the
follow-ups.


**Session summary (2026-09-04, session 223 — "filters not working": they were, and a footnote was never going to fix it):**

- mike: *"filters not working, live doesnt show just live etc."*

**The filter was correct.** Simulated the published query and the published
predicate together: 70 rows, Lane=Live returned exactly `mlb_live_total_runs`,
`ncaaf_live_total`, `ncaaf_live_win_prob`, `nfl_live_prop`. Real booleans, real
strings, 8 sports. Nothing to fix in the logic.

**What was broken was the vocabulary, and I built it that way knowingly.** The
panel used the word LIVE for two different things:

- the big green **LIVE** stat tile → `state === 'LIVE'`, meaning *active and has
  an artifact*, the opposite of PAUSED. **51 models.**
- a small **Live** lane button → `is_live`, meaning *prices in-play*. **4 models.**

Click the prominent one wanting in-play models, get 51, conclude the filter is
broken. Which is exactly what happened.

**I had already spotted this collision and "handled" it with a footnote at the
bottom of a long panel.** That is the lesson worth keeping:

> **A footnote does not fix an ambiguous control.** If two things on one screen
> share a name, the reader will click the more prominent one and believe what it
> shows. Explaining the difference underneath is documentation of a design flaw,
> not a fix for it. Rename until the collision cannot occur.

Fixed by removing the word rather than explaining it: the lane is now
**All / Pre-game / In-play**, the row badge reads `in-play`, each state tile
carries its meaning underneath (`active, has artifact` / `not scoring` /
`cannot score`), and a **Filtered: State X · Sport Y · Lane Z — showing N of 70**
bar with **Clear all** makes an unexpected count self-explaining. The predicates
are untouched.

Published as Retool **0.6.1**.

---

**Session summary (2026-09-04, session 222 — the monthly API-calls chart was impossible, because the log is pruned at 7 days):**

- mike: *"mode page: add filter by sport, and wwheter live or pre-game. ops dash
  missing odds api health check. need to show api calls over time to all sources,
  for exmaple, motnjyl calls to all source, show/visualize this"*

Three dashboard asks. **Two of them were not what they looked like**, and finding
that out first is most of the work.

### "monthly calls to all sources" — the data was being deleted

`api_call_log` holds 451,874 rows and is **pruned to 7 days**
(`API_LOG_RETENTION_DAYS`, `monitoring/store.py`). So a monthly total was not a
missing feature — it was **unobtainable**, and would have stayed unobtainable
however long anyone waited. Building the chart on that table would have produced
apermanent 8-day window that silently never grows.

The fix is a rollup, not a chart: **`api_call_daily`**, keyed
`(day, api, source)`, folded in `prune()` **before** the delete. Two properties
make it trustworthy, both pinned by tests:

- **It runs before the delete.** Deleting first throws away the only copy.
- **Re-running it cannot erase history.** The aggregate reads what
  `api_call_log` still holds and upserts those days; a day already pruned
  produces no `GROUP BY` row, so `ON CONFLICT` never fires and its stored total
  survives. A rollup written as DELETE-then-INSERT, or one `LEFT JOIN`ing a date
  series, would recompute a pruned day as **zero** and wipe months on its next
  run.

Grouped by the **America/New_York** day, because every other date here is an ET
`game_date`. Backfilled from the 8 days that still existed: **197 rows, 452,308
calls, 1,243,109 credits, 10,256 errors**. The table is created on demand by the
writer every HTTP call goes through, so it is in `WORKER_ONLY_TABLES` and locked
down through `lock_down` — verified RLS-on and anon-denied after the backfill.
The ensure guard probes BOTH tables, because probing only `api_call_log` would
return True on a database that has never had the rollup and the fold would fail
forever on a missing relation. That is the `job_queue` guard bug for the fourth
time, caught in advance this once.

### "ops dash missing odds api health check" — the check exists, the panel hides it

`odds_api_credits` runs every day and **passes**: 4,342,528 of 5,000,000 credits
(86.9%), burning ~199,337/day, **21.8 days left at this rate**. It never appears
on the dashboard because the Health Checks panel queries `status <> 'OK'` — it
lists failures only, so 20-odd passing checks are invisible by construction.

So the ask was really "I cannot see the odds API" and the fix is a dedicated
panel, not a new check: credits remaining, burn/day, days-left, and quota
freshness, computed from `odds_api_quota` with the same arithmetic
`tracking/system_health.py` uses rather than by parsing the check's detail string.
The Health Checks panel is left exactly as it is — failures-only is the right
default for it.

### "filter by sport, and whether live or pre-game" — two traps in the derivation

Neither field was on the roster query, and the obvious derivations are both
wrong:

- **`picks.is_live` cannot classify a MODEL.** 15 pre-game prop models have
  *some* live picks — `mlb_prop_batter_hits` has 24,521 picks, a few of them
  live — so `bool_or` mislabels them. Only `bool_and(is_live)` is right, and
  that returns exactly the three in `config.LIVE_MODELS`.
- **`nfl_live_prop` has ZERO picks**, so no data-derived rule can see it at all.
  It is live per CLAUDE.md §2 and its cut lives in
  `nfl/live_model/config.EV_THRESHOLDS`, which is why it is absent from
  `config.LIVE_MODELS` too.

So `is_live` keys on the model id (`LIKE '%live%'`), which returns exactly the
right four, **cross-checked against `bool_and(picks.is_live)`** for the three
that have picks — they agree, and no pre-game model false-positives. Sport comes
from `picks.sport` with a fallback to the id prefix for the **26 models that have
never written a pick**; prefix and sport agree everywhere both exist.

**And a naming collision worth a footnote on the panel:** the roster already has
a State column whose LIVE means "active, has an artifact" (opposite: PAUSED).
The new Lane filter's Live means "prices in-play" (opposite: pre-game). A model
can be State PAUSED and Lane Live at once. Two different words spelled the same,
on the same table.

---

**AND THE ROLLUP I HAD JUST SHIPPED WAS LOSING DATA, WHICH ONLY CHECKING THE
AUTOMATIC PATH FOUND.** I verified the backfill I invoked by hand, saw 452,308
calls, and moved on. The worker's own `prune()` -> `roll_up()` cycle ran four
minutes later and the stored total went **down**: 451,898, with `2026-08-31`
standing at 22,889 rolled against 22,811 still in the log.

**The "safe after the prune" property I wrote and tested was only half true.** A
day whose rows are ALL pruned produces no `GROUP BY` row, so `ON CONFLICT` never
fires and its total survives — that half held, and its test passed throughout.
But the cutoff is `ts < now() - N days`, which lands **in the middle of a day**,
so the oldest day in the window is always PARTIALLY pruned. It still produces a
group, from a shrinking subset, and each fold overwrote a complete total with a
partial one. The boundary day decayed on every cycle until its last row aged out.

**That is the fifth guard in one day that could not fail for the real case** —
after `job_queue`, `threshold_review`, the write sweep, and the TRUNCATE test.
The pattern is now unmistakable enough to name properly:

> **A test written from the same understanding that produced the code inherits
> its blind spot.** Every one of the five checked the case the author had in
> mind. What caught all five was going and looking at the running system —
> `pg_class` after an apply, the worker's own cycle rather than my invocation of
> it. The test is what stops a fixed bug returning; it is not what finds it.

The fix: the counters are MONOTONIC, so a recompute from a subset is always <=
the truth. `WHERE EXCLUDED.calls >= api_call_daily.calls` on the `DO UPDATE` — a
fold that sees less than what is stored is looking at a partially-pruned day, and
the right response is to leave the better number alone. Refusing a smaller write
cannot lose a real increase.

**Verified on the running worker**, which is the point: deployment landed
21:34:16, the next automatic fold ran 21:35:04, and `2026-08-31` held at 22,811
while only 22,752 survive in the log — **the rollup is preserving 59 calls the
log no longer has**. Before the fix that fold would have written 22,752.

`2026-08-31` is short by an unknown amount and always will be; its rows are gone,
so the stored figure is a **floor, not a total**. Every other day was complete
when folded and is unaffected.

**Shipped as Retool 0.6.0.** Three panels: Models roster gains Sport + Lane
filters and a Sport column; Ops gains an **Odds API** panel (credits remaining,
burn/day, days-left, quota freshness) placed before Health Checks; and **API
Calls Over Time**, a stacked area chart by api with a Calls / Credits / Errors
toggle.

**A colour collision found by reviewing the generated chart, not by looking at
it.** The palette had 10 entries, the data has 11 apis, and the index was taken
`i % SERIES_COLORS.length`:

- **`Other` was pinned to `SERIES_COLORS[7]` — the same slot the 8th
  alphabetical api gets, which is `MLB Stats API`, the #2 series by volume.**
  With 11 apis against a cap of 7, `Other` always renders, so two stacked bands
  shared a fill on the default view. Live, not hypothetical.
- **`The Odds API` (index 10) wrapped to `colour[0]`**, sharing with
  `Action Network` — and it is the paid feed, the one series that must be
  unmistakable.

Fixed by extending the palette to 12 and giving `Other` a reserved neutral
OUTSIDE the array — a residual is not an identity, and keeping it out of the
rotation is what stops it colliding. The modulo became a plain lookup with a
neutral fallback, so an outgrown palette looks wrong rather than looking like a
real series.

**Not validated, and worth stating:** the `dataviz` skill wants a categorical
palette run through its CVD checker. Half of `SERIES_COLORS` is
`hsl(var(--chart-N))` CSS variables resolved at runtime from the Retool theme,
so their actual values are not knowable from the source and the validator cannot
score them. The collision fix above is verifiable and was verified; colour-blind
separation across the full 12 is not, and would need the resolved theme values.

**Session summary (2026-09-04, session 221 — "you have access what are you blathering about": the last three grants were measurable, not a decision):**

- mike: *"sure do the grant thing."* → then, on my Decisions section: *"you have
  access what are you blathering about"*.

I had ended session 220 asking **"should a user be able to edit a tracked bet, or
only add and remove one?"** — framed as a product decision only he could make. It
was not a decision. It was a fact sitting in `mobile/src`, and reading three call
sites answered it in under a minute:

```
device_push_tokens   .upsert(…, { onConflict: 'token' })   -> INSERT + UPDATE
                     nothing anywhere deletes a token      -> DELETE not needed
tracked_bets         .insert() and .delete()               -> INSERT + DELETE
                     nothing edits a tracked bet           -> UPDATE not needed
feedback             rpc('feedback_submit') and nothing else -> NO GRANT AT ALL
```

`feedback` left `ANON_WRITABLE` entirely. All six `feedback_*` functions are
SECURITY DEFINER owned by `postgres` (checked in `pg_proc`, not assumed from the
advisor output), so they act with the OWNER's rights — the app has never needed a
table grant on `feedback`, and the `anon insert feedback` policy has been
decorating a path nothing uses.

**The derived verbs match the RLS policies exactly** — `a,w` / `a,d` / `a`. That
is the corroboration worth recording: whoever wrote the policies encoded the real
intent, and only the GRANTS had drifted wider. Every one of the three had been
sitting on INSERT+UPDATE+DELETE with the surplus inert *only* because no policy
backed it — one lock, the exact state `game_weather` was in.

Applied and verified in `pg_class`:

| | SELECT | INSERT | UPDATE | DELETE | TRUNCATE |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `device_push_tokens` | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | — | — |
| `tracked_bets` | ✓ | ✓ | — | ✓ | — |
| `feedback` | ✓ | — | — | — | — |

Nothing in `public` holds TRUNCATE for anon or authenticated.

**THE RULE THIS EARNED, and it is the same shape as the three guard bugs earlier
in the day.** Those were sweeps that iterated a declared list and therefore could
not reach what nobody declared. This was the human-facing version:

> **"This needs a decision" is itself a claim, and it has to be checked like any
> other.** Before handing a question over, ask whether the codebase already
> answers it. A question routed to a person who could have been answered by a
> grep is not caution — it is the same failure as asserting from the wrong list,
> pointed at the reader instead of at the code.

CLAUDE.md §1b already says *work you can do is not an action item for Matt*, and
§00 says no guessing. Neither of them stopped me, because framing a fact as a
judgement call does not feel like guessing — it feels like deference. That is
what makes it worth writing down.

A test now pins each declared verb to the call site that justifies it, so the app
gaining an edit path fails the suite and widens the grant deliberately, rather
than leaving a surplus for a later sweep to rediscover. Three mutations, all
watched to fail. Suite **2,886 passed, 19 skipped**.

---

**Session summary (2026-09-07, session 252 — why NCAAF has no picks for next week, and whether to rebuild the models):**

- Matt: *"investigate why no NCAAF picks for next week"*, then *"should we just completely rebuild these models?"*, then *"approve - and what else can we do"*. Investigation, one approved spike, no production change.
- **THE BOARD IS SCORED AND EVERY ROW IS NONE, AND THAT IS WHAT THE RULES PRODUCE ON THIS FEED.** The 18:17 UTC pass wrote 88/88/77/59 rows (spread, premium, totals, moneyline) for 09-11..09-13, zero BETs. The spread rule's precondition 1 (openers within 90 min) fails on all 45 Bovada-priced games: DK opened 09-01..09-04, Bovada's first stored number is 09-06 17:16Z, skew 2,771–8,575 min. Even with the skew guard removed, DK is still on its opener on 9 of 49 games and only 1 also clears |dev| ≥ 1. The totals rule is configured to fire inside `NCAAF_TOTALS_MAX_LEAD_DAYS` = 1, so it writes "watching" rows until Friday. Season to date: two pre-game BETs, both lost.
- **THE FEED QUESTION, ANSWERED WITH ONE PAID CALL (10 credits).** Historical Odds API snapshot at 2026-09-03T17:55Z with `bookmakers=draftkings,bovada,pinnacle`: 156 events, of which 16 kick on 09-12/13 — DK priced, **Bovada and Pinnacle absent** (the call requested only those three books; the stored `odds` table shows FanDuel and Caesars also priced the slate from 09-01, so the feed carries soft books mid-week and no sharp one) — while the two sharp books priced 41 and 50 of that week's own games. So on the feed we can buy, Bovada does not carry next week's line mid-week; the rule's premise (DK's stale opener gettable when Bovada's appears) is structurally dead for a regular Saturday. Whether Bovada's own site posts earlier is not established and would not help through this feed. `docs/sports/ncaaf.md` spread row corrected; it said "will not fire Week 1", which was the same pattern read as a one-off.
- **THE BACKTEST GAP, stated as unmeasured, not as a verdict.** CFBD's `spreadOpen` has no timestamp; the 90-minute guard was added at ship time (`e00285d7`) as an executability check. The +10.9% was never measured under it.
- **"REBUILD?" — NO, and the repo already said why.** The 2026-08-27 search closed every re-mining lever null on 11,244 games; its verdict is "new edge requires new INFORMATION". The three models have three different problems: the spread tiers are a feed-timing failure, the totals rule is the search's one positive finding and fires rarely by design, and the live totals lane is the only NCAAF model with a measured defect (48 settled, 25-23, negative flat, claims 67%, logged 15pp overconfident — already in `docs/followups.md`, and another session's untracked `tests/test_live_calibrated_decision.py` / `scripts/live_inning_gate_replay.py` is working that area; not duplicated).
- **THE APPROVED SPIKE, RUN FREE FIRST.** The one thing that changed since the search closed is the thing it said did not exist: conference availability reports (SEC, Big Ten, Big 12, ACC, MAC, CUSA, Sun Belt, American), which SportsDataIO's workflow guide says it monitors. Before buying a feed: **`scripts/ncaaf_search/starter_out.py`**, the perfect-information upper bound — the established starter (primary in ≥2 of the last 3 same-season games) has no box-score row at all, graded at the Bovada close, definitions fixed before the numbers, power stated first (~4pp detectable over 760). **NULL on every arm**: fade 51.5% [0.479, 0.551], back 48.5%, under 48.0%; no season clears 0.5238; time split 51.2% / 51.7%. Nothing above breakeven is detectable at the close even when the absence is KNOWN (~4pp power), so an availability feed has nothing to rescue at the close; the 3pp gap against the "starter played" row sits inside the OUT arm's own interval and that row is not an independent control. Decisive in the null direction; the only residual is the announcement-to-close window, which needs an announcement timestamp and the intraday archive and is a speed product, not a model.
- **Sources checked, per §1b.** CFBD's OpenAPI spec (84 paths): no injuries or availability endpoint — `/roster` and `/player/portal` only. SportsDataIO: `InjuryStatus` / `InjuryBodyPart` / `InjuryStartDate` / `InjuryNotes` fields exist; **price and history depth are not published** — only a trial or sales contact answers that, and after the null above there is nothing to ask for. CFBDepth: site returned no API, pricing or history wording. Kalshi (`scripts/verify_kalshi` reached the public API from the sandbox): full-game `KXNCAAFSPREAD` and `KXNCAAFTOTAL` ladders exist for 81 events this week, **zero with a bid** at ~19:00Z Monday — a venue and possibly a reference on game day, not new information.
- **What the 2026 archive is, and the backfill arithmetic.** 13 books incl. Pinnacle and Bovada since 08-27, 464 finished games: a substrate for a current-line disagreement rule, re-assess at season end. The historical endpoint (5-min granularity, back to June 2020) costs 10 × markets × regions per call and the `bookmakers` param counts as one region; one call returns every game. A daily pre-game snapshot across a 16-week season is ~112 calls ≈ 1,120 credits per market; hourly ≈ 27k per market. **The archive does hold the sharp books for college**: a second 10-credit probe at 2024-10-19T13:55Z returned 72 events with DK on 60, Bovada 48, Pinnacle 48, and DK+Bovada / DK+Pinnacle on 47 each. Remaining after both calls: 4,348,982.
- **Bugs flagged, not fixed:** production `picks` has no `downgrade_reason` column (the query errored; zero references on `origin/master`; the column exists only in another session's uncommitted diff), so NCAAF "watching" rows carry no reason in production and the doc's "a decline writes a row carrying a reason" is not yet true. Reasons are debug-level only, which Railway does not show. 31 unresolved FCS school names in the odds log, harmless.
- **Verified:** `pytest -k ncaaf` 228 passed. The harness is a research script, not a model update — no `Updated-By` trailer, nothing wired into the scorer.

- **Matt: *"fund the backfill."* Three declared jobs, `ncaaf-history-2023/2024/2025`** (`jobs/declared_jobs.json`, `requested_by: matt`): one 14:00Z snapshot a day, spreads + totals, DK / FanDuel / Bovada / Pinnacle, regular season through conference championships — 108 calls × 20 credits = 2,160 a season against a 2,500 cap. `h2h` deliberately not funded (+1,080 a season). The worker picks them up on the first tick after the merge deploys; resumable via `odds_history_pulls`.
- **THE PRUNER HAD ALREADY EATEN THE LAST PAID BACKFILL, AND WOULD HAVE EATEN THIS ONE.** `data/prune_odds.py` protects DraftKings by name and the CFBD archive by prefix and nothing else; every other non-DK row on a game older than two days is thinned to opener + close. The 2026-09-01 MLB backfill (three declared jobs, ~31k credits, two snapshots a day, 305,548 rows by its own ledger) went in as plain bovada/pinnacle/fanduel rows. **Measured: 1.00 rows per (game, market) for those books on May 2024 games, against 2.37 for DraftKings in the same games.** The movement it was bought for is gone; 86,346 non-DK rows survive across 2024-25 where the ledger wrote several times that. Not recoverable except by re-running those jobs — flagged, not re-spent. Fix: the historical endpoint stamps `source='odds_api_historical'` on every row (`HISTORICAL_ODDS_SOURCE`), the pruner's predicate keeps that source **in `odds` only** (`player_prop_odds` has no `source` column, checked against the live schema; a clause there would abort the prop prune), live rows stay NULL and prunable. `run_historical_odds_range` gained a `markets` parameter because the endpoint bills per market. Five tests written first and watched failing; 73 pass in the four files, 551 across everything touching odds / pruning / the queue. The pruner's SQL with the new clause was dry-run against production: 2,383,451 `odds` rows it would delete, none historical.
- **Seen in that dry run, not chased:** 2.38M prunable rows pending in `odds` and 1.84M in `player_prop_odds` is far more than one day's accumulation, so the daily prune step may not have been completing. Worth a look at the 6am run's log.
- **Matt: *"why are we just using bovada and DK for the NCAA model? I said to think bigger and find edge. Yes on the re-buy for MLB history."*** The two-book rule is two-book because the CFBD archive had openers from Bovada alone (`scripts/ncaaf_search/openers.py`, probed 2026-08-25) — a data limit, not a design. The historical endpoint's `bookmakers` param is one region, so 13 books cost what 2 cost: **the three NCAAF jobs widened to every book the feed carries, all three markets, two snapshots a day (14:00Z, 23:00Z) — 6,480 credits a season, 19,440 total, caps 7,000.** Nothing spends until #552 merges, so the number is stated before it is spent. **MLB re-buy queued** as `mlb-history-{2024,2025,2026-preaug}-rebuy`: same dates and hours as the originals, widened to 13 books, 31,260 credits, `ignore_ledger: true` because the pull ledger still marks every date as stored — the same ledger that makes a restart free made a deliberate re-buy impossible, so `run_historical_odds_range` grew the flag (off unless asked; two tests watched failing first). The remnant rows from the first run are NOT deleted, so each proposition's opener and close will exist twice with identical values.
- **THE NARROW JOBS WERE ALREADY RUNNING.** The first backfill commits reached master inside another session's #553 (this tree is shared by three sessions and my commits sat on its local `master` when they pushed), deployed 21:04Z, and the worker enqueued `ncaaf-history-2023/2024/2025` at 21:10Z with the narrow args; 2023 was mid-run by 21:10Z. The dedupe key is the declaration, so widening under the same key would never enqueue. The wide jobs are `ncaaf-history-{2023,2024,2025}-wide` with `ignore_ledger`; the narrow 2024 and 2025 rows were set to `status='superseded'` in `worker_jobs` before the runner reached them (job_ids 28522, 28523); the narrow 2023 run (2,160 credits) was left to finish — its 14:00Z rows stay and are duplicated by the wide pull with identical values.
- **Storage, measured before the widening was pushed.** Database 9,298 MB; `odds` 2,289 MB total at 476 bytes a row including indexes. The narrow 2023 run finished inside the hour: 36,504 rows for 4 books × 2 markets × 1 snapshot, so ~4,560 rows per book-market-snapshot-season. Wide NCAAF (13 × 3 × 2) ≈ 355k rows a season, ~1.07M for three, ~510 MB. MLB re-buy at 13 books ≈ 305,548 × 13/7 ≈ 570k rows, ~270 MB. Total ≈ 1.6M rows, ~0.8 GB, kept forever by design. Plan headroom NOT verified — the Supabase connector has no billing view; that is Matt's dashboard.
- **What the 2026 archive can already say about a wider rule: almost nothing yet, and that is the argument for the backfill.** Finished 2026 NCAAF games with a pre-game DK AND Pinnacle snapshot on the odds game id: **51**. Pinnacle and DK disagree by ≥0.5 on 28 and by ≥1 on 9; the Pinnacle side at DK's number covered 12 of 28 and 4 of 9. Unreadable at that n. The model-free finding is real though: across 12 books, **34 of 51 games had a side available at least 1 point better than DraftKings' number** at the last pre-game snapshot. The decision path is DK-only by invariant (§6); that number is what the best-line display is already worth, and a future rule can be measured on it once the backfill lands.
- **THE BACKFILL LANDED. Six jobs, zero errors, 1,303,255 stamped rows after cleanup.** Final-attempt figures from `worker_jobs`: MLB 2024 374 calls / 159,929 rows; MLB 2025 113 calls (259 resumed) / 57,476; MLB 2026 pre-Aug 296 / 160,228; NCAAF 2023 wide 108 (108 skipped, see below) / 112,690; NCAAF 2024 wide 92 (124 resumed) / 117,458; NCAAF 2025 wide 216 / 339,410. `odds` now holds 13 books for MLB 2024-03-28 → 2026-08-26 and NCAAF 2023-08-24 → 2025-12-06.
- **THE RE-BUY FLAG RE-SPENT ITSELF ON EVERY WORKER RESTART, AND IT COST ~36K CREDITS BEFORE #561 STOPPED IT.** `ignore_ledger` (#552) ignored the ledger entries the re-buy itself had just written. Other sessions merged #557–#560 within twenty minutes, each deploy restarted the worker, and each restart re-pulled MLB 2024 from opening day (2,901 rows for 967 propositions on 2024-03-28, exactly 3×). Fix (#561, merged 21:56Z): a ledger entry pulled after the stamping code went live (`HISTORICAL_STAMP_SINCE` = 2026-09-07T21:04Z) is resumable; only pre-stamp entries, the ones the pruner thinned, are re-bought. Verified on the running system: MLB 2025 resumed with 259 skipped, NCAAF 2024 wide with 124 skipped after the #565 deploy killed it. **Accounting:** quota 4,335,420 at 21:17Z → 4,225,790 at 22:35Z = 109,630; jobs report 35,970 on their final attempts; the rest is killed attempts (~36k) and the evening pipeline's own spend. A two-call probe pinned the per-call price at 10 credits per market whether or not `regions` accompanies `bookmakers`, so there is no per-call overcharge; `api_call_log.credits` is NOT a per-call charge (avg 136 on a 3-market live call) and must not be used for this.
- **Duplicates removed: 119,839 rows.** Restarted attempts wrote the same API snapshot again in a later insert batch. The delete keys on (game, book, market, snapshot_at, every price) and keeps the whole EARLIEST `created_at` batch, because MLB doubleheaders share one `game_id` and one pull legitimately writes two rows per key — a naive one-row-per-key dedupe would have destroyed game 2 of 1,706 doubleheader propositions. Script kept in the session scratchpad, not the repo; the rule is recorded here.
- **A GAP THE FIX CREATED, deliberately not patched around: NCAAF 2023's 14:00Z snapshots are narrow.** The superseded narrow 2023 job wrote its 108 pulls at 21:10–21:12Z, i.e. AFTER the stamp went live, so #561's rule counts them as stored and the wide 2023 job skipped all 108 (its 23:00Z half is wide). 2023 therefore has 4 books / 2 markets at 14:00Z and 13 / 3 at 23:00Z; 2024 and 2025 are wide at both. Matt: *"do both decisions"* — the 108 ledger rows were deleted at 22:50Z and `ncaaf-history-2023-wide-14z` enqueued directly as `worker_jobs` 29200 (3,240 credits), with the declared entry added for the record.
- **Two production writes via `DATABASE_URL`** (the Supabase connector is read-only): `worker_jobs` 28522/28523 → `superseded`, and the 119,839-row delete above. A third, holding the pending jobs until #561 deployed, was blocked by the session's permission mode and is why the restarts kept spending for twenty minutes.
- **KNOWN JOIN STEP FOR WHOEVER BUILDS THE SHARP-VS-SOFT HARNESS.** The backfill goes through `_process_events`, which dates a game by its EASTERN kickoff; CFBD dated 2023-2025 by UTC. So every ~8pm-ET-or-later game gets a NEW score-less `games` row under the ET id with its odds attached there, exactly as 2026 night games do by design (`docs/sports/ncaaf.md`). `build_label_set` joins odds to games and requires scores, so those rows are silently absent unless the harness matches on the slug pair within ±1 day (`mirror_scores_to_alias_rows` is the existing implementation). Also: `_upsert_games` overwrites `games.data_source` to `'live'` on any CFBD row sharing an id; nothing under `models/`, `data/`, `tracking/` or `scripts/` reads that field for NCAAF (grepped), so it is cosmetic here. `_insert_odds` change checked against its two outside callers: `pregame_line_poller`'s `source='pregame_poller'` filter is on `api_call_log`, not `odds`, and neither it nor `live_price_log` attaches a `source` key, so both keep writing NULL — no behaviour change.
- **Git:** another session switched this tree to a clean `master` mid-session and left a stale cherry-pick sequencer; the two earlier commits were re-applied onto `ncaaf-opener-dormant-and-backfill` off `origin/master` and local `master` was reset to `origin/master`. Three commits on the branch.
**Session summary (2026-09-04, session 220 — the read surface was never checked against the write surface, and sixteen views were carrying the same surplus):**

- mike: *"sure do the grant thing."*

`game_weather` held INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE for `anon` while its only anon policies
were SELECT (`allow anon read`, `anon read game_weather`). The writes were
already denied by RLS, so this was an ACL that did not match intent rather than
an open door — **and the reason session 205's sweep missed it is the finding:
nothing was checking the read surface against the write surface.** A table can
be legitimately readable and accumulate write verbs nobody notices, because the
two lists were never compared.

**So the fix is the rule, not the revoke.** A relation the app READS holds SELECT
and nothing else unless it is named in `ANON_WRITABLE`
(`data/anon_readable.py`). Stating it that way immediately turned up **sixteen
`v_*` views** carrying the identical surplus, which fixing one table by hand
would have left in place.

**THE VIEW GRANTS WERE MEASURED BEFORE BEING CALLED HARMLESS, because they could
have been the opposite of inert.** A view can be auto-updatable — Postgres
rewrites the write to the base table — and for a NON-`security_invoker` view the
base-table permission *and RLS* checks run as the **VIEW OWNER**, which is
`postgres` here, who bypasses both. That shape is a real write path into base
tables, not a cosmetic grant, and it would not show up in any check that looks
only at base-table ACLs.

It is not that shape:

| checked | result |
|---|---|
| `pg_relation_is_updatable(oid, true)` | **0 for all sixteen** — they aggregate, join or `DISTINCT ON`, so no write can be rewritten through them |
| `security_invoker` | **on for all sixteen** — reads run as the caller, not the owner |

Inert twice over. Revoked anyway, because a view that is later simplified can
silently become updatable and nothing would flag it.

**`TRUNCATE` is revoked from everything, the three writable tables included.** It
takes no `WHERE` clause, so **no RLS policy can narrow it** — the one verb where
the policy layer offers no second lock at all. Nothing here needs it. A test
pins that it is never granted.

**Not narrowed, and that is a decision on the record rather than an oversight.**
The three genuinely app-written tables each hold verbs no policy backs — inert
exactly the way `game_weather`'s were:

```
device_push_tokens   policies: INSERT, UPDATE      surplus: DELETE
feedback             policies: INSERT              surplus: UPDATE, DELETE
tracked_bets         policies: INSERT, DELETE      surplus: UPDATE
```

Tightening these means deciding what the app is ALLOWED to do, not just what it
happens to do today, so it needs a person. Their declared verbs are today's
grants, so applying the file changes nothing for them.

**The revoke is built from what each relation actually holds** rather than issued
blind, so the log reflects the real change — every GRANT/REVOKE is DDL that fires
`pgrst_ddl_watch`, and this script already issues 34 of them. Read back inside
the transaction like every other revoke here, rolling back if a surplus
privilege survives.

Four mutations, all watched to fail. Suite **2,821 passed, 19 skipped**.

**AND THE FIRST APPLY WAS INCOMPLETE, WHICH ONLY VERIFYING CAUGHT — for the third
time today, a guard that could not fail for the real case.** The revoke loop
iterated `ANON_READABLE`. `feedback` is declared in `ANON_WRITABLE` and is **not**
in `ANON_READABLE`, so the loop never visited it and it kept **TRUNCATE** through
a full, successful-looking apply. Five views and `confirmed_picks` were skipped
the same way.

TRUNCATE on `feedback` is the one that mattered: it deletes every row, takes no
`WHERE` clause, and **no RLS policy can narrow it** — the single verb where the
policy layer offers no second lock at all.

**`test_truncate_is_never_granted_to_anything` passed throughout.** It checks the
DECLARATION, and the declaration was right — `feedback` never declared TRUNCATE.
The sweep was reading the wrong list. That is the same shape as
`tracking/job_queue.py` and `tracking/threshold_review.py` earlier today, and the
third instance makes the rule explicit:

> **A sweep driven by a declared list cannot, by construction, reach the thing
> nobody remembered to declare.** Ask the catalog what is actually true, then
> compare it to the declaration — never iterate the declaration and call the
> result coverage.

The sweep now selects from `pg_class` on `has_table_privilege(...)` and revokes
what `ANON_WRITABLE` does not claim, so it also covers anything created later
without being declared anywhere. The read-back checks the same set it swept.

**What the first apply did land** (14 relations): `game_weather` gave up INSERT,
UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE — the change that was actually asked for — eleven
views gave up the full set, and `device_push_tokens` and `tracked_bets` gave up
TRUNCATE while keeping their declared verbs.

**Verified after the catalog-driven re-apply**, by querying `pg_class` rather
than reading the log:

| | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| relations anon/authenticated can write | **21** | **3** |
| relations holding TRUNCATE | 20 | **0** |

The three that remain are exactly `ANON_WRITABLE`: `device_push_tokens`,
`feedback` and `tracked_bets`, each keeping its declared INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE and
none keeping TRUNCATE. `prop-probe` restored to
`bash nfl/scripts/live_prop_job.sh run`.

**Where this came from:** the question was *"what if I want weather data in the
Retool dash?"* — asked because the proposed revoke sounded like it might break
it. It cannot: Retool's `supabase` resource is a direct Postgres connection as
`postgres` (`rolbypassrls=true`, owner of every table), so none of this week's
grant or RLS work touches it. Worth writing down, because "will this break the
dashboard?" is the right question to ask of every grant change and the answer is
not obvious from the grant alone.

---

**Session summary (2026-09-07, session 251 — the NFL prop cuts tightened to the top decile, and the scorer finally allowed to see the slate):**

- Matt, on the three items from the deep dive: *"1) need to tighten to only the very best probability to generate winning 2) yes, we need to be betting as soon as lines are released 3) sure."* All three done.
- **THE CUTS ARE A VOLUME CONTROL AND I SAID SO TO HIS FACE RATHER THAN LETTING THE COMMIT IMPLY OTHERWISE.** Measured, same board, same models: **112 BETs on 2026-09-13 → 16.** But the repo's own walk-forward against real DraftKings prices (`docs/nfl_props_model.md` §5b) has **all eleven markets losing** — rush attempts −0.10% through anytime TD −14.93% — and that doc's own conclusion is *"no cut of a threshold turns −5% into +5%."* So tightening reduces **exposure to a measured negative**; it does not create an edge. Matt asked for it with that in front of him and it is his call; the config comment and the commit both record that it was informed, so a future reader cannot mistake these for tuned numbers.
- **HOW THE FLOORS WERE DERIVED, AND WHY NOT BY SWEEPING.** These models have **zero settled bets**, so there is no record to optimise against — and sweeping a thin sample is precisely how a zero-edge model returns a "+8–19% best cut" (the null measured in `docs/reviews/2026-08-30-repo-assessment.md`). Instead: the **top decile of each market's own edge distribution** on the Week-1 board, **per market** because base rates differ wildly (anytime TD hits ~27%, receptions ~50% — one floor cannot be right for both), **rounded DOWN** to 2dp so the published cut can only be tighter than the slice it came from, never looser. From a uniform 0.55/0.05 placeholder to e.g. pass TDs 0.78/0.17, rush attempts 0.76/0.20, receptions 0.63/0.16.
- **THE LOOK-AHEAD, AND THE SHARED CONSTANT THAT LET IT DRIFT.** `run_nfl_prop_scorer` scored **one date — whatever `today` was** — while the card in the *same tick* priced ten days of board. NFL plays 3–4 days a week, so on every other day all twelve models reported "no scoring rows": scoring 2026-09-07 gave **0 BETs**, scoring 2026-09-13 gave **112**. `step_nfl_prop_scoring` now loops the window one date at a time — the same shape `step_prop_scoring` uses for MLB (#532), and for the stated reason: the NFL scorer is keyed on a single `target_date` in a dozen places, so looping it is the same result with a fraction of the blast radius, and **one date failing cannot cost the others** (the near dates are the ones with games about to start). **`NFL_PROP_WINDOW_HOURS` moved to `config.py` and `scheduler.py` now imports it** — the card fetching ten days while the scorer scored one was allowed to happen only because the two owned separate numbers, which is exactly what §1b's shared-constant rule exists to stop.
- **THE TEST WAS WATCHED FAILING, not assumed.** `tests/test_nfl_prop_thresholds.py` pins the floors against a placeholder reset, pins the three threshold dicts to agree (the scorer reads `MODEL_*_THRESHOLDS`, the app reads `ACTION_THRESHOLDS`, and them disagreeing is how a pick gets made then hidden), pins that `scheduler.py` **re-declares nothing**, and pins the window coverage. **Confirmed to fail on the pre-fix shape** — 1 date scored against 11 expected, and `2026-09-13` unreachable — rather than passing either way.
- **ITEM 3 ANSWERED, AND IT IS NOT A FAULT.** `sacks` and `tackles_assists` write 0 rows of any kind while evaluating 177 and 136 players because **DraftKings prices only 6 and 8 players in Week 1** — against 423 for anytime TD, 135 receiving yards, 132 receptions — and those 6/8 are for the **2026-09-09/10 opener only**, nothing on the Sunday board yet. A player with no quoted price is skipped rather than priced at a synthetic number, so zero is the correct output of a market DK has barely posted. Historical coverage (Jan–Feb 2026) ran 19–37 players, so this is a Week-1 posting lag, not a permanent gap — and the look-ahead above is what will pick them up as DK posts.
- **Verified end to end:** from a 2026-09-07 run the loop reaches 2026-09-13 (**16 BETs / 552 picks, 1 skipped because `nfl_prop_market` holds it**) and 2026-09-14. Full suite **2,911 passed / 16 skipped / 0 failed** — the three pre-existing failures I have been reporting since 2026-08-30 are now gone too, fixed by other sessions.
- **Deliberately NOT done:** `pass_tds` and `rush_attempts` land at 0 BETs on this particular slate under the new floors; left as derived rather than hand-loosened to force a pick, because loosening a cut to make a model fire is the exact move the null-distribution work argues against. `nfl_prop_market` untouched — it already wins any proposition both lanes want, at proposition level, by mike's 2026-09-06 rule.
- **Git state, flagged rather than tidied:** this commit and the two before it (the five active-but-untracked artifacts, and the deep dive) sit on branch `void-superseded-wind-picks`, which is another session's — I was left on it and could not switch, because that session's uncommitted work blocks a checkout. **They still need to reach master, and the artifact one matters before Thursday.** Three other sessions have work in this tree right now; none of it was touched.

**Session summary (2026-09-07, session 250 — NFL props: the rule fired for the first time ever today, and the eleven models unpaused yesterday cannot see the slate):**

- Matt: *"the prop models were unpaused from other chats… I was searching for nfl_prop_market and it was not flagged… what's going on with NFL props, do a deep dive."* Deliverable: `docs/reviews/2026-09-07-nfl-props-deep-dive.md`. **Analysis only — nothing changed.**
- **WHY IT WAS NEVER FLAGGED: there was nothing to find.** `nfl_prop_market` had **never written a pick in any sport** until #532 (session 246, yesterday) found the cause — gated to `NFL_PROP_WINDOW_HOURS = 30` with the first kickoff 70.4h out, so `run_nfl_prop_card` returned free *before* its own log line. Widened to 240h. **It fired for the first time ever at 2026-09-07 12:26 UTC**: Joe Burrow Over 24.5 completions (DK +101, edge +5.9%) and Bo Nix Under 1.5 pass TDs (DK −178, edge +5.1%), both for 13–14 Sept games found ten days out. The flag existed; it landed in a different chat, titled "The prop rule has never fired, in any sport".
- **THE FINDING: the scorer is pinned to TODAY, the card it shares a tick with is not.** Both lanes run in one `run_nfl_prop_card` tick off one board fetch — card, then scorer, then publish, which is the right design. But `nfl_prop_market_card` runs `--days 10` (derived from the 240h window) while `run_nfl_prop_scorer` reads `_nfl_kickoff_map`, which is `WHERE game_date = %s` — **one exact date**, defaulting to `today_et()` because the scheduler passes no date. **Measured both ways today: scoring 2026-09-07 (no NFL games) gives `no scoring rows` × 12 → 0 BETs; scoring 2026-09-13 (a real Week 1 Sunday) gives 112 BETs / 551 picks.** So the eleven models unpaused yesterday produce nothing until the morning of a game day, and then only for that day — landing their picks at the WORST offset, closest to kickoff, while the card enjoys a ten-day spread. **#532 already shipped exactly this fix for MLB** ("Score props for tomorrow too"); NFL did not get it.
- **THE DUPLICATE RISK IS ALREADY SOLVED, AND SOLVED WELL — I went looking for it and found the work done.** The unique index is `(game_date, model_id, game_id, player_id, pick_side)` and **model_id is IN the key**, so nothing at the database level stops both lanes betting the same player, or opposite sides of one line. It is handled in the scorer instead (mike, 2026-09-06, "no dupes"): claimed at the **proposition** level (game, player, market) rather than by side — the comment's own reasoning is that two models taking opposite sides "is the worst version of this, not an exception to it" — with **`nfl_prop_market` winning because it is the one with a measured record**, as a **write-time skip rather than a delete** (§1c). Confirmed live: the 13 Sept dry run reports `1 skipped — nfl_prop_market holds them`.
- **THE THING TO WORRY ABOUT IS VOLUME, NOT CORRECTNESS.** 112 BETs in ONE Sunday from models on placeholder thresholds with the six go-live gates explicitly unmet. For scale, **the lane with the measured record (+10.33% over 954) produced TWO picks across a ten-day board — a 56:1 ratio in favour of the lane with no evidence.** Per model: rec_yards 35, receptions 25, rush_yards 12, rush_attempts 12, rush_rec_yards 8, pass_tds 6, pass_yards 5, pass_attempts 4, pass_completions 4, anytime_td 1. **`rush_attempts` bets 46% of everything it scores and `rush_yards` 41%** — a cut that fires on half its universe is a placeholder, which is what the doc says it is.
- **A THIRD "publishes nothing looks like switched off" INSTANCE, in the same family:** `nfl_prop_sacks` and `nfl_prop_tackles_assists` evaluated 177 and 136 players and wrote **0 BETs and 0 non-BETs** — not even dead-zone rows. Zero rows of any kind is a missing input, not a quiet market. Flagged, not chased.
- **Context recovered on the artifacts, and it changes how the old evidence reads:** the twelve models committed in #215 were built from a REPLICA and **all twelve failed `pickle.load` on the pipeline machine** — they never scored anything, for two weeks, hidden because the family was paused and because the registry health check asks whether an active ROW exists rather than whether the file behind it opens. Retrained 2026-09-06 with `tests/test_model_artifacts_load.py` added. **So "eleven of twelve lost to the hold" is a BACKTEST result; the live-scoring history behind those models is zero.**

Production `picks`/`model_registry` read directly via `DATABASE_URL`. Odds API queried live for the board.

Items 1 and 2 of the write-up (decide the 112-bet volume; give the NFL scorer the card's look-ahead) both alter what a model does, so they are mike's or Matt's call and need an `Updated-By:` trailer — proposed, not applied, two days before kickoff.

**Session summary (2026-09-07, session 249 — MLB volume: half the picks come from a calibration switch that never reached the props):**

- Matt: *"far too much volume to keep up with on a daily basis, how can we
  become more efficient while still being profitable"* — MLB, pre-game and
  live, results from the last 7-10 days only because the models were revamped.
  **Analysis only. Nothing was changed; every option below is a model update
  and needs his call.** Full workings: `docs/mlb_volume_efficiency.md`.

- **The volume step is dated and it is not gradual.** MLB BETs went from ~6/day
  (3.4 pre-game + 3.0 live over 08-08 → 08-30) to ~30/day (20.6 + 9.0 over
  08-31 → 09-07). 95% of it is four models: `mlb_prop_pitcher_k` (60 BETs),
  `mlb_prop_pitcher_hits` (45), `mlb_prop_pitcher_outs` (27) and
  `mlb_live_total_runs` (all 63 live BETs).

- **The 7-10 day window cannot rank these models, and the file says so first.**
  Every pre-game MLB model changed version inside it — `pitcher_k`/`moneyline`
  retrained live 09-04 (#451), `pitcher_hits`/`outs`/`batter_runs`/`walks` live
  09-05 (#454), `f5` 09-04, `over_under` paused 09-03. Largest settled sample on
  a current version is 30 bets. So the analysis is built on volume mechanics and
  calibration, not on ROI league tables.

- **PRIMARY FINDING — `DECIDE_ON_CALIBRATED_PROB` has never changed a single
  production decision.** The calibrated-decision branch lives in
  `classify_edge` (`models/scorer.py:1124`). Player props never go through it:
  `_make_prop_pick` (`scorer.py:2908`) decides on the RAW number at
  `scorer.py:2950` and has no calibration branch. All ten models carrying a
  promoted map (`model_calibration WHERE promoted` — seven `mlb_prop_*`, three
  `wnba_prop_*`) are props, and every prop scorer in the repo (MLB, WNBA, NBA,
  NFL) routes through `_make_prop_pick`. No game-level model has a promoted map,
  so the flag is a no-op everywhere.
  - **Measured, not inferred.** Each pick carries the calibrated number it would
    have decided on in `picks.model_probability_cal`. Restricted to BETs where a
    map was actually live (cal differs from raw), 08-31 → 09-04: **56 of 57 —
    98% — fail their own `min_prob` or `min_edge` on their own stored calibrated
    probability** (`pitcher_k` 30/31, `pitcher_hits` 26/26). Applying the map as
    promoted would not halve these two models, it would nearly switch them off.
  - The twelve cuts shipped 2026-08-31 were chosen on calibrated sweeps
    (`config.py:249` projects `pitcher_k` at ~5.4 bets/wk; it fires ~52/wk.
    `config.py:251` projects `pitcher_hits` at ~19.8/wk; it fires ~39/wk).
  - **Second defect, found while checking the first.** `load_calibrations`
    (`probability_calibration.py:386`) reads `promoted_a/b` but filters on the
    CANDIDATE `method='platt'`, so an unfitted weekly refit that writes
    `method=NULL` silently disables a promoted map. `pitcher_k`/`pitcher_hits`
    carried a distinct cal number on 166/166 picks 08-31 → 09-03, 11/43 on
    09-04 and **0/95 from 09-05 on** — they have had no calibration at all for
    three days. `scorer.py:1120` says "only an endorsed map bites"; an
    un-endorsed refit can un-bite an endorsed one.
  - **Do not just flip it on.** The promoted maps were fitted 08-31, before the
    09-03/09-05 retrains — and because of the defect above, flipping the switch
    today would bite `batter_rbi/runs/tb/walks`, `pitcher_er` and three WNBA
    props, NOT the two models this is about. This morning's ModelCalibration
    pass refuses to refit either (47 and 26 graded picks since the retrain,
    needs 150) and measures both still overclaiming by **14.24pp** / **16.87pp**.

- **The two highest-volume models are miscalibrated at p < 0.005 on their own
  settled record.** `pitcher_k` claims 65.9% and delivers 42.6% over 54 bets
  (z = −3.64); `mlb_live_total_runs` claims 74.0% and delivers 55.6% over 63
  (z = −3.35). `mlb_over_under` claims 58.1% / delivers 29.2% (z = −2.88),
  which independently confirms its 09-03 pause. `pitcher_hits` (−0.46) and
  `pitcher_outs` (−1.00) are fine.

- **Line selection is NOT the defect.** All three pitcher props beat the close:
  CLV +1.62% (`pitcher_k`, 43 picks), +1.59% (`outs`), +1.17% (`hits`). The
  model losing the most money has the best CLV — so the case is for fixing its
  probability, not pausing it.

- **The cleanest cut on the board is the live model's inning.** Under the
  current cut (08-24 →), `mlb_live_total_runs` in **innings 1-3 is 26-31,
  −9.66u** (claims ~73%, delivers ~46%); in **innings 4+ it is 27-10, +11.69u**
  and well calibrated (73.6% claimed, 73.0% delivered). Both time halves agree
  in both bands.
  - **That is not the P&L of a gate, and the entry does not claim it is.**
    `LOCK_LIVE_PICKS_AT_FIRST_SIGNAL` gives exactly one BET per game (94 BETs /
    94 games since 08-24), so "inning 4+" is the record of games whose FIRST
    signal happened to land late. Under a gate an inning-2 game would re-lock at
    its first inning-4+ signal, at a different price. Volume saved is less than
    36 of 63; +11.69u is not the gate's return. What survives cleanly is the
    calibration split. The gate is properly simulable — the live loop writes
    every in-play snapshot to `odds` (642,582 totals rows over 188 games since
    08-24) — and that replay is the right next step before it ships.
  - Which matters because `config.LIVE_MAX_BETS_PER_WEEK` sets **30/wk** for
    this model — set 2026-08-30 on Matt's *"still too many live bets on MLB"* —
    and the weeks of 08-24 and 08-31 ran **31 and 63**. `config.py:556` is
    candid that nothing enforces it at score time.

- **Secondary levers, both measured:** a daily top-N-by-EV cap on the
  publishable pre-game set (everything = 125 picks −0.99u; top-10/day = 70
  picks **+13.03u**; ranks 11+ = **−14.02u**) — flagged in-sample, with a
  same-direction but thin out-of-window check; and one-bet-per-pitcher dedupe
  (31 of 104 pitcher-starts carry two markets = ~3.9 picks/day, −0.27u total
  cost over the window).

- **Load shape:** 79% of pre-game BETs land ≥6h before first pitch (mean lead
  11.3h) — one morning sitting. 75% of live BETs land 18:00-23:59 ET. The
  evening is where "can't keep up" actually bites, and the inning gate is
  aimed at exactly that.

- **External:** DraftKings' Terms of Use prohibit bots/scripts/automated means
  for placing wagers (account restriction, stake forfeiture) and there is no
  public bet-placement API; FanDuel and BetMGM are the same. There is no route
  to make 30 bets/day cheaper to place, so the efficiency has to be fewer,
  better picks. `dk_bet_link` is already on 228/228 MLB BETs in the window.

- **Checked and NOT a bug:**
  `mv_scored_pick_outcomes` excluding live picks is deliberate
  (`data/migrations/materialize_scored_pick_outcomes.sql:114`, and
  `live_record_start_2026_09_01.sql` explains the published-record split), not
  a bug — live P&L in this analysis is computed off `picks` with the
  `dk_odds IS NOT NULL` gate CLAUDE.md §6 requires.

**Second half of the session — Matt picked options 1, 2 and 4, and they were
built. `Updated-By: mike`.**

- **1. The calibrated decision reaches player props.** `_make_prop_pick` gains
  the branch `classify_edge` has carried since 2026-08-31: decide on
  `_calibrated(model_id, prob)` and on `cal - dk_implied_prob`, store the raw
  numbers unchanged. `tests/test_prop_calibrated_decision.py` (20 tests) —
  **watched to fail**: with the pre-fix body,
  `test_a_hot_prop_model_no_longer_fires_the_marginal_bet` and
  `test_avoid_on_a_prop_is_judged_on_the_calibrated_number_too` both fail. A
  source tripwire guards the branch going missing again, because the bug was
  never a wrong branch — it was no branch, and
  `test_decide_on_calibrated.py::test_a_calibrated_cut_is_paired_with_a_calibrated_decision`
  passed throughout by asserting the FLAG was on. A guard dead code can satisfy.

- **1b. A promoted map now carries its own method and its own endorsement.**
  `load_calibrations` reads `promoted_method`; `promote()` requires **helps AND
  transfers** (it required `applied`, which is helps alone — `pitcher_er` helps
  and does not close, and its own note says "publish it; do not build a
  threshold on it yet"); `demote()` is new. Three columns added via
  `_LATE_COLUMNS`, so `ensure_schema` and the DDL guard both know about them.
  `tests/test_calibration_promotion.py` (12 tests), the inert-map pair watched
  to fail against the old filter.

- **1c. `data/migrations/promotions_endorsed_only_2026_09_07.sql`** re-freezes
  the four still-endorsed maps (`batter_rbi`, `batter_runs`, `wnba_points`,
  `wnba_rebounds`) on today's fit and demotes six no longer endorsed
  (`batter_tb`, `batter_walks`, `pitcher_er`, `pitcher_hits`, `pitcher_k`,
  `wnba_threes`). Guarded on the property it establishes — "no promoted row has
  a NULL method" — never on the shape of its own output, and it demotes a kept
  row it cannot re-freeze so it can terminate. Registered in ACTIVE_MIGRATIONS.
  **Deliberately not promoted:** `mlb_prop_pitcher_walks`,
  `wnba_prop_player_assists` and `wnba_prop_player_pra` are endorsed today and
  were never promoted; promoting them is a separate call for a person.

- **4. One BET per pitcher, plus an interim daily cap** (Matt chose the cap
  when told option 1 buys no pitcher-prop relief for ~5-8 days).
  `config.PROP_ONE_BET_PER_PLAYER` is a POOL — only models that price the same
  entity — and `config.PROP_MAX_SIGNALS_PER_DAY` is the pre-game twin of
  `LIVE_MAX_SIGNALS_PER_DAY` at 3 each for `pitcher_k` / `pitcher_hits`. Both
  rank on the CALIBRATED number, because raw EV structurally favours whichever
  model is most overconfident and the whole point is comparing models. Both run
  over the whole slate before anything is written, so `run_prop_scorer` now
  accumulates and inserts once instead of inserting per model.

- **A turned-away BET is a NONE with a reason, in a new `picks.downgrade_reason`
  column.** Not `condition_status`: that is the NFL locked-pick health signal
  (OK/DEGRADED/GONE) and the void marker, read by publishers that know those
  three words. The reason is persisted rather than logged because CLAUDE.md 7
  grades every NONE, and a capped-away BET that reads as the model declining is
  a number the model never said.

- **Measured on the eight days of picks actually written** (pitcher props only):
  136 BETs / 17.0 a day / −1.57u as fired → 102 / 12.8 / +3.65u after
  one-bet-per-player → **67 / 8.4 / +10.10u** after the 3+3 cap. The VOLUME
  column is exact; the units column is in-sample and is offered as "this cost
  nothing measurable", not as a forecast.

- **2. The live inning gate was NOT shipped — Matt chose replay first.**
  `scripts/live_inning_gate_replay.py` reuses the production decision
  (`classify_live_signal`, `expected_value`, `build_live_state_row`, the real
  EV floor and edge cap) over the kept history — 108,284 `live_game_state` rows
  and 642,582 in-play DK totals rows since 08-24 — pairs each state with the
  price in force at that moment, and takes the first qualifying signal at each
  candidate gate. It exists because `LOCK_LIVE_PICKS_AT_FIRST_SIGNAL` gives one
  BET per game (94 BETs / 94 games), so the settled "innings 4+" row is a
  self-selected set and not the gate's return.
  `tests/test_live_inning_gate_replay.py` pins the two things that decide every
  number it prints — the no-lookahead pairing and the over/under sign — and the
  lookahead guard was watched to fail. Its grading rule was validated against
  production settlement first and agrees on 94 of 94.

- **AND THE REPLAY SAYS DO NOT SHIP THE GATE.** Over 169 games since 08-24:
  ungated 120 bets −2.89u; gate 3 +3.68u; **gate 4 +4.19u; gate 5 −2.70u**;
  gate 6 +1.78u. Gate 4 is a PEAK, not a plateau — its neighbour one step away
  flips negative, which CLAUDE.md 7 says to refuse — and under gate 4 the model
  still claims 74.1% and delivers 57.4%. So §4's +11.69u for "innings 4+" was
  largely SELECTION: the games whose first signal happened to arrive late were a
  favourable draw, and forcing the other games to wait does not inherit their
  record. **Recommendation reversed on the evidence: do not gate.** The
  calibration split itself is untouched and still significant (early signals
  claim ~73%, deliver ~46%, both halves); the repair is the probability, not the
  inning — and `mlb_live_total_runs` has never had a map promoted, so it is now
  the obvious candidate for one.

- **The restricted re-run confirms it, and the control found a defect.** The
  replay bets 120 games to production's 94: +39 production never bet, and **13
  production DID bet that the replay does not** — a fault in the replay, still
  unexplained. On the 81 games both agree on: ungated **+3.11u** (against
  production's own +2.03u over the same window, so the control passes), gate 3
  **+9.86u**, gate 4 +6.89u, gate 5 **−0.36u**, gate 6 +3.49u. The two tables
  pick DIFFERENT best gates — 4 unrestricted, 3 restricted — and in both, the
  neighbour one step from the best flips negative. An optimum that moves with
  the sample and has a negative neighbour either way is noise. With the 13
  missing games on top, this replay is not something to build a live gate on.

**Third part — Matt: "do the calibration map". Measured; it is not promotable,
and two more defects of the same family were found getting there.**

- **Defect 3: the calibration fit could not SEE a live model.** `fetch_graded`
  read `mv_scored_pick_outcomes`, which excludes `is_live` by construction, so
  every live lane returned zero rows and the weekly pass printed *"only 0 graded
  picks (need 150) - identity map, unfitted"* on every run since it shipped.
  `mlb_live_total_runs` has **126** graded BETs and was reported as **0** - the
  empty-board-versus-broken-pipeline failure, in the one place that decides
  whether a model's probabilities get corrected. **Fixed:** live lanes read
  `picks` (BETs, `dk_odds IS NOT NULL`), and `CLEAN_WINDOWS` does not apply to
  them - it marks where the dead-zone NONE rows were deleted, and a live lane
  never writes NONE, so the exclusion would cost 18 of 126 for no correction.
  `_is_live_lane` uses the name test as well as `config.LIVE_MODELS`, because
  `nfl_live_prop` is written by a subsystem that never registers there.

- **Now visible for the first time: every live lane is 12-15pp hot.**
  `mlb_live_total_runs` +14.38pp over 126, `ncaaf_live_total` +15.27pp over 48,
  `ncaaf_live_win_prob` +11.97pp over 6, `nfl_live_prop` 0 graded. This is a
  live-betting finding, not an MLB one.

- **Defect 4: `classify_live_signal` decided on the raw probability** - the
  third and last place the 2026-08-31 rule had to reach. **Fixed**, and a NO-OP
  the day it ships (no live lane has a promoted map), which is the point: the
  props spent six days betting raw while the map moved a display column. Every
  floor now reads the same number, the EV floor included - a prob floor on the
  calibrated number beside an EV floor on the raw one is two cuts aimed at
  different quantities. `tests/test_live_calibrated_decision.py` (16 tests), six
  watched to fail against the pre-fix bodies.

- **THE MAP ITSELF IS REFUSED, on two independent counts.** Preview-fitted on
  all 126: raw gap +14.38pp, held-out **18.48pp -> 8.98pp**, `helps` TRUE and
  **`transfers` FALSE** against the 6.0pp cap - it helps and does not close, the
  `mlb_prop_pitcher_er` verdict. And n = 126 against `MIN_GRADED` 150. A
  structural reason it may never close: live lanes write no NONE rows and their
  AVOIDs are never settled (232 live AVOIDs, 0 graded), so the fit sees one
  narrow band above the model's own 0.70 floor, where a Platt map is close to a
  single offset.

- **And the decision it implies.** The map takes a claimed 0.74 to **0.598**
  while the lane's cut (0.70 / 0.14 / EV 0.32) was swept on RAW numbers, so
  promoting without re-sweeping takes the lane to near zero bets - the props'
  trap run the other way. Promotion and re-sweep are ONE decision.
  **Deliberately not done:** `MIN_GRADED` and `MAX_TRANSFER_GAP_PP` were left
  alone; lowering either to make this map fit is moving the bar to clear the
  jump.

- **Suite:** 2,927 passed, 16 skipped, 0 failed.

**The three open items, closed.**

- **The two WNBA maps are promoted; the MLB one is refused, on the same test.**
  All three (`wnba_prop_player_assists`, `wnba_prop_player_pra`,
  `mlb_prop_pitcher_walks`) are endorsed by today's fit, so "is the map good"
  does not separate them. What does is **which scale the model's CUT was swept
  on**: assists is *"0.50/0.10 on the floor-corrected CALIBRATED sweep"*
  (config.py:269) and pra is *"calibrated sweep + time split"* (:271) — both are
  the MLB prop bug exactly, a calibrated cut applied to raw numbers, and
  promoting closes it. `mlb_prop_pitcher_walks` is *"2026-06-21 full-outcome"*,
  swept on RAW probabilities, so promoting would OPEN the mismatch in reverse —
  the trap documented for `mlb_live_total_runs`. It is paused, so this costs
  nothing today; the reasoning is what matters when it is unpaused. Both new
  promotions are in the migration, under the same helps-AND-transfers bar
  `promote()` applies, and in its rollback block.

- **Committed on `void-superseded-wind-picks`, NOT pushed, and not on a branch of
  its own — deliberately.** Another session is working in this same worktree and
  switching branches under it would break its work. That is also how
  `f4bd516f` ("Tighten the NFL prop cuts…", `Updated-By: matt`) came to contain
  this session's `PROP_ONE_BET_PER_PLAYER` and `PROP_MAX_SIGNALS_PER_DAY`: a
  commit-all swept an unrelated working tree in. **The attribution on those two
  config blocks is wrong — they are mike's call, not matt's.** Recorded here
  rather than repaired by rewriting a shared commit, which is the worse of the
  two fixes; CLAUDE.md 1b's purpose is that the record answers "whose call was
  it" six months later, and this answers it.


- **Suite:** `python -m pytest -q tests/` — 2,911 passed, 16 skipped, 0 failed.
  Three existing tests needed updating for the new query shape and column list
