**Session summary (2026-09-06, session 248 — a live pick reached Discord and never reached the app, and the pre-game card posted twice):**

- Matt sent a screenshot of #ncaaf: a `UCLA ML (live)` card at 1:07 AM, and the
  `Ole Miss vs Louisville Under 55.5` card **twice** at 12:29 PM. One NCAAF bet
  in Discord that the app never showed. Two separate bugs in one screenshot.

**Bug 1 — the app's live board had no midnight lookback.**

- The pick: `pick_id 1701189`, `ncaaf_live_win_prob`, UCLA ML -154, prob 0.7423,
  edge 0.1360, `created_at 2026-09-06 05:07:06Z` (1:07:06 AM ET), and
  **`game_date = '2026-09-05'`** — the game kicked off 10:37 PM ET on the 5th.
- `useLivePicks` asked `fetchLivePicks(todayET())`, and at 1:07 AM ET `todayET()`
  is `2026-09-06`. `.eq('game_date', date)` matched nothing. The live loop scored
  the game, the Discord producer posted it and push delivered it — all three
  resolve their window through `config.live_slate_dates()`, which carries
  yesterday until 6am ET precisely because a game carries the game_date of its
  KICKOFF. The app was the only surface without that window, and it went dark at
  midnight while the game was in its fourth quarter.
- #512 had already fixed the *other* half of this the same day (the date was
  frozen in `useState`, so a warm app polled yesterday forever). Recomputing a
  SINGLE date per fetch does not help a game that is genuinely on yesterday's
  date, which is why this survived that fix.
- **Fixed:** `liveSlateDatesET()` in `mobile/src/lib/format.ts` is the app's
  mirror of `config.live_slate_dates()` — today first, yesterday added while the
  ET hour is under `LIVE_SLATE_LOOKBACK_UNTIL_HOUR_ET` (6). `fetchLivePicks` and
  `fetchLiveGameStates` read `.in('game_date', dates)`; `useLivePicks` resolves
  the window per fetch and returns it, and `PicksHomeScreen` feeds the same
  window to `useLiveGameStates` so a late game's score and clock keep updating
  after the rollover instead of freezing. The games read still bounds on
  "kicked off and not yet settled", so yesterday's finished games drop out on
  their own and the wider window costs nothing.
- **Verified:** `mobile/scripts/verify_live_slate.ts` (10 checks, watched fail 3
  at the boundary with the pre-fix one-date body restored), three new source
  tripwires in `tests/test_live_slate_midnight.py` (watched fail on
  origin/master, pass here), tsc error set byte-identical to origin/master (62
  pre-existing, 0 in touched files), `verify_live_status` /
  `verify_live_tracked` / `verify_signal_counts` / `verify_live_record_start`
  all pass.

**Bug 2 — the pre-game card posted twice, and the ledger says it posted once.**

- `push_sent` holds exactly ONE `discord_signal` row for
  `NCAAF_2026-09-06_louisville_ole-miss:ncaaf_over_under` (sent_at
  12:29:11.358 ET, message `1546195572803772550`), and `picks` holds exactly ONE
  BET for that game+model. One row, one message id, two cards in the channel: a
  read → post → ledger **race**, not a duplicate pick. The `new_bet` (push) row
  is stamped 12:29:11.106, 252ms earlier, so the push path was in the same
  window.
- The order of those three steps is deliberate and must not change — nothing is
  ledgered unless the POST succeeded, so a down webhook leaves the signal
  eligible for the next pass. What changed is that there are now TWO processes
  running them: `signal_publisher.publish_new_signals` (shipped 2026-09-05) is
  called from the pre-game line poller on `pollers` every 30 seconds AND from
  the scheduler's card polls, the refresh pass and the daily pipeline on
  `worker`. The ledger cannot close that window, because it only learns about a
  send after the send.
- **Fixed:** `tracking/publish_lock.py` — a Postgres advisory lock, held by a
  session and visible cluster-wide, wrapping the whole sequence. A run that
  cannot take it returns 0 and does nothing, because the holder is at that
  moment publishing the very signals it would have published; the batch stays
  un-ledgered and the next pass takes it. Applied to all three publishers
  (§1b: assess a change to one surface against all of them) —
  `notify_discord_signals`, `notify_discord_live` and `notify_signal_changes`.
  The Discord duplicate was visible because Discord renders a card; the push
  half sends a member the same alert twice, which is worse and invisible.
- **Verified:** `tests/test_publisher_lock.py` (10 tests, including a
  re-entrancy test where the `pollers` run lands mid-POST of the `worker` run;
  5 watched fail on origin/master), full suite 2691 passed / 16 skipped.

**Checked and NOT a bug: the Ole Miss pre-game pick itself.** Every gate the app
applies passes on it — `signal_type='BET'`, prob 0.7176 ≥ 0.65, edge 0.1827 ≥
0.0, dk_odds -115 ≥ the -200 floor, `ncaaf_over_under` neither paused nor
retired in `model_action_thresholds` or in the bundled fallback; RLS on `picks`
is open; `game_date='2026-09-06'` is today's board; the row cap is nowhere near
binding (643 non-live rows for the date, BET ordered ahead of NONE); and
`fetchUpcomingNcaafPicks` uses `.gt(game_date, today)` so it cannot shadow it.
It should render. Left as a question for Matt in case that was the pick he meant.

**Found in passing, NOT fixed (flagged only): the same game exists twice in
`games` under two ids.** `NCAAF_2026-09-05_ucla_california` (`data_source
'live'`, commence 02:37Z) and `NCAAF_2026-09-06_ucla_california` (`data_source
'cfbd'`, commence 02:30Z) are one football game keyed by ET date and by UTC
date. Both now carry the same final 45-24, so nothing double-counts today, but
two rows for one game is a settlement and a track-record hazard. Harmless to the
fix above (picks reference the 09-05 id, and the cfbd row is excluded by the
"not yet settled" bound).

**Session summary (2026-09-06, session 247 — the line ruler is drawn in the mode's own idiom, so Over is the book's line):**

- Matt, with a competitor's MLB Leaders board beside ours: *"I want to fix how
  over under is displayed. Here is how a competitor does it for when user
  selects over."* The screenshot: a dropdown reading **Over**, a tick ruler
  whose labelled stops are **0.5 / 1.5 / 2.5**, a centre pill on **0.5**, and a
  headline **"O 0.5 Hits"**.
- **What ours did, and why it was worse than it looked.** Session 245's modes
  (`lib/hitMode.ts`, shipped the day before) made Over shift the bet a whole
  number UP — ruler 1 meant Over 1.5 — so that no two modes could ever name the
  same bet. Three costs, and the third is the one that matters:
  1. The user did the arithmetic. The ruler said `1`, the headline said
     `2+ Hits`, and `1.5` — **the only number their sportsbook prints** — was
     the smallest text on the row.
  2. Two vocabularies in one control: the ruler counted in whole numbers while
     the mode was named in the book's.
  3. **Over 0.5 was unreachable in Over mode.** The most-bet prop line there is
     existed only as "At Least 1". A user who picked Over and dragged to the
     bottom of the ruler landed on Over 1.5 and had no way down.
- **The fix: the mode chooses the VOCABULARY, and the ruler is drawn in it.**
  At Least keeps whole stops and the fan's idiom (`1+ Hits`); Over and Under
  print the book's own half-point line on every tick, in the centre pill, and
  in the headline (`Over 0.5 Hits`). `LineRuler` gained a `format` prop — it
  still snaps on the integer STOP INDEX, because a scroll offset divides
  cleanly into one, and the face is a rendering of the stop rather than the
  state.
- **Every mode now resolves a stop to the SAME line.** `selectionFor(n, mode)`
  is `{ line: n - 0.5, side }` for all three. Switching modes therefore renames
  the bet without moving it, the ceiling is one number (the
  `+ (hitMode === 'under' ? 1 : 0)` notch is gone), and Over starts where the
  book starts. **At Least 1 and Over 0.5 are now openly the same bet under two
  names**, which is the honest outcome: they always were.
- **Two idioms, two homes, and neither is spoken in the other's sentence.**
  `thresholdLabel` is the fan's (`2+`, `1 or fewer`); the new `bookLineLabel`
  is the book's (`Over 1.5`). The odds pill's off-line caption and the
  add-to-betslip explainer both describe **what a book posts**, so both moved
  to the book's — the explainer had started reading *"The board is on Over 0.5
  Hits; BetMGM only posts 2+"*, two vocabularies in one breath. The
  equivalence between them lives in exactly one place now: the mode picker
  sheet, where all three appear together, which is what a picker is for.
- **Matt's call on the fan translation: book idiom ONLY in Over/Under.** The
  small `Over 0.5` secondary label that session 245's UX review added beside
  the headline survives in **At Least mode only** — there the headline is the
  fan's idiom and the book's number would otherwise be nowhere on screen. In
  Over/Under the headline IS that number, and printing it twice is noise.
- **Verified.** `verify_hit_rate.ts` grew the new mapping as a table
  (stop x mode -> line, side, headline, ruler face) and **18 of its checks were
  watched failing against the old mapping** before passing on this one — a test
  that passes without the fix is not a test. `verify_line_legs`,
  `verify_stats_odds`, `verify_stats_board`, `verify_betslip` pass. `tsc`
  error set **byte-identical to `origin/master`** (34 pre-existing, 0 in
  touched files), diffed against a `git worktree --detach origin/master`.
  `python -m pytest -q tests/`: **2678 passed, 16 skipped**.
- **UX review: eight findings, all eight addressed** (`frontend-ux-designer`,
  Mobbin connected — the sportsbook comparators are still absent from its
  library, so the ruler references are the value-ruler pattern from
  fitness/finance apps, which is where iOS actually solves this):
  1. *The tick pitch collided.* A face carrying a decimal is up to four
     characters, and at the fixed 26pt pitch "23.5" and "24.5" abutted with a
     point of air — one run of digits at default Dynamic Type, overlapping
     above it, and the opaque centre pill grew wide enough to hide its
     neighbours. **The pitch now follows the widest face**
     (`Math.max(26, faceChars * 8 + 6)`), which leaves At Least at 26 and
     gives Over/Under ~38.
  2. *The fix cured Over/Under and broke At Least.* The betslip explainer
     quotes the board headline and the book's line in ONE sentence, so
     hard-coding the book's notation made it read "The board is on 2+ Hits;
     FanDuel only posts Over 2.5". **`modeLineLabel` is the switch**, and
     `propLineSheetInput` takes it as a parameter rather than assuming an
     idiom. This is the general lesson of the session: the moment a second
     vocabulary exists, every place that names a line has to be TOLD which one.
  3. *The off-line caption tripled in width* in a 62pt column with no
     `numberOfLines` — "Under 224.5" wrapped and made one row of a 25-row
     board taller than its neighbours. **Now the book's own short form**
     ("O 224.5" / "U 224.5"), one line, in the board's idiom; the pill's
     accessibilityLabel keeps the full words.
  4. *The picker sheet was declared the only home of the equivalence and never
     stated it.* Two rows print "1+ Hits" and "Over 0.5 Hits" with nothing
     saying they are the same bet. **The subtitle now names both numbers.**
  5. The ruler's VoiceOver value was a bare face; it now announces the whole
     bet ("Over 0.5 Hits") via a `describe` prop, since the side and the stat
     live on other elements and this is the one element a screen-reader user
     drives.
  6. The sheet's two a11y label branches had drifted into two shapes; one now.
  7. **Under's top stop came back.** Dropping the `+1` was tidy and wrong:
     Under n names "n-1 or fewer", so without it "10 or fewer Hits" was
     unsayable while "10+ Hits" was. The stops are the same set; the CEILING
     is not, and the comment now says which.
  8. A comment still claimed the caption was printed in the fan's idiom, two
     lines above the code that had stopped doing it. Rewritten.
- **`ux_scan --changed`: 3 findings, all three identical on `origin/master`**
  (the same three pressables, shifted by the diff) — 0 new in touched files.
  The ruler-tick one is deliberate: the ticks are
  `accessibilityElementsHidden` under the adjustable wrapper.
- **Raised as a follow-up, not fixed here** (`docs/followups.md`):
  `PlayerStatsScreen` still headlines its threshold in the fan's idiom only, so
  a member drilling in from an Over-mode board crosses an idiom boundary with
  no bridge. Whether the mode should ride along in the navigation params is a
  decision, not a bug.
- **Not verified: rendering.** All the geometry above is computed from the
  design tokens and SF Pro advance widths, not from a screenshot. The Hits
  ruler in Over mode on a small device would confirm finding 1 in ten seconds.
- **Pre-existing failures found in passing and NOT fixed** (all three fail
  identically on `origin/master`, so none is this change's):
  `verify_stats_pill.ts` (1), `verify_custom_model_filters.ts` (4),
  `verify_units.ts` (24 — needs a live DB). Flagged, not touched.
**Session summary (2026-09-06, session 246 — why the prop rule never fired, in any sport):**

- mike: *"why arent the NFL prop models picking any picks for week 1? other NFL
  models have been firing"*, then *"we had a different method for picking prop
  bets. what the fuck happened."*
- **THE HEADLINE: `models/market_relative.py` — the de-vig-the-sharp-book rule,
  described in its own docstring as "the one construction in this repo with a
  blind-tested positive result" — HAS NEVER WRITTEN A PICK. In any sport.**
  `select count(*) from picks where model_id like '%prop_market'` returns **0**.
  Three sports, three unrelated causes, none of them modelling:
  - **NFL** — gated to `NFL_PROP_WINDOW_HOURS = 30`. Measured 2026-09-07 01:56Z,
    first Week 1 kickoff 2026-09-10 00:20Z: lead **70.4h**, so
    `run_nfl_prop_card` returned free. The job was registered and healthy; the
    early return happens BEFORE its `log.info`, so silence was the expected
    signature of the gate, not evidence of a break. **Fixed here.**
  - **MLB** — `models/mlb_prop_market.py` (built #366) has **no card script, no
    scheduler job, no pipeline step, no `config.py` entry and no entry in
    `paper_tracker._PROP_MARKET_STAT_BY_MODEL`**. Its only mention outside
    itself is a comment in `prune_odds.py`. It is a library nothing imports, and
    it could not settle a pick even if it made one. NOT fixed here.
  - **WNBA** — ported 2026-08-31. Pinnacle WNBA prop quotes stop **2026-08-30**.
    It shipped the day after its input ended, has run in every refresh pass
    since, and reports `{'flags': 0, 'published': 0}` forever. 8 WNBA games exist
    after that date, so the prop INGEST is a second gap. NOT fixed here.
- **The general lesson, and it is the same one #531 addressed for schedulers: a
  PRODUCER whose correct output is sometimes nothing cannot be watched by
  watching its output.** A card that publishes 0 because it is gated, unwired,
  or starved looks identical to a card that publishes 0 because the market is
  efficient. #531 gave jobs a heartbeat; nothing equivalent exists for these.
- **Why 30h, factually.** It bracketed the two offsets the backtest graded
  (T-24h, T-3h) and stopped just past the earlier. The finding that justifies
  polling argues the other way: only 13% of edges are the same proposition at
  both offsets, so each poll finds NEW bets. The only thing that argued for
  stopping was a ~1.26M-credit estimate. mike: *"I dont care about credit
  costs"*, then *"1) yes"* on the measured figure. **Widened to 240h**, matching
  `NFL_POLL_HORIZON_DAYS`. Measured: a 16-event pass is **484 credits**, so
  hourly across a 126-day season is ~1.46M against 4,528,091 remaining.
  Pinnacle does quote at T-70h — the fetch returned all eight sharp markets.
  No offset beyond T-24h has been graded and publishing LOCKS insert-once;
  `created_at` vs `game_time` recovers the offset so the season measures it.
- **`--days` was pinned at 2**, tuned to the 30h window. Widening the window
  alone would have polled hourly for ten days while pricing only 48 hours —
  applied, paid for, doing a fraction of the work. Now derived from the window.
- **BUG, fixed: `run_nfl_prop_scorer` could never complete a run.**
  `_nfl_pregame_cutoff_map` COALESCEs `games.first_pitch_at` (TEXT) with
  `nfl_team_game_stats.commence_time` (TIMESTAMPTZ), casting only the operands
  of the comparison, not the returned arms → `COALESCE types text and timestamp
  with time zone cannot be matched`. `data/first_pitch.pregame_cutoff_sql` has
  the same shape and is CORRECT there — it takes one alias and both of `games`'
  columns are text. Verified against production after the fix: 13 games, 13
  cutoffs.
- **FOUND, NOT SHIPPED: all twelve `nfl_prop_*` artifacts committed in #215 are
  unloadable** — `XGBoostError: input stream corrupted`. Not version skew: 31
  other xgboost artifacts load on the same interpreter and same run, five of
  them declaring the same xgboost 3.4.1. `model_artifacts` holds no copy, so the
  committed bytes are the only ones. `docs/nfl_props_model.md` §6 already said
  these must be "retrained where `model_registry` lives" rather than committed
  from the replica; that step was skipped. Nobody noticed for two weeks because
  the family was paused (nothing opens a paused model) and because
  `system_health`'s `model_registry` check asks whether an ACTIVE ROW exists,
  never whether the file behind it deserialises. **The unpause and the retrain
  are held back** — one model retrained cleanly, the other eleven are pending,
  and the count-model training path is ~20x slower per Optuna trial than the
  gamma path (5 CV folds vs 3 explains ~2x of that; the rest is unexplained and
  made an 11-model run untenable in one sitting).
- **`models/saved/.gitattributes` added** (`* -text`). The root file carries the
  identical rule for `ncaaf_live/data/artifacts` and has since a rebase crashed
  the suite; `models/saved` was never covered. Patterns are relative to their
  own directory, which is why this is `*` and not `models/saved/*`.
- **MLB measurement, reported as NOT trustworthy.** `scripts/mlb_prop_market_sweep.py`
  grades the rule on the only Pinnacle MLB data that exists (2026-08-27..09-05,
  10 dates): negative at every cut from 2pp to 7pp, +0.17% at 8pp, and negative
  in both halves of a time split. **That number is not reportable as a verdict.**
  The grading check that §7 demands first found our computed `batter_total_bases`
  over-rate at **24.7% against DraftKings' own de-vigged 34.9%** — a −10.1pp gap
  over 1,585 rows, the same fingerprint that condemned NFL tackles+assists in
  §5b (−9.1pp, one-sided). total_bases is 187 of 201 bets at 5pp, all but 14 on
  the over. **Our data was then checked against the official MLB box scores for
  2026-09-05 and is EXACT** — 321 of 321 batter lines match on total_bases and
  at_bats, nobody missing, and the population over-1.5 rate is 33.3% against
  DK's 34.9%. So the column is right and the sweep's JOIN is selecting a biased
  subset (DK quotes ~11 batters/game in our data, and the joined subset runs
  ~8pp below the population even restricted to AB>=3 starters). Unresolved; the
  MLB ROI numbers must not be used until it is.
- **Deliberately NOT done.** The twelve stay paused. `mlb_prop_market` is not
  wired — measuring first was the right order and the measurement is not yet
  trustworthy. No threshold was borrowed from NFL to MLB.

**Session summary (2026-09-06, session 245 — the go-live gate comes out of the app's copy entirely):**

- Matt, an hour after 242 shipped: *"Remove all paper trading. I see spread
  opener for NCAAF has the same message. Update that one as well and any
  others."* Session 242 had scoped the fix to IN-PLAY models, on the reasoning
  that CLAUDE.md §2 makes the >= 50-settled gate a pre-game rule. **That was the
  wrong cut, and the tell was available before shipping**: `ncaaf_spread` is
  pre-game, so it kept the line, and the Models tab in the screenshot 242 was
  answering had `Spread (Opener)` sitting two rows above the card being
  complained about. The objection was never *which models the gate applies to* —
  it is that narrating a model's distance from being backed announces paper
  trading, on a platform whose §2 opens "The platform is LIVE — this is not a
  paper-trading system."
- **Swept every user-facing surface carrying it. Four, and the search that
  bounded it is the useful part**: `GO_LIVE_SETTLED_PICKS`, "not backed",
  "paper", "unproven" and "too few to judge" across `.ts/.tsx/.py/.md/.sql`,
  which also confirmed **no Discord producer, email, dashboard or App Store
  string carries the gate** — it was app-only, so the fix is app-only.
  1. `thinSampleCaption` — now takes NO arguments and returns one string for
     every model, **"Too few to judge yet"**.
  2. `ExplainerScreen` — "A model isn't backed for real money until it has a
     real sample behind it — 50+ settled picks with positive ROI."
  3. `OnboardingModal` — the same claim on the "Graded in public" slide.
  4. `TrackRecordScreen`'s intro — the same claim again, including the in-play
     exception clause 242 had just added to it.
- **What was kept, and why it is not the same thing.** Each rewrite keeps the
  sample-size honesty and drops the status claim: a record a handful of bets
  deep is greyed out and marked too early to read, rather than hidden until it
  looks good. That is `MIN_PICKS_FOR_COLOURED_ROI` arithmetic — 0-1 renders
  -100% — not a gate. `SettingsScreen`'s "the live models are unproven" was
  deliberately left standing and raised as a decision: removing a risk
  disclosure from a paid gambling product is not something to infer.
- **`GO_LIVE_SETTLED_PICKS` is deleted from `lib/recordStart`.** Nothing
  branched on it; its only job in the app was being quoted at members. **The
  gate itself is untouched** and still governs backing server-side
  (`models/backtester.GO_LIVE_MIN_PICKS = 50`). Whether that business rule
  survives is a separate call for Matt — a copy change must not decide it.
- **The rule worth keeping from 242+243 together: a copy guard that greps for a
  WORD cannot see the same claim made as a NUMBER.**
  `verify_live_record_start.ts` has banned "paper" in `screens/` and
  `components/` for days and passed throughout, while four surfaces told members
  their models were not backed yet. The rule went to `.claude/rules/frontend.md`
  (path-scoped, loads on any `mobile/` file) rather than CLAUDE.md, which is at
  its 36,000-byte cap — `tests/test_claude_md_size.py` caught the first attempt
  and it was right to: the rule governs front-end copy, not every session.
  Measured story in `docs/rules_evidence.md`.
- **The UX review caught that honesty had become CONDITIONAL.** The three
  removed sentences were unconditional — they qualified the whole board — so a
  model at 12 settled bets was left with a coloured ROI and no qualifier
  anywhere. The Record intro now says every sample here is small and a few dozen
  bets tells you little either way: no number, no bar, no "backed". It also
  caught that the caveat did not survive the tap — `BuiltInModelDetailScreen`
  rendered the same 1-bet number in full bet-green — and that the caption fired
  beside an em-dash ROI on all-unpriced models, where the Models tab guards it.
- **Declined: echoing "the live models are unproven" onto the Live tab.** The
  reviewer is right that the disclosure now sits only on a Settings row. Adding
  a caveat to a surface, on a change whose brief is removing them, is Matt's
  call, not an inference — raised with him.
- **The §7 rule is enforced, not just written down.** Two checks added to
  `verify_live_record_start.ts`: no screen or component carries gate copy, and
  the app defines no gate constant to render. The second is the half that holds
  — a screen cannot interpolate a number the app does not define. **Both were
  watched FAILING** on the exact copy that shipped before being kept.
- **Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` error set **byte-identical to origin/master**
  (60, 0 in touched files), against a fresh `git worktree --detach
  origin/master`; `verify_live_record_start.ts` **ALL PASS** (20 checks);
  `python -m pytest -q tests/` **2588 passed, 16 skipped**; `ux_scan` **0
  blockers**, and all 5 should-fix findings traced by `git blame` to `2bc1854`,
  none on a line this diff touches (the count reached 11 only because
  `BuiltInModelDetailScreen` joined the changed set).
  `frontend-ux-designer` reviewed before the PR opened.
**Session summary (2026-09-06, session 244 — the Live tab was empty 81% of the time, so it became a segment):**

- Matt asked a navigation question: keep Live as its own bottom tab, or make it
  a filter inside each sport so the control reads `Picks | Signals | Live
  Signals`? He asked for the UX designer agent and Mobbin on it.
- **The measurement decided it.** `picks` where `is_live AND signal_type='BET'`,
  30 days to 2026-09-06: **175 live BETs on 25 of 31 days**, ~7/day (max 35),
  first-to-last span 2.8h → roughly **5.3h of board occupancy per active day**,
  so the board was **empty ~81% of the clock**. And the board is sport-scoped
  (`LiveScreen` filtered to the selected sport), so per sport: MLB 123 bets / 25
  days, NCAAF 51 / 4, WNBA 1 / 1, and **zero in 30 days for NBA, NHL, NFL, UFC
  and GOLF** — for five of eight sports the tab was empty 100% of the time. In
  September, an NFL user tapped Live and got an empty state every single time.
- **The precedent was already in the app.** `PicksHomeScreen`'s own docstring:
  the `Today | Signals` control "replaces the old separate Picks and Signals
  tabs (which both showed BET picks and read as redundant)". Live was the third
  state of the same object. Both screens already called `useSportFilter`, used
  the same `PickCard`, the same `useLiveGameStates`, the same `EmptyState`; the
  Live header was a lossy copy of the Picks header (no BetslipButton, no
  InfoTooltip, and a bare `SportToggle` with neither `available` nor
  `signalCounts`, so it showed no muting and no badges).
- **Mobbin agreed, via the UX agent.** DAZN pins a `LIVE` pill into the date
  strip; FotMob and theScore put live inside the date segment (theScore badges
  the Scores TAB ICON instead); Apple Sports uses `Yesterday | Today |
  Upcoming`; Peanut ships a literal `LIVE NOW •` segment with a red dot;
  Fixtured shows a "Happening now" section that only exists when something is.
  A live TAB appears only where live is the whole product (Fanatics Live, Azar).
- **Shipped:** the tab is gone (six tabs → five) and Live is a **conditional**
  third segment on Picks — rendered only when the selected sport has an in-play
  pick standing, so with nothing live the control reads `Today | Signals`
  exactly as before and no empty slot is there to learn to ignore. The segment
  appearing IS the live indicator. Cross-sport liveness rides a red dot on the
  sport chips (`SportToggle` `liveSports`), which is strictly more than the tab
  ever said — the tab was always visible but never named the sport. Same 6pt
  `colors.avoid` dot as `GameStatusPill`'s LIVE pill, so there is one live mark.
- **Live picks are addable to the betslip now (matt's call, overriding the
  recommendation to keep it off).** The trap: `fetchPicksForDate` excludes
  `is_live` rows by construction, and `useResolvedSlip` PRUNES any key its board
  cannot resolve — so on the old board a live add would have landed, ticked the
  badge, and then silently deleted itself a second later. `useResolvedSlip` now
  resolves against pre-game **and** live picks, and holds pruning while either
  half is in flight or errored.
- **Bugs found and fixed on the way.** `useSportFilter.load()` validated the
  persisted sport against a hand-written list of six that omitted **NFL and
  NCAAF** — selecting either and cold-starting put you back on MLB, in the month
  those two are in season. Fixed in its own commit, validating against `SPORTS`
  so it cannot drift at the next sport. `useLivePicks` also froze the ET date in
  `useState`, so an app left warm across midnight polled yesterday's slate
  forever; it now recomputes per fetch and uses the shared `todayET()`.
- **Also:** the live poll is gated on the segment being open (30s there, 120s
  otherwise) — Picks is open for most of a session, so a flat 30s poll would
  have been a permanent cost to watch for something that happens ~19% of the
  time. The sport-change reset keeps the view when the new sport is also live
  instead of ejecting a user from a game. The DK-only / ~45s-stale caveat moved
  with the board and renders only on the live segment (it is wrong on the
  multi-book pre-game board). `SportToggle`'s badge literals became
  `font.size.nano` / `colors.textInverse`. `LiveScreen.tsx` and the already-dead
  `LiveGameBanner.tsx` are deleted; `RootStackParamList` lost its `Live` entry so
  a stale `navigate('Live')` is a type error rather than a silent no-op.
- **Deliberately NOT done:** live picks were not merged into the Today list
  sorted by edge (Apple Sports pins live above FINAL and it works there) — a
  locked pre-game pick and a churning in-play pick in one ranked list read as
  comparable bets of the same kind, which is a bad reading under CLAUDE.md §1c
  and fights `fetchPicksForDate`'s deliberate `is_live` exclusion. No push
  deep-linking was built: `usePushNotifications` registers a token but installs
  no response listener and `NavigationContainer` has no `linking` config, so a
  live push tap still lands wherever the user left the app. That is the
  investment that actually matches a ~45s-stale, time-critical pick, and it is
  now unblocked — `Picks: { view }` is the param it needs.
- **The UX review caught a crash the scan cannot see.** Wiring live picks into
  `useResolvedSlip` put `useFocusEffect` on `BetslipBar`'s path — and BetslipBar
  is mounted in `App.tsx` as a SIBLING of `<NavigationContainer>`, so
  `useNavigation()` throws there. Every user with something in their betslip
  would have crashed on render, on every screen. Confirmed by reading the
  installed `@react-navigation/core` source, not inferred. Fixed by splitting
  `useLivePicks` (focus-aware, for screens) from `useLivePicksUnfocused` (plain
  effect, for app-root consumers) over a shared core — the rule `useTodayPicks`
  has always followed. **`ux_scan` has no cross-file reachability**, so this is
  now a `.claude/rules/frontend.md` rule plus a test that walks the import graph
  symbol by symbol from BetslipBar; reintroducing the bug turns it red.
- **The other review findings, all addressed:** the segmented control got the
  same horizontal `ScrollView` SportToggle uses (three labelled segments plus a
  dot overflow at roughly the first accessibility text size on a 375pt screen,
  and at plain xxLarge on a 320pt one); the `route.params.view` deep link moved
  out of a `useState` initializer into an effect that clears the param (Picks is
  the initial tab and never unmounts, so the initializer could never fire for a
  tap made later — the Settings row promised a navigation it never performed);
  `SignalLockCard` counts live picks on the live segment instead of signals;
  `useLivePicks` sets `loading` on refresh so pull-to-refresh actually spins, and
  routes errors through `errorText()` rather than putting raw Postgres on the
  home screen; the error banner shows the live error on the live segment;
  `available` became the union with `liveSports`, so a sport whose only rows are
  in-play no longer renders muted AND live-dotted; the auto-switch off an emptied
  live board now says so with a toast instead of swapping the list in silence.
- **The live caveat was rewritten for contrast and height.** It was `colors.med`
  on `colors.bg` — 1.97:1, under the 4.5:1 floor, and `theme.ts` had already
  struck that amber from the grade ramp for exactly this. Inherited verbatim from
  LiveScreen, but this change promoted it from a tab nobody opened onto the home
  screen. It is now one line (`DraftKings only · prices up to ~45s old`) in
  `textSecondary` with the amber carried by the icon, and the full §6 disclosure
  hangs off an InfoTooltip — which also returns ~70pt above a board that had
  gained a filter bar it never had as a tab.
- **A live betslip leg says it is live.** `ParlayLeg.isLive` (the required field
  made the compiler find all three other leg constructors), an `IN PLAY` tag on
  the leg card, and the ~45s caveat repeated above the "Open with" row — the slip
  is the screen the bet is placed from, and the caveat was only on the board it
  was picked off. The §6 hand-off was already correct by construction:
  `fetchLivePicks` returns `bookRows: []`, so a live leg can only ever price at
  DraftKings.
- **The live indicator now clears when a game ends.** `fetchLivePicks` tests
  "in progress" as `commence_time <= now AND home_score IS NULL`, and
  `games.home_score` stays NULL until next-morning settlement — so a finished
  game kept the board (and, now, the segment and the chip dot) alive until ~6am.
  Filtered client-side on `gameStatus()`, which reads the live-state snapshot.
  **This is a patch, not the fix:** the state poller is MLB-only, so other sports
  fall through to the blind-window branch. The durable fix is server-side and is
  an open question for Matt.
- **The one shared thing:** three files were declaring the same 6pt red dot by
  value. `components/LiveDot.tsx` now owns it, imported by GameStatusPill,
  SportToggle and the segmented control.
- **Verified:** tsc error set **byte-identical to `origin/master`** (26, all
  pre-existing in `src/lib/queries.ts`, 0 in touched files), diffed against a
  `git worktree` at `origin/master`, not a stash. pytest **2592 passed / 16
  skipped** vs master's **2588 / 16** — the +4 is this branch's own new tests.
  `ux_scan --changed` reports two FEWER findings than master and no new ones.
  Both new test files were **watched failing first**: `test_mobile_sport_filter`
  3 of 4 red on the old hook naming NFL and NCAAF, `test_mobile_live_segment` 14
  of 15 red on master.
**Session summary (2026-09-07, session 244 — six wind picks stop being official, without being deleted):**

- mike: *"I deleted older wind picks from the discord and they should not be
  stored as official picks."*
- **First, what the state actually was.** #517 merged 2026-09-06 17:21Z and is
  live: the monitor has written **71 rows** since (36 wind, 35 opener) against
  ZERO for its whole prior life, so both of yesterday's bug fixes are confirmed
  working in production rather than just in tests.
- **There were SIX picks, not five.** `TB @ CIN Under 51` locked at 16:00 on
  09-06 — after the analysis predicting a sixth and 81 minutes before the fix
  merged. The prediction and its confirmation nearly touch.
- **All six had lost their premise.** Four had the wind forecast collapse
  (14.0→7.1, 12→6.5, 11→2.9, 11→3.8 mph against an 11 mph bar), and two were the
  retractable-roof games that were never eligible. That is the 56.6% persistence
  number playing out worse than measured, on the live slate.
- **VOIDED, not deleted — and §1c gained the rule.** §1c protects a bet against
  LINE MOVEMENT; it does not protect a row a model emitted while firing outside
  its validated window. But deleting one throws away the evidence that it
  happened, which is how the bug was found. So `result='NO_ACTION'` (the repo's
  existing void, already excluded from net/ROI/record app-wide),
  `condition_status='VOID'`, reason in `condition_note`; `created_at`, line and
  price untouched, and the insert-once lock intact so nothing silently re-fires.
- **`scripts/void_picks.py`** — reusable, dry-run by default, `--apply` to
  write. **Refuses a pick already graded WIN/LOSS/PUSH**: voiding one of those
  is rewriting a settled result, not correcting a bug, and that is the line §1c
  will not let this cross. Refuses to match every pick without a filter, and
  refuses an empty reason (a void with no reason is indistinguishable from data
  loss later). 12 tests.
- **Verified after applying.** `v_public_track_record` shows `nfl_wind_totals`
  at **0 picks / 0 wins / 0 losses / 0 profit_flat**, and every settlement query
  in `paper_tracker` is bounded on `result IS NULL`, so the games playing on
  09-13 cannot re-grade them back in. Selection was bounded by
  `--before 2026-09-06T17:21:24Z`, the merge timestamp of the gate — a
  principled cutoff rather than a hand-listed set of ids, though the dry run was
  read line by line before applying.
- **The card stays running** (mike's call). Gate, roof fix and monitor are all
  live, so a pick firing inside 4 days on a known-open roof is a legitimately
  different pick from these six.
- **One test of mine was wrong and the code was right** — it asserted
  `profit_flat`/`profit_kelly` were bound params when they are SQL literals,
  matching `paper_tracker`'s own NO_ACTION path. Fixed the test.
- **Noted, not done.** CLAUDE.md is now **38 KB** against the ~30 KB ceiling the
  file sets for itself.

**Session summary (2026-09-06, session 243 — the wind model was firing at 8 days out, and the gate that used to stop it was a side effect):**

- mike: *"NFL wind model is firing too many picks. Nearly half the game are
  unders... should we wait for closer to game so the weather can be more dialed
  in but also don't want to lose edge."* Branch `nfl-wind-fire-window`.
- **The count was right and the framing had a false tradeoff in it.** 5 of 11
  outdoor Week 1 games qualified (45%) against a validated ~38 bets/season, about
  19% of outdoor games. But waiting does not cost edge: the module docstring
  calls the rule "a standing closing-line inefficiency, not a race... can be
  placed at any time up to kickoff", and `CALIBRATED_UNDER_RATE` RISES as the
  lead falls — 0.5489 at 7 days, 0.5671 at 3, 0.5735 at 1. Waiting is worth
  ~+2pp. The only thing firing early bought was the lock.
- **Every live pick was outside the calibration, and the published probability
  said so if you read it.** Leads at lock: 7.2 / 7.5 / 8.0 / 8.2 / 8.7 days.
  `model_probability` on all five: **0.5489 — the (7, 11) row exactly**, i.e.
  the clip value, so not one of the five carried a measured number.
- **Nothing had gated firing since 2026-09-05.** `MAX_CALIBRATED_LEAD` bounds
  the probability table; the stake used to return 0 past it and 2026-09-05 changed it to
  clip (Matt: "we should return stakes 7 days for signal picks"). That was right
  on its own terms — a probability and a stake describing different bets is a
  bug — but **the zero stake had been the de-facto firing gate**, and
  `run_nfl_poll` hands the card `--days 10`. `scheduler.py` claimed in its own
  docstring that firing "stays inside each model's VALIDATED window"; that
  became false the moment the clip landed, and nothing tested it.
- **The fix is `MAX_FIRE_LEAD = 4.0`, in the model rather than in a caller's
  argument** — every caller gets it, and the card can keep being run at
  `--days 10` so the eval board still sees the whole slate. mike chose 4 over 7:
  7 is only the boundary of the arithmetic, 4 is the runbook's Thursday scan and
  the closest honest match to where the day-3 headline validation lives.
  `Updated-By: mike`. Applied to `select_bets` AND `evaluate_board` — those two
  have come apart over a lead rule once already, and the comment in
  `evaluate_board` records it.
- **The five existing picks stay** (§1c). They were picks; the window tightening
  over them does not retract them.
- **Two bugs found while measuring, both recorded in `docs/followups.md`, neither
  fixed here.**
  - `nfl_pick_status_history` holds **0 rows for every model** and
    `condition_status` is NULL on all five picks. The monitor runs clean and
    logs "0 locked pick(s) observed" hourly: `pick_eval.py` writes the bare
    nflverse `game_id` and calls it "the join key to picks", but the publisher
    writes `NFL_{nflverse_id}`, so the lookup misses every time and exits 0.
    Load-bearing — the lock is justified by a history table that has never been
    written to.
  - **43 of the 2026 schedule rows have a BLANK roof and are scored as
    open-air.** They are exactly five retractable venues (`ATL97`, `DAL00`,
    `HOU00`, `IND00`, `PHO00`); prior seasons carry `open`/`closed` per game
    because the game has been played. `INDOOR_ROOFS = {"dome","closed"}`, so a
    blank is outdoor. **Two of the five live picks are on that basis** — BUF @
    HOU (NRG) and BAL @ IND (Lucas Oil), both of which usually close the roof.
    It also inflates the fire rate directly: the outdoor denominator should be
    9, not 11.
- **Ran the card to check, and it answered mike's question directly.**
  `--dry-run --days 10`, 0 credits: 5 games over the wind bar, all at leads
  7.2-7.5, all correctly held as "watching, not betting" — including **TB @ CIN
  at 11.1 mph, which the old code would have locked today as a sixth pick.**
  And **`CLE @ JAX` locked on 09-05 at a 14.0 mph forecast now reads 4.3 mph** —
  the lowest on the board, still seven days out. The pick stands (§1c) and the
  premise for it is gone, and nothing says so anywhere, because of the monitor
  join bug above. That is the clearest argument for the gate that exists: a
  forecast at 8 days is not a forecast, and the lock made it permanent.
- **The eval board's waiting branch is checked AFTER pricing** so a row that is
  merely waiting keeps its line, price and edge. Gating before pricing would
  blank exactly the window a locked pick spends between `MAX_FIRE_LEAD` and its
  own lock, which is the window someone would want to inspect.
- **One caller outside the live path had to opt out.**
  `replay_wind_card.py --lead 5/6/7` builds a frame at that lead and is how
  leads 5-7 in `CALIBRATED_UNDER_RATE` were checked; inheriting a DEPLOYMENT
  policy would have made it print "no qualifying bets", indistinguishable from
  a calm week. It now passes `max_fire_lead=--lead`, and a static tripwire
  fails any future `nfl/scripts/` caller that forgets — nothing under there is
  otherwise exercised by the suite.
- **A test in the existing suite could never have failed.**
  `test_the_eval_board_agrees_with_the_card` asserted
  `bool(rows[0]['qualifies'])`, and `qualifies` is the string `"1"`/`"0"` —
  `bool("0")` is True. Found by writing a case that should have failed it.
  Fixed to `int()` first.
- **Verified.** New `tests/test_nfl_wind_fire_window.py` (11 tests) run against
  the pre-fix model first: **8 failed**, including all five real lock leads;
  all pass after. Two tests in `test_wind_stake_beyond_calibration.py` failed on
  the change and were retargeted rather than deleted — Matt's clipping property
  is unchanged and still pinned, they now pass `max_fire_lead` explicitly to
  isolate the stake question from the firing question, and a new test asserts
  the default gate. Full suite **2604 passed, 16 skipped**.
- **Then: "Do it all." Both followups fixed, and the persistence question
  measured.**
- **The monitor join.** `pick_eval.eval_row` now normalises to the
  `NFL_`-prefixed id. Fixed at that layer on purpose — wind and opener both feed
  the one helper, so fixing it in either model would have left the other broken.
  Tests run against the unfixed code first: 2 of 4 fail, and the one that
  matters asserts the `latest_per_pick` LOOKUP resolves rather than asserting a
  string format.
- **The roof.** `weather.open_air_mask` + `RETRACTABLE_STADIUMS`. The venue set
  is DERIVED, not remembered: a stadium is retractable iff its history contains
  an `open` or `closed` row, and two tests re-derive it from `games.csv` so the
  constant cannot drift — one checks the set matches, the other checks every
  blank-roof row is one of them (the premise of the whole fix). A blank roof at
  a NON-retractable venue is deliberately unaffected.
  **Visible end to end:** the Week 1 board went from 11 "outdoor" to **9
  open-air**, dropping exactly BUF @ HOU (NRG) and BAL @ IND (Lucas Oil), and
  the waiting list from 5 games to 3.
- **THE PERSISTENCE MEASUREMENT — `nfl/scripts/forecast_persistence.py`, free,
  0 credits.** Full write-up in `docs/nfl_wind_lead_evidence.md`.
  **Only 56.6% of games flagged at lead 7 are still flagged at lead 3** (47/83).
  A long-lead card is not finding more edge, it is betting a set that is ~43%
  transient forecast noise and locking it insert-once. Lead 7 is the longest
  lead any source we have can reach, and skill only degrades, so the 7.2-8.7d
  picks were worse than this. That is the volume answer mike asked for.
- **The settlement columns do NOT support the gate and must not be quoted as if
  they did.** DROPPED hit 57.1% under (n=35) and SURVIVORS 39.1% (n=46) — the
  opposite of the gate's rationale. It is noise: CI [40.7, 73.5], and the time
  split destroys it (2024 DROPPED 38.5% n=13, 2025 68.2% n=22, opposite signs on
  adjacent seasons). Recorded as a non-finding rather than dropped, because the
  next person to look will find the same cell and needs to know it was seen.
- **A finding that is bigger than this session and is Matt's/mike's call.** On
  the only two seasons measurable this way, the wind flag shows **essentially no
  edge over the base rate**: flagged at lead 3 is 47.5% under (n=101) against a
  46.0% base rate (n=378), versus a claimed 56.71% and a 52.38% breakeven.
  **Consistent with what the model already says, but NOT the same measurement.**
  `wind_totals.py`'s LIVE RISK block says 2024 and 2025 both lost on OBSERVED
  wind — ERA5 / the nflverse column, the rule's ceiling. This is the lead-3
  ISSUED-FORECAST flag, which is what production bets, so it is the first
  measurement of the DEPLOYED population for those seasons rather than a
  restatement. Read beside it: the 2024-25 outdoor base rate is 46.0% against
  the 51.74% the model shrinks toward, so the flag added +1.5pp over a base that
  had itself moved. 2 seasons cannot overturn a 10-season validation. **Nothing
  was changed on it.** What it does is put a number on how long "variance or the
  market has learned" can keep being the answer.
- **Deliberately not done.** `DEPLOY_THRESHOLD` stays at 11.0 mph though the
  published validation is at 12 — a second, independent lever, and
  `BUCKET_DIAGNOSTIC` puts the strongest bucket at 11-13, so it wants its own
  measurement rather than being bundled in here. The forecast-persistence
  question mike's framing implies — how often a game flagged ≥11 mph at 8 days
  is still flagged at 3 — is measurable from the 298,944 forecast/ERA5 pairs
  and was NOT measured; `nfl/scripts/validate_wind_forecast.py` rebuilds the
  cache in ~30 min and that is the honest way to answer it.

**Session summary (2026-09-06, session 242 — the go-live gate stops being quoted at models it does not apply to):**

- Matt, on a screenshot of the NCAAF Models tab: *"Remove the paper trading for
  live win probability. we are not doing paper trading."* The Live Win
  Probability card read **"1 of 50 settled · too few to judge"**. PR (draft) off
  `claude/remove-paper-trading-win-probability-q14qig`.
- **The word "paper" was never on the screen — the NUMBER was doing the work.**
  `verify_live_record_start.ts` has guarded the literal word in `screens/` and
  `components/` for days and passed the whole time. But `50` is
  `GO_LIVE_SETTLED_PICKS`, and quoting a model's distance from the go-live gate
  says the same thing the banned word says: *not backed yet*. A copy guard that
  greps for a word cannot see a rule being cited at something it does not
  govern.
- **The gate is a PRE-GAME rule and the app already said so — on two screens out
  of four.** CLAUDE.md §2 makes the ≥50-settled gate per-model, and
  `ExplainerScreen` and `OnboardingModal` both already carry the exception
  ("in-play models are the exception, and are unproven"). `ModelsScreen` and
  `TrackRecordScreen` did not, so the two surfaces that render a NUMBER
  contradicted the two that render the PROSE. The Live Win Probability row was
  being held to a bar the rest of the app says it is not held to.
- **What changed.** `isLiveModel()` (already in `lib/thresholds.ts`, already the
  repo's definition of an in-play model, mirroring the `model_id LIKE '%_live_%'`
  predicate in `v_public_track_record`) now branches the thin-sample sub-line on
  both surfaces. Pre-game rows are untouched: "1 of 50 settled · too few to
  judge". In-play rows drop the gate and keep the caution: "1 settled · too few
  to judge" on the Models tab, "· too few to judge" on the Record tab. The
  accessibility label branches with the visible text. The Record tab's intro
  paragraph picks up the in-play exception the Explainer and onboarding already
  carried.
- **The small-sample caution was deliberately NOT removed.** It is a different
  claim from the gate: `MIN_PICKS_FOR_COLOURED_ROI` (10) exists because
  `ncaaf_live_win_prob` is 0-1 and renders **-100%**, and the honest thing to say
  about one settled bet is that it is one settled bet. Removing the gate is
  Matt's instruction; removing the arithmetic would be a different change, and
  the greying rule is untouched. Flagged to him as reversible.
- **Both surfaces, not one, per CLAUDE.md §1b.** The mechanical test — *if this
  had been a problem on the Record tab, would we have noticed?* — answers "only
  once someone screenshotted it", which is exactly the case the rule is for.
  `TrackRecordScreen` was quoting the same gate in the same shape three screens
  away.
- **A guard caught its own bypass.** The first draft of the code comment
  explaining the change used the phrase "paper trading", and
  `verify_live_record_start.ts` failed on `ModelsScreen.tsx` — its regex scans
  whole files, comments included. Reworded, not exempted.
- **The UX review found the change had CREATED a divergence, and it was
  right.** Branching the caption inline on two screens left one state rendering
  four sentences — two wordings, two different counts (`stats.picks` vs
  `wins + losses`), and a pre-game branch on the Record tab that never said "too
  few to judge" at all. Pulled into `thinSampleCaption(modelId, settled)` in
  `lib/recordStart`, the shape `RETIRED_RULE_CAPTION` already uses in
  `lib/modelMeta` for the same failure. Both callers now pass settled PICKS —
  the unit CLAUDE.md §2 states the gate in — so the number and the word agree.
- **The in-play caption lost its count too.** "1 settled" was the third
  appearance of that number on the row; the line above already reads
  "1 pick · 0-1". The count earns its place only in the pre-game branch, where
  "of 50" makes it a progress bar. Live rows read **"Too few to judge yet"**.
  Rendering nothing was rejected: the greyed ROI would then be the only signal,
  and grey does not explain itself.
- **A contrast bug on the line that exists to prevent a misreading.** `subtle`
  and `modelSub` are `textTertiary` on `bgCard` — ~3.4:1, below AA, named
  explicitly in UX_REVIEW §5. Both moved to `textSecondary`; each is used only
  by the row in question, so the fix is confined.
- **Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` error set **byte-identical to origin/master**
  (60 errors, 0 in the touched files), diffed against a
  `git worktree --detach origin/master` checkout rather than a stash;
  `scripts/verify_live_record_start.ts` **ALL PASS** (18 checks);
  `python -m pytest -q tests/` **2588 passed, 16 skipped**;
  `node mobile/scripts/ux_scan.mts --changed` **0 blockers**, 2 pre-existing
  should-fix findings on lines this diff does not touch
  (`ModelsScreen.tsx:660` font-size literal, `TrackRecordScreen.tsx:174`
  unlabelled Pressable). `frontend-ux-designer` reviewed before the PR opened,
  per `.claude/rules/frontend.md`.
- **Not done, deliberately:** no change to `GO_LIVE_SETTLED_PICKS` or to the
  gate itself — it is a real business rule (CLAUDE.md §2) and still governs
  pre-game models; no change to the Explainer or onboarding, which were already
  correct; nothing server-side (this is display only — no threshold, no
  `Updated-By:` trailer, mobile UI is explicitly not a model update).

**Session summary (2026-09-06, session 241 — the parlay save → edit → save round trip, and the exit that scrolled away):**

- Matt, on the saved-parlay flow: *"I just tried to save a parlay and it was
  very jankey. I saved it, then went to save and could only get with DK. Then I
  tried to edit and it duplicated the records. And then I was stuck and couldn't
  get back to the main app."* Three symptoms, three separate defects, all in
  `mobile/src`. PR #509 (draft).
- **The duplicate.** `SavedParlaysScreen.editInBuilder` seeded the builder and
  nothing else, and `useSavedParlays` had no `update` — its only verb was
  INSERT. So saving an edit filed a SECOND parlay beside the original, and so
  did tapping "Save parlay" twice. The saved parlay's id now travels with the
  restore payload (and is set by the builder's own first save), so a later save
  writes over that record — same id, same `createdAt`, same list position, with
  `updatedAt` marking the edit.
- **The binding has to outlive the SCREEN, which is the part that was missed
  first time.** The UX review caught it: the Betslip is POPPED mid-edit on the
  commonest gesture there is — "Find players to add" navigates to the tabs
  (which pops it) and adding a leg pushes a FRESH Betslip. Held in component
  state the binding died on that trip and the save inserted a duplicate again,
  on the path where adding a leg is half of what "edit" means. It lives in a
  module store now (`setEditingParlayId` / `useEditingParlayId`), dropped when
  the slip empties or is cleared, so a slip sharing nothing with the parlay it
  came from can never overwrite it.
- **"Could only get with DK" is the same round trip, one step later.** A leg
  rebuilt by `savedLegToParlayLeg` came back `pick: null` with an empty
  `bookPrices`, so `toSavedParlay` read it as a HAND-ENTERED CUSTOM leg and
  dropped the game id, every per-book betslip link and the DK link. The
  re-saved parlay could then only hand off to DraftKings, with every leg marked
  "no link — add manually". A restored leg now carries its snapshot
  (`ParlayLeg.saved`) and re-saves as itself; `legPriceAtBook` reads coverage
  off that snapshot instead of crediting every book with a leg it has no link
  for. **The DK-only decision path is untouched (§6)** — this is the "Open
  with" row and the hand-off links, which are display and hand-off.
- **The exit scrolled away.** The Betslip renders its own header
  (`headerShown: false`, so no stack back button) and the close chevron sat at
  the TOP OF THE SCROLLVIEW — and "Save parlay" is at the bottom, so reaching
  it scrolls the only way out off screen. The title row is pinned now; the
  subtitle is descriptive rather than a control, so it scrolls, and the
  separator appears on scroll like every native stack header in the app.
- **Also fixed:** `updateSavedParlay` recomputed `sport` from the legs that
  re-resolved, relabelling an aged-off NFL save as "MLB" (a save's sport is
  part of its identity — preserved with the id and the created time); the
  dropped-legs warning was `colors.med` at 12pt on white (2.20:1, half the AA
  floor) with colour its only signal, now the house warn banner; an update that
  drops legs confirms first, because there is no undo behind it; `undoDelete`
  called `restore()` inside a `setState` updater.
- **Verified:** mobile `tsc` error set byte-identical to `origin/master` (26,
  all pre-existing in `src/lib/queries.ts`), 0 in touched files, compared
  against a detached `origin/master` worktree; `pytest -q tests/` 2587 passed,
  16 skipped; `ux_scan --changed` 0 blockers / 17 should-fix against master's 3
  and 18 on the same six files. NOT verified in the app — no simulator here, so
  the four flows worth clicking through are named in the PR.
- **Left for Matt:** whether the first save should still bind the slip (it
  kills "save twice = two copies" but removed the only fork gesture — a "Save
  as new" secondary is the alternative); which book he is set to, which decides
  whether the FIRST save's DK-only hand-off was a bug at all; and whether the
  Stats "Building your betslip" banner should auto-clear after one round trip
  rather than waiting to be dismissed.

**Session summary (2026-09-05, session 240 — "when there is a bet from the model, post the pick": publishing moves to whoever WRITES the pick):**

- mike, angry and right: *"None of the UFC picks are getting pushed to the
  Discord, there is only a 31 Aug."* Measured before answering — `push_sent`
  holds ONE `discord_signal` row for a UFC lock_key, sent 2026-08-31
  (`ryan-spann/mario-pinto:ufc_total_rounds`). MLB has ~150 over the same
  window. A previous session had reported UFC as publishing on the strength of
  that single row; as a general claim it was wrong.
- **Four causes, one per card, only the last of which is a live bug.** Before
  2026-08-23 Discord signal posting did not exist (`c1ec48eb`, #221), so the
  08-01/08-08/08-15/08-22 cards had no route. The 08-29 card's six signals all
  carry the `:early` lock_key suffix and are excluded permanently and
  deliberately. The 08-31 post is the look-ahead branch added the day before,
  working. **The 09-05 card is the bug**: two BETs crossed 3 and 13 minutes
  before their own fights and neither was postable.

  | pick | written | fight starts | first pass after | posted |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `oumar-sy/bukauskas` ML -140 | 18:17:44Z | 18:20:00Z | 18:22Z | no |
  | `mario-pinto/spann` ML -195 | 18:27:16Z | 18:40:00Z | 19:17Z | no |

- **The cause is WHO PUBLISHES, not Discord, thresholds or guards.** Locking
  and delivery ran only at the end of `scripts/refresh_pass.sh`. Every other
  writer wrote picks and waited for it — including the pre-game line poller,
  which re-scores every 30 seconds and said so in its own docstring: *"It does
  not settle, notify, or health-check."* MLB never showed the gap because first
  pitches are hours apart; a UFC card starts a fight every 20-30 minutes.
- **Shipped (#505, `Updated-By: mike`): `tracking/signal_publisher.py`** —
  `publish_new_signals()` runs capture -> push -> Discord, the same three
  functions the pass calls, in the same order, under the same `push_sent`
  ledger and the same guards. Publishing sooner cannot publish anything MORE
  than the pass would have. Per-surface try blocks (a broken webhook must not
  suppress the push) and it never raises, because its callers are loops.
  `pregame_line_poller.poll_once()` calls it when the scorer reports a new BET
  — gated on that, so the ~95% of quiet ticks stay free. `scheduler.run_nfl_poll`
  and `run_nfl_prop_card` call it too (§1b: assess the change against every
  model — both write picks outside the pass, and inside the NFL fast window
  that lag can outlast the kickoff).
- **Deliberately not done:** the daily free pick, the restatement, the X post
  and track-a-bet line alerts stay pass-only — a 30-second loop must not be
  able to fire them. No scoring, pricing or §1c behaviour changed.
- **Verified:** `tests/test_signal_publish_on_write.py`, 12 tests, and the
  three carrying the fix were watched failing first (no publish / wrong date /
  the stale docstring). Full suite 2585 passed; the two failures
  (`test_claude_md_size`, `test_refresh_pass_parallel`) are byte-identical to
  `origin/master` and in untouched files.
- **Flagged, not chased:** the swapped-orientation UFC `games` rows carry
  DIFFERENT commence times (`mario-pinto_ryan-spann` 18:40Z vs
  `ryan-spann_mario-pinto` 19:00Z), so the started-game guard's answer depends
  on which orientation a pick landed on. Known since session 209.

**Session summary (2026-09-05, session 238 — "if I have the app for that Sportsbook, it should just open my app": the build now asks iOS whether each book's app is installed, and the App Store is only for members who don't have it):**

- Matt, with DraftKings' App Store page on screen (its one button reading
  "Open"): *"Everything is looking great. But now when I click on DK it takes
  me to the App Store. This is good if I don't have the app. If I have the
  app for that Sportsbook, it should just open my app."* Branch
  `claude/dk-app-deep-linking-tctv79`.
- **Which tap, measured.** Of the 19 upcoming BET picks at the time, 16 carry
  a DraftKings betslip link (15 MLB, 1 NCAAF) and open the app through it;
  the 3 without one are all NFL card picks. With no link, session 226's chain
  went link → `dksb://` → **App Store** → site, and the scheme step could
  never succeed: iOS answers `canOpenURL` only for schemes declared in the
  build's `LSApplicationQueriesSchemes`, and the build declared none — so
  `canOpenURL('dksb://')` was `false` on every phone, installed or not, and
  the store was the first thing that opened. Session 226 stated that gap and
  chose the store as the better blind default; this session removes the
  blindness rather than picking the other default.
- **The change.** `app.json` declares `dksb` in
  `ios.infoPlist.LSApplicationQueriesSchemes` and moves `version` 1.0.0 →
  1.1.0. New `isBookAppInstalled(book)` in `lib/sportsbookLinks.ts`: `true` /
  `false` from `canOpenURL` on iOS for a book with a verified scheme, `null`
  otherwise (no scheme, Android, or an error) — **an unknown is not a no.**
  `openBookBetslip` asks first: installed → betslip link → app by scheme →
  site, never the store; not installed → the App Store whatever link we hold
  (Matt, 2026-09-04) → site; unknown → link → store → site, exactly session
  226's chain minus the scheme step that could not be queried. New
  `hooks/useBookAppInstalled.ts` for surfaces; the parlay hand-off sheet
  hides "Get {book} on the App Store" only on a definite yes.
- **Why the version moved (a native change, not an OTA).** An Info.plist key
  ships only in a new binary. The OTA guard refuses this push on its own
  (`mobile/app.json` changed); a bundle carrying this JS onto a 1.0.0 binary
  would read `canOpenURL` as "not installed" and send a member WITH the app
  to the store even from a pick with a link — a regression on the one path
  that worked. `runtimeVersion.policy = appVersion`, so 1.1.0 keeps that
  bundle off 1.0.0 binaries by construction (TESTFLIGHT.md's own convention).
  The cost, stated: 1.0.0 installs stay on the last 1.0.0 bundle until they
  take the new build. The build itself is Matt's: **Actions → Mobile
  TestFlight build → Run workflow** on master after merge, one iOS build
  from the EAS allowance.
- **Every book, not just DraftKings** (Matt, on seeing the first cut: *"This
  should be the same for all the Sportsbook apps"*). Only DraftKings has a
  scheme the repo trusts (`dksb://`, session 47); ten web searches for
  FanDuel's, BetMGM's, Caesars', Fanatics', BetRivers', Hard Rock's, Bally's,
  betPARX's and Rebet's found none named anywhere public, and the IPA
  analysers that would show an Info.plist are off the sandbox's allowlist.
  So each of the nine carries **`candidateSchemes`** — one to three plausible
  spellings naming the SPORTSBOOK product, 21 in all, declared in the plist
  beside `dksb` (22 of Apple's 50) — under a trust rule that makes a wrong
  guess free: iOS answers `true`
  only for a scheme some installed app registered, so a `true` from ANY
  candidate proves the app is there; a `false` proves nothing (the guess may
  be wrong) and leaves the answer unknown, which is the pre-detection chain.
  A candidate is asked about and **never opened** — `fanduel://` might be
  FanDuel's fantasy app — so an installed book with no link opens by its
  site, a universal link iOS routes to the app. The device pass (below)
  is what turns a candidate into a verified scheme: a book that opens its
  app from a no-link tap has one that is right. **No bare brand name is a
  candidate** — `fanduel://` is as likely FanDuel's fantasy app, `betmgm://`
  its casino, `caesars://` its rewards app, and a yes from a sibling would
  hide the store from the member it was meant for (UX re-review; the first
  cut had four such and they were dropped). The queries run in parallel:
  they sit on the tap-to-open path. Android has no `<queries>`
  declaration, so it stays unknown there — and its store page is
  DraftKings' only, so its chain is unchanged.
- **Verified.** `verify_book_links.ts` grew the detection checks — every
  bettable book asks (a verified scheme or candidates), every scheme asked
  about is declared in `app.json` and nothing is declared that no book asks
  about, the cap of 50, a `true` from any scheme is proof and a `false` only
  from the verified one, a candidate is never opened, the version is off
  1.0.0, the question is asked before anything opens, each of the three
  routes never reaches the surface it must not (installed never the store,
  not-installed never the app), the hand-off's store row hides only on
  `true` — and **the new checks were watched FAIL against `origin/master`'s
  source** before passing here. tsc error set byte-identical to master (32 = 32, all in the documented
  `queries.ts` / `scripts/` baselines). `verify_discord_link`, `verify_line_legs`,
  `verify_preferred_book` all pass; `verify_stats_pill` fails one assertion
  ("with no licensed glyph it renders NOTHING") **identically on master** —
  pre-existing, not touched. ux_scan on the touched files: two `should`
  a11y findings on the hand-off sheet's two Pressables, both present on
  master at the same Pressables.
- **UX review (frontend-ux-designer), two passes: no blockers.** First
  pass, three Should-fixes and one Consider, all taken — the hand-off sheet
  dismisses on backdrop tap (the X alone sits in the corner a thumb cannot
  reach; mirrors `HitModeSheet`), "Open in {book}" and "Add {leg} to slip"
  get VoiceOver roles and labels, and `useBookAppInstalled` re-asks on
  AppState `active` so the store row clears once the member installs and
  comes back. Declined: relabelling the line chips "Get {book}" when not
  installed (widens into `BookLinesRow` / `BetslipBooksRow`; Matt's call).
  Second pass on the every-book delta: the bare-brand candidates above
  (taken) and `Promise.all` over the queries (taken); the claim that a
  book's web root is a universal link is unverified per book — either
  outcome is the app or its site, never the store, so flagged only.
- **NOT done, stated:** the device pass. The sandbox cannot open an app, so
  "a DraftKings pick with no link opens the DraftKings app", "with the app
  deleted the same tap opens the App Store", and — for each other book he
  has installed — "a chip with no link opens the book, not the store" are on
  Matt to tap through on the 1.1.0 build. A book that still reaches the
  store with its app installed has no right candidate yet; say which.

**Session summary (2026-09-05, session 239 — calibration comes out of the app UI entirely, and the opening-signal experiment link with it):**

- Started as the Record tab only; Matt widened it twice. First *"Remove
  calibration and remove experiment section"* against a screenshot of the
  Record tab, then *"Make sure it's removed from the records tab"*, then
  **"Calibration should be removed everywhere in the UI."** The final scope is
  every user-visible calibration surface in the mobile app.
- **What came out, by surface.** Model detail: the "Calibration — stated odds
  vs reality" chart and the "Cal error" StatTile on the Model card. The pick
  reasoning card: the "Calibrated" row that displayed `model_probability_cal`,
  and the "XGBoost + Platt calibration" wording on Model probability. The
  Explainer: the Platt-scaling paragraph, rewritten to say what the number
  MEANS without naming the method, and the "Why we're different — calibration,
  not hype" section, rewritten as "Why we're different — the record, not a hero
  number" on the same argument (we publish the whole record, including the
  models that aren't working). Onboarding slide 1: now "Graded in public, not
  hype". The Record tab and the onboarding body both lose the "and calibration
  error under 5%" clause from the go-live gate sentence. Settings: the live
  betting sub no longer says "still calibrating".
- **Three modules deleted** once nothing rendered them:
  `src/components/CalibrationChart.tsx`, `src/lib/calibration.ts`,
  `mobile/scripts/verify_calibration.ts`.
- **This is a DISPLAY change, not a model change** — no `Updated-By:` trailer
  (CLAUDE.md §1b: mobile UI does not need one). Models are still Platt-scaled,
  the ≤5% go-live gate still holds server-side, and
  `tracking/calibration_watch.py`'s weekly sweep still posts. Only what users
  see changed.
- **Verified by grep:** zero user-visible calibration text left in `mobile/src`.
  The eight surviving hits are internal — code comments in `types/index.ts` and
  `lib/thresholds.ts`, the `calibration_score` type field, and one column in the
  model-registry `select`. `model_probability_cal` is still fetched and typed;
  nothing renders it now.
- **The UX review caught a regression this change introduced, now fixed.**
  Rendering `EquityCurve` unconditionally (the previous round's fix) meant its
  "Not enough settled days yet to chart." card showed during the FIRST LOAD and
  on error — a false statement about data still in flight, sitting directly
  under "Couldn't load the record". It is now held back while
  `loading && rows.length === 0`, and unconditional after that.
- **Two more real defects from the review, fixed.** Removing the "Cal error"
  StatTile left "Holdout acc" alone in its `statRow`; `StatTile` is `flex: 1`,
  so it stretched edge to edge over an 80pt card, with "Holdout ROI" stacked
  below it in the same shape — the one block breaking the 2-up grid every other
  section on that screen uses. The two are now one paired row, with the ROI tile
  rendering an em-dash when `holdout_roi` is null so the pair never degrades
  back to a stretched single (this also fixes the pre-existing prop-model case).
  And the rewritten Explainer claimed "closing-line value on every settled
  pick" — CLV is NOT on every pick, as the same app says two screens away
  (`${clv.count} picks with CLV`); it now reads "where we have it".
- **The go-live gate got its carve-out.** With the calibration clause gone, the
  Explainer and onboarding were the app's only statement of the gate and read as
  an absolute promise — which `nfl_live_prop`, LIVE at zero settled picks with
  the gate deliberately not met (CLAUDE.md §2), contradicts. Both now name
  in-play models as the exception. The wording deliberately does NOT claim the
  Live tab labels them unproven, because it does not: grepped `LiveScreen.tsx`
  for "unproven|beta|experimental" and got nothing.
- **The gate number stopped being hard-coded.** Three rewritten sentences said
  "50" while `GO_LIVE_SETTLED_PICKS` sat in `lib/recordStart.ts` — and
  `TrackRecordScreen` was importing that constant already, stating the same
  number two ways 88 lines apart. All three now interpolate it.
- **App Store copy corrected** (`mobile/APP_STORE_METADATA.md`): it advertised
  "calibration error" in the Promotional Text, "calibration metrics" in the
  Performance-tab bullet, and "Calibration error is shown live in the
  Performance tab so you can audit the model honestly" — a feature that was
  never in the Performance tab and now exists nowhere. The listing is the first
  UI a user reads, and advertising a deleted feature is an App Store review
  risk. New promo text is 165 of the 170-char limit (asserted in the edit).
- **Considers taken:** the Explainer's vague "rescales its scores" became "fits
  a correction on held-out folds and applies it to every score it publishes";
  the onboarding body stopped repeating its own title; the heading became "the
  whole record, not one number" so "hero number" is defined where it is used.
- **Declined:** the two `SettingsScreen` VoiceOver blockers at :423/:434
  (icon-only Pressables, real, but pre-existing and on lines this diff does not
  touch — they want their own change); re-adding a distinguishing claim to "Why
  we're different", which is now a restatement of the track-record promise
  rather than a differentiator (a product call, raised with Matt).
- **`ux_scan.mts` after the fixes: 17 findings, byte-identical to the reviewer's
  measured set and 0 new vs master's 18** — the one that disappeared is the
  deleted experiment `Pressable`.
- **Left alone deliberately, raised with Matt:** `dashboard/app.py`'s "Model
  Calibration Plot" (his own internal analysis tool) and the weekly
  "📐 Calibration watch" Discord embed, which posts to `DISCORD_WEBHOOK_OPS`,
  the internal ops channel, not a member-facing one. Also `model_probability_cal`
  and `calibration_score`, still selected in `queries.ts` and typed but rendered
  nowhere — harmless payload, and real data worth keeping fetchable.
