**Session summary (2026-09-05, session 235 — "we really need a permanent fix": the latest-line views become state tables the writer maintains):**

- Branch `claude/stats-page-lines-issue-7ubsii`. Matt's screenshot, the Stats
  board at 4pm ET on a college-football Saturday: *"Couldn't load today's lines
  — canceling statement due to statement timeout (57014)"*, and *"we really
  need to fix this issue and have a permanent fix."* Fourth session on the
  same toast (221, 222, 223 before it).
- **Measured before touching anything (§1b), as `authenticated`, 157 games and
  401,873 prop rows for the day.** The Stats read — `v_latest_prop_odds_all_books`,
  today, `batter_hits` + alternate, the app's exact ORDER BY / LIMIT — ran
  **10,512 ms alone**, no contention needed: session 222's skip scan walks every
  (market, player, book) key of every game before the market filter applies —
  34,124 skip-scan steps with 34,078 heap fetches (the newest pages are never in
  the visibility map), 4× the day it was measured on. `v_latest_dk_odds`, which
  the Picks screen and the UFC / NFL / NCAAF look-ahead cards read and which no
  session had rewritten, ran **17,945 ms**: a sequential scan of 1,089,880
  DraftKings rows and a 105 MB disk sort to return 280. `v_live_game_state_latest`
  walked 212,088 rows to return 2 (2,001 ms). The postgres log: **282 timeouts
  in 24 h, 189 of them v_latest_dk_odds, 35 the prop view, 20 the live view**;
  the edge log put them on three app builds (12001 / 11901 / 12101). The old
  poller seed from session 222 was gone (`pg_stat_activity` clean); the day was
  simply bigger. Session 222 had named the structural fix for exactly this case.
- **Fix — `data/migrations/latest_line_state_tables.sql`.** Three current-state
  tables, one row per current line, kept by triggers on the log tables:
  `latest_odds` (game, market, book), `latest_prop_odds` (game, market, player,
  book, line — one row for a standard market, every line of the newest pass for
  an `*_alternate` market, as `alternate_prop_lines_view` defined it),
  `latest_live_game_state` (game). Insert triggers are STATEMENT-level over a
  transition table (one DISTINCT ON per batch, so an executemany row costs one
  upsert and a bulk load costs one pass); update triggers are ROW-level behind a
  WHEN clause on the key / type / value columns (Postgres refuses a transition
  table on a column-listed trigger — the local Postgres caught that on the first
  run), so relabelling a row `in_play` recomputes its key from the log and a
  bulk update of `source` fires nothing. DELETEs are not trapped: the pruner
  keeps every key's newest pre-game row by construction, and a transition table
  on a million-row prune is a tuplestore it cannot afford; `latest_lines_rebuild_*`
  reconciles a range if that invariant is ever broken. The four views are now
  plain joins with identical columns, order and types; `v_latest_dk_odds` gains
  the pre-game filter the other three always had (it used to publish an in-play
  price as "the latest DK line" after first pitch — §6).
- **Applied to production in the safe order, all through the MCP:** tables +
  triggers at 20:30:16 UTC (`execute_sql`), THEN the backfill in 60-second
  chunks — props by `game_date` (10 calls, 381,820 rows), odds by `snapshot_at`
  (10 calls, 350,881 rows), live state (1 call, 472) — THEN a diff of the new
  tables against the OLD views for today inside one statement each: prop
  (10,058 rows), game-line (3,441) and live (472) outputs identical both ways;
  the DK view differed on 25 rows, all 25 verified in_play rows of started
  games, the deliberate change. THEN the view swap (`apply_migration`
  `latest_line_state_tables`, the views section; the history row says where
  the rest was applied). The old views served throughout.
- **Measured after, same role, same slate:** Stats read **136 ms** (was
  10,512); `v_latest_dk_odds` today **2.8 ms** (was 17,945); game lines today
  **10.9 ms**; live state **0.8 ms** (was 2,001); the Picks screen's every-market
  prop read **205 ms**. The writer's cost, an explained single-row insert inside
  a rolled-back transaction, cold: **6.8 ms on odds, 14.9 ms on props**,
  foreign-key check included (the `zz_probe_book` rows were rolled back; 0
  remain). State tables: 85 MB + 157 MB. **Zero statement timeouts in the
  postgres log from the swap onward**; `get_advisors(security)`: nothing new.
- **Tests.** `tests/test_latest_line_state.py` runs the migration on a REAL
  Postgres (`LATEST_LINE_TEST_DSN`; skips where there is none — the sandbox
  has /usr/lib/postgresql/16, so it ran here): newer replaces / older ignored,
  in-play never becomes the latest pre-game line, every book kept and the
  consensus hidden, a 60-row bulk insert keeps the newest, relabel-to-in_play
  falls back to the previous pre-game row and the reverse repair restores it,
  the date filter is answered from `games`, a standard market keeps one row
  when the line moves, an alternate market keeps every line of the newest pass
  only and a late row cannot add a stale line, books and players are
  independent keys, the live tie is broken by state_id across statements, the
  rebuild reproduces the trigger state and never regresses it, the API roles
  can read and never write, the migration is idempotent. **All 16 watched
  failing under eight mutations of the migration** (each guard removed in
  turn); two mutations passed on the first attempt and exposed test gaps (a
  tie inserted in one statement, no assertion that the DK view is DK only),
  both closed. `tests/test_latest_odds_views.py` repointed at the new file:
  columns pinned to the app's four column lists, every view reads its state
  table and never the log, the exclusions survived, every log table has its
  trigger, the API roles are read-only, and no `ACTIVE_MIGRATIONS` file
  re-creates these views over the log. `data/anon_readable.VIEW_BASE_TABLES`
  declares the three tables (read only through security_invoker views, so the
  caller needs SELECT while no `.from()` names them), `apply_anon_grants`
  grants them, `lock_down_sql` refuses them; pinned in `tests/test_anon_readable.py`.
  Full suite: **2,591 passed** (2,590 + the one that failed for a missing
  import, fixed).
- **Deliberately not done.** No app change: the views kept their contract, so
  the Stats / Picks / Teams reads and `fetchAllPages` are untouched (no UX
  review needed). The `useTodayPicks` eight-screens-no-cache issue from session
  223 is unchanged and matters far less at 3 ms a read. `v_latest_inplay_odds_all_books`
  (DISTINCT ON over in-play rows, read by the live pick cards) was not
  converted: it is not in the timeout population and in-play rows are a tenth
  of the log; the same shape applies if it ever appears there. The skip-scan
  migration file stays as history; `idx_prop_odds_line_snap` stays (the
  recompute path and the pick-detail lookup use it). Promoted to
  `.claude/rules/data-integrity.md`: *"the newest row per key" over an
  append-only log is a state table the writer maintains, never a view the
  reader computes* — evidence in `docs/rules_evidence.md`.

**Session summary (2026-09-05, session 234 — the At-Most side is missing from the FEED, and the parser is exonerated):**

- Merged #493 at Matt's word, then: *"follow up with the at least / most etc
  discovery and implementation."*
- **The question the lock was never asked.** #493 greys the At Most control out
  when none of the member's books prices that side. It was built on counts of
  what OUR TABLE holds — which answers "we have no Under", not "there is no
  Under". Bally Bet showed 1,614 standard `batter_hits` rows and zero Unders;
  BetRivers 6,637 rows and 58. A book pricing strikeouts with no Under is not
  credible, so the live hypothesis was that `prop_odds_ingestor` dropped their
  Under outcomes.
- **Measured off the raw feed, before our parser: it does not.** Bally Bet and
  BetRivers really are over-only on the standard MLB markets they serve;
  FanDuel, Fanatics and Hard Rock are over-only on stolen bases; DraftKings,
  BetMGM, betPARX, Fanatics, Hard Rock, ReBet and Caesars return both sides on
  everything they serve. Our stored rows matched the feed exactly. **The parser
  is exonerated and the lock is correct** — for those pairs the At-Most side
  does not exist to be shown, and no amount of extra requesting produces it.
  Full table and the reasoning in `docs/rules_evidence.md`.
- **A wrong "obvious" fix, killed by measuring it.** The tempting cleanup was to
  teach `markets.oneWayMarket` that `batter_home_runs` is one-way, since it
  reads 0 Unders at DraftKings and FanDuel. Across all history that market
  carries **55,263 rows WITH an Under** of 580,838 — ESPN BET 97.3%, Pinnacle
  100%, betPARX 95.8%. betPARX is bettable, so shipping that would have told a
  betPARX member nobody offers a side they can actually bet. `oneWayMarket`
  stays as it is; per-book coverage is what handles the rest.
- **Implementation: the side question folded into the EXISTING probe**, not a
  second one. `scripts/probe_market_coverage.py` (from #492) already walks
  every outcome; it now also records `sides` — per (market, book) Over/Under
  counts — and `one_sided`, the pairs with one side and not the other. So the
  answer is re-derivable as the declared job that already exists, rather than
  the one-off script this session started with. §1b: prefer extending the
  shared thing.
- **"One-sided" is `bool(over) != bool(under)`, never a ratio.** A book that
  posted 36 Overs and 1 Under still offers the side; a ratio test would grey
  out a control the member could have used. Pinned by its own test.
- **Converged with #499 independently.** My coverage analysis surfaced
  `batter_doubles` (4 bettable books), `batter_triples` (2) and
  `batter_stolen_bases_alternate` (3) as served-but-never-requested, and
  `pitcher_hits_allowed_alternate` / `pitcher_walks_alternate` as requested
  but never served. A parallel session had already shipped all of it in #499
  while this ran; nothing was re-done. I had also overstated the second
  finding as "no book serves them" before checking the probe's own
  `empty_but_supported` key — it distinguishes "the API does not know this
  key" from "no book priced it", and these were the latter.
- **Verified.** `tests/test_market_coverage_probe.py` +3 (21), each WATCHED
  FAILING: reading the side from only the `name` field, and degrading the
  one-sided flag to `under < over`, each turn the suite red; reverting turns it
  green. Full suite **2,571 passed**.
- **Ops note.** The probe ran on Railway's `prop-probe` service (§1b route 2),
  which builds from `master` and skips deploys unless a watched file changes —
  so it was temporarily pointed at this branch with a matching watch pattern,
  and **restored afterwards to branch `master`, start command
  `bash nfl/scripts/live_prop_job.sh run`, watch pattern
  `nfl/scripts/DISABLED_never_matches`**, verified by re-reading the config.
  22 credits.

**Session summary (2026-09-05, session 237 — Doubles and Triples were never blank because the market didn't exist; nobody had asked):**

- Follow-through on Matt's *"check all the sportsbooks we get betting lines for
  and see if they publish stats under different keys and sync everything up
  with the stats tab."* Session 233 answered the BOOK half (Caesars and FanDuel
  serve the milestone/alternate key, not the standard one). This entry is the
  MARKET half: which keys does the feed serve that we have never asked for.
- **Ran `scripts/probe_market_coverage.py` against a live event, one market per
  call, 30 MLB keys — 44 credits.** Stored data cannot answer this: an empty
  table means "no book prices it" and "we never asked" identically. The probe
  separates them, because an unsupported key 422s, a supported-but-unpriced key
  returns an empty market, and a served key returns books.
- **Result, and it is our gap, not the source's:**
  - `batter_doubles` and `batter_triples` are **SERVED** — and the Stats board
    has carried Doubles and Triples columns since it shipped, both permanently
    blank. Added to `PROP_MARKETS_BATTER`.
  - `batter_stolen_bases_alternate` is served; added to `PROP_ALT_MARKETS["MLB"]`.
  - `batter_at_bats`, `pitcher_home_runs_allowed` and `pitcher_pitches` are
    **keys the API does not know.** Those three columns are correctly blank
    forever; no amount of syncing helps.
  - `pitcher_hits_allowed_alternate` and `pitcher_walks_alternate` came back
    "supported, nobody priced it" — matching zero rows across a full day of
    live passes. **Removed**, at ~2.5 credits per event call each, every pass.
    `pitcher_earned_runs_alternate` and `pitcher_outs_alternate` are the same
    and were never added; a test now pins all four out.
  - `batter_strikeouts` is supported but was empty on the probed event. NOT
    added — one event is not a population. Needs a wider probe.
- **The app-side cause of the blanks was structural, not a missing key.** A
  stat reaches its prop MARKET *through its model id*
  (`STAT_KEY_TO_MODEL` -> `PROP_MARKET_BY_MODEL`), so a stat nobody models has
  always rendered a dash however much data existed. Football hit this same wall
  earlier today and got `FOOTBALL_STAT_TO_MARKET`; this adds the general
  one-stat-at-a-time version, `STAT_KEY_TO_MARKET`, as a FALLBACK after the
  model route. `propModelForStat` still returns null for Doubles and Triples,
  so no "Add to play" affordance appears and no model is implied.
- **MLB board: 12 priced columns of 18 -> 14.** The two new ones render the
  line column, the odds pill, the Live/Final labels and the off-line caption,
  and tap through to AddLineSheet like any other Stats leg. They inherit
  session 233's At Least / Over / Under control and its book-availability
  greying unchanged.
- **New invariant test, the one football already carried:**
  `test_the_mlb_board_and_the_mlb_pull_are_one_set` walks BOTH routes from the
  board to a market key and asserts set equality against
  `config.PROP_MARKETS_ALL` in both directions — a pulled market nothing can
  display is credits spent every pass, and a column with no market is a
  permanent dash. It is the drift that is being pinned, not the current values.
- **The UX review found three things and all three shipped**, because each was
  a claim on screen that the data does not support:
  - **The hit-rate colour ramp is absolute** (good 60%, mid 40%, else bad) and
    **saturates on a rare-event column**: at "1+ Doubles" every hitter in the
    league is 10-30%, so the whole column renders the bad end of the ramp
    beside a +450 price that may well be the better side of that number — and
    one tap to "No Doubles" turns it into a wall of green beside heavy chalk.
    Before this change the column had no price beside it and the red was merely
    uninformative; with a price it contradicts the number next to it.
    **`hitRateColorDiscriminates` now switches the ramp off when every row on
    screen sits in one band**, and the percentage and x/N sub-label carry it —
    both always printed, so colour is never the only carrier. **Orthogonal to
    session 234's repaint of the same function** onto the accessible
    `colors.grade*` tokens, which landed on master while this was in flight and
    is composed with it here: WHICH colours, and WHETHER to colour, are
    different questions, and a ramp that says the same thing about every row
    says nothing about any of them at any contrast level.
  - **A slip of nothing but Stats line legs labelled the odds-implied number
    "Model"** and told the reader it "only clears because the model's combined
    probability beats the price". Doubles and Triples have no model at all, so
    this change is what makes a zero-model slip reachable. The tile now reads
    **"Implied"** and the hold note says these are the reader's own lines at
    the books' own prices. Pre-existing since line legs shipped; the leg card
    one level down already got it right ("implied 18%").
  - **`STAT_KEY_TO_MARKET` is keyed `SPORT:key`, and the guard is the point.**
    `SeasonTotalsRow` keys are shared across sports, so a bare map would let
    the next entry (`steals`, `threes`, `blocks`) resolve a basketball column
    onto a baseball market with nothing failing. Football gets an early return
    instead, because there neither league has a prop model at all.
- **Raised by the review and NOT shipped, each a product call rather than a
  correctness one** — all four are in the reply to Matt: the ruler ceiling
  (`maxLineN` gives Doubles a 1-10 ruler when one line exists, so nine
  positions are dead — same today for Home Runs and Stolen Bases); batter
  "Strikeouts" sharing a label with pitcher "Strikeouts" while one is blank and
  the other full; the default L10 window topping a rare-event board with 3/10
  noise; and `PlayerStatsScreen` having no add-to-betslip path for a line leg.
- **Verified:** 2,551 tests pass. All four new assertions watched failing
  against deliberate mutations, the four new ones included. `tsc` error set
  byte-identical to `origin/master` (60 = 60), 0 in touched files. `ux_scan`
  finding set identical to master across all four touched files (20 = 20, 0
  new). `npx tsx mobile/scripts/verify_market_coverage.ts` — 21 checks, ALL
  PASS, and watched failing (4 of them) with the sport guard removed.
  `frontend-ux-designer` review run before the PR per the standing rule.
- **CLAUDE.md was 84 bytes under its 36 KB gate** and the new §9 row tipped it
  over. Two rows in that table pointed at the same file
  (`docs/probability_calibration.md`); they are now one row, which is where the
  bytes came from. Worth knowing: the gate is close, and the next session that
  adds a line will hit it.
- **The NCAAF half, left open at first, was ANSWERED the same session** by the
  1pm ET pass (17:35 UTC) rather than pruned off the two-event probe:
  - The pass asked **all 68 events** (`68/68 events in scope`, nothing dropped)
    and got props from 31: **9,187 rows, 615 players, 7 books, 590 credits,
    219.6s.** The ~590 projection from the three-event probe was exactly right
    — the second data point that projection needed.
  - `player_rush_attempts` and `player_sacks` returned **zero rows**, and
    **the chunking is what separates "empty" from "never asked"**: markets go
    out five per call and a 422 kills its whole chunk, but three of the four
    dead keys rode in chunks whose OTHER members came back full. So the chunk
    succeeded and the market was empty. `player_sacks` was **alone in its
    chunk**, which is genuinely ambiguous from row data — the probe's
    one-market-per-call result resolves it. **A market alone in a chunk cannot
    be diagnosed from stored rows.**
  - **The prune had to be per league, and the naive version would have shipped
    a regression.** The two football leagues share one stat catalog AND one
    market map (`FOOTBALL_STAT_TO_MARKET`, bare-keyed, reached by an early
    return because neither league has a prop model). The NFL prices both keys
    — 3,206 and 3,718 stored rows — so pruning them from the shared map blanks
    two WORKING pro columns to fix two dead college ones. A new
    `FOOTBALL_MARKET_NOT_PRICED` carries the per-league exclusion, and a test
    was watched failing against exactly that naive prune. This is the same
    drift the `SPORT:key` guard was added for two commits earlier, showing up
    in the other map.
  - **A claim of mine was wrong and is corrected in the config comment and
    `docs/market_coverage.md`: this is NOT a credit saving.** The Odds API
    bills per market RETURNED and these returned nothing, so they were already
    close to free (590 credits over ~340 calls, with 185 of those against the
    37 events that returned no props at all). The saving is **~68 fewer calls
    per pass and ~20% off the runtime**. The reason to drop them is that a
    request nobody answers is a lie in the config about what the sport offers.
  - **Found in passing, not fixed:** neither prop ingestor writes to
    `api_call_log` — both use bare `requests.get` with only
    `record_quota_headers` — so **prop credit spend is invisible in that table
    for every sport**. The 590 figure came from the worker log. Recorded in
    `docs/market_coverage.md`; worth fixing when the ingestors are next
    touched.
- **The college prune's UX review found a false sentence the prune itself
  created**, and two more things worth having:
  - **"No sportsbook posts Sacks lines." became a claim the app contradicts
    two taps away.** It was true while a column unpriced in one sport was
    unpriced in all of them; the NFL prices sacks and carries and college does
    not, so a flat claim makes the APP look wrong rather than the market look
    thin. The note now names the league, for every sport — it costs the other
    eight nothing and is true for all of them.
  - **College Defense went 1-of-6 priced to 0-of-6** — sacks was its only
    priced column — so walking those six chips produced six identical
    sentences, which is how a reader learns to stop reading them. When the
    whole active group is unpriced the note is said once, about the group.
  - **`FOOTBALL_MARKET_NOT_PRICED` gained a counter-clause and real types.**
    The constant is named for a MEASUREMENT ("we asked, no book answered") but
    its mechanism is "drop the LINE column", and the two come apart the first
    time someone stops pulling a market they could have had — an entry added
    for that reason would make the board's caption a lie about the books, with
    nothing failing. Typed `Partial<Record<Extract<Sport,'NFL'|'NCAAF'>,
    ReadonlyArray<keyof SeasonTotalsRow>>>`, which makes both typos this map
    invites — `NCAA` for `NCAAF`, `sacks` for `def_sacks` (the LABEL is
    "Sacks") — compile errors instead of silent no-ops. Both watched failing.
  - The direction pill's VoiceOver label said **"Show bets that are"** and now
    reads "Threshold direction": it is the one control that outlives the price
    on an unpriced column, so it was the last thing on that board still
    promising a bet.
- **Two `verify_*` scripts broke on the copy change and were rewritten to
  assert MEANING, not literal source** — the third time today. `verify_hit_rate`
  pinned the pill's exact label string; it now asserts the pop-up shape
  (label + value + hint) and that the label does not say "bet".
  `verify_stats_odds` pinned the exact unscoped sentence; it now asserts the
  sentence names the league AND that the unscoped form is gone. Both watched
  failing. **A guard written against a string is a guard against editing.**
- **Logged to `docs/followups.md`:** these pruned lists are measurements with
  **no drift detection** — if college books start pricing carries mid-season,
  we have stopped asking, so no rows appear, so nobody looks, and the column
  stays dark until a person re-runs the probe. Filed with the `api_call_log`
  gap, since both are about a measurement that lives nowhere queryable.
- **Deliberately not done:** WNBA's Steals/Blocks gap (board has the columns,
  `PROP_MARKETS_WNBA` lacks the markets, NBA pulls both) could not be probed:
  no WNBA games on the slate today.

**Session summary (2026-09-05, session 236 — the Movement tab and the per-card Context button come off the Picks surface):**

- Matt: *"Remove both the movement tab under picks and remove the context
  button on each bet card."* Two removals, no replacement asked for. PR #498.
- **Movement tab.** `PicksHomeScreen`'s segmented control goes from
  `Today | Signals | Movement` to `Today | Signals`. Removed the `movement`
  view, its subtitle branch (`N toward · N against`), its empty state, and the
  paragraph of the header tooltip that described it. `lineMovementBoard.ts`
  keeps `signalCountsBySport` (the sport toggle's badge, unrelated to the
  board) and loses `movedSignals` / `movementTally` / `MovedSignal` with the
  board they fed.
- **Context button.** Removed from `PickCard` along with `PickContextSheet`,
  which nothing else referenced — the file is deleted. **No context was
  lost:** `PropContextCard`, `TrendStrip` and `TaleOfTheTapeCard` all still
  render on `PickDetailScreen` under the same conditions the sheet used, and
  both `PickCard` call sites (`PicksHomeScreen`, `LiveScreen`) navigate there
  on tap. The sheet was a shortcut, not a home.
- **Per-pick line movement is untouched**, and that was the read of the ask:
  the *tab* and the *button*, not the feature. `LineMovementCard` and
  `PickTimingCard` still render on PickDetail and `movementFromLatest` still
  draws the movement chip beside the locked number on the card, so §1c's
  "timing is data" surface is intact.
- **The UX review found the real cost of the second removal, which was
  accessibility, not information.** The "Context" pill was the one labelled,
  VoiceOver-visible thing on the card saying it led somewhere — and a settled
  pick renders no action buttons at all, so with the pill gone VoiceOver read
  the whole card as inert text. The card's Pressable now carries a role, a
  spoken label and a hint. Same class of finding on the segmented control:
  with two options instead of three, its selected state is the entire
  wayfinding of the screen, and it was announcing as static text.
- Two 44pt-floor violations were carried, not introduced, and were fixed
  because this diff now owns those controls: the sub-tabs were ~25pt tall
  (`spacing.xs` → `spacing.sm`, ~32pt, plus hitSlop) and the compact
  Add-to-betslip / Track pills ~34pt (vertical hitSlop 6 → 11). Side slop on
  the pills deliberately stays at 6 — they sit ~8pt apart and wider side slop
  would let Track swallow taps aimed at its neighbour.
- **Declined, in the PR body:** the `#FFF4E5` and `fontSize: 10` literals the
  scan still reports in the two touched files (pre-existing, identical on
  master — tokenising them is a theme change); a full-width two-half
  segmented control; and a chevron disclosure on the card, which would put an
  affordance back in the row Matt just asked to clear. The last is a decision
  for him, not cleanup.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` error set byte-identical to a
  `git worktree --detach origin/master` baseline (26 pre-existing TS2352s, all
  in `src/lib/queries.ts`), **0 in the touched files**.
  `ux_scan.mts --changed`: 0 blockers, 4 should-fix, all four the pre-existing
  token literals. Master's baseline over the same files carried **1 blocker**
  (the deleted sheet's unlabelled close X) and 2 a11y should-fix, so the change
  removes three a11y findings and adds none. `pytest` was not run: it is not
  installed in this sandbox and the diff touches no Python.
- OTA-eligible — no native module, no `package.json` / `app.json` change.
- **Left for Matt:** `PickDetailScreen`'s card ordering. The context cards the
  sheet used to show in one tap now sit below AllBooksCard, the betslip card,
  the track card, PublicBettingCard, ClvCard, injury and weather — reachable,
  but a tap and a scroll away. Reordering that screen is its own change and
  was not proposed unilaterally.

**Session summary (2026-09-05, session 235 — the wind bet that showed in the app and not in Discord, and the repair path that still read the abandoned table):**

- Matt: *"There was an NFL wind bet signal that showed in the app but not
  discord. Have that bet retroactively post to discord."*
- **It posted. Verified in the ledger, not inferred**: both Week 1
  `nfl_wind_totals` picks carry a `discord_signal` row at **12:22:29 ET with a
  message id**, and a `new_bet` push at 12:22:27 — so the app, push and Discord
  all carry them. `NFL_2026_01_CLE_JAX:nfl_wind_totals` (Under 40.5, Wind 14
  mph) and `NFL_2026_01_BUF_HOU:nfl_wind_totals` (Under 44.5, Wind 12 mph),
  both for the Sep 13 kickoff.
- **Nothing had to be repaired to make that happen, and finding that out was
  most of the session.** #489 (session 232) is the fix and it was already
  deployed. Running its selection SQL against production returned both picks as
  eligible: thresholds cleared (prob 0.5489 ≥ 0.52, edge 0.0534/0.0597 ≥ 0.03,
  −105 ≥ −200 floor), model unpaused, kickoff eight days out, no ledger row.
- **What was actually missing was anything that could RUN the producer.**
  `notify_discord_signals` is called from exactly one place — the refresh pass
  at :17 — and the 11:17 pass had been killed mid-run by the deploy chain
  (#491/#493/#494/#492 deployed between 15:20Z and 15:46Z; the scheduler logged
  its restart at **15:22:48Z**). The 12:17 pass ran clean and posted them.
- **The tell to remember**: `push_sent` last held a `discord_signal` at 09:22
  ET while three MLB picks written at 11:22 sat unposted beside the wind ones.
  A producer that is only ever invoked by one cron is indistinguishable from a
  broken producer whenever that cron misses.

**Two things #489 left behind, both fixed (PR #497).**

- **THE RESTATE PATH WAS STILL ON `opening_signals`.** #489 moved
  `_new_signals` onto `picks` and left `_locked_signals` and `_delete_posted`
  where they were — so the one path whose entire job is to REPAIR a channel
  still read the table whose gate caused the damage. Measured against
  production for `game_date = 2026-09-13`: the capture-table query returns
  **0 rows**, the picks query returns **both wind picks**. A restatement of
  that slate would have posted nothing and said nothing about it — as
  incomplete as the post it was correcting, in the same silent shape.
- **The delete half was worse.** Joined to the capture table it can only find
  CAPTURED message ids, so an uncaptured pick's stale post would have survived
  its own correction and left two numbers standing in the channel — the one
  outcome worse than either number alone.
- Both now read `picks`, through the same `model_action_thresholds` cut and the
  same synthesised `lock_key`, so all three producers select from one board.
- **No started-game guard on the restate path, deliberately**, and it is pinned
  by a test so it does not get "fixed" later. `_new_signals` refuses a started
  game because ANNOUNCING a bet nobody can still take sends a member to a live
  number. A restatement is not an announcement: it corrects something already
  published, for a date that is normally over. The guard would make restating a
  past slate a permanent no-op.
- **`publish_discord_signals` job type** so "post this now" has an answer that
  is not "wait for the next cron" (§1b: a handover is a last resort). It calls
  the ordinary producer by its ordinary name on the worker, which polls the
  queue every five minutes. Nothing is bypassed — the started-game guard, the
  thresholds cut and the ledger all live inside that function — so it posts what
  is unposted and no more, and a second run posts zero. It changes WHEN the
  producer runs, never what it selects, which is why it needs no date-list gate
  the way `notify_discord_restate` does: it cannot reach a pick already sent.

**Verified.** Five new notifier tests (both producers select `FROM picks p` and
neither names the capture table; the restate producer applies the app's action
filter clause for clause; first BET only per §1c; no started-game guard; the
delete half resolves through `picks`) — **four watched failing against
`origin/master`**, the fifth pins a property the change must not break. Four
job tests: registration, the optional-date validator (`2026-9-5`, `tomorrow`,
`20260905` rejected), that it calls `notify_discord_signals` and passes nothing
widening, and that it never reaches the restate producer or the ledger —
asserted off the **code object**, because the docstring explains why it is not a
restatement and would satisfy a grep. Both new SQL statements executed against
production before shipping. Full suite **2,541 passed**.

**Deliberately not done.** No route was built to force the post inside this
session: `prop-probe` has no current build snapshot (every deployment since
its watch-pattern guard went in is SKIPPED with `snapshotId: null`), and the
MCP cannot repoint a service's source branch, so the only way to run new code
on the worker is to merge it. That is the gap `publish_discord_signals` closes
for next time — it does not close it for this time. The three MLB picks the
killed pass had missed went out on the same 12:22 pass and were left to the
ordinary producer rather than special-cased.

**Session summary (2026-09-05, session 234 — the game time went under the name, and SPOT became a graded matchup difficulty):**

Two asks from Matt, one after the other, and the second reversed part of the
first day's work. PR #495.

- **"Add the time of the game and who they are playing under name … for all
  sports."** Every Players row and every Teams row now carries
  `9:40 PM ET · @ SEA` under the name. Sourced from `games`, NOT the MLB/WNBA
  matchup views — "all sports" is the whole ask and the football boards have no
  matchup feed at all, so a matchup-view subline would have shipped to two
  sports and skipped six. `statsBoard.buildSlateGameIndex` keys both sides of
  every game on the slate date, which also covers UFC (its `games` row stores
  fighter names where a team abbrev would be), and resolves a doubleheader to
  the game a bettor can still act on.
- **This reverses 2026-09-04's "nothing under the player name"**, which is the
  entire reason the SPOT column existed. Flagged to Matt rather than silently
  applied.
- **"Update spot column to just be difficulty of that match up and have a
  bigger scale besides low med and high."** SPOT → GRADE: one letter, A+ to F.

**The measurement that mattered.** Before replacing the three tiers I checked
what they actually classified in production, and two of the three were badly
mis-centred:

| metric | shipped cliffs | what they classified |
|---|---|---|
| opposing starter ERA | >=4.60 / <=3.40 | 32% / 38% / 31% — fine |
| opposing lineup wOBA | <=.305 / >=.330 | 10% / **81%** / 10% |
| opposing lineup K% | >=.235 / <=.190 | 23% / **74%** / 3% |
| WNBA opp def rating | >=104 / <=98.5 | **77%** / 18% / 6% |

A WNBA row was called a favourable spot three times in four. The cliffs assumed
a ~101 league-average defensive rating; the measured 2026 figure is **106.5**.
The grade is now a percentile against measured anchors (median + IQR/1.349,
through a normal CDF), so it re-centres itself instead of drifting from a
remembered number. All thirteen grades populate, none above 10% — A+ 8.3, C
10.0, F 3.2 over 2,617 probable-starter days; WNBA runs A+ (COOP 115.8) to F
(GSV 97.6). An ungraded matchup is a dash, never a C.

**The fact behind the grade moved to the player detail screen** — Matt's own
alternative home for it on 2026-09-04. Without that the ERA would have left the
product for every sighted user.

**Two UX designer passes, and the second caught a defect I had just shipped.**
I tuned all five grade colours to ~5:1 contrast, which made them ISO-LUMINANT
(measured relative luminance 0.1596 A / 0.1603 B / 0.1570 C / 0.1528 D — B was
fractionally *lighter* than A). The ramp encoded category but not rank: a reader
could see two grades differed but not which was better, and deuteranopia
collapsed the green and the olive together. Replaced with three steps whose
lightness falls monotonically, all still AA. **Colour that encodes a rank has to
vary in lightness, not only in hue** — and the check that pins it computes the
luminances rather than trusting the comment above them.

Real bugs the reviews found and this fixed: UFC sublines printed `@ <fighter>`,
giving the two fighters on one card opposite prefixes for the same bout;
UFC/NHL rows never got Live/Final because the status was keyed on `row.team`,
which they do not have; "Live" printed twice per row; both boards froze their
clock at mount; the subline was `textTertiary` at 11pt (3.4:1, below the AA
floor); neither Stats list answered the pull gesture. Also: `hitRateColor` was
still painting the board's PRIMARY number at 2.22:1 / 2.20:1 / 3.55:1 — all
below AA — so both traffic lights are now on the one accessible ramp.

**Verified.** +48 checks across `verify_stats_board.ts` and
`verify_stats_pill.ts`; the grade cuts, monotonicity, reachability, the CDF,
`gradeSpoken` and the ramp's luminance ordering are executed, not grepped.
Twenty deliberate mutations each watched failing on the intended check —
**and two checks that survived their mutation were rewritten rather than
kept**, which is the rule working: a date-filter check whose fixture the bug
happened to satisfy, and a ramp check whose mutation was still monotone. `tsc`
error set byte-identical to `origin/master`. `ux_scan` on the changed set 22 →
6, all 6 pre-existing, and two pre-existing a11y BLOCKERS cleared.

**Left to Matt, in the PR body:** thirteen grade steps is finer than the error
bar on a season ERA (one band ~0.28 ERA against a ~0.5-0.65 sampling error — the
reviewer recommends 5); the per-row weekday; and the time-before-opponent order.
Also flagged: the GRADE column reaches two sports of eight, and a failed
`fetchTonightMatchups` still makes a broken view look identical to an off-day.

**Session summary (2026-09-05, session 233 — "nothing shows for Cesar's and FanDuel": we have their lines, and the side we do not have is the one that was on screen):**

- Matt, on the Stats page: *"only show the sportsbooks for the betting lines we
  are pulling. Nothing shows for Cesar's and FanDuel. I think we should have
  those lines … anything else we truly don't have we shouldn't show that
  option."*
- **Measured before proposing anything, and the premise turned out to be half
  right.** `v_latest_prop_odds_all_books` for 2026-09-05: FanDuel and Caesars
  are on all 15 games. Both post Hits, RBIs, Runs, Total Bases and HR — but
  through the MILESTONE market only (`batter_hits_alternate`: FanDuel 550 rows,
  Caesars 737; neither posts standard `batter_hits` at all, and
  `player_prop_odds` shows FanDuel never has). **The milestone market is
  OVER-ONLY.** Counting the twelve MLB stats the board offers, on the day:
  FanDuel priced the At-Least side of 8 and the At-Most side of **2**; Caesars
  8 and **3**; DraftKings 12 and 11.
- So the blank column was the At-Most view, not a missing book. Confirmed by
  running the real `buildQuoteIndex` over the day's real Betts rows: at
  At-Least 0.5, FanDuel quotes −260 and Caesars −280; at At-Most, both produce
  nothing and `bookPostsMarket` suppresses the column.
- **Nothing was removed from the picker, and that was Matt's call**: *"if we
  are getting betting lines for a Sportsbook we should show it as an option and
  display those lines. It doesn't matter if we don't have as many props as
  DK."* Checked against the data — every one of the ten bettable books has MLB
  prop rows today and game lines all-time, so there was no book in the list we
  do not pull.
- **Shipped instead:** the Over/Under control greys out (chevron dropped,
  reason under the ruler naming the book) when none of the member's books
  prices that side of the current stat, and the board snaps to the side they
  do have rather than stranding them — Matt's choice over letting the column
  answer with dashes. The picker rows gained an explanatory sub-line
  ("Hits: At Least only", "No Hits lines today"). It never disables or removes
  a row.
- **A slate-wide coverage read is not affordable and that shaped the design.**
  `explain analyze` on the odds view grouped by book: **17.5s** for one day;
  8.5s straight off `player_prop_odds` (172,462 rows for 2026-09-05). So
  coverage is computed from the rows the board has already loaded for the stat
  in view — per-stat, and free. A book with no rows for that stat is absent
  from the map, never asserted as uncovered app-wide.
- **The control FAILS OPEN.** While the lines load, when the read fails, when
  the slate has no unstarted game, and on a stat no book prices at all, both
  sides stay live: the hit-rate board is real research without any line, and
  must not lose a control to a slow query.
- **Three bugs in this session's own new code, caught on an adversarial re-read
  before the PR, each now pinned by a test:**
  1. The greyed pill's caption used `booksNoneName`, the subject of a NEGATIVE
     sentence — at two books it would have read "Neither DraftKings nor FanDuel
     post only At Least lines", the opposite of the truth. Now `booksName`.
  2. The picker's note was computed over the member's SELECTED books while the
     picker lists all ten, so a DraftKings-only member would have read "No Hits
     lines today" under FanDuel and Caesars — manufacturing the exact false
     impression this change exists to remove. Two maps now: the selected set
     drives the direction lock, every listed book drives the note.
  3. A feed row carrying a line but no price on either side seated a book in
     the coverage map with both sides false, and presence in that map is read
     everywhere as "we have something here". Such rows are now skipped.
- **The UX designer review (required, `.claude/rules/frontend.md`) returned one
  blocker and six should-fixes.** The blocker and its first should-fix were the
  two bugs already caught above — independently found, which is the review
  working. The other five are fixed in this branch:
  - The direction snap was silent and threw the choice away. It now REMEMBERS
    the side the member asked for, restores it the moment their books price it
    again, and announces the substitution once per (stat, book set).
  - The locked pill kept its fill, border and radius under `opacity: 0.6` —
    iOS for "a button you cannot use right now", so it invites another tap, and
    it put the text at ~3.5:1 on a chip. The chip is now dropped entirely
    (transparent fill and border, padding kept so the ruler does not shift) and
    the text sits at full `textSecondary`.
  - VoiceOver read the lock sentence twice (pill label and caption) and
    announced "dimmed" on an element declared as static text. The pill now says
    only its side and carries no `disabled` / `accessibilityState`.
  - Two coverage captions with two treatments, only one offering a way out.
    The lock reason now joins the EXISTING `noLinesRow` note — same info icon,
    same "Change your sportsbooks ›" tail, which is the one action that lifts
    the lock — so book-coverage copy lives in one place on this screen.
  - The snap also ran in Totals mode, where neither pill nor caption renders;
    it is now scoped to hit-rate mode. And the live pill gained `hitSlop`
    (36pt target, under the 44pt floor).
  - Picker rows now carry a sub-line in EVERY case ("Both sides for Hits
    today"), not only the thin ones — a slot used only for bad news makes a
    covered book look unchecked.
  - **Declined:** the segmented At Least / At Most control. It is a better
    shape and a bigger change than this fix warrants; Matt's call, noted in
    the PR rather than smuggled in here.
- **Verified.** `verify_stats_odds.ts` +32 checks, built on the real rows above
  and WATCHED FAILING: sabotaging `anyBookPostsSide` to be side-blind and
  letting an over price stand in for an under both turn the suite red (2
  failures), and reverting turns it green. `python -m pytest -q tests/` 2,490
  passed. `tsc` error set byte-identical to `origin/master`, 0 in touched
  files. `ux_scan --changed` 10 findings vs master's 11 — the direction pill's
  missing accessibility label was one of master's, and this change supplies it.
- **Not done, deliberately:** no coverage table or materialized view was built
  for the slate-wide answer. It is the only way to say "this book has nothing
  today" across every stat, and it is a worker job plus a migration; nothing
  in Matt's ask needed it, since he chose to keep every book listed regardless.
  Left in `docs/followups.md`.

**Session summary (2026-09-05, session 232 — the app and Discord are one board: publishing moved off the capture table):**

- Matt: *"There was an NFL signal last night at 8pm. Why didn't that post to
  discord."* Then, after the trace: *"So the app is correct and discord should
  post that signal bet... The app and discord should always show the same
  picks. They should be identical."*
- **The signal.** `picks` 1634280 — `nfl_wind_totals`, CLE @ JAX Under 40.5
  (-105, 1xBet), written 2026-09-04 20:00:19 ET. It cleared every threshold
  (edge 5.97pp vs 3.00pp, prob 0.5489 vs 0.52, -105 vs a -200 floor, unpaused).
  A second landed 2026-09-05 08:00 ET (BUF @ HOU Under 44.5). Neither published.
- **The app reads `picks`; Discord and push read `opening_signals`.** That put
  the capture step between a pick and its channel — a gate the app does not
  have. `capture_opening_signals` reached `NFL_LOCK_AHEAD_DAYS` (7) forward and
  the game was 9 days out, so no lock row was ever written.
  `config.py` claimed *"the wind card never reaches further than ~4 days out"*;
  `scheduler.NFL_POLL_HORIZON_DAYS` is 10.
- **The gap was not NFL's.** Over every eligible BET since the first Discord
  signal (2026-08-23 22:42 ET): **125 eligible, 119 published, 6 missed — all 6
  never captured, zero captured-then-unpublished.** Delivery was already 100%;
  capture was the entire leak. Bounding on the launch date mattered: unbounded,
  the same query says 39 of 161, but 33 of those predate Discord and were never
  eligible. Full numbers in `docs/rules_evidence.md`.
- **Both publishers now read `picks`** through the same `model_action_thresholds`
  cut the app's `passesActionFilter` applies, with **no date horizon at all** —
  a pick publishes when its game has not started, however far ahead it was
  written. `DISTINCT ON (game_id, model_id, player_id)` ordered by `created_at`
  keeps the FIRST BET as the bet of record (§1c), deliberately coarser than
  #487's new unique key so a pre-#311 side flip still collapses to one pick, and
  the synthesised `game:model[:player]` lock_key matches what capture minted so
  the `push_sent` ledger carries over. Counted before shipping: the first run
  publishes exactly 2 rows, no backlog flood.
- **Push was worse than Discord and was fixed with it.** It read
  `opening_signals` AND bounded on `game_date = target_date`, without even the
  look-ahead widening Discord had, and applied no `min_odds` gate — so no
  look-ahead pick in any sport could ever buzz a phone on the day it was written.
- **The stake, not the signal, was the model's real bug.** `model_under_prob`
  has always clipped a lead past `MAX_CALIBRATED_LEAD` (7) to the lead-7 row;
  `stake_units` returned 0.0 instead of clipping. So the model published a real
  probability and a real edge with no stake, and the publisher — which writes
  every card row as `signal_type='BET'` — locked it insert-once at `0.00u`. The
  stored `model_probability` of 0.5489 IS the lead-7 number, which is what makes
  this an inconsistency rather than a policy. Matt: *"we should return stakes 7
  days for signal picks."* The stake now clips exactly as the probability does;
  `calibrated_lead=False` still flags the row, and `evaluate_board` stopped
  disqualifying a bet its own card was carrying.
- **Cadence, measured against what Matt described.** Hourly Pinnacle/weather
  polling already ran from T-10d (wider than the 7 he described — left alone).
  The 10-minute tier reached only T-3h, not T-24h: `NFL_FAST_WINDOW_HOURS`
  3 → 24, ~750 extra credits a kickoff-day against a 4.71M balance and a
  46–76k/day burn. In-play ESPN state 10s → 5s (free, not an Odds API call).
  The in-play LINE polls were first held at 60s and then taken to **5s** on
  Matt's call, cost stated and accepted. What 5s buys is precise: the feed
  republishes every ~40-46s (136 snapshots over 2.5h, ~7 consecutive polls
  served the identical payload), so it cannot make a quote fresher than that
  floor -- it catches each new snapshot within 5s instead of up to 60s, the
  same trade the MLB loop took. **`LIVE_DAILY_CREDIT_CAP` 12,000 -> 60,000 had
  to go with it**: the anchor poll is slate-wide, so 12x the rate is 12x that
  component (1,800 -> 21,600, ~23,940 a Sunday) and the old cap would have
  bound by early afternoon -- the exact "silent hole in the record" that file
  warns about. ~+30k a game day, ~1.1M over 18 weeks against 4.71M remaining.
  Prop/derivative/halftime polls stay at 60s: the prop feed's own republish is
  ~60s and each is per-game rather than slate-wide.
- **`NFL_LOCK_AHEAD_DAYS` 7 → 10** — no longer a display gate, but it still
  bounds the CLV / opening-signal shadow track, and at 7 that track was silently
  dropping NFL look-ahead picks. 10 matches the poll horizon.
- **Backfilled the two September picks' stakes** (0.00u → 0.83u / 0.79u,
  slate-shaded by the card composition at each run). `created_at`, side, line
  and price untouched — only the stake, which was never a number the model
  chose. Discord was deliberately NOT backfilled (Matt: *"do not backfill for
  discord"*); parity holds from the fix forward.
- **Verified.** `origin/master` 2488 passed / 0 failed; branch 2496 passed / 0
  failed — empty error set both sides. The 12 rewritten parity tests were run
  against a detached `origin/master` worktree first: **7 of 12 fail there**, and
  3 of the 4 new stake tests fail there too.
- **The NFL in-play lane went LIVE.** Matt, after the gate was raised with him:
  *"NFL should be live out of the gate, we should not do paper trading and delay
  this being an available feature."* Taken as his call and recorded in CLAUDE.md
  section 2 so a later session does not "restore" the gate.
  - **Scope is one lane, not four.** `EV_THRESHOLDS` lists four model ids but
    only `nfl_live_prop` has an implementation (`models/pass_attempt_bias.py`,
    live pass attempts); nothing assigns the other three. That mattered: 
    `DERIVATIVE_MARKETS` is all half and quarter lines and `games` stores only
    full-game scores (plus MLB's F5), so a `totals_q3` pick could not have been
    settled by anything in this repo. Those lanes need a scores source BEFORE
    they may write.
  - **`nfl/live_model/pick_writer.py`** writes BET decisions to `picks`; passes
    stay in the JSONL audit log, where the "was a guard eating candidates"
    question belongs. Settles through the existing NFL prop path
    (`prop_market` + normalised `player_key` -> `nfl_player.attempts`), the same
    stat the pre-game `nfl_prop_pass_attempts` grades on.
  - **The game id was the whole problem.** A Decision carries ESPN's event id,
    `picks.game_id` is a FK keyed `NFL_{season}_{week}_{away}_{home}`, and the
    book's event id is a third unrelated string. `Quote` already carried the
    book's team names for exactly this reason ("the only thing the two feeds
    share is who is playing"), so the executor now puts them on every decision's
    context and resolution joins `games` on (both teams, kickoff +/- 1 day). It
    REFUSES rather than guesses: an unsettleable pick is worse than none.
  - **A near-miss worth keeping.** The writer first reused
    `models.scorer._insert_picks`, whose column list carries neither
    `prop_market` nor `player_key` -- psycopg2 ignores extra dict keys, so every
    live pick would have been silently unsettleable with nothing raising. It has
    its own INSERT now, and `test_every_row_key_is_actually_bound_by_the_insert`
    pins the row keys against the statement's placeholders both ways.
  - Thresholds are `min_prob 0.0 / min_edge 0.0` **by design**: the cut is EV,
    in `nfl/live_model/config.EV_THRESHOLDS`, applied before a decision is
    recorded. A second cut in the action filter would write picks and then hide
    them -- the exact divergence this release removes.
  - The lane lock is restated rather than imported: `from models.x import` under
    `nfl/` resolves to whichever `models` package sys.path reaches first and
    `tests/test_nfl_model_imports.py` fails the build over it, so
    `test_the_lane_lock_matches_the_scorers_canonical_predicate` is the drift
    guard the shared import would have been.
- **Not flipped, and why:** `nfl_live_halftime`, `nfl_live_deriv`
  and `nfl_live_stale` stay unimplemented and now cannot write: no half or
  quarter scores exist to settle them. The measurement that preceded the
  go-live decision is kept because it is the baseline the gate will be judged
  against: **zero `nfl_live%` rows in `picks`, zero in
  `model_action_thresholds`, `nfl_live` nowhere in `config.py`.**
- **Not done, deliberately.** The 4 MLB capture misses of 08-24/08-26 pass every
  guard in the capture predicate and remain unexplained — `pipeline_runs` only
  reaches back to 2026-08-27. The new publishers make them moot going forward.
  No stake gate was added to `passesActionFilter`; with the stake fixed there is
  no 0.00u pick left to gate, and adding one would be a second cut the two
  surfaces could drift on.

**Session summary (2026-09-05, session 231 — the college prop probe came back 12x cheaper than I said it would, and Matt turned it on):**

- The probe queued in #488 ran on the worker (job 17136, `done`, 13s, wrote
  nothing). Its self-reported credits were checked against `api_call_log`
  rather than trusted — the header-delta method undercounts (19 vs 26), and
  the call log is what every other cost number in this repo comes from.
- **Measured: 8.7 credits per event, 68 events, ~590 credits for one full
  pass.** 13 of 20 requested markets returned, from 7 books, 0 rejected.
- **I had told Matt ~6,800 for one pass. It is ~590 — wrong by twelve times,
  and wrong in a way worth naming**: I measured MLB's cost per market honestly
  and then EXTRAPOLATED it to college, which is an estimate wearing a
  measurement's clothes. Two assumptions did it: that our 120-game slate was
  the population (the Odds API lists **68** — it never carries the Division
  III fixtures our `games` table holds from CFBD), and that credits scale with
  markets REQUESTED rather than with what comes BACK (college serves few
  markets at few books, so a call is 1.7 credits, not the 12 projected). Both
  errors pointed the same way.
- **The scoping I built did nothing today** and I should not claim it for the
  low cost: all 68 events resolved, all had a DK line, none hit the cap. The
  feed is already scoped to games books price. The gates stay as a ceiling.
- Matt: *"Yes turn on NCAAF prop odds."* The flag gated nothing until now —
  #488 deliberately shipped no scheduler entry — so this session added
  `run_ncaaf_prop_odds` and the job: **9am / 1pm / 6pm ET, daily**, minute 35
  (clear of the :17 refresh pass and the :25 NFL prop card). Three passes is
  ~1,800 credits a Saturday; hourly would be ~8,300. Both affordable, so the
  cadence is set by how often a college prop line is worth re-reading — these
  are the Stats board's research column, not a model input — rather than by
  money. Daily rather than Saturday-only because college plays Thursday and
  Friday nights, and a day with no games costs NOTHING: the ingestor lists
  events first and that call is free (measured, `credits` null).
- **Verified.** `tests/test_ncaaf_prop_odds.py` +3 (18): the kill switch is a
  variable in both places that read it and the code default stays off; the
  schedule is the three named hours and not hourly, and carries no
  `day_of_week`; and the empty-slate return provably precedes any paid call.
  Each watched failing under a mutation (made hourly; defaulted on). Full
  suite **2,474 passed**.
- **Not done, on record:** seven of the twenty markets returned nothing on the
  three probed games, including carries and sacks, which the board now has
  columns for. Three games is not enough to prune a market list — the first
  real pass measures coverage across all 68, and that is when they go.

**Session summary (2026-09-05, session 230 — the same bet, eleven times: a lock that released when a pick was graded):**

- Matt, with a screenshot of the `mlb_prop_pitcher_hits` detail screen: *"The
  same bet showed multiple times for a signal. It should just be the first
  one? How can we make sure we are displaying the right model picks and
  records? This should mirror what we are sending to retool and discord but it
  all comes from the DB."*
- **They were eleven rows in `picks`, not one row rendered eleven times.**
  `Logan Allen Over 4.5 Hits`, `MLB_2026-09-04_DET_CLE`, player 671106: the
  first at 17:21Z DK +116, then a copy every ten minutes from 22:14Z to
  23:43Z, all DK +117, all settled WIN.
- **The mechanism.** Both pick locks asked for `result IS NULL`, so a pick left
  the lock set the moment `settle_picks` graded it. That is harmless while
  "graded" implies "the game is over" — and it stops being harmless when one
  game_id covers two games. The DET/CLE doubleheader (already logged in
  `docs/followups.md`) put game 1's final score on the row while game 2's
  `commence_time` (23:46Z) kept the pre-game cutoff open, so each evening
  refresh pass scored the player again and the next settle pass graded the new
  row ~2 minutes later, releasing the lock for the pass after it. The `odds`
  table shows the two games plainly: in-play snapshots 18:xx–21:xx, a gap at
  22:00, in-play again from 23:xx.
- **What it did to the published record.** Every surface counts rows, so all of
  them moved together. Published window (>= 2026-09-01, settled, current cuts):
  **132 picks / 72-60 / +7.38u / +5.59% as published, against 112 / 62-50 /
  +5.68u / +5.07% deduplicated** — 20 of 132 settled BETs, 15%, were copies.
  Across all 144,669 pre-game pick rows ever written: 63 duplicates, every one
  MLB, every one this bug.
- **Discord was the only surface that got it right**, and that is the answer to
  the second half of the question. It never counts `picks` rows: it reads
  `opening_signals` (one row per `lock_key`) and joins the pick with
  `ORDER BY p.created_at LIMIT 1` — one signal, one message, first row wins.
  The app and Retool both read `v_public_track_record`, so they already mirror
  each other exactly; what neither could survive was a table that held the same
  pick twice.
- **So the fix is in the table, not in three read paths.** Deduplicating in the
  app would have left Retool wrong, and deduplicating in both would be two
  definitions of "the same pick", drifting.
  - `models/scorer.py::_locked_prop_keys` and the game-level `locked_pairs`
    query now key on a pick's EXISTENCE. A pick that exists is the bet of
    record whatever its result (§1c).
  - `_final_game_ids` — new — is the guard `_game_started` cannot be: it asks
    whether the game is OVER rather than whether the clock has passed. All four
    prop loops (MLB batter, WNBA, NBA, MLB pitcher) check it.
  - `uq_picks_one_row_per_pick` (migration `picks_one_row_per_pick.sql`, in the
    active list) makes `(game_date, model_id, game_id, player_id, pick_side)`
    unique on pre-game rows. It is **self-healing**: while duplicates remain it
    RAISEs a NOTICE with the count and creates nothing, so it can ship before
    the cleanup runs. `_insert_picks` gained `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, because
    an IntegrityError inside `executemany` costs a whole pass its picks.
  - `scripts/dedupe_picks.py` removes the existing rows — BET beats non-BET,
    then earliest `created_at`, then lowest pick_id — dry-run by default, with
    a JSON snapshot beside the `picks_log` audit trail.
- **RUN on Matt's approval the same session** (*"Yes run python -m
  scripts.dedupe_picks"*). The sandbox has no `DATABASE_URL`, so the script's
  exact SQL went through the Supabase MCP; **69 rows** removed, not the 63
  measured an hour earlier — six more had been written in between by the
  racing-pass shape, which is its own confirmation that the invariant had no
  enforcement. Snapshotted first into `public.picks_dupes_removed_20260905`
  (a table, not a file: the sandbox is ephemeral and §1b says extracted data
  belongs in Supabase), on top of the `picks_log` audit trail.
  `uq_picks_one_row_per_pick` created immediately rather than waiting for the
  next pass, and `mv_scored_pick_outcomes` refreshed.
  **0 duplicate keys remain.** The published record is now **112 picks /
  62-50 / +5.68u / +5.07%**, the two published views reconcile (MLB 101 /
  +1.75u, NCAAF 11 / +3.93u), and the detail screen returns one Logan Allen
  row: pick 1597529, DK +116, WIN.
- **One row had to be REGRADED, and it is the interesting part of the
  cleanup.** The two copies of `Blake Snell Over 5.5 Ks` (2026-05-09) had been
  graded *differently* — the first row `NO_ACTION`, the copy `LOSS` — so the
  old record counted the loss through the copy. Deleting the copy would have
  silently dropped a real loss. `player_game_log` has Snell at 5 K in 3.0 IP,
  so Over 5.5 lost; pick 1969 is now `LOSS` / -100. **The general lesson: a
  duplicate is not always two identical rows, and deduplicating blind can
  delete the only row carrying the right answer.** Check what the copies
  disagree about before choosing a survivor.
  - `one_row_per_pick`, a CRIT health check, says every morning whether the
    invariant still holds.
- **The key deliberately omits `scored_line`.** A copy written after the line
  moved is the same bug wearing a different number, and it is the exact shape
  §1c's NCAAF example calls out (Over 44.5 churned to Over 54.5). Alternate
  lines do not collide with it: they are odds rows, and the scorer still writes
  one line per player and side (measured — one key in the last month carried
  two lines, itself a released-lock re-score).
- **Tests:** `tests/test_pick_lock_survives_settlement.py`, 16 tests, each
  watched failing on the pre-fix tree (7 fail without the source changes; the
  survivor-rule tests cover new code). Full suite **2,472 passed**.
- **Found, not fixed:** `picks.player_id` and `picks.is_live` exist in
  production and in neither schema file — a first-time setup builds a table the
  scorer cannot insert into (`docs/followups.md`). The doubleheader game_id
  collision itself is still open; this change contains its effect on the record
  but not its effect on which game a pick is graded against.

**Session summary (2026-09-05, session 229 — college football player props, built and measured before they are scheduled):**

- Matt asked what "an NCAAF prop ingestor" meant — the phrase was mine, from
  #486's report that his approved NCAAF alternates had nothing to ride. Given
  the answer he said *"Yes do it"*.
- **What the gap actually was.** Alternates are extra thresholds on a market
  we already collect; college football had NO player props at all.
  `player_prop_odds` holds 2.82M MLB rows, 157k WNBA, 104k NFL and **zero**
  NCAAF, while the Stats tab has listed college players since the CFBD player
  log landed. Every one of those rows showed a dash where every other sport
  shows a line.
- **Slate size is what makes this different, and it is measured, not
  assumed.** One Saturday (09-05): **120** NCAAF games, **70** with a
  DraftKings game line, **39** FBS vs FBS, **34** priced by our own models.
  A prop pull is one paid call per event, so all 120 would cost more than the
  entire MLB program — and the 50 unlined games are Division III (Lakeland at
  Carthage) with no player props to sell us. `scope_events` keeps only events
  DraftKings has already lined, under `NCAAF_PROP_MAX_EVENTS`. Self-maintaining:
  no hand-kept "big games" list to rot by November.
- **The game id is RESOLVED, never constructed** — the NFL ingestor's lesson,
  and it bites harder here: the feed writes "Ohio State Buckeyes" while CFBD's
  canonical spellings carry accents and punctuation the feed drops ("San José
  State", "Hawai'i"). `resolve_odds_api_school` already solved exactly this for
  game lines, so props reuse it rather than growing a second, differently-wrong
  copy. An event resolving to no known game is SKIPPED: an orphan prop row
  joins to nothing and looks like coverage forever.
- **Nothing is assumed about the feed.** The Odds API uses one key namespace
  for both football leagues and documents neither for college, so the 24
  requested keys are the NFL's under the college sport key; a key it does not
  serve 422s its own chunk (alternates chunked apart from standard markets)
  and the **probe reports which markets, books and players actually came
  back**. That is the number Matt gets before this is scheduled —
  `RUN_NCAAF_PROP_ODDS=0` until then, and the probe is queued as a worker job
  (`ncaaf_prop_odds`, `jobs/declared_jobs.json`) rather than handed over as a
  command.
- **The app half, which is why the board was blank and would have stayed
  blank.** Every sport reaches its prop MARKET through its model id, and
  `rawPropModelForStat` returns null for NCAAF by design (it has four
  game-level models and no prop model), so `propMarketForStat` returned null
  with it — the data alone would have changed nothing on screen. NCAAF now
  resolves through `NCAAF_STAT_TO_MARKET`, a direct stat→market map used only
  for that sport. Twelve of its eighteen board stats get a market; six get
  none because no book prices them, and those columns stay correctly blank.
  `propModelForStat` still returns null, so the board never offers "Add to
  play" on a college row — these lines are research, never a pick.
- **Verified.** `tests/test_ncaaf_prop_odds.py` (14): the DK-line scope, the
  unresolved skip, the resolved id, the per-pass ceiling, the gate being
  optional for a backfill, alternates never sharing a chunk, a rejected chunk
  costing only its own markets, an unrequested book never being written, the
  probe writing NOTHING, the job defaulting to probe and capping its sample,
  and a cross-language check that every market the app's map asks for is one
  the ingestor pulls. Each watched failing under a mutation (gate dropped,
  raw feed names, plain slicing, any book accepted, ceiling removed). Two
  assertions from session 228 said NCAAF had no prop ingest — true when
  written, false now, updated with the reason. Full suite **2,470 passed**.
  tsc: 33 non-cast errors, identical to master, none in touched files.
  `ux_scan` on the four touched files: 11 findings vs 11 on master, **0
  blockers vs 1**. Every verify script passes; `verify_stats_pill`'s one
  failure is the pre-existing BookMark check, byte-identical on master.
- **UX review (frontend-ux-designer, Mobbin connected — six references
  pulled): Ship with fixes, no blockers, five Should-fixes and four
  Considers, ALL TAKEN**, and one of them was a real bug I would have
  shipped:
  - **`def_tackles` was mapped to `player_tackles_assists`, and the two count
    differently.** CFBD charges a shared tackle as a HALF (5 solo + 9 assists
    is stored as 9.5); the book counts solo + assists at FULL credit and would
    say 14. The hit-rate percentage and the price beside it would have been
    about different quantities — §5 of `docs/best_line.md` in its ugliest
    form. The mapping is gone and the market is out of the pull; it returns
    when the board carries a full-credit column, which is a new StatDef.
  - **`player_anytime_td` was priced honestly but LABELLED as the column.**
    A leg reading "Over 0.5 Rush+Rec TDs" is a proposition no book sells (and
    the market also pays on a return TD the board never counted). A bet made
    from that column is now called **Anytime TD** on the pill, the sheet and
    the slip, while the column header keeps its own name
    (`markets.propDisplayLabel`).
  - **Six of the eighteen football columns had no market at all**, and
    `noLinesNote` had no branch for it — the LINE column simply vanished, and
    "no book prices this" looked exactly like "the board is broken". It now
    says so in six words.
  - **The note said "today's games" on a sport whose slate is Saturday from
    Sunday onward.** It dates itself now, like the column header already did.
  - **A one-way market rendered a silent column of dashes**: anytime TD has no
    Under price, so the "At most" view nulled every quote while a side-blind
    `bookPostsMarket` still answered "posted". The check learned about sides
    and names the reason. Pre-existing on MLB home runs, newly reachable here.
  - Considers taken: the map now serves **both football leagues** (see
    below); football boards start filtered to the slate, with the two places
    that decide the default sharing one function; the dead `todayPicks` and
    `propModel` reads are gone, which also removes a whole-day picks fetch
    from this screen and makes the board's independence from models
    structural.
  - Left on record by the review, not taken here: the player-name row wants a
    device check against a long school name now that the LINE column takes
    62pt (static read only — no device); and same-game correlation treats two
    college line legs as independent, which matters more on a football board
    than on MLB (pre-existing, `LINE_LEG_MODEL_ID` → `other`).
- **The NFL board had the same bug and I extended the fix rather than shipping
  the asymmetry.** `PROP_MARKET_BY_MODEL` carries no `nfl_` entry, so NFL
  resolved its market through a model it does not have — **103,693 NFL prop
  rows were already stored and invisible**, and college would have landed in
  the same blank column. One `FOOTBALL_STAT_TO_MARKET` now serves both; the
  two boards already share column keys by design. Neither league gains an
  "Add to play" affordance: `propModelForStat` still returns null for both.
- **A guard that dead code could satisfy, caught in my own verifier.** One new
  check ended `... && x || y`, which the trailing `||` made true whatever the
  first half said. Rewritten to assert both leaderboard rows pass the market
  name AND the header still reads the stat — then watched failing under three
  mutations.
- **Not done, on record:** there is still **no NCAAF prop model** — a college
  prop row is research, and a model is its own piece of work on the CFBD
  player log. The ingestor is not on any schedule until the probe's number is
  accepted.

**Session summary (2026-09-05, session 228 — alternates on every pass, and for WNBA, NBA and NFL):**

  Alternates on every pass, at 11,000 to 14,000 credits a day, instead of

  yes. Same with NFL NCAAF."*
- **Every pass.** `PROP_ALT_REFRESH_MIN` now defaults to 0; the gate
  short-circuits and a positive value restores the cadence without a code
  change. The approved number is the measured one from session 227
  (8 MLB markets × ~2 credits × 705 calls/day); tomorrow's scheduled
  readback reports what it actually cost.
- **Basketball** rides the same ingestor and gate: five WNBA keys, eight NBA
  (no `player_double_double_alternate` — a Yes/No market has no milestones).

- **NFL** rides `nfl_prop_odds_ingestor.py`: eleven keys, appended to the

  (`_market_chunks`), so a key the API rejects 422s its own chunk and never

  last week (week 1 has not opened), so the first card measures it.

  ingestor is keyed on nflverse ids and `americanfootball_nfl`. That is an
  NCAAF prop ingestor first, in `docs/followups.md`, which replaces the
  WNBA/NBA decision entry.

  carries alternates and NCAAF does not; football alternates never share a

  does not; a rejected alternate chunk costs only itself (every standard
