**Session summary (2026-09-03, session 213 — the Stats tab shows the user’s sportsbook’s current line for every player and team, separate from the models; and the pre-game poller is wiping prop rows):**

- Matt, on a screenshot of the MLB Stats board (1+ Hits, L10, Playing today):
  *"The players are playing today but don't have a better line for that stat."*
  Every one of the eight visible players showed an em-dash in ODDS. Then, after
  the first fix was up: *"We need to display all lines regardless of bet status
  … see a current line for a player or team so that I can do research on
  players and bet what I want off of that data … If they select FanDuel we only
  show FanDuel, if the user had DK selected then we only show DK. Only do this
  feature on this tab. It works separately from the models."* Branch
  `claude/player-stats-line-availability-di7o6w`, PR #448.
- **Measured in production before touching code. DraftKings was pricing all
  eight.** At 14:20 ET DK had a `batter_hits` line for **184 players across all
  9 MLB games; 60 held a pick.** Two games with fully confirmed lineups held
  zero.
- **Two causes, and only one of them was known.** The known half is the lineup
  gate: a batter is scored only once `lineup_slots.is_confirmed` is true
  (session 148). The NEW half: **the pre-game line poller is deleting prop
  picks.** `pregame_line_poller` calls `run_scorer(only_games=…)` whenever DK
  moves on a game; the game scorer's non-BET housekeeping delete is scoped by
  `game_id`, not by model, so it takes that game's prop NONE and AVOID rows with
  it and never re-creates them. `picks_log`: INSERT 36 at :20, DELETE 36 at :24,
  nothing until the next hour. Prop non-BET deletes were **0/day through
  2026-08-29 and 8,467 → 23,047/day from 2026-08-30**, the day the poller
  shipped. **No prop BET row was ever deleted** — §1c holds for the bet of
  record.
- **NOT FIXED, deliberately — Matt's decision.** The delete is accidentally
  load-bearing: `_locked_prop_keys` locks on ANY unsettled row including NONE,
  so without the delete a dead-zone player could never later cross into a BET.
  The clean fix is to lock props on BET only, matching the game lock; that
  changes which picks fire, so under §1b it needs Matt's name. Logged in
  `docs/sports/mlb.md` and `docs/followups.md` (`[needs-decision]`).
- **What shipped, after Matt's direction: the LINE column is the user's own
  sportsbook, and only that book.** `usePreferredBook` drives it; the header
  reads "DK" / "FD" / "MGM". **No DraftKings fallback** — "only show FanDuel"
  means a FanDuel user sees FanDuel's number or a dash. Measured: **FanDuel
  posts no `batter_hits` line at all on 2026-09-03** while every book prices
  moneyline/spread/total on all nine games, so a FanDuel user's Players board
  is honestly sparse and the screen says so in words ("FanDuel doesn't post
  Hits lines today. Switch sportsbook to see one.") rather than showing a column
  of dashes. `SportsbookIndicator` sits under the sport toggle on both boards,
  with a new `fallsBackToModelBook={false}` so its label does not promise a
  fallback this tab does not do.
- **No model in the cell.** Pick precedence is gone; the cell is the book's
  current number for the line the RULER is on ("2+ Hits" is Over 1.5, a price
  on 0.5 is a different bet — docs/best_line.md §5). A retired model no longer
  blanks the line either: new `statCatalog.propMarketForStat` is
  retirement-blind, because the line comes from the market, not from a model.
  The one thing a pick still does on this tab is unlock **"Add to betslip"**
  — a parlay leg IS a pick — and it appears only where the model scored this
  exact line (`slipPickFor`), as a button, with no edge or EV beside it. That
  keeps the Betslip → Stats round-trip working. Judgement call, flagged.
- **Teams got a LINE column too** ("a current line for a player or team").
  New `buildTeamLineIndex`: the selected book's number for each team's next
  game, from that team's side — its moneyline, its spread (−1.5 favourite /
  +1.5 dog), or the game total (over). The market follows the stat
  (`teamLineMarketFor`: Over% → total, ATS% and margin → spread, everything
  else → moneyline). Header reads "FD ML" / "FD SPRD" / "FD TOT".
- **Both boards are bounded to games that have NOT started.** Replaying the
  Teams read against production showed Pittsburgh's "latest" FanDuel moneyline
  at −50000 — four runs up, mid-game. The refresh keeps writing `open` rows
  after first pitch (§6), so a date bound alone prints in-play prices under a
  "current line" header. `unstartedGameIds` bounds both indexes; a game with
  no `commence_time` is kept (fail open).
- **The sheet is one book.** New `StatsLineSheet` replaces `PlayerOddsSheet`
  (deleted): the selected book's price, a "Bet on <Book>" deep link, "Switch
  sportsbook" (opens the picker and closes the sheet — the board re-prices
  under it), and the betslip button where a pick exists. No all-books table,
  no model tiles — those are the pick detail's job.
- **The name join refuses rather than guesses.** `normalizePlayerName` (the
  mirror of `data/name_match.py`); any key two spellings share is dropped on
  both sides. Reads are bounded to the sport's slate game ids because the odds
  views have no sport column and `player_points` is both NBA and WNBA.
- **Verified, and every guard WATCHED FAILING.** `verify_stats_odds.ts`: **45
  checks** on the real 2026-09-03 rows, all pass. Removing each guard in turn
  fails it: DK-fallback 2, no exact line 2, no ambiguity refusal 3, no sport
  bound 1, betslip at any line 1, bookPostsMarket ignores the book 1, away
  team gets the home side 4, spread not flipped 2, team index ignores the book
  4, stat→market map collapsed 2, started games leak through 3. Earlier in the
  session a break edit landed in the wrong function and produced a FALSE PASS;
  scoping the edit to the function under test is what made it a test. `tsc`
  error set identical to `origin/master` (57, all in `queries.ts`, 0 in touched
  files). `verify_stats_board`, `verify_hit_rate`, `verify_retired_models`,
  `verify_betslip`, `verify_line_shop`, `verify_preferred_book`,
  `verify_book_lines`, `verify_slip_stability`, `verify_team_board`,
  `verify_pick_timing` all pass. Full pytest **2,306 passed** on the merged
  tree (no Python changed after that run).
- **Replayed against production, not fixtures.** The Players read for the
  screenshot's board returns a DK price at 0.5 for 7 of the 8 players; the
  eighth, Tyrone Taylor, has no hits line at any book and is not in the CHC
  lineup — on the board because "Playing today" filters by team, not lineup.
  The Teams read returns FanDuel's moneyline/spread/total for every game.
- **Second UX designer review (the redirected change): 1 Blocker, 8
  Should-fix, 5 Consider. Every Blocker and Should-fix applied; 4 of the 5
  Considers too.** The Blocker: the sheet's body `Pressable` was accessible,
  which groups every child into ONE VoiceOver element so the three buttons
  could not be reached as buttons — `accessible={false}`, and the backdrop got
  its role. Should-fixes: the pill's hint said "Opens FanDuel" when the tap
  opens a sheet; the Teams header "FD SPRD" used an abbreviation no book uses
  and a bare moneyline pill sat under 44pt — header is now the book only
  (plus the weekday on a future slate, via a shared `format.weekdayET`) and
  EVERY cell carries a caption ("ML", "−1.5", "o8.5"); the "switch sportsbook"
  instruction in the no-lines note had no control — the note is now the
  button, opening the picker in place, on both boards; the betslip button
  appeared on some rows and not others with no word why — one tertiary caption
  ("The model has a pick on this line. Parlays are priced at DraftKings."),
  which also owns up to the slip pricing legs at DK; the "Bet on" button now
  uses `bookButtonColors` like the betslip's hand-off (DK green for DK);
  `hitSlop` on the switch row; the `SportsbookIndicator` moved ABOVE the sport
  toggle so it sits in the same slot as on the Picks tab. Considers applied:
  the picker no longer closes the sheet on cancel (an effect on the store's
  book, not a comparison in the picker's `onClose` — which runs in the same
  tick as `setBook` and would have read the stale value); the flat book row;
  a Teams-side no-lines note; and a "lines post once books price today's
  games" note for the pre-open hour. Declined: a second Under line in the
  sheet for totals — the cell and the Bet link are the Over side, and an
  Under price with no action behind it is half a feature.
- **`ux_scan` set diff after the review: `a11y-pressable` 10 → 9** (the
  Blocker), everything else pre-existing and line-shifted. 46 checks in
  `verify_stats_odds.ts`, all pass; `tsc` still identical to master.
- **Not a model update** under §1b — display only; nothing here reaches a
  scorer and `tests/test_multi_book_odds.py` is untouched. No `Updated-By`.
- **Outstanding for Matt:** JS-only, ships over the air on merge; force-quit
  and relaunch twice. And the poller decision above.

**Session summary (2026-09-04, session 217 — 2026-09-01 is the official live date, and the app now mirrors Retool by reading the same view):**

- Matt: *"Just mirror retool for now. But only start tracking bets as of 9/1 and
  on, that will be our official live date. We will set up retool to do the same,
  but only start with games 9/1 and on."*
- **THE PUBLISHED RECORD WENT FROM 1,262 SETTLED PICKS TO 76.** Measured at the
  cutover: **691-568-3, +4.89%, +61.2u → 46-30-0, +15.40%, +11.7u**, six models
  firing since 9/1 (`mlb_live_total_runs` 26 at +19.0%, `mlb_prop_pitcher_hits`
  18 at +42.1%, `mlb_prop_pitcher_k` 18 at -29.8%, `mlb_prop_pitcher_outs` 8 at
  +60.6%, `ncaaf_live_total` 5, `ncaaf_live_win_prob` 1). **Those per-model ROIs
  sit on 1-26 bets and are not evidence of anything** — they are simply what the
  window currently holds, and four of the six are live lanes or pitcher props.
- **THE MIRROR IS ONE VIEW, NOT TWO MATCHING QUERIES.** Both published views were
  a UNION of two branches answering different questions — a re-graded
  full-outcome branch for the MLB/WNBA core models, and a settled-BET-as-fired
  branch for everything else. Retool reads the second kind, so the second kind
  now serves EVERY model and the re-graded branch is deleted. The app's Models
  tab moved off `v_model_full_outcome_record` onto `v_public_track_record`
  (`fetchPublishedModelRecord`, mapped into the existing `FullOutcomeRecord`
  shape so no consumer changed). **Retool's `q_performance` is now
  `SELECT * FROM v_public_track_record ORDER BY picks DESC` — one line, because
  the logic lives in the view rather than in two hand-written copies.**
- **Reading `picks` instead of the matview also dissolves yesterday's bug in the
  published record**: no allow-list and no double exclusion of in-play picks, so
  the live lanes and UFC are in on the same terms as everything else. The
  `(is_live IS NOT TRUE OR model_id LIKE '%\_live\_%')` clause is KEPT — a
  dedicated live lane counts, a pre-game model's in-play pick does not (§6).
- **WHAT DELIBERATELY DID NOT MOVE TO 9/1.** `v_model_full_outcome_record`,
  `v_model_full_outcome_picks` and `mv_scored_pick_outcomes` keep 2026-04-14 and
  their re-graded semantics: that is the threshold-sweep tool, and §7's
  EVALUATION RULE needs every scored pick — BET, AVOID and dead-zone alike. **A
  cut cannot be swept on three days of BET-only history.** Publishing and
  sweeping are now different objects, which is the point rather than a
  contradiction. `OpeningComparisonScreen` and `probability_calibration`
  likewise keep their longer windows; both are analysis, not the record.
- **The date is now stated ONCE.** It was spelled out in five files and four
  screens' copy, which is why moving it was a nine-file change;
  `mobile/src/lib/recordStart.ts` is the single source, `config.PAPER_TRADING_START`
  is its backend twin, and the view carries the gate that actually filters.
- Three simplifications fell out, each verified dead rather than assumed: the
  `mlb_over_under` honest-era gate (9/1 is past 7/05), the
  `model_id <> 'mlb_prop_batter_hr'` exclusion (retired models have no
  `model_action_thresholds` row, so the inner join drops them), and the
  five-model/prop-prefix allow-list that split the two branches.
- Copy fixed as well as data: the built-in detail header said *"Since 2026-04-14
  · at current thresholds"*, which became wrong the moment the source changed —
  now *"Since 2026-09-01 · bets as placed"*.
- Verified: migration applied and both views reconcile to the same 76 / 46-30 /
  +15.40% (per-model and daily agree); `get_advisors(security)` clean of any new
  finding; `verify_live_record_start.ts` (11 checks) **watched to fail** — a
  mutated branch that re-hardcoded the old date and pointed the hook back at the
  re-graded view tripped 4 of them; `verify_model_inputs`, `verify_unpriced_pnl`
  and `verify_daily_results` still pass; tsc error set identical to
  `origin/master` (33, line offsets only, all pre-existing in the queries.ts
  baseline); Python suite 2,394 passed.
- **Rollback is one migration re-run** — the previous two-branch definitions are
  captured in the migration header.
- **THE UX REVIEW SAID DO NOT SHIP, AND IT WAS RIGHT ON ALL FOUR.** Every
  blocker was verified against the code before fixing, and every one was real:
  (1) **the on-device cache still held ~3,200 pre-9/1 rows** — `settledPicks.v2`
  was never bumped and `mergeSettled` had no floor, so an upgrading member would
  have seen April-onward numbers under "since Sep 1" headers while a fresh
  install saw none. Key bumped to v3 AND a floor added, so the next move of the
  date is self-healing rather than another bump. (2) **Custom-model backtests
  were labelled with the live date but read the April-gated sweep matview** —
  five months of picks under a three-day label; `ModelEditScreen` was already
  telling the truth ("since Apr 14"), which is how the reviewer caught it. Now
  a named `BACKTEST_START` with its own copy. (3) **The built-in detail screen
  showed two contradictory pick counts a scroll apart** — the record header from
  the published view, the list beneath from the ungated full-outcome view, and
  copy asserting they were "the exact set". `fetchModelFullOutcomePicks` is now
  bounded to the same window. (4) **"stays paper-only until it clears 50+ picks"**
  in Track Record copy — a §2 violation that the reset made actively false, since
  every model is now under 50. Rewritten.
- **Two [should-fix]es taken because the thin window makes them matter:** ROI is
  no longer coloured green/red below 10 settled picks (a green **+60.6% on eight
  bets** was the most screenshot-able number on the board) and the row says
  "N of 50 settled"; and the Models list gets an EmptyState for a sport with
  nothing settled yet, which otherwise rendered a wall of "0 picks · — · —"
  indistinguishable from a failed fetch. Also: the reset is now explained at the
  top of "Read this first", one vocabulary ("settled bets, as posted") across the
  three surfaces that had three, and `OpeningComparisonScreen`'s local
  `PAPER_START` renamed and moved into `recordStart` beside the other windows.
- **Two self-inflicted bugs, both caught by tooling rather than by me.** An
  import-insertion heuristic put `import { LIVE_RECORD_START }` *inside* a
  multi-line `import type {` block in `queries.ts`, and the empty-state edit
  added a second `ListHeaderComponent` to a FlatList that already had one.
  **Both were hidden for two runs because my own typecheck command filtered out
  `queries.ts`** — the file I was editing. Do not grep the file under edit out of
  its own typecheck; diff the error SET against master instead, which is what
  surfaced them.

**Session summary (2026-09-04, session 216 — eleven models told the dashboard they had never fired; the whole of UFC was one of them):**

- Matt, with the app's Models tab and Track Record beside it: *"Update stats
  and data on the models that match the records we have or are feeding to
  retool web application. Check the repo for that connection."*
- **The connection is `monitoring/store.py::model_performance`.** The Retool app
  (<https://signalbaseskinny--betting-model-models-ops.retool.app>) was ported
  from it on 2026-08-31 against the same Supabase; its queries live in Retool,
  not in this repo, so whatever that function reports, Retool reports. There is
  no Retool MCP connector in this session, so the app itself could not be
  edited from here.
- **THE DEFECT: a model the matview cannot grade reported settled=0, and the
  page renders zero as "registered · awaiting first pick".** `profit_units`
  exists ONLY in `mv_scored_pick_outcomes`, and that matview carries an explicit
  model allow-list and excludes in-play picks twice. Everything outside it
  joined to nothing. Measured on production: **eleven models, 182 settled BETs,
  every one reading as never fired** — `mlb_live_total_runs` 94 settled 58-36
  **+12.25u** (the app's best MLB model), the two retired live lanes, all three
  UFC models (21 bets), `wnba_spread`, and both F5 secondary markets. CLAUDE.md
  §7 exactly: an empty board and a broken pipeline look identical.
- **Fixed with a second `agg` arm reading those models out of `picks`, scoped
  by `NOT EXISTS` against the matview on `model_id`.** The scope is the whole
  point and two narrower filters are both wrong: a `_live_` name pattern fixes
  the five live lanes and leaves UFC broken, and `is_live` would fold every
  pre-game prop model's in-play picks into its pre-game row (§6 — pre-game and
  in-play prices never mix; it would have moved `mlb_prop_batter_hits` by 116
  picks it never took pre-game). The anti-join is disjoint by construction and
  empties itself as the allow-list grows. Verified on production: 33 rows,
  **0 models split across arms**, 0 false zeros.
- **Checked and deliberately NOT changed.** Two things that look like the same
  bug and are not: retired models keep their record on the dashboard through
  the existing no-threshold-row arm (`test_registered_not_firing.py` pins it —
  losing it would silently rewrite history), and the 133 pre-2026-07-05
  `mlb_over_under` picks missing from the matview are the honest-era gate
  (`ou_record_views_honest_era_gate.sql`), not a gap.
- **THE OTHER HALF OF THE ANSWER: the app's Models tab and the dashboard are
  MEANT to disagree, and now say so in `docs/monitoring.md`.** The dashboard
  reads `signal_type='BET'` from the matview — *what the model told us to bet
  at the time*. The app reads `v_model_full_outcome_record` — *how today's cut
  performs over every scored pick, re-graded*. Same database, same day:
  `mlb_moneyline` is **-3.25u (-3.3%)** on one and **+7.39u (+23.8%)** on the
  other; `mlb_over_under` -43.5% vs -6.7%; `mlb_prop_pitcher_outs` +5.8% vs
  +12.5%. **The app's column is in-sample** — every cut was swept on this same
  history — so the dashboard's is the number to quote to a member and the app's
  is the one for judging whether a cut is worth keeping. Documented rather than
  "reconciled": making one read the other's source deletes a question.
- **Both screenshots reconcile to production**, so nothing was wrong with them:
  Pitcher Outs 188 · 101-87 · +12.5% · +$2355.15 matches the view to the cent,
  and Track Record 687-563-3 / +5.1% / +62.8u had simply moved on to
  691-568-3 / +4.89% / +61.2u by the time it was queried.
- Verified: `tests/test_ungraded_model_performance.py` (7 checks) **watched to
  fail first** — 4 fail on `origin/master`'s `store.py`, all pass with the arm.
  Full Python suite 2394 passed, 0 failed. No mobile change, so no UX review.
  Not a model update — dashboard plumbing, no `Updated-By:` trailer.
- **Outstanding for Matt: Retool does not inherit this.** Its `q_performance`
  needs the same second arm or it keeps showing all eleven models as awaiting
  their first pick; `monitoring/store.py` is the reference implementation.

**Session summary (2026-09-04, session 212 — the last RLS-off table closed, and the same guard bug recurred within the hour):**

- mike: *"do the remaining seven too."*

**IT WAS EIGHT, NOT SEVEN, AND MY OWN NUMBER WAS THE STALE ONE.** The count came
from a query I had run an hour earlier. Re-measuring first found
`nfl_odds_cache_backup` — 37 MB, 6,769 gzipped blobs of `nfl/data/odds_cache`,
moved into Supabase by another session in the meantime, closing the gap CLAUDE.md
§1b names. §1b again: never estimate what you can measure, and a measurement goes
stale on a repo four sessions are working in at once.

**The eight split three ways, and the split is the whole design:**

| | what | handling |
|---|---|---|
| `brand_assets` | a **fourth create site** — `scripts/fetch_brand_avatar.py` creates it on demand and already carried its own hand-written REVOKE | `WORKER_ONLY_TABLES`, `lock_down()` beside the CREATE |
| 7 archives | no create site under that exact name: six dated repair backups plus the odds cache | `ARCHIVE_TABLES`, admin sweep only |
| the repair script's backup | `DROP` + `CREATE TABLE AS` on a name from `--backup-table`, so every re-run makes a **fresh open table** | locked at the create site; no fixed name to sweep |

The archives are **locked rather than dropped** because a repair is reversible
only while its backup exists. Whether any is still worth keeping is a RETENTION
decision, and a grant change does not get to make it.

**`lock_down_sql`'s roster check became a read-surface check**, to admit that
third case. It now refuses anything in `all_readable()` — RLS with no policies
denies anon regardless of its GRANT, which is the only way this change can break
something — and **validates the identifier**, because the value is interpolated
into SQL and one caller takes it from the command line. psycopg2 cannot
parameterise an identifier, so that check has nowhere else to live.

**AND THE GUARD BUG HAD ALREADY RECURRED, IN CODE MERGED AN HOUR AFTER THE FIX.**
`tracking/threshold_review.py` (#466) gates its early-return on
`schema_is_current()` with no `rls=` or `revoked_from=` — so on a database where
its two tables exist but are still open, it returns True and the `lock_down()`
below never runs. It worked only because RLS was already on. Twice in one day is
the §7 bar for a rule, and the rule here is about the TEST, not the code:

**a guard named after the place it was written does not generalise.** Three
guards are now DERIVED from `WORKER_ONLY_TABLES` — every create site calls
`lock_down`, every `schema_is_current` on a worker-only table passes `rls=` and
`revoked_from=`, and every literal `lock_down` target is declared. The old
job_queue-specific test is gone; the derived one covers it and everything after
it.

**The third guard closes a hole the first mutation run found.** Dropping
`brand_assets` from the declaration while its create site keeps calling
`lock_down` changes NOTHING at runtime — which is the problem. It silently falls
out of the admin sweep and out of every read-back, so nothing is checking it any
more. That mutation passed until the guard existed.

**Two corrections the tests made to me, both worth keeping:**

- I asserted `model_artifacts` would be invisible to the create-site regex
  because `trainer.py` holds its DDL in `ARTIFACT_DDL`. Wrong — the literal is
  still in the source, so all six worker-only tables are accounted for and the
  assertion is now `unaccounted == []`.
- The apply script **skips** a table absent from `pg_class` rather than rolling
  back. An archive can legitimately be dropped later, and a tidy-up in one place
  must not break the grant sweep everywhere. Same shape as
  `_function_signature` returning None for a missing function.

Eight mutations, all watched to fail. Suite **2,330 passed, 2 skipped**.

**Applied, and the result is a clean invariant rather than a count:**

```
0 of 84 public base tables have RLS off
0 have FORCE RLS on
84 have RLS on
```

Six tables actually changed (`brand_assets` plus five repair backups); the rest
logged `0 lock-down statement(s) (already closed)`, `nfl_odds_cache_backup`
included — another session had locked it between my measurement and the apply.
The app's read surface is untouched: all 33 anon-readable relations still
readable.

**THE PROBE THEN FAILED, CORRECTLY-SHAPED AND WRONGLY.** `verify_worker_rls.py`
was written for three non-empty tables and treats 0 rows visible as a failure.
`model_auto_pauses` and `threshold_reviews` are legitimately empty, so it exited
1 on a false positive — and that is worth recording because the fix is about
honesty, not coverage: **a count cannot distinguish "genuinely empty" from
"filtered out by RLS".** Those are warnings now, saying exactly that. What proves
exemption for an empty table is `current_user` being owner or `BYPASSRLS` plus
the write probe, not the count. The probe also gained a `FORCE` RLS check — the
one setting that would actually deny the worker — and stopped crashing on a table
absent from `pg_class`.

**The re-run is clean, and the row counts corroborate two independent records:**

```
connected as 'postgres', rolbypassrls=True
brand_assets                            rls=True force=False rows=2
model_artifacts                         rls=True force=False rows=4
model_auto_pauses                       rls=True force=False rows=0      (warned)
odds_history_pulls                      rls=True force=False rows=1042
threshold_reviews                       rls=True force=False rows=0      (warned)
worker_jobs                             rls=True force=False rows=15
mlb_pitcher_stats_pre_rebuild_20260903  rls=True force=False rows=35547
mlb_team_stats_pre_rebuild_20260903     rls=True force=False rows=4651
nba_team_stats_pre_rebuild_20260903     rls=True force=False rows=1140
nfl_odds_cache_backup                   rls=True force=False rows=6769
nhl_team_stats_pre_rebuild_20260903     rls=True force=False rows=4800
odds_pre_first_pitch_relabel_20260903   rls=True force=False rows=6565
wnba_team_stats_pre_rebuild_20260903    rls=True force=False rows=1215
worker_jobs write probe: insert/read-back/update/delete all succeeded
rolled back
SUCCESS — all 13 declared closed tables (2 inconclusive, see warnings)
```

`mlb_pitcher_stats_pre_rebuild_20260903` at **35,547** matches the figure
`docs/team_stats_leak.md` recorded when that backup was taken, and
`odds_pre_first_pitch_relabel_20260903` at **6,565** matches the row count of the
first-pitch label repair in session 207. Two counts written down before this
change and reproduced by a query through RLS afterwards — which is the strongest
form the read probe can take.

**Found on the way, not fixed:** `game_weather` grants anon INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE
while its only anon policies are SELECT. The writes are **inert** — RLS denies
them — but the ACL does not match intent, the same shape as the `odds` finding in
session 205 and a leftover the session-205 sweep missed. `mobile/src` only ever
SELECTs it; every writer is server-side over `postgres`
(`data/ingestors/weather_ingestor.py`). Revoking is one line and safe. Left for a
decision rather than widening a second ask unilaterally.

---

**Session summary (2026-09-04, session 211 — RLS on the three worker-only tables, and the guard that had to go inside the helper):**

- mike: *"enable rls on those three tables."*

`worker_jobs`, `odds_history_pulls` and `model_artifacts` lost their
anon/authenticated grants on 2026-09-03, which left **one** lock, and it is an
ACL. A future hand-written `GRANT` — or a `GRANT ... ON ALL TABLES IN SCHEMA
public`, which is how the grant arrived in the first place — re-opens them with
nothing behind it, because **a table with RLS off applies no policy check at
all.** RLS with zero policies is deny-all for any role that is neither the table
owner nor `BYPASSRLS`, so a re-granted ACL stops being sufficient on its own.

**Measured before enabling it**, because RLS with no policies denies everything
and the failure mode is a worker that silently stops writing:

| checked | result |
|---|---|
| owner of all three | `postgres` |
| `postgres` `rolbypassrls` | true — **and** it owns them, so exempt twice over |
| `service_role` `rolbypassrls` | true |
| the worker's actual connections | `usename=postgres` via Supavisor (`pg_stat_activity`) |
| views / matviews selecting from them | **none** (`pg_depend` → `pg_rewrite`) |
| references in `mobile/src` | **none** |
| anon / authenticated privileges | **none already** |

`FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` is deliberately not used — that is the one that
subjects the OWNER to the policies and would stop the worker dead. Pinned by a
test, because `FORCE` is one word away from the statement we do want.

**A ONE-OFF MIGRATION WOULD NOT HOLD, and that is now a repo rule with a third
data point.** All three tables are created on demand by the code that writes
them, which is how `model_artifacts` came back with the full anon grant between
two sweeps of the schema hours apart. So the revoke + RLS pair is stated once in
`data/anon_readable.py::lock_down_sql` and called from all three create sites —
`tracking/job_queue.py`, `data/ingestors/odds_ingestor.py`,
`models/trainer.py::_store_artifact`.

**AND THEN THE `.claude/rules/` FILE CAUGHT ME MID-CHANGE, WHICH IS THE POINT OF
PATH-SCOPED RULES.** I had written, in a comment, *"both statements are
idempotent, so calling this on every ensure_schema() costs a catalog lookup and
nothing else."* `operations.md` loaded on opening `tracking/job_queue.py` and
says the opposite in measured numbers: **`ALTER TABLE ... ENABLE ROW LEVEL
SECURITY` takes ACCESS EXCLUSIVE whether or not RLS is already on**, and every
one fires Supabase's `pgrst_ddl_watch` — PostgREST then answers **503 to the
whole app** while it rebuilds its schema cache. 11.6 hours of database time and
~3,600 forced reloads across seven modules, 2026-09-01. My comment was the exact
reasoning that caused that outage.

Two things followed:

- **The gate went INSIDE the helper.** `lock_down(conn, table)` calls
  `data/ddl_guard.schema_is_current` itself and returns `()` when the table is
  already closed, so a call site cannot forget it. `lock_down_sql()` stays as the
  ungated builder for the admin script and the tests. `trainer.py` mattered most:
  a full retrain sweep stores ~70 artifacts, so the ungated form would have meant
  70 forced cache reloads per sweep.
- **`job_queue`'s existing guard was a guard dead code could satisfy.** Its
  `schema_is_current(...)` early-return fires BEFORE the new lock-down, and
  without `rls=True, revoked_from=API_ROLES` it answers True on a database where
  `worker_jobs` exists but is still open — so the lock-down would never run. Both
  parameters already existed on the guard for exactly this; they just had to be
  passed.

**Two existing tripwires caught the change and were updated rather than
loosened.** `test_the_artifact_table_revokes_at_its_own_creation` pinned the old
`trainer.ARTIFACT_REVOKE` symbol; it now asserts the same invariant — revoke
after the CREATE, before the first write — through the helper. And
`test_ddl_guard`'s job_queue fixture had `relrowsecurity=False` as its
"already current" state, which is no longer true.

`test_ddl_guard` also learned that `lock_down(` is a guarded path, rather than
each caller being exempted: an exemption pre-approves whatever that file later
becomes, and a test that rejects the gated helper pushes the next caller towards
the ungated one. That new escape hatch has its own guard-the-guard case —
`test_the_lock_down_helper_is_itself_guarded` — because if `lock_down` ever stops
calling `schema_is_current`, every caller silently becomes an unguarded DDL site
while the offender test goes on passing.

**Nine new guards, every one watched to fail.** Suite **2,306 passed, 2
skipped**.

**Applied on the Railway worker** (deployment `cfd31cde`, SUCCESS) — each table
logged `2 lock-down statement(s)`, i.e. the catalog gate correctly said "needed",
and all read-backs passed or the transaction would have rolled back. Verified in
`pg_class` afterwards: **RLS on, `FORCE` off, 0 policies, anon and authenticated
hold nothing** on all three. `get_advisors(security)` confirms the two
ERROR-level `rls_disabled_in_public` lints for `worker_jobs` and
`odds_history_pulls` are **gone**; all three now report `rls_enabled_no_policy` at
INFO, which is the locked shape `docs/followups.md` documents as expected. The
app's read surface is untouched — all 33 anon-readable relations still readable.

**AND THE WORKER WAS MEASURED, NOT ARGUED.** The structural argument was sound —
owner plus `rolbypassrls`, exempt twice over — and it was still an argument. It
cannot be tested from the sandbox or the Supabase MCP: that connection is
`supabase_read_only_user` and `SET LOCAL ROLE postgres` fails with *permission
denied to set role*. So `scripts/verify_worker_rls.py` ran on the worker over the
same `DATABASE_URL` (deployment `d6de1a35`):

```
connected as 'postgres', rolbypassrls=True
model_artifacts:      rls=True owner=postgres rows_visible=4
odds_history_pulls:   rls=True owner=postgres rows_visible=1042
worker_jobs:          rls=True owner=postgres rows_visible=15
worker_jobs write probe: insert/read-back/update/delete all succeeded (read back 1)
rolled back
SUCCESS — the worker reads and writes all 3 RLS-enabled worker-only tables
```

**The read half is the subtle one, and it is why the row counts are in the log.**
A non-exempt role hitting an RLS table with no policies does not get an error — it
gets **zero rows**. So the check that proves exemption is a `SELECT` returning the
count already known to be there; an exception would only have proved a missing
GRANT. The counts match exactly. The write probe checks the read-back too, because
under RLS an INSERT can succeed while the row stays invisible. Everything rolled
back in a `finally`.

**Scoped to the three asked for, and the other seven named.** Ten `public` base
tables have RLS off; the rest are `brand_assets` plus the six
`*_pre_rebuild_20260903` / `odds_pre_first_pitch_relabel_20260903` backups from
this week's repairs. None is reachable by anon or authenticated today. Left alone
deliberately — widening a three-table ask to ten without asking is the same
mistake in the other direction. In the Decisions section of the reply.

---

**Session summary (2026-09-04, session 210 — the PUBLIC grant swept off every declared callable, and why the revoke has to come before the grant):**

- mike: *"sweep the PUBLIC grant off the 19 stats rpcs."*

Session 208 found the trap and fixed it for the two functions it was revoking
(`feedback_reply`, `has_active_subscription`): `REVOKE ... FROM anon,
authenticated` silently removes nothing while `=X/postgres` — PUBLIC — sits in
the ACL. It did **not** apply the other half. The 25 functions the app *does*
call were granted `EXECUTE` to `anon, authenticated` on top of a PUBLIC grant
that was still there, so the explicit grant was decoration: **anyone holding the
anon key, and anyone holding no role at all, could call them either way.**

**20 of the 25 declared callables carried it** — the 15 sport stat variants,
`fighter_window_totals_ufc`, `team_stats_board`, and both `custom_model_*`. The
five `feedback_*` functions were created by a migration that already named
PUBLIC.

**Implemented as a rule over the manifest, not a one-off script**, because §1b's
lesson from `model_artifacts` this week is that a one-off revoke does not hold.
In `scripts/apply_anon_grants.py`, per name in `RPC_ANON_CALLABLE`:

```python
stmts.append(f"REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION {one} FROM PUBLIC")
stmts.append(f"GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION {one} TO anon, authenticated")
```

**The order is load-bearing and is pinned by a test.** `REVOKE ALL FROM PUBLIC`
strips nothing from `anon`'s own explicit grant — but if the two ever swapped,
the revoke would land *after* the grant and take EXECUTE from PUBLIC while the
named grants stood, which reads identically in the log and is only wrong if a
role was relying on PUBLIC. `test_public_is_stripped_from_every_declared_callable_before_it_is_granted`
asserts the revoke's index is below the grant's, and was watched to fail with
the two lines swapped.

**A third in-transaction read-back added**, so the whole apply rolls back if
PUBLIC still holds anything on a declared callable — alongside the two from
session 208 (anon can read every declared relation; anon can call every declared
RPC). All three ran green before the commit.

**Verified in production after the apply, by querying `pg_proc` rather than
trusting the exit code** (`has_function_privilege` for the truth, `proacl::text`
for the shape — my first `'(^|,)=X/'` regex on the ACL read all 20 as *false*
because it misses the leading `{`, which is exactly the kind of false clean bill
of health §7 is about):

| | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| public functions total | 28 | 28 |
| callable by PUBLIC | 21 | **1** |
| callable by anon | 27 | **25** |
| `feedback_reply`, `has_active_subscription` | anon-callable | `{postgres=X/postgres,service_role=X/postgres}` |

The remaining one is **`log_picks_changes()`**, in `RPC_LEFT_ALONE` for the
reason session 208 recorded: it returns `trigger`, PostgREST will not expose it,
Postgres does not check EXECUTE when a trigger fires, and it is what makes
`picks_log` deletes recoverable. Revoking gains nothing.

Suite **2,268 passed, 2 skipped**. Applied on the Railway worker via
`prop-probe` (deployment `35cbf220`, SUCCESS: 35 table statements + 53 function
statements = 1 `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` + 25×2 + 2); `prop-probe` restored to
`bash nfl/scripts/live_prop_job.sh run`.

**Not done, and still open:** `my_access()` does not exist in production (session
208's find) — the app's `fetchAccess()` throws on it, and the fix is either
applying the never-run `add_discord_link_and_whop_memberships.sql` or changing
the app. Not a grant change, and not mine to pick. `docs/followups.md` carries
it, and so does the Decisions section of the reply.

---

**Session summary (2026-09-04, session 208 — the function grants, and the grep that would have broken four sports' stats screens):**

- mike: *"do the function grants too."*

`ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES IN SCHEMA public REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTIONS FROM anon,
authenticated`, plus explicit `GRANT EXECUTE` on the 25 the app needs and a
revoke on the 2 it does not. All **28** production functions accounted for.

**THE FINDING THAT MATTERS: THE LITERAL GREP IS WRONG, AND WRONG IN THE
DANGEROUS DIRECTION.** A grep for `.rpc('...')` in `mobile/src` returns 17
names. Four call sites in `queries.ts` build the name at runtime:

```ts
const fn = sport === 'NFL' ? 'player_window_totals_nfl'
                           : 'player_window_totals_ncaaf';
await supabase.rpc(fn, {...})
```

The real surface is **24**. Sweeping on the literal list would have revoked
EXECUTE on eight functions the app reaches through a ternary — the **NBA, WNBA,
NCAAF and NFL stats screens** — silently. `tests/test_anon_readable.py` resolves
both forms, and carries a **guard-the-guard** case pinned to three
ternary-only names, because a resolver that stops resolving fails OPEN: the
surface looks smaller and the next sweep gets bolder.

**Three more things measured rather than assumed, each of which would have broken
something:**

| checked | result |
|---|---|
| RLS policy expressions | only `auth.role()` / `auth.uid()` — `auth` schema, out of scope of a `public` revoke |
| column defaults on tables anon writes | only `now()` and `nextval()` — **which is why sequences stay**: `tracked_bets.id` needs anon's USAGE/UPDATE on its sequence to INSERT |
| `public` functions inside the 14 app views | **none**, so no `security_invoker` view read can break |
| helpers called by SECURITY INVOKER RPCs | `custom_model_picks` and `custom_model_backtest` both call **`_jsonb_text_array`**, so the CALLER needs EXECUTE on it even though the app never names it |

**`log_picks_changes` left alone deliberately.** It returns `trigger`, so
PostgREST will not expose it and Postgres does not check EXECUTE when a trigger
fires. Revoking gains nothing and risks the `picks_log` audit trail that made
this week's deletes recoverable — an asymmetric bet with no upside.

**A live bug found on the way, not fixed:** the app calls
`supabase.rpc('my_access')` and `fetchAccess()` does `if (error) throw error`,
but **`my_access()` does not exist in production**. It and `has_app_access()` are
defined only in `data/migrations/add_discord_link_and_whop_memberships.sql`,
which has never been applied; the superseded `has_active_subscription()` is what
actually exists. CLAUDE.md §6 describes the intended design, not the deployed
one. Left alone — applying a never-run migration that creates billing-adjacent
tables is not a side effect of a grant change. `docs/followups.md` carries it.

Eleven guards in the file now, five new ones watched to fail. Suite 2,261 →
**2,266 passed, 2 skipped**. The fifth warning is an xgboost serialized-model
notice from master's newly committed `.pkl`, not from this change — checked.

**AND THEN THE FIRST APPLY WAS WRONG, WHICH THE VERIFICATION CAUGHT.** The run
reported success, the default privilege was correctly revoked — and
`has_active_subscription` was **still callable by anon**. Its ACL carried
`=X/postgres`: an empty grantee is **PUBLIC**, Postgres grants EXECUTE to PUBLIC
on functions by default, and anon is a member of PUBLIC. So
`REVOKE ALL ... FROM anon, authenticated` removed nothing and said so cheerfully.
**21 of 28 public functions carry that PUBLIC grant.**

`data/migrations/add_discord_link_and_whop_memberships.sql` already spelled it
`FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated` — I read that line while enumerating the
surface and did not carry it across. The revoke now names PUBLIC, and the applier
**reads the revoke back inside the transaction** and rolls back if anon can still
call what was supposed to be revoked, because "the statement ran" is not the same
claim as "the privilege is gone". Twelfth guard, watched to fail.

Only one function was actually affected — `has_active_subscription`, a superseded
STABLE gate. The other 20 with a PUBLIC grant are ones the app must call anyway,
so the explicit named grants there are belt-and-braces rather than load-bearing.

**RE-APPLIED and verified.** `has_active_subscription`'s ACL is now
`{postgres=X, service_role=X}` and anon cannot call it; `feedback_reply` stays
uncallable; **all 25 declared callables still work**, including the three
ternary-only ones and the `_jsonb_text_array` helper; the trigger function is
untouched. The function default is `{postgres=X, service_role=X}` and the
sequence default is deliberately unchanged.

**THEN THE PUBLIC GRANT SWEPT OFF THE CALLABLES TOO** (mike: *"sweep the PUBLIC
grant off the 19 stats rpcs"*). Postgres grants function EXECUTE to PUBLIC by
default, so a function with an explicit `anon=X` was usually **also** callable by
every other role — including any role added later. The applier now emits
`REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION … FROM PUBLIC` **before** the named grant for every
declared callable, so the end state is *PUBLIC holds nothing, anon and
authenticated hold exactly EXECUTE*.

**Order is load-bearing** and has its own guard: revoke after grant would strip
the grant it just made. Both mutations watched to fail — the strip removed, and
the strip moved after the grant.

Safe because it was checked first, not after: all **20** functions carrying the
PUBLIC grant already held explicit `anon=X` **and** `authenticated=X`, so PUBLIC
was never the only source for any of them. `log_picks_changes` keeps its PUBLIC
grant — it returns `trigger`, is not API-exposed, and is the `picks_log` audit
path.

A second read-back was added alongside the first: the transaction refuses to
commit if PUBLIC still holds EXECUTE on any declared callable. Same reason as
before — a revoke that did not bite reports success.

**One correction to my own reading on the way:** the pre-flight query printed
`public_has_execute = false` for all 20 because my regex `'(^|,)=X/'` misses the
leading `{` in `{=X/postgres,…}`. The `WHERE has_function_privilege('public', …)`
clause was the truth and had already selected them. The wrong column nearly
became the wrong conclusion.

**Session summary (2026-09-03, session 210 — a live NCAAF total was bet 0.6s after a touchdown, against the book's pre-touchdown price):**

- Matt: *"a live Wake Forest pick fired. What was the delay? It said 44.5 on
  the live over and I went to DraftKings immediately and it said 50.5."*
- **The measured timeline** (Akron @ Wake Forest, `ncaaf_live_total`, pick_id
  1573940; every row from `odds`, `picks_log`, `push_sent`, ESPN drive
  wallclock and the `pollers` deploy log):

  | ET | Event |
  |---|---|
  | 19:50:35 | DK publishes live total 45.5 |
  | 19:51:42 | DK publishes **44.5**, Over -105 — the price we priced on |
  | 19:51:51 | ingested (+9s, Odds API round trip) |
  | ~19:52:2x | **Wake Forest touchdown** (ESPN drive wallclock) |
  | 19:52:43.6 | loop SEES the score, collapses odds cadence to the 3s floor |
  | 19:52:44.2 | pick written: Over 44.5 @ -105, edge +15.77%, BET |
  | 19:52:44.8 / 19:52:45.1 | push / Discord (0.58s / 0.87s — delivery was never the problem) |
  | 19:53:21 | DK re-hangs at **50.5** |

- **The score trigger caused the pick.** `POLL_ODDS_TRIGGER_SEC=3` exists to
  pull odds the instant a score lands, on the reasoning that "the cached price
  is most wrong" then. That is true, and it is the reason NOT to bet it: the
  loop pulled odds 0.6s after seeing the touchdown, got DraftKings' payload
  still stamped 23:51:42, and priced the NEW score against the OLD line.
- **Both existing guards passed it, and neither was wrong.** The quote was
  62.2s old against `LIVE_QUOTE_MAX_AGE_SEC` (90) and the edge 0.1577 against
  `MAX_EDGE_CAP` (0.18). Both are CLOCK-relative; no bound on a quote's age or
  an edge's size distinguishes a quiet 62-second-old price from a 62-second-old
  price with a touchdown in the middle of it.
- **The fix: an EVENT-relative third guard.** `data/live_quote_guard.py`
  (sport-agnostic, stdlib-only, the `live_price_log.py` shape) — a `ScoreClock`
  per game, and `quote_predates_score`, which declines any market the book
  stamped before the moment our state feed showed the new score. Self-clearing:
  the block lasts exactly until the book republishes, so a real post-score
  price is never held back. Tolerance knob
  `NCAAF_LIVE_SCORE_LAG_TOLERANCE_SEC` defaults to **0**.
- **MLB wired too** (mike, after the first pass shipped). It reads the score
  change out of `live_game_state` rather than keeping a `ScoreClock`:
  `run_live_scorer` is invoked fresh per trigger, so an in-memory clock would
  report first sight forever — a guard dead code can satisfy. The SQL was
  validated against real history before it shipped: 5/5 exact matches on games
  up to 1,501 state rows, and a game whose score never moved correctly returns
  None. **Measured cost across all 127 live MLB BETs:** the guard declines 18
  (14.2%), but 10 are already dropped by the 30s age bound, so the MARGINAL
  effect is **8 of 127 (6.3%), which went 4–4**. Worth recording what this does
  NOT show: those 18 split 6 Over / 5 Under and the Unders went 4–1, so unlike
  the NCAAF case they are not hindsight bets on a run that already landed. The
  MLB argument is fillability, not outcome.
- **Wired into NCAAF and NFL** (CLAUDE.md §1b — same touchdown physics, NFL
  kicks off in a week; `Executor.evaluate` already holds both `state` and
  `quote`, so NFL wires itself with no live-loop changes — verified that
  `GamedayWorker` builds ONE `Executor` at startup, `workers/gameday.py:149`,
  so the per-game `ScoreClock` actually persists across passes rather than
  resetting to first sight every tick). Both sports stamp the score with OUR
  clock, not the feed's `state.ts`, which would narrow the blocked window.
  **MLB is left alone and correctly scoped:** `models/live_scorer.py` already
  has the age bound (`LIVE_ODDS_MAX_AGE_SEC`, 30s) and the edge cap
  (`LIVE_MAX_EDGE_CAP`, 0.2) — it is missing only the score guard.
- **The sweep behind that scoping.** All 139 true live BETs, next DK publish
  after each pick: ~50 `mlb_live_total_runs` BETs moved **0.0** every time — a
  run moves a baseball total 0.5-1, a touchdown moves a football total ~6. Of
  9 NCAAF matches, 2 pre-date in-play logging entirely (it shipped 2026-08-29
  17:00 ET; their -11.0 and -7.0 deltas are artifacts of 2h and 40min gaps),
  leaving 7 real ones, of which this is the only ≥3pt adverse jump.
- **Verified:** both new tests watched FAILING with the guard disabled (§1b —
  a test that passes without the fix is not a test); 28 pass in
  `tests/test_live_quote_staleness.py`, 25 in
  `nfl/live_model/tests/test_executor.py`; full suite 2409 passed / 3 failed,
  an error-set **identical to `origin/master`** run in a detached worktree
  (`test_refresh_pass_parallel`, two `test_system_health_checks` — all
  pre-existing, none touched here).
- **The guard lives under `data/`, not `models/`, and the repo's own tests are
  why.** The first version imported `models.live_quote_guard` at module level
  in the NFL executor. That passes under pytest — repo root is already on
  sys.path — and would have raised `ModuleNotFoundError` on every scheduled
  run, because the NFL worker runs with **cwd=nfl/**, where `nfl/models/` is
  the only `models` on the path. Two existing tests caught it
  (`test_no_nfl_script_uses_the_bare_models_import`,
  `test_nfl_hook_resolves_from_the_workers_cwd`) — the lane would have
  degraded silently to the age bound it was being given to fix. Now at
  `data/live_quote_guard.py`, reached from NFL by the same lazy repo-root
  bootstrap `workers/gameday.py` already uses for the price log, degrading with
  a logged warning if the platform is absent so `nfl/` stays self-contained. A
  new test runs the real import from the real cwd.
- **Not done:** the pick itself stands (§1c — a pick is a pick); nothing was
  deleted or re-priced. Whether a pick would have fired at DK's corrected 50.5
  cannot be read from the record, because the lane locks at first BET.

**Session summary (2026-09-03, session 209 — the Models tab and the Record tab priced different picks; UFC Total Rounds was +13.0% on one and -26.6% on the other):**

- Matt, with two screenshots: *"These UFC numbers don't match."* Models tab:
  Fight Winner 2-1 +19.1%, Total Rounds 8-5 **+13.0% (+$168.63)**, Method
  3-2 **+14.5% (+$72.73)**. Record tab, same models, same W-L: Fight Winner
  +19.1%, Total Rounds **-26.6%**, Method **0.0%**, sport headline 13-8 -12.9%.
- **Same picks, two money rules.** Both tabs count the same 21 settled UFC
  BETs at the current cuts. The Record tab reads `v_public_track_record`,
  whose `other` branch (UFC/NHL/NBA/golf/NFL/NCAAF) has, since
  `require_price_for_published_units` (2026-08-31), kept every pick in the W-L
  but summed `profit_flat` and stake over **priced** picks only — an unpriced
  pick is settled at a -110 that never existed. The Models tab computed its
  row on the device in `computeBuiltInModelStats`, which summed `profit_flat`
  and staked $100 on **every** pick, fabricated -110 included. 6 of the 13
  Total Rounds picks and all 5 Method picks have `dk_odds NULL`; those 11 rows
  are 8-3 at +$90.91 a win, which is the whole gap: rounds is 3-4 -$185.92 on
  the $700 that was actually priceable, and +$354.55 of invented money on the
  six that were not. Fight Winner is fully priced, so it agreed.
- **Fixed by one rule in one place.** `flatPnl(pick)` in
  `mobile/src/lib/thresholds.ts`: no price → $0 profit, $0 stake, W-L kept.
  Every client tally now goes through it — `computeBuiltInModelStats` (Models
  tab rows and the built-in detail header for sports the full-outcome view
  does not grade), `computeCustomModelStats` (the settled-pick fallback half
  of a custom model's backtest, which now matches the server half's
  `profit_units NULL` convention so `mergeStats` sums like with like), and
  `dailyResults.tally` (the "Yesterday's results" recap). Discord's recap
  already did this (`record_only`), so after this every surface prices the
  same picks.
- **Both rows now say why.** "13 picks · 8-5 · 6 unpriced" on the Models tab
  and "8–5 · 13 decided · 6 unpriced" on the Record tab, only when the count
  is non-zero — a winning record beside a negative ROI should explain itself
  on the row, not in a support thread.
- **Verified by watching it fail.** `mobile/scripts/verify_unpriced_pnl.ts`
  carries the real 21-row UFC settled set (values copied from `picks`) and
  asserts the Record tab's numbers out of the three tallies. Before the wiring
  it reproduced the screenshot to the cent (+$168.63 / $1300, +$72.73 / $500,
  daily recap $162.34 / $200); after it, all 20 checks pass. The two compute
  functions moved from `hooks/useCustomModelStats.ts` into the pure
  `lib/customModelBacktest.ts` (re-exported from the hook) because importing
  the hook under tsx drags react-native in; that is the only reason for the
  move. `verify_daily_results`, `verify_retired_models`,
  `verify_custom_model_filters`, `verify_tracked_performance` and
