**Session summary (2026-09-03, session 207 — the default privileges revoked, and the trap that creates given its own tripwire):**

- mike: *"do the alter default privileges too."*

**Why the sweep alone was not enough.** Revoking the grant on 51 existing tables
fixed the present, not the future: Supabase's default privileges hand
`anon=arwdDxtm` to every NEW table in `public`, so the next one arrives open.
Not hypothetical — `model_artifacts` is created on demand by
`trainer.py::_store_artifact` and came back with the full grant between two
sweeps of the schema hours apart, the same day.

`ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES IN SCHEMA public REVOKE ALL ON TABLES FROM anon,
authenticated`.

**IT CREATES A TRAP, AND THE TRAP IS THE PART WORTH THE CODE.** A new table or
view the app needs now arrives with **no SELECT either**, and that fails
*silently* — PostgREST answers a permission error, the client folds it into
`error`, and the screen renders empty with nothing in any log near the cause.
That is the exact failure shape §7 keeps warning about, and shipping the revoke
without a guard would trade a security hole for a reliability one.

So the read surface is **declared, not inherited**: `data/anon_readable.py` lists
the 33 relations anon may read plus `subscriptions` as authenticated-only, and
`tests/test_anon_readable.py` parses every `.from('...')` in `mobile/src` and
fails if one names something the manifest does not. "Somebody forgot the GRANT"
is now a red test before the PR opens. Asserted in **both** directions — an entry
nothing reads is also a failure, so the list cannot silently accrete.

The manifest matched the app exactly on the first run: **34 relations, none
missing, none stale**. All 34 were already readable, so the grants are a no-op on
today's schema; the mechanism is for what comes next.

**Two limits, measured and stated rather than discovered later.**
`ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` is **per grantor role**, and `public` tables carry TWO
entries handing anon `arwdDxtm` — one from `postgres`, one from `supabase_admin`.
This changes the `postgres` one, which is the role everything here creates tables
as (worker and pollers over `DATABASE_URL`, and the Supabase SQL editor). The
`supabase_admin` entry needs membership in that role, which `postgres` does not
have on a managed project — latent, not live, since nothing in this repo creates
tables as it.

**Sequences and functions deliberately left alone.** The same defaults grant anon
`rwU` on every new sequence and `EXECUTE` on every new function. The function
half matters — it is how five `SECURITY DEFINER` `feedback_*` RPCs became
anon-callable — but revoking it means every new RPC needs an explicit
`GRANT EXECUTE` or PostgREST 404s it: the same silent-failure surface, needing the
same declared-list-plus-test treatment. `docs/followups.md` carries the shape of
that fix rather than a vague note.

A script rather than a `.sql` file, because the read surface is a Python list a
test cross-checks against `mobile/src`; duplicating it into SQL would let the two
drift, which is the failure this exists to prevent. Same reason
`data.threshold_sync` generates its SQL from `config.py`.

Six guards, five watched to fail. Suite 2,255 → **2,261 passed, 2 skipped**.

**APPLIED and verified.** The `postgres` grantor's table default ACL now reads
`{postgres=arwdDxtm, service_role=arwdDxtm}` — **anon and authenticated are
gone**, so the next table created in `public` arrives closed. Read surface
intact: all **33** declared relations still readable by anon, `subscriptions`
still authenticated-only, and still exactly **4** tables writable by
anon/authenticated. Unchanged and documented rather than discovered: `postgres`
sequences (`anon=rwU`) and functions (`anon=X`), and the whole `supabase_admin`
set.

**Note on numbering:** this is 207, not 206 — another session claimed 206 for the
pitcher-stats rebuild while this work was in flight. Two sessions running
concurrently against one log is now a recurring collision.

---
**Session summary (2026-09-03, session 206 — Phase 2: the pitcher table rebuilt, and the honest number f5 has been waiting three sessions for):**

- mike: *"rebuild the pitcher stats."*
- **The table could not be re-aggregated in place.** `mlb_pitcher_stats` has
  `innings_pitched`, `earned_runs`, `strikeouts` and `walks` columns, and they
  are NULL on every historical row AND on 2026's. Only the derived rates were
  ever written, so the rebuild had to come from `player_game_log`.
- **The source was validated before it was trusted**, which is the reusable
  move here: a leaked table is still a true record of SOMETHING — the season
  finals — so rebuilding a full season from the candidate source and
  correlating against it tests the source with no independent oracle needed.
  `player_game_log` scored **0.957 / 0.920 / 0.720** for 2023 / 2024 / 2025,
  degrading exactly in step with its game coverage (87 / 81 / 74%). A faithful
  source with a hole, not a wrong one.
- **`innings_pitched` is baseball notation.** 5.2 is five and TWO THIRDS; only
  .0/.1/.2 occur across all 135,010 rows. Summing it directly inflates every
  ERA by a mean **+0.025**, falling to **+0.002** once converted to outs. My own
  first validation query had this wrong and was redone — the bias is small
  enough to look entirely plausible either way, which is the danger.
- **The team-label question was settled empirically, not with an alias map.**
  `games` and `player_game_log` appear to disagree (CHW/WAS vs CWS/WSH), but
  joining on `game_id` shows `pgl.team` matches one of the game's two teams
  **verbatim on all 30,798 starter rows, zero mismatches**. The apparent label
  bug is really a coverage hole: those games are absent from pgl entirely.
- **`era_last3` deliberately replicates a WEAKER definition.** The daily ingest
  computes `AVG(era)` over the last three stored rows — the mean of three
  season-to-date rates, a smoothed near-duplicate of `era` — not a true rolling
  last-three ERA. My first build used the truer statistic, which would have
  measured a system nobody deployed and silently redefined ~21% of f5's
  importance. That is a model update under §1b, so it was queued as a decision
  instead. The old table was consistent in the same way for the wrong reason:
  `era_last3` WAS the season-final constant, so it equalled `era` exactly.
- **Result: 27,278 rows, 2019-2025, 972 pitchers.** Zero constant-ERA
  pitcher-seasons remain in any season, against essentially all of them before.
  2026 refused by the script's own guard and verified untouched.
- **All four MLB models re-measured, and the verdict splits three ways.** The
  leaked seasons collapsed to the honest one's level in every case:
  `mlb_f5_moneyline` 0.63-0.64 → a 0.534-0.582 band, mean **0.558**;
  `mlb_moneyline` converging on **0.559** from the other direction, its one
  honest season RISING 0.529 → 0.559; `mlb_over_under` revealed as having **no
  signal at all** (mean 0.507, 2026 at **0.486**, below chance, zero of six
  folds clearing 0.55); `mlb_runline` **unstable rather than good** — mean 0.555
  hiding a 0.460-0.618 swing with its base rate moving 0.364 → 0.495.
- **The obvious objection was measured and rejected.** Games where both starters
  carry a non-null ERA fell only **4.2%** (16,828 → 16,120), and 2019 and 2020
  GAINED coverage because the old table had holes of its own. A 4% change in
  rows does not move AUC by 0.08. The rows that did leave are disproportionately
  early-season starts — exactly where a season-final ERA on a first start was
  purest leak.
- **Two process failures worth recording.** `walk_forward_eval` defaults to
  `config.SPORTS['MLB']['train_seasons']`, which stops at 2024, so my first run
  never reached the only honest season — a default that quietly answers a
  different question. And `TaskStop` left an orphaned python process contending
  on the database for ~20 minutes before I noticed it in the process list.
- **18 invariant tests, 10 mutations, all caught.** One initially slipped
  through: the last-3 fixture had too few starts for the window slice to matter,
  so the sliced and unsliced means coincided. Extended to six starts until it
  bit — a test that passes against its own mutation is not a test.
- **Deliberately NOT done:** no retrain. Production still serves f5's
  leak-trained `.pkl` and its registry `holdout_accuracy 0.6415` is fiction, but
  retrain-vs-pause is one decision for mike under the standing freeze and the
  `Updated-By` rule. Three follow-ups queued instead: the pgl StatsAPI backfill,
  the ingest's last-3 keying on `player_name` (names collide — two Nolas), and
  the true-rolling-last-3 change as a `[needs-decision]` model update.
- PR #436. Suite 2,276 passed, 1 failed (`test_refresh_pass_parallel`,
  pre-existing — confirmed on an `origin/master` worktree).
**Session summary (2026-09-03, session 205 — the anon grant on `odds` is inert; the one on `worker_jobs` is not, and the doc said the opposite):**

- mike: *"fix the anon grant on odds."*

**What the `odds` grant actually is, stated more carefully than I stated it when
I flagged it.** `anon=arwdDxtm` is the Supabase default and `odds` is not an
outlier: **57 of 80 public tables** give anon INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE. On `odds`
RLS is enabled and **both policies are SELECT-only**, so a write is denied by
RLS whatever the grant says. Calling it "the full default grant on the table the
whole system prices from" overstated it — it is a second lock that was missing,
not an open door.

**But the sweep for its blast radius found a real one.** 9 of 80 tables have RLS
**off**, and two of them carry the full anon grant:

| table | RLS | anon | consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `worker_jobs` | **off** | `arwdDxtm` | anon could INSERT into the queue the Railway worker **claims and executes every five minutes** |
| `odds_history_pulls` | **off** | `arwdDxtm` | anon could write the credit-spend ledger |

**`docs/followups.md` recorded both as "not currently an open door."** That was a
false negative, and the mechanism is worth keeping:
`information_schema.role_table_grants` only shows grants the CURRENT role can
see. It returns **0 rows for `odds` too** — a table whose `relacl` demonstrably
reads `anon=arwdDxtm`. The earlier session read 0 rows as "no privileges" when
it meant "you cannot see them". **`pg_class.relacl` and `has_table_privilege()`
are the authoritative reads.** §7's "read the result, not the intent", in its
other form: a null result taken as evidence of absence.

- **`data/migrations/tighten_anon_write_grants.sql`.** `odds` loses
  INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE/REFERENCES/TRIGGER and **keeps SELECT** — the app
  reads it directly (`fetchOddsHistory`) and through `v_latest_dk_odds` /
  `v_latest_odds_all_books`, both `security_invoker=on`, so they run as the
  CALLER and need the caller's SELECT. `worker_jobs` and `odds_history_pulls`
  lose the whole grant; neither has any `mobile/src` reference.

- **RLS deliberately NOT enabled on those two in the same migration.** RLS with
  no policy locks out every connection that is not the table owner, and the
  worker's role was not verified to be that owner. The revoke closes the hole on
  its own; adding the second lock is not worth gambling the job queue blind.
  Left in `docs/followups.md` as the remaining half.

- **The four tables the app genuinely writes** — `device_push_tokens`,
  `feedback`, `tracked_bets`, `game_weather` — are the only ones carrying a
  permissive write policy, and a test asserts the migration never touches them.
  Six guards, all watched to fail: revoking ALL on `odds` (which takes SELECT
  with it), dropping the `worker_jobs` revoke, revoking `FROM PUBLIC` instead of
  the named roles, widening the sweep to `tracked_bets`, and enabling RLS blind.

- **APPLIED, and verified.** `odds` now reads `anon=rm/postgres` — SELECT and
  MAINTAIN only, writes gone, and `anon` SELECT still true so the app keeps
  working. `worker_jobs` and `odds_history_pulls` read
  `{postgres, service_role}`. `tracked_bets`, `feedback` and
  `device_push_tokens` untouched. The Supabase security advisor no longer lists
  either table under `rls_disabled_in_public` — with no anon grant they are out
  of the PostgREST surface entirely.

- **AND THE ADVISOR SURFACED A THIRD, WORSE ONE THAT MY OWN SWEEP HAD MISSED:
  `model_artifacts`.** RLS off, `anon=arwdDxtm`, and it holds the **`.pkl`
  payloads** the scorer loads when the file is missing from disk
  (`trainer.py::load_model`). A row there IS a model: anon could UPDATE a
  payload and change what every model predicts, or DELETE one and take the
  model offline.

  **It was not in the sweep I ran fifteen minutes earlier.** The table is
  created on demand by `_store_artifact` with `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` and
  no revoke, so it reappeared carrying the full default grant. **That is the
  real lesson here: a one-off revoke does not hold against a table created at
  write time.** The durable fix is `ARTIFACT_REVOKE`, executed immediately
  after the CREATE and before the first INSERT; the migration line only cleans
  up the instance that already existed. A test asserts the ordering
  CREATE → REVOKE → INSERT.

- **BOTH APPLIED AND VERIFIED** on the worker (`run_sql_file`, one transaction,
  "Applied and committed" both times):

  | table | before | after |
  |---|---|---|
  | `odds` | `anon=arwdDxtm` | **`anon=rm`** — SELECT + MAINTAIN; `anon` SELECT still true, so the app works |
  | `worker_jobs` | `anon=arwdDxtm`, RLS off | **`{postgres, service_role}`** |
  | `odds_history_pulls` | `anon=arwdDxtm`, RLS off | **`{postgres, service_role}`** |
  | `model_artifacts` | `anon=arwdDxtm`, RLS off | **`{postgres, service_role}`** |
  | `tracked_bets`, `feedback`, `device_push_tokens` | full grant | **untouched** |

- **THEN SWEPT, on mike's word: "do the remaining 53 tables."** Measured at run
  time it is **51**, not 53 — the earlier figure was arithmetic over a snapshot
  that had already moved (`model_artifacts` appeared and was revoked in between,
  and the team-stats rebuild added tables). The count is not hard-coded, for
  exactly that reason.

  **The rule, not a list:** revoke the write privileges from anon and
  authenticated on every `public` table EXCEPT those carrying a **permissive
  write policy** for those roles. A write policy is the only evidence anyone
  meant to allow a write from the app; the rest is Supabase's default handed out
  by default privileges and never asked for. Written as a re-runnable `DO` block
  that `NOTICE`s each table, because a hard-coded list goes stale and the rule
  does not — `model_artifacts` proved that the same day.

  **Every client was checked BEFORE writing it, not after:**

  | client | credential | write surface |
  |---|---|---|
  | mobile app | anon / authenticated | **`tracked_bets` and `device_push_tokens`. That is all** — two call sites in `mobile/src`. Both keep their grant. |
  | its RPCs | anon | every `SECURITY INVOKER` one is **STABLE** (Postgres forbids writes there); every VOLATILE one (the five `feedback_*`) is **SECURITY DEFINER**, so it runs as owner and needs no caller grant |
  | 8 edge functions, incl. all billing | `SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` | `service_role` keeps `arwdDxtm` and is never named. `discord-link` also holds the anon key but only for `auth.getUser()` — no table access. |
  | worker, scheduler, dashboard, Retool | `postgres` over `DATABASE_URL` | owner, unaffected |

  So there is no client path a revoke here can reach. **SELECT is never touched**
  — several of these are read through `security_invoker` views that run as the
  caller, and losing SELECT fails silently: PostgREST returns a permission error
  the client folds into `error` and the screen renders empty.

  The block ends by ASSERTING that `tracked_bets`, `device_push_tokens` and
  `feedback` still hold their write privileges, and raises (rolling the whole
  thing back) if not — so a policy dropped later cannot quietly let the loop
  catch the betslip.

  Six guards, five watched to fail, including one for a trap specific to this
  file: `run_sql_file` strips a leading `BEGIN;` and trailing `COMMIT;` by
  regex, and the `DO` block has its own `BEGIN`/`END` inside dollar quotes. The
  test pins that the stripping leaves it balanced and that `format()`'s `%I`
  survives (psycopg2 does no interpolation when `execute` gets no params).

  **APPLIED and verified.** Of **81** public tables, exactly **4** are still
  writable by anon or authenticated — `device_push_tokens`, `feedback`,
  `game_weather`, `tracked_bets`, i.e. precisely the four carrying a write
  policy. Nothing else. The app's own surface intact: betslip INSERT and DELETE,
  push-token INSERT, and reads on `odds` and `picks` all still true. **59 tables
  still readable by anon**, so no read was collateral.

  **Chased one flag rather than waving it through:** `anon` cannot SELECT
  `subscriptions`. It never could — `relacl` shows no `anon` entry at all, where
  `odds` and `picks` read `anon=rm`. Its grant had been write-ONLY, so revoking
  the writes emptied the entry and Postgres dropped it. `authenticated=rm`
  survives, which is the role the app reads billing under. Not a regression, and
  it is the "1 of the 51 anon cannot even read" seen in the pre-flight count.

  **Zero tables** now have RLS off while still reachable by a PostgREST role
  (the shape the advisor reports as `rls_disabled_in_public`); it was three
  earlier today.

- **Also noticed, not acted on:** `REVOKE ALL` leaves `m` (MAINTAIN) on `odds`
  for anon — `VACUUM`/`ANALYZE`/`REINDEX` rights, not a data write. And the
  advisor still reports `mv_scored_pick_outcomes` selectable by anon (a matview
  has no RLS at all), plus five `SECURITY DEFINER` feedback RPCs callable by
  anon, which is how in-app feedback is meant to work.

---
**Session summary (2026-09-03, session 204 — the pre-game bound moves off the schedule, and the derivation it moves onto is wrong for 7 games):**

- mike: *"close the first_pitch_at gap, my call."* The gap flagged when session
  203's branch merged: master's `pregame_cutoff_sql()` bounds on
  `COALESCE(first_pitch_at, commence_time)` because the scheduled start runs
  late, but the prop price bound shipped that day still used `commence_time`
  alone.

**How big the gap was, measured rather than assumed.** On the 30 most recent
MLB games carrying a `first_pitch_at`, **1,926 of 3,919 player+market keys
(49%)** had their "pre-game" DK price taken from inside the window between
actual first pitch and scheduled start. 1,855 of them (96%) still have a real
pre-game quote once the bound tightens; **71 lose their price entirely** and
simply do not fire, which is the right outcome for a prop DK never quoted
before the game. 450 get a different price, 18 a different line.

**`models/scorer.py`.** `_pregame_cutoff_map` is new and deliberately separate
from `_commence_time_map`: the latter also supplies the `commence_time`
**stamped** on each pick, which must stay the scheduled start because it is
what the app shows and what the board sorts by. All five prop lanes now bound
the price read AND the started-game guard on the cutoff, and keep the schedule
for the stamp. NFL needed its own map (`_nfl_pregame_cutoff_map`) because it
reads its slate from `nfl_team_game_stats`, not `games`.

**THEN THE DERIVATION TURNED OUT TO BE WRONG, and only because the numbers
were looked at before shipping.** The mean offset of −18.7 minutes is a
summary of two different populations:

```
p05 .. p95      -16.2 .. -10.0 min     the real, systematic offset
continuous to   -36.0 min
        GAP     (35 minutes, nothing at all)
then            -71.0, -340.0, -349.8, -375.0, -375.9 x2, -386.0
```

**7 of 415 games** carry a `first_pitch_at` hours before their scheduled start
— six of them ~6 hours, which is the signature of a day-night doubleheader
whose second game was matched to the FIRST game's live state. Preferring one of
those does not tighten the bound; it throws away an afternoon of genuinely
pre-game prices.

**And it already had.** `relabel_in_play` bounds on the same expression, so it
has stamped **6,565 game-level `odds` rows across those 7 games as `in_play`**
— 4,322 on `MLB_2026-08-29_ARI_SF` alone, running from six seconds before the
bogus "first pitch" to two seconds before the real start. Every pre-game reader
now skips them.

- **The clamp.** `data.first_pitch.SUSPICIOUS_EARLY_MINUTES = 60`, chosen to sit
  inside the 35-minute gap the data leaves, so it cannot be nudged either way
  and change which games it catches. `trusted_first_pitch()` is its Python twin
  for readers that parse timestamps themselves. **No clamp on the late side** —
  +39 to +114 minutes are rain delays, and a game that truly started late truly
  has more pre-game quotes. After it: 408 games use the real first pitch, 7 fall
  back to the schedule, and the most-early cutoff is −36.0 minutes instead of
  −386.0.

- **Three call sites had hand-copied the helper's output** rather than calling
  it (`market_movement`, `relabel_in_play`, `wnba_prop_market`), so the clamp
  would have reached none of them. All three now call `pregame_cutoff_sql()`,
  which is what "named once so the call sites cannot drift" was supposed to mean.

- **`_is_pregame_snapshot` took `first_pitch_at` as an optional third argument
  and not one of its five callers passed it.** An optional argument nobody
  passes is not a guard, it is dead code that reads like one — §7's "a guard
  that dead code can satisfy is not a guard", in the file that rule protects.
  All four feature engines now pass it. **Measured before wiring: ZERO
  game-level (game, market) keys change**, because the game-level odds feed does
  not snapshot in that window the way the prop feed does. So no feature moves
  and no retrain is implied; it is wired so the bound is already right when live
  state reaches the other sports.

- **`games.first_pitch_at` was only ever an ADD COLUMN**, never in `SCHEMA_SQL`,
  so a database created from the schema alone did not have it — which is how a
  test fixture built from the real schema failed on `no such column`. Added to
  the base table.

- **Seven new guards, each watched to fail** under the exact mutation it exists
  to catch: the price bound reverting to the schedule map, the started-game
  guard reverting, the pick STAMP switching to the cutoff (the half a careless
  fix breaks), the cutoff map hand-rolling its SQL, the NFL map inner-joining,
  the feature-engine callers dropping the third argument, and the clamp being
  disabled. Suite 2,202 → **2,217 passed, 2 skipped**.

- **One fixture was asserting behaviour the data cannot produce.** Master's
  `test_the_actual_start_wins_over_the_schedule` used an 80-minute gap; the real
  p05..p95 is −16.2..−10.0, and 80 is past the new clamp. Retuned to 16 minutes,
  the measured median.

- **DONE, mike: "run the repair and merge to master."** Merged as #437, then
  the dry run on the worker confirmed the set exactly -- 6,565 rows across 7
  games, per-game counts matching the read-only measurement.

- **The dry run also showed the safety net would not have survived.** The JSON
  snapshot lands on ephemeral Railway container disk, and the flip is NOT
  self-reversing: once a row reads `open` the repair's own query no longer
  finds it, and widening to `snapshot_type='open'` would sweep up rows that
  were legitimately open all along. So the backup IS the undo, and it now goes
  to a **table in Supabase**, written and COUNT-VERIFIED inside the same
  transaction as the update (#438). A short copy rolls the whole thing back.
  `REVOKE`d from anon/authenticated by name. Checked rather than assumed:
  `DBConnection` sets `autocommit=False` and `execute()` does not commit, so
  the CREATE TABLE AS, the count, the REVOKE and the UPDATE are one
  transaction. Two of the six new tests were watched to fail -- removing the
  count check, and moving the backup after the update.

- **APPLIED on the worker, and verified in the database rather than from the
  log.** The apply container's own log lines were not retained (only a later
  idempotent re-run survives, reading "No rows carry an in_play label from a
  bogus first pitch"), so the outcome was confirmed by query:

  | check | result |
  |---|---|
  | backup table `odds_pre_first_pitch_relabel_20260903` | **6,565 rows, 7 games, all holding `in_play`** |
  | those rows in `odds` now | **6,565 `open`, 0 not** |
  | still mislabelled on the 7 games | **0** |
  | `in_play` rows before their scheduled start on OTHER games | **33,091, untouched** |
  | backup ACL | `{postgres, service_role}` -- anon/authenticated absent |

  The backup and the update commit together, so a backup holding all 6,565
  rows is itself the evidence the update committed. Undo, if ever needed:
  `UPDATE odds o SET snapshot_type = b.snapshot_type FROM
  odds_pre_first_pitch_relabel_20260903 b WHERE b.odds_id = o.odds_id;`

- `prop-probe` restored to `master` / `bash nfl/scripts/live_prop_job.sh run` /
  the never-match watch pattern. Worker redeployed on the merge and is running
  its schedule normally.

- **Noticed in passing, NOT changed:** `odds` itself still carries
  `anon=arwdDxtm` -- the full default grant, on the table the whole system
  prices from. The backup table created today does not. That is a separate
  security item, not this session's to fix silently.

**Then mike ruled on all three open decisions in one line** — *"pause over_under,
retrain f5 - updated by mikem yes on era_last3"* (read as `Updated-By: mike`; the
session user directing directly, which is exactly the case §1b's attribution
rule names):

- **`mlb_over_under` PAUSED.** Below a coin flip in the only honest season.
  The config comment records the full fold table and states the unpause path as
  a REBUILT MODEL, not a threshold — no cut rescues a classifier that does not
  rank; moving a bar on a 0.50 AUC only changes how many coin flips get bet.
  Also recorded in the commit: a `config.py` edit does not reach production
  until it is MERGED and `threshold_sync` runs at 6am, and a hand edit to
  `model_action_thresholds` for immediate effect would be REVERTED by that same
  sync if the branch is not on master first (§6).
- **`era_last3` became a true rolling window**, and the point of the change is
  that BOTH SIDES MOVED TOGETHER. New `data/pitcher_rates.py` holds the
  arithmetic and both the daily ingest and the rebuild call it. If they computed
  it differently the model would train on one definition and be served another
  and nothing anywhere would fail — the numbers would just quietly stop meaning
  the same thing. That is §1b's shared-helper rule, applied to the exact failure
  it exists to prevent.
- **2026 needed a surgical update, not a rebuild.** Its rows are the ingest's and
  genuinely as-of-date, so `rebuild` refuses them — but leaving 2026 on the old
  smoothed definition while every training season carried the new one would be a
  train/serve mismatch on 21% of f5's importance. A new `--recompute-last3` mode
  updates ONLY `era_last3`/`k9_last3` in place; verified against the backup as
  3,400 of 3,601 windows changed with `era`, `xfip` and `whip` **byte-identical**.
  It walks the STORED rows rather than the pgl starts, so a probable starter who
  was scratched still gets a window — those rows are still scored.
- **The ingest's name-keying was fixed while it was open**: the window keyed on
  `player_name`, and names collide (two Nolas). It keys on `player_id` now.
- **The better feature did not change the verdict.** f5 re-walked under the true
  rolling definition: mean **0.5566**, 2026 **0.5356** — against 0.5583 / 0.5365
  smoothed. Statistically identical. The measurement was re-run BEFORE the
  retrain precisely so the registry would carry a number matching what is
  deployed, and it was worth knowing that the definition change bought accuracy
  nothing even though it is the more honest statistic.
- **The retrain's two traps, both avoided by passing arguments explicitly:**
  `models.trainer` defaults to `config.SPORTS['MLB']['train_seasons']`, which
  stops at **2024**, and to `test_season` **2025** — so a bare
  `--model mlb_f5_moneyline` would have trained on 2019-2024 and held out 2025,
  ignoring the only honest season entirely. Trained 2019-2025, holdout 2026.
  (The same default had already cost one wasted walk-forward run earlier in the
  session.)
- **§11a added to `docs/sports/mlb.md`** — a banner over the whole model-state
  section saying every AUC in it was measured against leaked tables, with the
  honest walk-forward table beside it. The old v3 F5 row is marked SUPERSEDED
  rather than deleted: it claims AUC 0.691 on a "2024 holdout" that was inside
  its own training seasons, which is worth keeping visible as provenance.

**Session summary (2026-09-03, session 185 — Phase 1 shipped: four team-stats tables rebuilt, and the bigger leak found underneath):**

- mike: *"do phase 1."*
- **Guard tests written FIRST**, as the scope promised, so the rebuild is
  verified by something other than whoever wrote it: no row may claim more
  `games_played` than the team had actually played by its own `as_of_date`, and
  no season may have fewer than 5 distinct snapshots. They failed on a missing
  module before the module existed.
- **`data/team_stats_rebuild.py` rebuilds from `games` alone.** MLB 16,000 games
  → 37,059 rows; NBA 8,284 → 32,698; NHL 8,991 → 38,840; WNBA 1,510 → 6,677.
  All four pass both invariants. Every season now has 44-242 distinct
  `as_of_date` values where it had 1 or 2.
- **Three things nearly went wrong, and each is now a test.**
  1. **The first dry run was scoped to all 18 seasons and would have DELETED
     2026** — the only season with real rate stats. `already_a_series()` now
     refuses it, and the guard's WIRING has its own test, because inverting
     `if protected and not force` left the helper's test passing while the
     rebuild overwrote 2026.
  2. **`runs_home` does not exist** — MLB spells it `runs_per_game_home`. The
     INSERT failed against the real table AFTER the DELETE had run in the same
     transaction, and rolled back only because Postgres aborts the whole
     transaction. `test_every_emitted_column_exists_in_the_real_table` parses
     `db_setup.py` and catches it with no database.
  3. All four tables were backed up to `*_pre_rebuild_20260903` first, row
     counts verified, and REVOKEd from `anon`/`authenticated` (§7).
- **A row dated D contains only games STRICTLY BEFORE D**, since
  `_team_stats_before` selects `as_of_date <= game_date`. Pinned by test.
- **Measured result, mixed, which is the honest answer:** `mlb_moneyline` 2026
  AUC **0.529 → 0.566** (better generalisation on FEWER training rows, since
  2019 now has NULL rate stats and drops out; its leaked-vs-honest gap narrowed
  from 0.084 to 0.033). `mlb_f5_moneyline` **0.560 → 0.562** — unchanged.
- **Why f5 did not move: the bigger leak is in `mlb_pitcher_stats`, carrying 40%
  of that model's feature importance.** `d_starter_era_last3` (0.213) and
  `d_starter_era` (0.186) are its top two features, and every historical row
  holds the pitcher's SEASON-FINAL ERA on every start — Aaron Nola's 33 rows for
  2024 all read 3.57. Seasons 2019-2025 average **1.0** distinct ERA values per
  pitcher-season; 2026 averages **17.2**. `era_last3` is constant too.
- It is exactly reconstructable from `player_game_log` (`innings_pitched`,
  `p_earned_runs`, `p_strikeouts`, `p_walks`, `p_hits_allowed`): era, k9, bb9,
  whip and every last-3 variant are EXACT. Documented as the next rebuild.
- Phase 1 as scoped is done; the scope was incomplete. Suite: 2,252 passed, 1
  failed (pre-existing).

**Session summary (2026-09-03, session 184 — the leak is four sports, and the rebuild is scoped):**

- mike: *"scope the fix. then run the same count(DISTINCT as_of_date) check for
  WNBA, NBA, NHL, NCAAF."* Ran the check first, because its answer changes the
  scope — and it did.
- **Four of five tables are leaked. NCAAF is clean, and is the template.**

  | table | historical seasons | 2026 | verdict |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `mlb_team_stats` | 2 | 141 | leaked |
  | `nba_team_stats` | 1 | 31 | leaked |
  | `nhl_team_stats` | 1 | 143 | leaked |
  | `wnba_team_stats` | 1 | 70 | leaked |
  | `ncaaf_team_stats` | **14-16 weekly, every season** | 14 | **clean** |

- **Verified against `games` rather than assumed**, because "the dates look
  wrong" is not evidence that the CONTENT is wrong. NBA BOS season 2023 stored
  57 wins at `2022-09-01` — before that season began — against an actual 57 that
  season and 51 the season before. WNBA CON 2025: stored 11, actual 11, prior
  28. NHL BOS 2025: stored 33, actual 33. Every row is its own season's final.
- NBA and NHL are stamped in the PRIOR calendar year, which could legitimately
  have meant "previous season's numbers as a pre-season prior". It does not —
  that is precisely why the check was against actual results rather than against
  the dates.
- **The rebuild is scoped in `docs/team_stats_rebuild_scope.md`**, split by what
  is actually recoverable from data already held:
  - **Tier 1, exact, from `games` (87k rows, 2009-2026):** games_played, wins,
    losses, run/point/goal differential, home/away splits. Every sport. This
    alone revives three of the eight dead features and removes the counting-stat
    leak.
  - **Tier 2, MLB rate stats from `player_game_log` (481k rows, 2019-2026):**
    `team_era`, `team_whip`, `iso` exact; `team_fip` near-exact; `ops`,
    `k_pct`, `bb_pct`, `babip` approximate (no HBP or SF columns); **`woba` and
    `wrc_plus` not recoverable at all** — and they rank 4th and 7th by feature
    importance, so that is a real decision, not a footnote.
  - **Not recoverable:** NHL rate stats (`nhl_skater_stats` has **0 rows**),
    historical injuries (`injuries` starts 2026-04-05).
- **A coverage gap found while scoping:** `player_game_log` shows 28 distinct
  MLB teams for most seasons and 26 for 2025, against 30. Team labels are not
  mapping, and any aggregate built before that is fixed would quietly omit
  clubs.
- **Proposed the test that would have caught all of this**, cheap and permanent:
  *no stats row may claim more `games_played` than that team had actually played
  by its own `as_of_date`*. `BOS 2023 @ 2022-09-01, games_played 82` fails it
  instantly. Plus a companion assertion that a season has more than a handful of
  distinct `as_of_date` values. Both belong in the suite BEFORE the rebuild, so
  the rebuild is verified by something other than whoever writes it.
- Phase 0 of the scope is a freeze: no MLB/NBA/NHL/WNBA retrains against the
  current tables. Nothing was changed this session beyond documentation.

**Session summary (2026-09-03, session 183 — the MLB models are trained on the future: `mlb_team_stats` carries season-final numbers under a season-start date):**

- mike: *"do 1 and 2"* — diagnose the f5 2026 break, and run walk-forward across
  the other models. Doing the second answered the first, and the answer is much
  worse than an f5 problem.
- **`mlb_team_stats` has TWO rows per historical season — `YYYY-01-01` and
  `YYYY-10-01` — and both carry the COMPLETED season.** LAD 2024, dated
  `2024-01-01`: `games_played 162`, ops 0.781, wrc_plus 109.0. Every historical
  training game resolves `as_of_date <= game_date` to that January row, so the
  model is told how each team performed across the whole season before
  predicting a game inside it. 2026 is stored as **141 daily snapshots**
  (`games_played` averaging 77) and is the only season featurised honestly.
- **Walk-forward across all four MLB game models, AUC:**

  | model | 2022 | 2023 | 2024 | 2025 | 2026 |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | `mlb_f5_moneyline` | 0.636 | 0.628 | 0.640 | 0.633 | **0.560** |
  | `mlb_moneyline` | 0.613 | 0.605 | 0.605 | 0.615 | **0.529** |
  | `mlb_over_under` | 0.547 | 0.573 | 0.574 | 0.569 | **0.508** |
  | `mlb_runline` | 0.613 | 0.631 | 0.525 | 0.636 | **0.568** |

  **Every model drops in the one honest season.** `mlb_over_under` lands on a
  coin flip. The honest range is ~0.51-0.57, not the 0.60-0.64 the leaked
  seasons advertise.
- **This reverses my last two conclusions in a row, which is the point of having
  run the check.** Yesterday's single 2026 holdout said "f5 has no signal" and I
  nearly recommended retiring it. The walk-forward then said "it worked for four
  seasons and broke in 2026" and I recommended diagnosing the break. The break
  is that 2026 stopped lying.
- **Eight of 25 features are dead as well**, constant zero across every training
  season: `home_win_pct`, `away_win_pct`, `d_run_differential`, both
  `injury_adj`, both `starter_out`, both `has_returnee`. XGBoost cannot split on
  a constant, so they are ignored — the model has no notion of team record at
  all. They do NOT explain the 2026 drop (a constant is ignored in training, so
  its inference value changes nothing) and I said so rather than folding them
  into the story. `injuries` starts 2026-04-05; `home_starter_out` is 1.0 for
  every 2026 game, which is separately wrong.
- **Do not retrain against this table** — more trials on leaked features fit the
  leak better. The rebuild is reconstructable from data we already hold: `games`
  covers 2009-2026 so wins/losses/run differential are exact by date, and
  `player_game_log` (481k rows) can give as-of-date rate stats. Then re-measure
  with `walk_forward_eval`.
- **The f5 pause/retire decision is deferred again, deliberately.** Pausing on a
  number produced by a leaked comparison would be the same mistake pointing the
  other way.
- Written up in `docs/team_stats_leak.md`; the leakage rule in
  `.claude/rules/data-integrity.md` gained this instance and a companion rule
  about constant features. Worth running the same `count(DISTINCT as_of_date)`
  check for WNBA, NBA, NHL and NCAAF — nothing about the pattern is MLB-specific.

**Session summary (2026-09-03, session 182 — the Models tab says what each sport's models look at):**

- Matt: *"On the models tab, when on a specific sport, explain to the user what
  data elements we consider in the models."*
- **A collapsible card at the top of the Built-in list**, `ModelInputsCard`,
  titled "What {sport} models look at". Collapsed it is one line of headline
  plus a chevron, so the record stays the first thing on a scanning screen;
  expanded it lists the inputs as chips under plain-language group labels
  ("Starting pitchers", "Bullpen", "Weather and park", "Umpire" …), names the
  sources, and closes on the one line every sport shares: the inputs set the
  probability, the DraftKings line decides the pick (§6).
- **The copy is a DESCRIPTION of the feature lists, not a mirror of them**
  (`mobile/src/lib/modelInputs.ts`, one entry per sport in the toggle). It was
  written from `features/feature_engine.py`, the MLB / NBA / WNBA prop engines,
  the golf and UFC engines, `live_game_features.py`, `ncaaf_live/serve.py`,
  the NFL card rules in `docs/sports/nfl.md` and the market-relative prop rule.
  Two things it deliberately leaves out: line movement and public-betting
  splits, which are shown beside a pick but are NOT model inputs (the
  market-movement features exist in `features/market_movement.py` and are not
  wired into any scorer); and the twelve NFL usage-prop models, which are
  trained but all paused and so hidden from the list.
- **The header comment says the copy moves with the feature list.** A feature
  change is now a two-file change; the alternative — deriving chips from the
  raw column names — would have put `d_starter_era_last3` in front of a user.
- **`verify_model_inputs.ts` (54 checks)** pins coverage (every sport in the
  toggle has an entry; every listable built-in model belongs to a sport that
  has one), register (no raw feature name, no model id, no "paper"), shape
  (chip length ≤ 64 so a pill never wraps into a paragraph), and the
  DraftKings closing line. **Watched to fail:** a mutated copy with a raw
  feature name, a model id and "paper trading" injected tripped three checks;
  the chip-length check tripped on its own on the first honest run (an NHL
  market chip at 66 chars, since split in two).
- Four pre-existing a11y findings in `ModelsScreen.tsx` fixed in passing (the
  icon-only + button, the segment pills, both model rows); the one that
  remains is the `fontSize: 10` caption — the theme has no size below 12.
- **The UX designer review (Mobbin answered): ship with fixes, 0 blockers, 6
  should-fix, 4 consider.** All six applied: two copy errors ("issued 1–4 days
  out" contradicted the card's 7-day calibrated lead; "listed starter
  scratched" named a feature no model has); nine chips that were RULES and
  THRESHOLDS ("fires at 11 mph", "8+ points off the total", "at least 20
  rounds") reduced to the input they depend on — a number copied into prose
  drifts from config.py, and the detail screen already shows the live cut;
  one VoiceOver stop per group instead of 38 chips for MLB; the feature chip
  lifted into a shared `TagChip` used by both the card and the model detail
  screen; "GOLF" → "Golf" in the card title. Two considers applied (one-line
  collapsed headline; glosses on SP+ and EPA). Two left for Matt: collapsing
  each group to a running line instead of chips, and whether vendor names
  (Open-Meteo, DataGolf, CollegeFootballData) belong in front of users.
- Verified: tsc error set identical to origin/master (57 lines); ux_scan on
  the three touched screens/components 0 new / 4 resolved (the remaining
  findings are pre-existing on the detail screen and the caption size).
  JS-only, ships OTA.

**Session summary (2026-09-03, session 182 — the parlay line shop stops comparing prices across different lines):**

- Matt: "Clean up the parlay inline shop" — the follow-up flagged in #415. The
  card's Betting lines chips already refused a book hanging the side off a
  different number (`docs/best_line.md` §5: Over 9.0 at −105 is not a better
  price on Over 8.5, it is a different bet). The betslip path did not:
  `lineShopForPick` (a leg's `bestBook`, the "line-shopped payout" on the
  Betslip screen) and `legFromPick`'s `bookPrices` (the "Open with" tiles and
  the hand-off) took any book pricing the side, at any line, including
  Pinnacle and Bovada.
- **Shipped (JS-only, OTA):**
  - `lib/markets.ts`: `marketForPick(pick)` and `rowIsSameBet(pick, row,
    market)` — one predicate for every surface that offers another book's
    price. Moneyline always matches; a lined market must match `scored_line`;
    a row with no line on a lined market is refused (every all-books view
    carries the column, so a missing one is a broken row). `pickLineQuotes`
    refactored onto it, no behaviour change. `lineShopForPick` now shops only
    bettable books at the same line.
  - `lib/parlay.ts`: `legFromPick` builds `bookPrices` from bettable books at
    the same line only.
  - `components/BetslipBooksRow.tsx`: "Open with" tiles price the slip at
    `BETTABLE_BOOKS` (10), not `LINE_SHOP_BOOKS` (13) — no Pinnacle / Bovada
    / ESPN BET tiles.
- **Verified:** 7 new checks (`verify_betslip` +4, `verify_preferred_book`
  +3), **written first and watched to fail** — before the fix a FanDuel
  +105 at 9.0 beat BetMGM −105 at 8.5 as the "best" price, and Pinnacle,
  Fanatics-at-0.5 and a line-less BetRivers row all became leg prices. After:
  `verify_betslip` all pass, `verify_preferred_book` 58 pass,
  `verify_book_lines` 43, `verify_line_shop`, `verify_slip_stability`,
  `verify_parlay_correlation`, `verify_nfl_movement` all pass. `tsc`
  identical error set to master. `ux_scan --changed`: 1 finding, the
  pre-existing `#B8860B` star in `BetslipBooksRow` — 0 new.
  The betslip fixtures gained `line: 1.5` on every row, because the rows the
  views return always carry it and the strict rule needs it.
- **UX designer review (`frontend-ux-designer`, before the PR):** "Ship with
  fixes" — no Blockers, 4 Should-fix, 2 Consider, on the one touched
  component. Mobbin answered (Solflare onramp list, Klarna offers,
  Booking.com room options as comparators; no sportsbook app in its
  library). Applied: the tile hint now explains an N/M partial tile ("that
  book doesn't post every leg at the same line"), the common case once legs
  price at the same line only; tiles carry `accessibilityRole` and a label
  that says coverage, best payout and "your sportsbook" (VoiceOver heard

  more than one book is fully priced (DK was starred "best" against nothing);
  the `#B8860B` star literal became `colors.best`; the doc comment says
  bettable books. Declined for now: a per-tile "why" toast on a partial tile
  (needs `legFromPick` to carry the gap reason out — Matt's call). Flagged,
  commented in code: `rowIsSameBet` accepts every row when the PICK has no
  `scored_line` on a lined market, a fixture/legacy path the scorer never
  produces. Outstanding elsewhere: `ParlayScreen`'s LineShopRow still says
  "Display-only — the DraftKings hand-off uses DK prices", stale since the
  preferred-book hand-off landed, untouched here.
- Not a model update — no `Updated-By:` trailer.
**Session summary (2026-09-03, session 182 — the tuner was validating on shuffled folds, and walk-forward says f5 did not fail, it BROKE):**

- mike: *"why holdout an entire season? why not hold out various intervals of all
  seasons?"* → *"Do it."*
- **RANDOM INTERVALS WOULD BE WORSE, and the season holdout was never the weak
  part.** Every feature here is a rolling window (`d_runs_last_5`,
  `d_runs_last_10`, `d_starter_era_last3`), so a held-out June game is described
  by May games that sit in the training set. A season holdout avoids that by
  construction and matches deployment.
- **The weak part was the TUNER.** `_xgb_objective` and
  `_xgb_multiclass_objective` used `StratifiedKFold(shuffle=True)` — randomly
  shuffled folds — while the final holdout was a clean season. So 100 Optuna
  trials optimised a different task: interpolating between games already seen.
  Replaced with `TimeSeriesSplit` (`_time_ordered_cv`), and `train_model` now
  sorts the frame by `game_date` before building the arrays, because the split
  is positional and an unsorted frame makes it an arbitrary partition wearing a
  better name.
- **Measured, and smaller than I claimed.** I said fixing the CV was probably
  worth more than any feature. It was not, for this model: best CV log-loss
  0.6503 (shuffled) → 0.6525 (honest), and the 2026 holdout barely moved
  (AUC 0.568 → 0.569, accuracy 0.560 → 0.561). The leak was real but small here.
  The fix is still right — the tuner now optimises the task the model is for —
  but it did not rescue anything.
- **THE WALK-FORWARD IS THE FINDING.** `scripts/walk_forward_eval.py` trains
  ≤T and tests T+1, walked across seasons, fixed params so the only moving part
  is the season. `mlb_f5_moneyline`:

  | test season | train n | test n | acc | AUC |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 2022 | 3,593 | 1,481 | 0.5841 | **0.6362** |
  | 2023 | 5,074 | 1,530 | 0.5902 | **0.6283** |
  | 2024 | 6,604 | 1,470 | 0.6000 | **0.6396** |
  | 2025 | 8,074 | 1,303 | 0.5848 | **0.6333** |
  | 2026 | 9,377 | 1,058 | 0.5529 | **0.5598** |

- **Four seasons at AUC ≈ 0.63, then a collapse to 0.56 in 2026.** The single
  2026 holdout said "this model has no signal" and I was about to recommend
  retiring it on that basis. Five folds say **"this model worked for four
  seasons and broke in 2026"** — a different problem with a different fix, and
  exactly the distinction one holdout cannot make. This is §7's plateau rule
  applied to the model rather than to a threshold.
- **So the f5 recommendation changes: do not retire it. Find out what broke in
  2026.** Candidates to check next: a feature source that stopped populating or
  changed shape this season, the 17.6% null-feature drop rate, and whether the
  break is gradual or steps at a date.
- Three mutations on the CV change and five tests on the harness. Suite: 2,232
  passed, 1 failed (pre-existing).

---
**Session summary (2026-09-03, session 181 — mlb_f5_moneyline retrained on all available data; it does not help):**

- mike: *"retrain it immediately - use all available data."* Done. **The retrain
  does not rescue the model, and the artifact was deliberately NOT registered.**
- **"All available data" is 2019-2025.** First-five scores do not exist before
  2019 — measured: 0 rows with `home_score_f5` for every season 2009-2018, then
  2,377 in 2019. Trained on 2019-2025 (9,377 rows after a 17.5% null-feature
  drop), holdout **2026** (1,058 rows) so the read is out of sample on the
  season the model is actually betting. 848s, 100 Optuna trials.
- **THE REGISTRY'S HEADLINE NUMBER FOR THE LIVE MODEL IS IN-SAMPLE.** The active
  build (20260512) records `train_seasons [2019..2025]` with
  `holdout_season 2024` — a season inside its own training set. Its 0.6415
  "holdout" accuracy is therefore not a holdout number at all. The last honest
  build (20260504) scored 0.5963 on a real 2025 holdout.
- **Like-for-like on the SAME 1,058-row 2026 holdout**, which is the only
  comparison that decides anything:

  | build | accuracy | AUC | Brier |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | ACTIVE 20260512 | 0.5643 | 0.5697 | 0.2509 |
  | NEW 20260903 | 0.5605 | 0.5684 | 0.2510 |

  The retrain is **marginally worse on all three**, and the gap is far inside
  noise. Both sit at **AUC ≈ 0.57** — very weak discrimination on the season
  being bet. More data did not add signal because the signal is not there.
- **The new build fails the go-live gate anyway**: calibration error **6.29%**
  against §2's ≤5% bar.
- Registration was disabled for the run (`REGISTER_TRAINED_MODELS = False`), so
  the live version is untouched and nothing could swap production to an
  uncommitted artifact (§7 — that has already cost a month of UFC picks and a
  four-week outage). The artifact sits in `models/saved/_baseline/`, which is
  gitignored by design for comparison builds.
- **Standing evidence against this model, all measured today:** 8-9 / **−18.5%**
  at its live cut once only bets above the −200 juice floor count; **no
  `min_edge` value works** at `min_prob 0.74` (every one negative, both halves
  negative); and now a retrain on every season that exists is no better than
  what is already live.
- **Decision left to mike: pause or retire.** Both are model updates and need a
  name on them. Nothing was changed.

**Session summary (2026-09-03, session 180 — the f5 sweep says no cut works, and the first-pitch guard is two guards):**

- mike: *"sweep the f5 min_edge and add the first pitch guard."*
- **THE SWEEP FOUND A BUG BEFORE IT FOUND AN ANSWER.**
  `calibrated_threshold_sweep._apply_price_floor` read
  `config.MODEL_MIN_ODDS.get(model_id)` — the raw dict, which returns None for
  the 52 models that never named a floor. So the sweep was choosing cuts on a
  population the scorer now refuses, which is the exact bug that function's own
  docstring describes one level down. Fixed to `config.min_odds_for`. Same fix
  in `scripts/emit_threshold_sql.py`, which generates the APP's action filter —
  left alone, the app would have shown picks the scorer declines to bet.
- **The fix changed the answer completely.** `mlb_f5_moneyline` at its live cut
  went from **23-11, +2.7%** to **8-9, −18.5%** once only placeable bets count.
  Most of its apparent record was bets at prices we no longer take.
- **No min_edge works.** Holding `min_prob` at 0.74, every value from 0.00 to
  0.12 is negative (−8.9% to −18.5%), all under MIN_SETTLED, both halves
  negative (−3.8% / −33.3%). And 0.00 through 0.08 are IDENTICAL at 18 bets —
  at that probability every surviving pick already has edge ≥ 0.08, so
  **min_edge is not a lever for this model at all**. The sub-1% edges only ever
  occurred on heavy favourites, which the −200 floor already removes.
- The harness's own recommendation is 0.58/0.10 (82-58, +7.9%, plateau 8/8) —
  but that abandons the probability cut entirely, quadruples volume to
  12.1/week, and its second half is **+0.2%**. Not shipped; §7 says report the
  neighbourhood and retrain rather than ship the least-bad cut.
- **THE FIRST-PITCH GUARD IS TWO GUARDS, because the data showed two failures.**
  507 signals were locked after first pitch: **485 had their PICK created after
  first pitch too** (never pre-game picks, §7 — capture should never have
  locked them), and 22 were legitimate picks whose CAPTURE merely ran late. The
  MIN/DET signal that held the health check red was one of the 22 — created
  **38 seconds before** first pitch, captured four minutes after.
- So capture is keyed on `p.created_at` (not the capture clock, which would
  delete the 22 legitimate signals from the shadow record — §1c), and delivery
  is keyed on whether the game has started by the time the card is built
  (posting one of the 22 sends a member to a game in progress at a pre-game
  number). Both fail OPEN on a missing timestamp.
- The delivery half is in Python, not SQL: these producers are executed against
  sqlite by the tests, where `::timestamptz` is a syntax error — the same lesson
  as yesterday's health-check fix, applied before it could bite again.
- `_still_pre_game` initially raised `NameError: timezone` — `discord_notifier`
  imported `date, datetime, timedelta` but not `timezone`. Caught by the tests.
- The restatement path is deliberately NOT guarded: it re-publishes a whole past
  slate as a correction, and dropping its started games would make the
  correction incomplete.
- Four mutations, all bite. Suite: 2,221 passed, 1 failed (pre-existing).

**Session summary (2026-09-03, session 179 — the juice floor, the health check nobody could fix, and the rules split):**

- Three instructions from mike in one message, all done.
- **THE -290 PICK.** `LAD ML F5  -290 @ FanDuel  3u to win 0.91u` reached the
  board because **`MODEL_MIN_ODDS` covered 17 of 69 models** — sixteen props at
  -140 and `ncaaf_moneyline` at -250 — and `.get(model_id)` returned None for
  the other 52, where **None meant NO FLOOR**. The pick was DK -330 against a
  0.7674 break-even with a model probability of 0.7729: an edge of **0.54%** on
  a Kelly fraction of 0.0023. `mlb_f5_moneyline`'s cut is `min_prob 0.74 /
  min_edge 0.0`, and a zero edge floor accepts exactly that.
  `config.min_odds_for()` now falls back to `DEFAULT_MIN_ODDS = -200` in all
  three places the floor must agree: the scorer's gate, the
  `model_action_thresholds` mirror, and the two scorer log lines that did
  `MODEL_MIN_ODDS[model_id]` and would have raised KeyError in the very branch
  that blocks the bet. `Updated-By: mike`.
- Blast radius **measured**: on the clean window, 3 of 38 `mlb_f5_moneyline`
  bets fail at -200 plus one live NCAAF row — about three picks a month. An
  all-history count says 29%, but that is dominated by prop rows written before
  the -140 floors existed; both numbers are in `config.py` so nobody re-derives
  the wrong one. Ran `threshold_sync` immediately: 0 rows carry a NULL floor,
  the pick dropped off the card without touching the pick row (§1c), and the
  juiciest price on today's board is **-140**.
- **THE HEALTH CHECK, red on every pass for three days, was ONE ROW.**
  `MIN vs DET Under 8.5` locked into `opening_signals` at 2026-08-31 19:45 ET
  against a **19:41 ET first pitch** — four minutes late. Never delivered, never
  deliverable, and `signal_delivery` counts postable signals from the last three
  days with no ledger row. It would have gone quiet on 09-04 by ageing out of
  the window rather than by anything being fixed. `_deliverable()` now drops
  signals locked after their own first pitch; production goes 1 to 0 flagged.
- That fix's first version used `::timestamptz` in SQL — correct against
  Postgres, and it broke all eight `TestSignalDelivery` tests, which run on
  sqlite. Comparing the raw TEXT is forbidden (§7, mixed shapes), so the rows
  are fetched and `_parse_ts` compares them in Python.
- **Root cause flagged, not fixed:** opening-signal capture has NO first-pitch
  guard. 507 rows all-time locked after their game started, 3 since 08-25.
- **THE RULES SPLIT.** Checked the Claude Code docs rather than guessing: a
  bigger file is the wrong direction (target under 200 lines; longer files
  "consume more context and REDUCE adherence"), and `@path` imports do not help
  because imported files load at launch anyway. The mechanism that does is
  **`.claude/rules/*.md` with `paths:` frontmatter — loaded only when Claude
  opens a matching file.**
- Four rule files: `analysis-and-thresholds.md`, `data-integrity.md`,
  `operations.md`, `frontend.md`. **27 of 74 rules are now path-scoped.**
  CLAUDE.md 38,756 to 32,370 bytes; the size cap drops 41,000 to 36,000 as a
  backstop rather than the plan.
- **Nothing rewritten, nothing lost, checked mechanically**: 74 rule leads
  before, all 74 present after across CLAUDE.md plus the rules files. What stays
  universal is what must be known BEFORE deciding which file to open — the
  evaluation rule, never-estimate, the sandbox rule, the pick rule, the
  verification standards — and a test pins three of those by name.
- Ten mutations across the three changes, all bite. Suite: 2,211 passed, 1
  failed (`test_refresh_pass_parallel`, pre-existing).

**Session summary (2026-09-03, session 178 — the card leads with the best book, "good to" on pre-game, and the missed X recap recovered):**

- mike, frustrated and right: *"this is not the right behavior it just needs to
  post the best book and price and for bonus, post a 'good to xx odds'"*;
  *"Yesterday record did not post to X but it posted to discord. Why. Fix it.
  Don't come back til you do."*
- **The card leads with the best price now.** It led with DraftKings and
  appended "also `-120` @ BetMGM" — burying the number the reader acts on behind
  the number the MODEL decides on. `publish_price()` returns the best BETTABLE
  price and its book, falling back to DK when the alternative is absent, equal
  or worse, on all three surfaces. The stake is grossed up by the PUBLISHED
  price: risking 1.39u at −139 while pointing at −120 lays 16% more than needed.
- **"Good to" on pre-game picks.** `price_bound()` existed and was live-only.
  All three pre-game producers now read `min_edge`/`min_odds` from the same
  `model_action_thresholds` row the scorer's cut comes from.
- **WHY THE X RECAP WAS MISSED, which was my error and worth writing down
  exactly.** The fix merged at 20:42 ET on 09-02 and I deleted the
  `x_results:2026-09-02` ledger row a minute later so the corrected record would
  post at 6am. **I read the Railway deploy stamp `2026-09-03T00:42Z` as ET** and
  believed it was already the 3rd. It was still the 2nd, the deploy had not
  finished, and the next refresh pass — OLD code, no day-is-over guard —
  re-posted a partial 8-4 and re-ledgered it. That row blocked the 6am post:
  Discord published 13-17, X published nothing. **§7's "use ET, never UTC, for
  today", made in reasoning rather than in code**, on the very fix that rule was
  being applied to.
- **Recovery ran on the worker, not as a handover** (§1b): new job type
  `publish_x_results` clears the stale ledger row and calls the ORDINARY
  `notify_x_results`, so the day-is-over guard is not bypassed — re-posting must
  not become a way to publish a partial. Job 9449.
- **It failed twice before it worked, both times my fault.** Attempt 1 and 2
  died on `AttributeError: '_CursorResult' object has no attribute 'rowcount'` —
  `data.db._CursorResult` exposes only fetchone/fetchall/fetchmany/`__iter__`,
  and I wrote the job as though `conn` were a DB-API cursor. Attempt 2 ran 19
  seconds before the fix's container went live (deploy started 11:33:07, ready
  11:35:20; the tick fired 11:35:01). Attempt 3 — the LAST one before
  MAX_ATTEMPTS killed it — succeeded at 07:40 ET.
- **Verified equal:** Discord `13-17-1 · -5.98u · -17.3% ROI`; the tweet
  `📊 Sep 2 results: 13-17-1, -5.98u, -17.3% ROI / MLB 13-17-1`.
- Today's 16 `discord_signal` ledger rows were cleared so the card re-posts in
  the new format (mike is deleting the old messages). Other dates untouched:
  97 rows remain.
- **Today's card in the new format**, showing the new books earning their place:
  `-105 @ FanDuel`, `-115 @ Fanatics`, `+100 @ BetRivers`, `-120 @ DraftKings`,
  each with a `good to` bound.
- Five mutations on the display change, two on the job. Two test files that
  asserted the OLD footnote behaviour were rewritten with the reason recorded;
  §6's tripwire (best_odds must never reach a WHERE clause) carried over
  verbatim. Suite: 2,166 passed, 1 failed (`test_refresh_pass_parallel`,
  pre-existing).

**Session summary (2026-09-03, session 179 — the app wears the real brand: @signalbasepicks mark, amber-on-navy chrome, icon set re-drawn):**

- Matt: *"Use this logo link to update the logo and color patterns in the app
  that make sense"* — the link being the X profile. Branch
  `claude/logo-color-patterns-update-hxmr52`. Front-end + assets + one worker
  script + one workflow; no DB, pipeline, scorer or threshold changes.
- **Getting the image was the work.** The sandbox cannot reach x.com,
  pbs.twimg.com, syndication, nitter, unavatar, jina, or even signalbase-ai.com
  (every CONNECT refused by the egress proxy); Supabase Storage holds no brand
  assets; Drive has none; the Lovable site predates the account. Per §1b the
  route was the system, not the box: NEW `scripts/fetch_brand_avatar.py` runs on
  Railway, asks the X API v2 for the profile with the account's OWN OAuth 1.0a
  keys (reusing `tracking/x_publisher.py`'s signing; a GET must fold its query
  params into the signature base, which the publisher's POST helper does not),
  and parks avatar + banner in a NEW `brand_assets` table (revoked from
  anon/authenticated, DDL gated on `ddl_guard`). Ran on `prop-probe` by
  pointing its start command at the script, scoping its watch pattern to the
  file and connecting the branch — a plain connect was SKIPPED because the
  service's watch pattern deliberately never matches. **Restored afterwards:**
  start command `bash nfl/scripts/live_prop_job.sh run`, watch pattern
  `nfl/scripts/DISABLED_never_matches`, source `master`; the next master push
  showed SKIPPED, i.e. the original behaviour.
- **Base64 through a chat window does not survive retyping.** The read-only
  MCP handed back the bytes, and my transcription of 10 KB of base64 came out
  31 characters long. So NEW `.github/workflows/fetch-brand-assets.yml`: a
  runner has open egress AND can commit, so the bytes never pass through a
  keyboard. `workflow_dispatch` is refused to the integration (403), so it
  fires on a push of the workflow file itself; it curls both pbs.twimg.com
  URLs, verifies the SHA-256 the worker recorded, and commits
  `mobile/assets/brand/x_avatar.jpg` + `x_banner.png` back to the branch.
  Hashes match the `brand_assets` rows.
- **What the brand actually is:** a stepped geometric S in near-black navy
  `#0B1320` on amber `#F2B01E` (400px JPEG); the banner is navy `#0B1220`,
  amber wordmark, `#8A97AB` captions, `#152034` watermarks. The app shipped a
  teal-gradient "SB" icon and iOS system-blue tint — nothing of the brand.
- **Icon set re-drawn, not upscaled** — NEW `scripts/render_brand_icons.py`
  measures the mark (bbox 92..307 of 400 = 54%; bars 48px, gaps 36px, stems
  alternating) and renders `icon.png` 1024, `adaptive-icon.png` (transparent,
  44% safe zone, amber background in app.json), `splash.png` 2048 (amber mark
  on navy — the banner's palette), `favicon.png`, and `assets/brand/mark.png`
  for in-app use. `app.json`: primaryColor `#F2B01E`, splash and adaptive
  backgrounds. **Icon/splash need a native build (mobile-build.yml); they are
  not OTA-able.**
- **Palette — the decision, and why `tint` is NOT amber.** Amber on white is
  1.9:1, so it can never be text or an icon on a light surface, and an amber
  tint would collide with the orange `med` confidence semantic. So
  `theme.ts` gains `brand`, `brandInk`, `brandNavy`, `brandNavyRaised`,
  `brandMuted`; `tint` moves `#007AFF` → `brandInk #0B1320` (18.6:1); the tab
  bar becomes the brand chrome (navy, amber active 9.8:1, muted inactive
  6.3:1); the betslip bar floats over it in raised navy with an amber badge
  (ink text 9.75:1); the sign-in screen shows the mark above its title.
  Content surfaces stay light; signal/P&L/confidence colours untouched.
  Documented in `mobile/docs/UX_REVIEW.md` §2.
- **The `frontend-ux-designer` review (Mobbin: connector authenticated but
  every search answered "requires a paid plan", so references were HIG + this
  app's own conventions). Verdict "ship with fixes", 0 blockers, 5 should-fix,
  all applied:** (1) stack headers still used React Navigation's default blue
  → a `NAV_THEME` from the tokens on `NavigationContainer`; (2) betslip bar and
  tab bar fused into one slab (raised navy on navy is 1.15:1, and the border
  was the same colour) → a 2pt amber top rule on the bar, the banner's bottom
  rule, plus a `brandSeparator` hairline on the tab bar; (3) ten plain-text
  links (no fill, border or icon) were 1.13:1 from body text → underlined, the

  `tint` and rendered near-black → new `info` STATUS token (the old blue,
  never a control) for it, the live inning pill and the sharp-score meter;

  Two "consider"s taken: the mark leads slide 1 of `OnboardingModal` (the

**Session summary (2026-09-03, session 172 — the NFL wind card had been failing on every run, behind a comment that said it could not):**

- Found while verifying #410's dependency change actually BOOTED, not just
  deployed — the worker deploy log carried a `ModuleNotFoundError`. It was not
  #410's (the identical error is in the PREVIOUS deployment's log at 04:00:05Z,
  before #410 merged), but it was real:

  ```
  from models.wind_totals import select_bets, UNIT_PCT, MAX_CALIBRATED_LEAD
  ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'models.wind_totals'
  ```

  `nfl_wind_totals` is a LIVE insert-once model (§1c) and its card had been
  producing nothing, hourly, into a log nobody was reading.

- **THE MECHANISM IS A PYTHON RULE MOST PEOPLE DO NOT KNOW.** `nfl/models/` has
  no `__init__.py`, so it is a **PEP 420 namespace portion**. The platform's
  top-level `models/` HAS one, so it is a **regular package**. When both roots
  are on sys.path the finder records the namespace portion and KEEPS LOOKING —
  and **a regular package found later wins over a namespace portion regardless
  of path order.** So the card's own `sys.path.insert(0, <nfl root>)` did not
  save it, and the failure is a hard raise, never a fallback. Both roots are
  always on the path on a scheduled run: `scheduler.py`'s `BASE_ENV` sets
  `PYTHONPATH=<repo root>` so these children can `import monitoring`.

- **THE BUG WAS PROTECTED BY A COMMENT ASSERTING IT COULD NOT HAPPEN.**
  `scripts/nfl_preflight.py` said the production cards were safe "because the
  scheduler runs them with cwd=nfl/". **cwd is not sys.path** — for
  `python scripts/foo.py`, `sys.path[0]` is the SCRIPT's directory and cwd never
  enters into it. `scheduler.py` does pass `cwd=ROOT/"nfl"`, so the comment
  looked corroborated by the code right next to it. Corrected in place rather
  than deleted, because the wrong reasoning is the useful part.

- `nfl/scripts/daily_opener_card.py` had already worked out the right fix
  independently (load by absolute path) — which is exactly why the opener card
  worked and the wind card did not. That implementation is now
  `nfl/_nfl_models.load_nfl_model`, one copy, used by the wind card, the replay
  card and backtest_ev.

- **Reproduced before fixing and verified after**: under the real invocation
  (`cwd=nfl/`, `PYTHONPATH=<repo root>`) the bare import resolved to
  `/home/user/betting-model/models/__init__.py`; after the fix the card runs
  past its imports all the way to `main()`, stopping only where THIS SANDBOX
  cannot reach `api.open-meteo.com` (proxy 403) — §1b, a sandbox limit, not a
  system one. `import models` still resolves to the platform package, so
  nothing was shadowed to buy this.

- `tests/test_nfl_model_imports.py` runs each card in a SUBPROCESS under the
  scheduler's real environment, because that is the only vantage point the bug
  was ever visible from — plus a source tripwire banning the bare import under
  `nfl/`. **Watched fail:** restoring the bare import fails 2; deleting the
  loader fails 3. Suite **2,154 passed**.

- **#410 itself verified clean:** worker and pollers online, `running: 1,
  crashed: 0`, deploy in **2m 5s** against a pre-trim build plus a 1.17 GB push
  that once stalled at ~65 KB/s for over half an hour.

**Session summary (2026-09-03, session 171 — the worker image was 60% GPU driver and dashboard it never runs):**

- Continuation of 169. A deploy on 2026-09-02 STALLED pushing the worker image:
  618 MB in, the push fell to ~65 KB/s and the container did not go live for
  over half an hour while `pollers` finished the same commit in 2.5 minutes. A
  1.17 GB image is not merely slow, it is a failure mode.

- **Measured from the installed tree, not guessed:**

  | package | installed | why it was there |
  |---|---|---|
  | `nvidia-nccl-cu12` | **475.7 MB** | transitive dep of `xgboost` on Linux |
  | `xgboost` | 239.0 MB | 19.3 MB as `xgboost-cpu` |
  | `plotly` | 58.9 MB | imported ONLY by `dashboard/app.py` |
  | `pydeck` | 47.5 MB | nothing imports it directly |
  | `matplotlib` | 37.0 MB | nothing imports it directly |
  | `streamlit` | 33.0 MB | imported ONLY by `dashboard/app.py` |

- **`xgboost` -> `xgboost-cpu`. Same project, same version, same import name,
  no CUDA kernels.** The repo asks for a GPU in exactly zero places (no
  `gpu_hist`, no `device='cuda'`, no `gpu_predictor`), so 475.7 MB of NCCL was
  riding along for a device that does not exist on the container. **Verified
  rather than assumed: all 37 committed xgboost artifacts predict
  BYTE-IDENTICALLY under xgboost-cpu** — same SHA over a fixed random matrix.
  Together with xgboost's own 239 -> 19.3 MB that is **~695 MB gone**.

- **streamlit + plotly moved to `requirements-dashboard.txt`.** They are the
  only packages in the repo that nothing outside `dashboard/` imports, and the
  dashboard runs on Matt's machine, never on the worker whose start command is
  `python scheduler.py`. Install is additive: `pip install -r requirements.txt
  -r requirements-dashboard.txt`. The three places that tell a human how to
  start the dashboard (CLAUDE.md §5, `docs/local_ops.md`, and
  `run_pipeline.py`'s own closing log line) all now name the install, because a
  split nobody can discover is just a broken command.

- **`tests/test_worker_deps.py` is the tripwire**, and it reads the requirements
  files as TEXT on purpose: what matters is what gets INSTALLED on the
  container, which is not observable from whatever environment a test happens
  to run in. It also pins the PREDICATE behind the split — the moment a module
  outside `dashboard/` imports streamlit or plotly, or anything asks for GPU
  training, the test fails rather than letting the container ImportError at boot.

- **Watched fail:** plain xgboost restored fails 1; streamlit back in the worker
  file fails 1; a non-dashboard module importing plotly fails 1; a module asking
  for `gpu_hist` fails 1; the dashboard file no longer saying it is additive
  fails 1. Suite **2,141 passed**.

- **THE `odds` COVERING INDEX WAS CONSIDERED AND DECLINED, and the reversal is
  the point.** Session 169 recommended it as the belt-and-braces follow-up while
  the query ran ~120x/hour. Re-measured once #398 was live: the DK pre-game
  subset is **744,586 rows** (35% of the table), `odds` already carries **443 MB
  of indexes** on a **5,725 MB** database, and a covering partial index with
  seven INCLUDE columns lands at roughly 100-150 MB PLUS a permanent write tax
  on the hottest table in the system — one the live loops write to constantly.
  The benefit it now buys is ~45 minutes a day of read time on a query that
  runs **5.5x/hour**. At 120x/hour that trade was clearly worth it; at 5.5 it is
  not. **Recorded so nobody re-derives it: if the poll interval drops, the
  re-seed interval shortens, or another caller starts hitting
  `last_known_prices`, re-run the arithmetic before assuming the answer is still
  no.**

- **Also verified this session:** #398's fix, in production. 23:18Z 4,291 calls
  / 88,398 s; 04:43Z 4,321 / 89,055 s — **+30 calls over 5.42 h = 5.5/hour
  against ~120/hour before, a 95% cut**, and 121 s/hour of database time against
  ~2,472. The mean is unchanged at 20,610 ms, which is the point: the query was
  not made faster, it was made rare.

**Session summary (2026-09-03, session 177 — the free surfaces name the cheaper book, five more books, and the books are NOT free):**

- mike: *"Yes @ book line"* and *"Do the extra books."*
- **The @book line, on both public surfaces.** The paid sport channels have
  named a cheaper book since 2026-08-30; the free Discord card and the tweet did
  not. The same pick reached three surfaces carrying three different amounts of
  information and **the two PUBLIC ones carried the least** — a shop window
  showing a worse number than the shop. `_free_pick_candidates` now selects
  `best_book`/`best_odds` (it never did), `_free_pick_embed` renders
  `better_price_note`, and `render_free_pick` gained `_better_price_clause` with
  the same three conditions the Discord card applies: a book must be recorded,
  not be DraftKings, and be STRICTLY better.
- On the tweet the clause is **dropped rather than truncated** when it would
  overflow 280 — a half-written price is worse than none. `_assert_no_link`
  still runs over the finished text; a book name is not a URL and cannot reach
  the 13x link rate.
- **THE FIVE NEW BOOKS ARE NOT FREE, AND THE EXISTING COMMENT SAID THEY WOULD
  BE.** `betrivers, hardrockbet, ballybet, betparx, rebet` all exist and cover
  MLB/NCAAF/WNBA/UFC — the two sports where this repo shopped fewest books. But
  they live in the **`us2`** region, and The Odds API bills markets × REGIONS.
  Measured against the live endpoint, one bulk MLB call for h2h+spreads+totals:
  the 8 books already on the list cost **3** credits, these 5 alone cost **3**,
  all 13 together cost **6**.
- The same doubling hits the per-event fetch (1 → 2 on a single market) and the
  PROP fetch, which uses the same param and is the larger consumer. Recent burn
  is ~35k credits/day against a 5,000,000 monthly plan (August used 737,085), so
  this moves toward roughly **2.1M/month — affordable, but real**, and stated
  rather than left to be discovered (§1b). The repo's own comment above
  `LINE_SHOP_BOOKMAKERS` says the param counts as ONE region; that is true only
  while every book named sits in one region, and nobody had tested the other
  case. Corrected in place with the measurement.
- **The marginal cost of books 2-5 is zero** — once one `us2` book is on the
  list the second region is paid for. All-or-nothing, not a dial.
- Display names added for all five (`Hard Rock Bet`, `Bally Bet`, `betPARX`,
  `ReBet`) plus `Fanatics`, on BOTH renderers, with a test that every offerable
  book has a human name — a members' channel printing "hardrockbet" reads like a
  bug.
- **A test fixture that tested the wrong branch.** The overflow test used a
  240-character label, which overflows 280 on its own and hits the pre-existing
  `[:MAX_TWEET]` truncation — a different, already-tested behaviour. Rebuilt at
  200 characters, where the tweet fits WITHOUT the clause and overflows WITH it,
  which is the only case the branch decides.
- Five mutations, all bite. Suite: 2,131 passed, 3 failed — same three as
  `origin/master`.

**Session summary (2026-09-03, session 175 — CLAUDE.md trimmed: every rule verbatim, the evidence moved out):**

- mike: *"go on the pr"* for the CLAUDE.md trim recommended earlier the same
  session.
- **The measurement that shaped it.** §1b and §7 were 12,731 and 13,008 bytes —
  **57% of a 44,824-byte file** — and their rule STATEMENTS were only **6% and
  8%** of those bytes. The other ~92% was the measured story behind each rule,
  already duplicated in `docs/sessions/`.
- **`docs/rules_evidence.md` is a VERBATIM copy** of both sections as they
  stood, made programmatically so nothing could be lost in transcription. The
  condensed sections were then written against it.
- **Result: 45,605 → 39,377 bytes on disk.** §1b 12,731 → 9,382; §7 13,008 →
  10,135. Less compression than the 6%/8% figure suggests, deliberately: a lot
  of what reads as evidence is OPERATIVE — the four sandbox routes, the
  `Updated-By:` trailer syntax and what counts as a model update, the clean
  windows for the evaluation rule, `config.live_slate_dates()`. Those stayed.
- **Verified no rule was lost, mechanically rather than by eye.** Extracted every
  bold statement from both sections before and after and diffed them: 57 → 59
  (two rules that were plain prose are now bold). Six appeared dropped; three
  were punctuation false positives and the other three were STORIES whose rules
  survive in full — "I queried what was STORED" → *check what the SOURCE offers,
  not what the table holds*; "four times I ended a turn with run this on your
  machine" → *a handover is a last resort with a reason attached*; the 100,116-
  credit volume → *a Railway volume is one copy, not a backup*.
- **`test_claude_md_size` tightened 45,000 → 41,000**, locking in the trim
  rather than leaving room to drift back. ~1.6 KB of headroom, about two new
  rules. The file's own footer asks for ~30 KB and it is NOT there yet; closing
  that gap means moving more evidence out, never dropping a rule. Reversible in
  one line if the headroom is too tight.
- One rule was added to §1b's cross-model rule while passing through: it now
  reads "the other models, sports, **or publishing surfaces**" — the session-171
  X/Discord divergence was that rule's shape one level out.
- Suite: 2,115 passed, 3 failed (was 4 — `test_claude_md_size` now passes).
