**Session summary (2026-09-03, session 174 — retention keeps the CLOSE, and the first version of it timed the pruner out):**

- mike: *"keep one non-dk snapshot per day."* Option 2 from `docs/best_line.md`
  §4, chosen over raising `PRUNE_NON_DK_KEEP_DAYS` wholesale (~19 GB).
- **What was actually missing was the CLOSE, not the count.** After `keep_days`
  the pruner already kept exactly one non-DK row per (proposition, book) — but
  it was the **OPENER**, and an opener cannot answer either question the
  retained history exists for: the best price available at DECISION time
  (Stage 2's re-sweep, for propositions that never got a scored row) or "did the
  best book beat DK at close?" — the feature this module's own docstring warned
  would need closing snapshots retained BEFORE anyone relied on it. So the
  change keeps opener AND close, which is two rows, not one.
- **Cost, measured not estimated:** one day of non-DK rows is 297,975 in `odds`
  and 105,105 in `player_prop_odds`, thinning to 2,787 and 6,594 distinct
  (proposition, book). The second retained row is about **+6 MB/day** against
  the ~210 MB/day full retention would cost.
- **The first version of this change broke the job, and only running it caught
  it.** A second anti-join beside the opener's made `--dry-run` fail with
  `canceling statement due to statement timeout`, on a job that finishes in ~80s
  unchanged — verified by checking out `origin/master`'s copy and timing both.
  Two anti-joins over 2.2M and 2.5M rows is not the same shape as one. **A
  pruner that times out prunes nothing, and the growth it exists to bound is
  silent** — this would have been invisible until the database was much larger.
  Fixed by computing both keep-rules with two window functions over a SINGLE
  scan; `test_each_tier_uses_exactly_one_anti_join` is the tripwire.
- **The close must never be an in-play price** (§6: an in-play row is a
  different proposition). The DESC ranking is partitioned BY the pre-game flag,
  so `rn_last = 1 AND is_pre` is the newest PRE-GAME row rather than the newest
  row that happens to be pre-game. Verified against real rows for 2026-09-01:
  540 non-DK rows over 270 propositions, 332 kept — between 1× and 2× the
  proposition count, as it should be.
- Stated honestly in the module: it is the last pre-game-TYPED row, which is not
  automatically the last row before first pitch (§7's leak trap — the evening
  refresh keeps writing `open` rows after the game starts). `snapshot_at` is
  retained with it so an analysis can still apply the `<= commence_time` bound;
  what this guarantees is that a row EXISTS to be bounded, where before there
  was none.
- `tests/test_prune_preserves_openers.py` was updated for the rename
  (`select_openers` → `select_keepers`); the opener rule itself is unchanged and
  still asserted.
- **Also this session:** the sweep was re-run at mike's request and is
  byte-identical — same five-day window, no settlements between runs. Nothing to
  report is the report.
- Suite: 2,114 passed, 4 failed — the same four as `origin/master`.

---
**Session summary (2026-09-03, session 173 — Stage 2 step 1: the best-price threshold sweep, and the first read says the free half is real and small):**

- mike: *"stage 2 go."* Recorded verbatim in `docs/best_line.md` §4 and here,
  because the flip commit lands weeks from now and this is its provenance. Also
  settled two open book questions: **keep Caesars** (`williamhill_us`) and
  **no espnbet** — both already matched the code, no change needed.
- **Shipped `scripts/best_line_threshold_sweep.py`.** Re-derives every pre-game
  cut on best-price edge. It writes nothing; a threshold change is a model
  update and needs a name on it, and `test_the_sweep_writes_nothing` pins that.
- **Reuses the calibrated sweep's statistics rather than copying them** —
  `grade`, `sweep`, `wilson`, `_apply_price_floor` are imported from
  `scripts/calibrated_threshold_sweep.py`. Plateau counting and the time split
  are the same discipline whichever price basis is underneath, and a second copy
  would drift.
- **The sample turned out to exist, which was the surprise.** The fear was that
  best-price stamping is five days old. But `mv_scored_pick_outcomes` grades
  BET, AVOID and dead-zone NONE alike, and the stamping runs on every scored
  row — so joined on `pick_id` the high-volume prop models already carry 500 to
  1,300 graded best-price rows each. A sweep is possible now; a *credible* one
  is not, and the script says which per model.
- **The column that matters is `same @best`:** the SAME pick set today's cut
  makes, paid at the best price. No threshold moves, no pick appears. First read
  — `mlb_prop_pitcher_outs` +3.5pp, `mlb_runline` +4.8pp,
  `wnba_prop_player_rebounds` +7.8pp, most others under a point. That half
  carries zero calibration risk and can ship first.
- **Nothing is shippable and the script says so in its own voice.** Every
  candidate sits in a five-day window, which cannot produce a credible time
  split, and the two models with the most evidence (`batter_hits` 1,302 rows,
  `batter_walks` 1,043) return NO profitable cut at all on best-price edge —
  the sweep working, not failing. Earliest credible flip: high-volume props,
  mid-to-late September.
- **A test that did not test what it claimed, caught by mutating.** The
  free-half column was keyed on `(date, probability, price)`. The first fixture
  written to catch that collision PASSED against the bad key, because rows
  sharing those three also shared the cut's verdict. The real collision is two
  picks agreeing on all three and DISAGREEING on edge — one qualifying, one not
  — which is what a prop slate looks like, and it credits the better price to a
  bet the cut rejected, in the flattering direction. Re-keyed on `pick_id`, and
  the fixture rebuilt until the mutation actually bit. §1b: a test that passes
  without the fix is not a test.
- **The probability stays RAW, deliberately.** Stage 2 changes the PRICE the
  decision is measured against. Folding in the probability-calibration flip at
  the same time would confound two changes whose effects point the same way.
- Retired models are excluded by JOINing `model_action_thresholds` rather than a
  hand-list — `batter_hr` (582 rows) and `batter_rbi` (1,085) were retired in
  session 170 and would otherwise have been handed recommended cuts.
- Suite: 2,107 passed, 4 failed — the same four as `origin/master`.
- **Not done:** no threshold written; no retention/prune change (mike has not
  picked from the two options); no new books; live untouched; CLAUDE.md trim
  recommended but not executed.

**Session summary (2026-09-02, session 172 — why pre-game picks read as DraftKings: two real bugs, one calibrated design decision, and a quarter of "best prices" that could not be bet):**

- mike: *"investigate why mlb/wnba/ncaaf/ufc picks continue to only come from
  DraftKings lines… they are all available in the odds api. design the best
  solution. this is only for pregame picks for now."* Branch
  `fix/pregame-best-line`. Design: **`docs/best_line.md`** (linked from §9).
  Ingest + display plumbing, so no `Updated-By` trailer.
- **The answer is three separate things and only two are bugs**, which is why
  the doc leads with the split rather than a fix list.
- **Bug 1 — two markets were fetched DK-only at the source.**
  `odds_ingestor._get_event_odds` hard-coded `bookmakers=ODDS_API_BOOKMAKER`,
  and it is the ONLY route by which MLB first-five and UFC round totals are
  fetched (both are "additional markets" absent from the bulk endpoint).
  Measured: `h2h_1st_5_innings` 704 DK rows and **zero** from any other book,
  UFC `totals` 891 and **zero**, while MLB `h2h`/`spreads`/`totals` and UFC
  `h2h` each carried 5-7 books. So **51 of 176 recent MLB pre-game BETs — every
  `mlb_f5_moneyline` pick — had nothing to shop against**. Same bug, same file,
  as the historical-odds one mike named on 2026-09-01.
- **The cost claim was wrong until it was measured.** Both the code comment and
  the first draft of the doc said "zero extra credits", reasoning from the
  one-region rule. An A/B against the live endpoint on the same event says
  otherwise: F5 DK-only bills 1 credit and returns one market; F5 with all seven
  books bills **3** and returns three, because the endpoint bills per market
  RETURNED and only the other books offer F5 spreads and totals. UFC round
  totals is one market either way and does not move. Net ~+30 credits/day
  against 4,900,852 remaining — and it buys F5 spreads and totals, which this
  repo has never held. §1b exists for exactly this: the rule was right and the
  conclusion drawn from it was not.
- **Bug 2 — `_best_game_price` never tried the UFC sibling orientation**, which
  `_get_dk_odds` has done since the 2026-08-29 card. A fight five books had
  priced could silently stamp NULL, and it was invisible because "no quotes" and
  "no better price" look identical.
- **Not a bug — the DK-decides invariant** (§6). `edge`, BET/AVOID, Kelly,
  settlement and CLV measure against DK because every threshold was swept there.
  Moving that is Stage 2 and it is mike's call, not a defect to fix in passing.
- **The coverage numbers that opened the investigation were wrong, and I caught
  it mid-flight.** "MLB 48%, WNBA 12%, UFC 0%" pooled six pre-feature weeks with
  four post-feature days — best-line stamping only shipped ~08-28. On the clean
  window (since 08-31) it is **69 of 70** pre-game BETs, and the single miss is
  the UFC DK-only market from Bug 1. WNBA was never broken; its season ended
  08-30. This is §1b's "name the query AND what it excludes" in both directions.
- **The finding that mattered most, and it was not what was asked:**
  `BEST_LINE_BOOKMAKERS` defaulted to `LINE_SHOP_BOOKMAKERS`, which includes
  **pinnacle and bovada** — neither bettable from the US. Of the 69 clean-window
  BETs carrying a best price, 35 named a non-DK book and **18 of those 35 named
  Pinnacle or Bovada**: over half of every "we found you a better number" claim,
  and **26% of all bets**, pointed at a price the bettor could not take, from a
  column whose own docstring says it is "what the bettor should actually take".
  Fixed with `BEST_LINE_EXCLUDE_BOOKMAKERS` (default `pinnacle,bovada`), which
  removes a book from SHOPPING while leaving it in the feed — Pinnacle is the
  sharp de-vig reference and Bovada carried the NCAAF opener signal, so dropping
  them from ingest would break both.
- **The one place this went beyond "pre-game only", deliberately:** the
  exclusion is a shared config constant, so live stamping stops naming
  unbettable books too. A price you cannot take is the wrong number on a live
  pick for the same reason (§1b). It changes which book a live pick names, never
  whether it fires, and the doc says how to make it pre-game-only if mike
  prefers.
- **Stage 2 is designed, not shipped.** Re-sweep every pre-game cut on
  best-implied edge, then flip qualification/Kelly/settlement in one change with
  `Updated-By: mike`; CLV stays DK-to-DK either way. Measured loosening on the
  clean window: **0.68pp average, 3.61pp max** (config.py's older ~2pp came from
  a different population — the re-sweep settles it, no point arguing first).
- **The retention argument, refined by measurement rather than assumed.** The
  worry was that `PRUNE_NON_DK_KEEP_DAYS=2` destroys the evidence for that
  re-sweep. It mostly does not: best-price stamping runs on EVERY scored row, so
  since 08-31 it is NONE 2,621/2,647 and AVOID 311/312 as well as BET 69/70 —
  §7's full-outcome requirement is already satisfied from the picks table. The
  residual gap is only propositions that get no row at all (`MAX_EDGE_CAP`) and
  pruned NONE rows. And raising retention is dearer than the config comment
  claims: measured 252,705 non-DK `odds` rows/day at 573 B plus 98,680
  `player_prop_odds` rows/day at 663 B ≈ **210 MB/day, 6.3 GB/month**, against
  the comment's ~2.7 GB/month, which counted one table. So the recommendation is
  NOT "raise it to 90 days" (~19 GB) but either accept the narrow gap or keep
  one snapshot per book per proposition per day (~1/20th the volume).
- **Verification:** eight new tests, each watched failing with its fix backed
  out — per-event back to DK-only, the 400 fallback removed, the exclusion list
  emptied, the sibling fallback removed, and the sibling reading the same side
  (which would report the opposite fighter's price). Two existing tests were
  rewritten because they asserted the OLD behaviour by naming pinnacle/bovada as
  the winning book; both now say why in a comment. Suite: 2,088 passed, 4
  failed, the same four as `origin/master` (`test_claude_md_size` — the §9 index
  row adds 55 bytes to a file already ~550 over its own limit —
  `test_refresh_pass_parallel`, two in `test_system_health_checks`).
- **The book list mike set the same day, and the two instructions that could not
  both be followed.** *"add fanatics / ceasars / wynnbet to betable books,
  remove william will and espn bet (shut down last year)."* Every name was
  checked against the live endpoint first, per the `curl` instruction already
  sitting above `LINE_SHOP_BOOKMAKERS`. `fanatics` is real and was added to both
  lists (MLB/NCAAF/WNBA, no UFC). `caesars` and `wynnbet` are **not keys this
  API returns** — Caesars IS `williamhill_us`, which the config comment has said
  since before this session, so "add Caesars, remove William Hill" is one book
  asked for and deleted in the same breath. `williamhill_us` was KEPT and
  flagged. `espnbet` was excluded from shopping but left in the feed: measured
  the same day it returned 82 h2h quotes at a **median age of 0.7 minutes**, so
  the "shut down last year" premise is not what the feed shows — but which books
  a bettor will use is his call, not the feed's, and this is one env var to
  reverse. `tests/test_best_line.py` now pins the Caesars mapping and asserts no
  book can be OFFERED that is not also FETCHED (the env-override path that would
  otherwise stamp a price from a book nobody collects).
- **Deliberately not done:** no backfill of `best_*` onto locked picks (§1c,
  timing is data); no line-aware shopping (a better price at a different number
  is a different bet, and the model probability was computed at the scored
  line); no UI change to which price members see as the headline.

**Session summary (2026-09-02, session 171 — X and Discord published different records for the same day, because only one of them refused a day that was not over):**

- mike: *"why is the daily recap record sent to X different from the one posted
  to Discord? needs to be the same and fired at the same time."* Branch
  `fix/recap-parity-x-discord`. Notifications plumbing, so no `Updated-By`
  trailer.
- **The cause is one missing guard, not a computation difference.** Both
  surfaces already read the same universe through the same functions
  (`_settled_rows` + `_tally` in `tracking/discord_notifier.py`). But
  `notify_discord_results` refuses a `game_date` that is not over, and
  `notify_x_results` did not. `--step settle` runs on all ~42 refresh passes
  and settles **TODAY**, so the first pass of the day on which anything settled
  tweeted a mid-slate fragment, ledgered it under `x_results:<today>`, and the
  6am run's call for the completed day then found that row and no-opped. X had
  therefore **never once published a finished day**.
- **Measured, not inferred** — `push_sent.sent_at` for what X actually tweeted,
  reconstructed against `picks.settled_at` under the recap's own predicates:

  | date | X tweeted | Discord posted |
  |---|---|---|
  | 2026-08-30 | 7-3 at 21:15 ET | 10-8 next day 10:38 ET |
  | 2026-08-31 | 1-1 at 20:36 ET | 14-14-1 next day 06:04 ET |
  | 2026-09-01 | **0-1** at 21:08 ET | **23-12, +9.91u, +26.1% ROI** next day 06:03 ET |
  | 2026-09-02 | 2-0 at 16:23 ET | not yet due; 8-4 as of the 20:15 ET check, slate still in progress |

  0-1 and 23-12 are the same day. The public account was showing a losing
  fragment of the best day in the window.
- **Fixed:** the same day-is-over guard, same ET clock, same comparison, placed
  before the connection is opened. Both surfaces now decline every refresh pass
  and both fire inside the same `step_settle` call of the 6am run, seconds
  apart, off the same query. They stay failure-independent as designed.
- **Renderer parity.** The tweet dropped PUSHES (2026-08-31 was 14-14-1 and it
  said 14-14 — a different record for the same day), stated no ROI where the
  embed does, and passed `by_sport: []` on every call, so the per-sport branch
  in `render_results` was dead code. All three now match the embed's headline.
  The all-time block stays Discord-only: it does not fit a tweet, and omitting
  a section is not the same as contradicting one.
- **The free pick had the identical defect and it was worse.** `_pick_free`
  ends in `random.choice`, and `notify_x_free_pick` called it a *second* time —
  so over the 22-candidate MLB pools of early September the tweeted free pick
  and the free channel's free pick agreed about 4% of the time, against this
  module's own charter that "X gets exactly what the free Discord channel
  gets". A seeded RNG would NOT have fixed it: 2026-08-30's two posts went out
  twelve hours apart over pools that had changed underneath. Discord now
  records its chosen `lock_key` in `push_sent.message_id` (empty on that kind)
  and X mirrors it (checked: the only reader of that column, `_delete_posted`,
  filters `kind = 'discord_signal'` AND joins `opening_signals` on lock_key, so
  a `discord_free:<date>` row can never reach a Discord message delete);
  if Discord has not posted yet, X waits a pass rather than
  choosing. If the pick Discord published is no longer a candidate, X posts
  NOTHING rather than substituting a different one.
- **Pre-existing bug found and fixed in passing:** `tracking/x_publisher.py`
  used `logger.success(...)` on a **stdlib** `logging` logger (that method is
  loguru's). It raised `AttributeError` on the success path of *both*
  publishers, was swallowed by the broad `except`, and every tweet that
  actually went out was then logged as `X results failed: 'Logger' object has
  no attribute 'success'`. Also `render_results` used `strftime("%b %-d")` —
  a glibc extension that is a `ValueError` on Windows, i.e. the one existing
  test covering it could never run on the machine this repo's only quality gate
  runs on (§7). Both fixed.
- **Verification:** nine new tests, each **watched failing** with its fix backed
  out (guard removed; old renderer spliced back in from `HEAD`; `by_sport`
  re-hardcoded empty; X re-choosing its own free pick; Discord not recording its
  choice). The guard test asserts the database was **never opened**, because
  without the guard the call still returns 0 in a sandbox — the connection
  failure is swallowed, so a return value proves nothing. Full suite:
  1,925 passed, 3 failed — the same three failing on `origin/master`
  (`test_refresh_pass_parallel`, two in `test_system_health_checks`),
  error-set identical, unrelated.
- **mike chose (2026-09-02):** merge and deploy; delete the
  `x_results:2026-09-02` ledger row so the corrected Sep 2 record posts at 6am;
  and the tweet's per-sport split takes the EMBED's alphabetical order rather
  than most-bets-first, listing every sport instead of the first four (they fit
  inside 280 characters; the line says how many were dropped when they do not).
  He left the four wrong tweets already standing alone.
- **Not done, deliberately:** the `x_results:2026-09-02` ledger row still points
  at the 2-0 partial, so without deleting it the first correct X recap is for
  2026-09-03. Left for mike — a second "Sep 2 results" tweet is outward-facing.
  The four wrong tweets already standing (ids in `push_sent.message_id`) were
  not touched; `post_tweet` already supports `reply_to` if a visible correction
  is preferred to deletion.

**Session summary (2026-09-02, session 178 — the Record tab was empty because the record view had outgrown the API timeout):**

- Matt: screenshot of the Track Record tab showing 0-0, "Couldn't load the
  record: [object Object]", calibration chart fine underneath. Branch
  `claude/records-not-showing-error-3g5lwl`.
- **Cause, measured not guessed.** `v_public_track_record` ran 14.3s and
  `v_public_track_record_daily` 12.3s as `authenticated` (EXPLAIN ANALYZE),
  against the 8s `statement_timeout` on that role. Every app read was cancelled
  (`57014`; edge logs show 500s on the view from 2026-09-01 23:00Z). The
  calibration chart survived because it reads `mv_scored_pick_outcomes` through
  an RPC. `pg_stat_statements` said mean 1.5s / max 2.96s — survivorship: it only
  records calls that finished.
- **Where the time went.** Both views re-graded the full-outcome universe on
  every read: 126,300 picks (planner estimated 6,532 — correlated `model_id`
  predicates), each probed in `player_game_log` / `wnba_player_game_log` through
  a `LATERAL ... LIMIT 1` under a nested loop: 71k index probes, ~8.5s, plus a
  cold seq scan of `picks`. The universe grows 2-3k rows/day by design (dead-zone
  NONE rows are the evaluation dataset), so this was always going to cross 8s.
- **Fix: read the grading that already exists.** `mv_scored_pick_outcomes` is
  the same CASEs over the same universe. Migration
  `track_record_reads_graded_matview.sql` (single idempotent DO block, in
  `ACTIVE_MIGRATIONS`) redefines `v_model_full_outcome_record` and the daily
  view's fo branch over it; `v_public_track_record` inherits. Thresholds still
  joined live; `other` branch verbatim; the markers the two older active
  migrations assert on preserved. Applied to production via the Supabase MCP
  at 23:11Z. **Now 0.9s / 0.09s** (record / daily, cold cache) as
  `authenticated`; what remains of the record's time is its own two scans of
  `picks` (the CLV and first/last-date CTEs), ~0.3s each cold.
- **Verified equivalent BEFORE applying, in single statements** (thresholds
  could not move underneath): record 20 rows / 0 differ, daily 202 rows / 0
  differ, `EXCEPT ALL` both ways after a matview refresh. First pass of the
  daily comparison showed 14 "differences" — they were the live-totals model's
  second MLB row per day from the `other` branch, which I had not carved out;
  the fo rows themselves matched exactly. Post-apply: 1,208 fo picks /
  $8,430.79 unchanged, record and daily still reconcile to the cent.
- **Freshness is the one behavioural change.** The fo branch now moves when the
  matview refreshes. It already refreshed after settle at 6am; `refresh_pass.sh`
  now runs `refresh-outcomes` after `settle` on every pass (~7s, CONCURRENTLY),
  so "graded as games finish" stays true. `tests/test_track_record_views.py`
  pins registration, single-statement shape, no player-log joins, live
  thresholds, the older migrations' markers, GRANTs inside the guarded
  branches, and the pass ordering — each watched failing on a mutated copy.
- **The `[object Object]` is a separate, already-fixed thing.** `errorText`
  (#389, OTA published 2026-09-01 21:36Z) renders this as
  "canceling statement due to statement timeout (57014)"; the phone in the
  screenshot was still on the older bundle. `useOtaUpdates` applies the new one
  on the next cold start / return from background.
- **Post-apply 500s were not this view's.** Two more 500s on `v_public_track_record`
  (23:16Z, 01:22Z) were app-wide: every PostgREST view the app opens timed out in
  the same second, while the refresh pass's `cleanup-picks` DELETE sat 12.9s
  waiting on a ShareLock on `picks` (01:23:08Z → 01:23:20Z). The worker also
  logged `relation "model_auto_pauses" does not exist` at 01:23:25Z. Both
  pre-existing; neither touched here.
- **Found in passing, NOT fixed:** the worker's pregame line poller
  (`data/ingestors/pregame_line_poller.py`, `DISTINCT ON (o.game_id, o.market)`
  over `odds` joined to unscored games) is hitting a statement timeout every
  ~2 minutes as `postgres` via Supavisor (23:00:55Z, 22:58:54Z, 22:56:53Z…).
  Separate feed, separate fix; flagged in the reply.
- **Not done:** no `apply_migration` history row (applied via `execute_sql`,
  then re-run as a recorded no-op); `player_game_log` has not been analysed
  since 2026-05-10 (480k rows) — irrelevant now that nothing hot reads it per
  pick, noted for whoever next touches autovacuum settings.
**Session summary (2026-09-02, session 170 — batter HR and batter RBI RETIRED: out of the app, out of every model total, picks kept):**

- Matt, from the Models tab screenshot: "Remove RBI and HR models from the UI and
  make sure they are omitted from being tracked with any model totals." Branch
  `claude/remove-rbi-hr-models-9u1dwf`. Treated as a RETIREMENT on the
  session-135 precedent (`mlb_live_win_prob` / `mlb_live_runline`), not a
  pause: a paused model still scores NONE rows and keeps its cut; a retired one
  is gone from the registry. `Updated-By: matt`.
- **The record at retirement, measured not recalled:** HR 256 settled BETs
  42-214, 16 unsettled, 13,766 NONE rows — already record-only and already
  excluded from the public record since 2026-07-04. RBI 293 settled BETs 214-79
  lifetime, 4 unsettled, but exactly ONE settled bet clears the 0.62/0.12 cut
  mike re-cut it to on 2026-08-31 (the sweep with the largest floor distortion
  on the board, 47.6% of rows refused by -140). Both `model_calibration` rows
  were refit this morning (RBI promoted, Platt a=0.759 b=0.467, n=9,711).
- **What actually stops them (the part the precedent would have missed):** the
  batter-prop scorer iterates its OWN `_BATTER_PROP_CONFIG` dict in
  `models/scorer.py`, not `PROP_MODELS` — removing the registry entry alone
  would not have stopped a single pick. Both entries removed there, from
  `PROP_MODELS`, all three threshold dicts, `MODEL_MIN_ODDS`,
  `PROB_ONLY_MODELS` (HR was its founding member), `MODEL_BET_SIZE_MULTIPLIER`
  (HR's 0.25 quarter-stake), `PROP_FEATURE_MAP`, `PENDING_RETRAIN_FEATURES`
  and the trainer's `_BATTER_MODELS` / `_BATTER_TARGET` (a retrain now raises).
  Both `.pkl` artifacts deleted; both active `model_registry` rows set
  `is_active = 0` and both `model_action_thresholds` rows DELETED directly via
  the Supabase MCP (the daily `threshold_sync` prunes them from master anyway
  once this merges — and RE-INSERTS them from master until then, see below).
- **NEW `config.RETIRED_MODELS`** (all four retired ids), the Python mirror of
  the app's `thresholds.RETIRED_MODELS`, and the two must not drift
  (`tests/test_retired_models.py::test_the_app_mirrors_the_same_set` parses the

  was written for.
- **The views needed nothing:** all four track-record views INNER JOIN

**Session summary (2026-09-02, session 169 — the front-end UX designer: a subagent, its checklist, and the scan that makes two reviews comparable):**

- Matt: "create a front end UX designer agent. The agent should review all
  front end components on any new features and leverage my MCP with Mobbin."
  Branch `claude/frontend-ux-designer-agent-tzj7x3`.
- **The repo's first in-repo agent.** Sentinel and Janitor live in Routines;
  this one is `.claude/agents/frontend-ux-designer.md` — a project subagent
  Claude Code delegates to proactively after a change under `mobile/src`, and
  that `/ux-review` (`.claude/skills/ux-review/SKILL.md`) runs on demand
  against the branch diff, a PR number, a branch or explicit files, with
  `--comment` to post the report on the PR. Read-only by construction: its tool
  list has no Edit/Write. Contract in `docs/agents_contract.md`, row in
  `docs/AGENTS.md`, one-paragraph rule in CLAUDE.md §5.
- **The checklist is a file, not a prompt** — `mobile/docs/UX_REVIEW.md`. Same
  reasoning as Janitor's worklist: a review whose criteria are re-derived each
  run finds different things each run. §0 is the product rules from CLAUDE.md
  restated as UI failures (a re-priced pick shown as the original, `best_*`
  feeding the decision, "paper trading" copy, `useSubscription().entitled`,
  UTC today, native module → TestFlight); §1–§8 are hierarchy, tokens, the
  five states, touch, accessibility, safe areas, copy, reuse-before-invention;
  §9 says which Mobbin comparators fit which pattern.
- **The deterministic half:** `mobile/scripts/ux_scan.mts`, plain Node, no
  deps (`.mts` so Node loads it as ESM without touching the app's
  `package.json`, which would make the OTA job refuse to publish). Nine rules,
  each mapped to a checklist line: hex outside `theme.ts`, `fontSize` literals,
  pressables with no a11y props (Blocker when icon-only, Should-fix with a
  `<Text>` child), UTC today, paper-trading copy, the entitlement gate,
  `allowFontScaling={false}`, and `SafeAreaView` from `react-native`.
  `--changed` scopes to the branch diff plus working tree; `--strict` exits 1.
  **Watched it fail:** a nine-violation fixture fired 8 of 9 on the first run —
  the paper-trading regex had a trailing `\b` that "trading" defeated. Fixed;
  9/9, and 0 on the clean fixture.
- **Baseline across all of `mobile/src`** (203 findings in 47 files), so a
  review reports a set diff and not a count:

  | rule | count |
  |---|---|
  | `a11y-pressable` | 145 (20 icon-only blockers) |
  | `font-size-literal` | 32 |
  | `hex-color` | 26 (backdrop alphas excluded) |
  | everything else | 0 |

  Zero paper-trading copy in production code, consistent with the 2026-08-29
  fix. The a11y number is the real finding of the day: 145 pressables silent
  or roleless for VoiceOver, 20 of them icon-only. Left for a follow-up, not
  fixed here — this session's scope is the agent.
- **Mobbin.** The official server is remote (`https://api.mobbin.com/mcp`,
  OAuth, Pro/Team plan); the community npm `mobbin-mcp` was unpublished
  2026-07-10, so the remote one is the only supported route. Declared
  project-wide in `.mcp.json` as `mobbin` and referenced from the agent's
  `mcpServers`/`tools` as `mcp__mobbin` (every tool on that server) rather
  than by individual tool name, because the sandbox's egress blocked
  mobbin.com and every write-up that lists them — the names could not be
  measured, so they were not guessed. **The OAuth login is Matt's to do** in an
  interactive session; until then the agent says so in one line and reviews on
  HIG and the app's conventions.
- **Later the same day, Matt connected Mobbin as a claude.ai connector and the
  names could be measured:** the server is `Mobbin` (capital M) with
  `search_screens`, `search_flows`, `search_sections`; the agent's allowlist
  said `mcp__mobbin` and would not have matched. Fixed to allow both spellings
  and the `mcpServers` reference dropped. The first real call answered
  `Mobbin MCP requires a paid plan` — so the connection is proven and the
  plan is the remaining gate.
- **Deliberately not done:** no hook that forces the review on every Stop
  (proactive delegation via the agent description plus the CLAUDE.md rule is
  the mechanism; a hook is the escalation if it gets skipped); no fixes to the
  203 baseline findings; the Streamlit dashboard is out of scope by decision.
- **Noticed in passing, not fixed:** CLAUDE.md is 48 KB against its own ~30 KB
  target, and carries a full session-132 summary at the bottom that belongs in
  `docs/sessions/2026-09.md`.

**Session summary (2026-09-02, session 169 — the 24.6-hour query nobody had measured, and two PRs merged for Matt):**

- Matt: "Merge" (both open PRs) then "Merge & fix outstanding issues".

- **Merged #390** (`f137a69`) — the health-check step had been failing on EVERY
  pipeline run for ~41 hours from one missing `WHERE` keyword. **Merged #384**
  (`a5a3a1e`) — the three agent faults. #384 was tested against `c98403e`, four
  commits stale, so it was test-merged onto current master first: clean merge,
  2,023 passed.

- **Closed #392 as obsolete.** Another session's #393 moved the same stray
  CLAUDE.md block twenty minutes earlier. Two sessions independently found the
  same red gate, which is its own signal: `b82a367` reached master as a DIRECT
  BRANCH MERGE rather than a PR, so nothing checked it on the way in.

- **THE BIGGEST FINDING OF THE WHOLE INCIDENT WAS NOT THE DDL STORM.**
  `pregame_line_poller.last_known_prices()` rebuilds a fingerprint map on EVERY
  tick of a 24x7 loop, re-reading DK's entire pre-game history for every
  unstarted game — ~1,525 games, ~142k heap fetches out of a 1.18 GB table.
  From `pg_stat_statements`: **4,291 calls, 88,398 s total, 20,601 ms mean —
  24.6 HOURS of database time**, more than DOUBLE the 11.6 hours the DDL storm
  cost. At a 30-second interval and a 20.6 s mean the loop spent ~69% of every
  cycle in this one query, and at the >60 s tail (measured: a bare
  `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` timed out at 60 s) it ran back to back and slept not at all.

- **The plan was never the problem, which is why an index was the wrong first
  instinct.** `EXPLAIN` shows a good plan: drive from `games` (1,525 unstarted),
  nested-loop into `odds` on `idx_odds_book_snap`. Nothing is missing. The cost
  is 142k random heap fetches, repeated every 30 seconds for no reason —
  **the poller already knows what it wrote.**

- Fixed by making the map caller-owned: `run_forever` seeds it once, `poll_once`
  folds its own writes in AFTER the commit (before, and a failed insert leaves
  the map claiming a price the database never took — the next tick then sees no
  change and never retries it), and a bounded `PREGAME_POLL_RESEED_SEC` (900s)
  re-seeds so another writer's move and started-game dead weight cannot drift
  forever. A tick that dies throws the map away rather than carrying a guess.
  **2,880 rebuilds a day become 96.**

- **`snapshot_at` is TEXT**, so `snapshot_at::timestamptz` is a non-immutable
  cast and CANNOT be indexed — worth knowing before anyone reaches for the
  obvious expression index. A covering partial index is still the right
  belt-and-braces follow-up, but it is DDL on the hottest table and belongs in
  a quiet window, not at 7pm ET with live games running.

- **Watched fail, not assumed:** always-rebuild fails 2; writes not folded fails
  2; folding before the commit fails 1; a failed tick keeping its map fails 1.
  Suite **2,032 passed**.

**Session summary (2026-09-02, session 132 — WNBA: model-first path CLOSED by experiment; the market-relative prop rule ported; my assists re-cut WITHDRAWN at merge):**
- Matt: "retrain WNBA models" -> "a model with ROI above the vig and positive units long term" -> "check all prop bets, even paused ones, we can use these in the playoffs". Branch `claude/wnba-market-rule-and-assists-recut`.
- **THE PRE-REGISTERED POINTS REBUILD STOPPED, in both availability modes.** Built `features/wnba_availability.py` (rotation rank, teammates-out vacated workload, usage from the never-read `fg_att/fg3_att/ft_att`), a minutes model that beat its naive baseline (holdout MAE 4.40 vs 4.62, season-wise OOF), and an NB dispersion head (OOF residual var/mean 3.2x). Trained 2019-2025 / holdout 2026, graded at real PRE-TIP DK prices (9,654 post-tip snapshots excluded): 52-54% win against 53.5-54.3% breakevens, best cells +0.1..+1.2% with plateau <=2/8. **Box mode grades as if we knew perfectly who plays tonight and STILL has no edge**, so per the pre-commitment: STOP, no re-cut. Harness: `scripts/wnba_points_rebuild.py`.
- **`is_starter` is 100% NULL for 2019-2025** (and in ALL ~105K NBA rows — the same dead role feature sits in 9 live NBA prop models). `_recent_starter()` therefore emits a constant 0 for every historical row. Availability must come from box-score presence, NOT `injuries` (WNBA rows only from 2026-06-07 -> NULL for 100% of train; dropna would delete the matrix).
- **Measured, and it is why the features are rank-conditioned:** each absent rotation regular adds ~2.3 min and ~0.9 pts to the bench players who remain, monotone through 3+ absences and REPLICATED in 2026 — but the starter-side effect REVERSED sign between 2019-25 and 2026 (blowouts ruled out; margins identical). A single pooled absence coefficient would have hurt starter props.
- **NEW `wnba_prop_market` — the NFL market-relative rule ported.** With the model-first path closed by experiment, the only edge class validated in this repo is market-vs-market (nfl_prop_market +10.2% train / +10.8% blind over 954; NCAAF opener +10.9%/1050) — and Pinnacle WNBA player props began flowing 2026-08-27 (points/rebounds/assists; it declines threes, itself information). `models/wnba_prop_market.py` IMPORTS the NFL module's `find_bets`/`best_per_prop`/`devig` outright so the two rules can never drift on de-vig, like-line discipline or dedupe; `scripts/wnba_prop_market_card.py` is plumbing (card -> -140 floor -> insert-once publish). Settlement rides `_settle_prop_picks` via the generalized `_PROP_MARKET_STAT_BY_MODEL` (FROM_PROP_MARKET sentinel, was NFL-only); a flag whose player_id cannot be resolved is SKIPPED LOUDLY, never published unsettleable. Wired into the daily step + `--step wnba-prop-market` + `refresh_pass.sh`. min_edge **0.05 PRE-COMMITTED from the NFL derivation** (6pp chosen greedily went negative blind). Deliberately NOT in PROP_MODELS (no artifact to train or health-check). **PAPER-FIRST; kill: no positive blind month at >=50 flags -> close, never re-threshold.** Live dry-run on real days: 8/28 two flags, 8/29 zero (105 like-line comparisons, clean), 8/30 one flag. ~1 flag/day. The two retro-gradable flags both LOST (n=2, recorded).
- **MY ASSISTS RE-CUT WAS WITHDRAWN AT MERGE, and the reason is the useful part.** I derived 0.54/0.02 (NB head, 290 bets +3.39%, 8/8 plateau) from a sweep on RAW artifact probabilities. Master had already re-cut assists the same day (mike, 0.50/0.10, 57 bets +23.1%) on a **floor-corrected CALIBRATED sweep**. Master also added `models/probability_calibration.py`: the scorer now decides on calibrated probabilities wherever a map is PROMOTED. Checked `model_calibration`: **promoted = points, rebounds, threes; NOT promoted = assists, pra.** So for points/rebounds/threes my sweep graded the wrong variable — production does not decide on the numbers I swept, and mike's threes/rebounds unpauses describe the real decision while my STOP verdicts for them do not. Took master's cuts on every threshold. The NB artifact (`wnba_prop_player_assists_20260831_213734.pkl`, r=13.56) and `scripts/wnba_assists_nb_head.py` ship UNREGISTERED; the scorer's NB routing is inert until something carries `nb_r`, and a test now pins that.
- **Two open items for whoever reconciles assists:** (1) assists is NOT promoted, so production decides on RAW probs — mike's calibrated-basis cut is applied to raw numbers at scoring time, which may be its own scale mismatch and is worth one look; (2) `mobile/src/lib/thresholds.ts` on master is stale against master's own `config.py` (July values for four WNBA props, and points/threes/pra/rebounds still in its PAUSED list) — harmless because the server table drives display, but it is drift.
- **The points rebuild's STOP verdict is unaffected by any of this** — that model was trained and graded end-to-end on one consistent scale.
- **Verification:** 43 new tests (20 availability incl. a look-ahead invariance test, 8 engine-context incl. v1 backward-compat byte-identity, 15 market-rule incl. selector-vs-settler map tripwires and insert-once). Reusable harnesses `scripts/wnba_points_rebuild.py` + `scripts/wnba_prop_sweep.py` (all five props, both distribution heads) are what the NBA port should reuse.
- **NOTE:** the local `.env` DATABASE_URL password is STALE (rotated); every local DB run needs it updated. This session reached production through a temporary scoped read-only role created and dropped via the Supabase MCP.

**Session summary (2026-09-01, session 168 — the fix verified in production, and the tripwire that would not have caught the next one):**

- Follow-up to 167, after Matt merged #389. **The fix works, measured rather
  than assumed.** Both Railway services deployed on `fb11939` at 21:39:20Z, and
  every write-time DDL statement went flat:

  | statement | baseline | after | old rate would have added |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `CREATE INDEX idx_api_call_ts` | 2,340 @ 21:58Z | 2,340 | ~17 |
  | `ALTER TABLE api_call_log ENABLE RLS` | 2,017 @ 21:58Z | 2,017 | ~16 |
  | `CREATE INDEX worker_jobs_pending_idx` | 3,482 @ 22:15Z | 3,482 | ~20 |
  | `CREATE TABLE worker_jobs` | 3,479 @ 22:15Z | 3,479 | ~20 |
  | `ALTER TABLE worker_jobs ADD COLUMN` | 3,479 @ 22:15Z | 3,479 | ~20 |
  | `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX worker_jobs_dedupe_idx` | 3,479 @ 22:15Z | 3,479 | ~20 |

  **The control that makes it proof rather than a quiet window:** over the same
  interval `INSERT INTO worker_jobs` went 3,351 → 3,365. The queue was running
  and calling `ensure_schema` on every one of those; the DDL simply stopped
  firing. `mean_exec_time` also held to the digit, which one new execution
  would have moved.

- **A hypothesis raised and then killed by measurement.** Session 167 flagged
  that `job_queue` is the only guarded site passing an EMPTY `revoked_from`,
  and that an untyped empty array in `r.rolname = ANY(%s)` might raise, roll
  back, and fall through to `return False`. It does not — `worker_jobs` is
  frozen too. Recorded because the reasoning was sound and the conclusion was
  wrong; the measurement is what settled it.

- **THE TRIPWIRE SHIPPED IN 167 WOULD NOT HAVE CAUGHT THE NEXT ONE, AND ONLY A
  MUTATION SHOWED IT.** `tests/test_ddl_guard.py` grepped for `ENABLE ROW LEVEL
  SECURITY`, so `tracking/threshold_review.py` — whose DDL is `CREATE TABLE IF
  NOT EXISTS` and nothing else — sat completely unguarded while the test passed.
  Widening it to `CREATE INDEX` and `ADD COLUMN` did NOT fix that either: the
  first rewrite still passed with `threshold_review` reverted to unguarded,
  because the module carries none of those three markers. **The test was scoped
  to the symptom that had already been found, not to the mechanism.**

- **`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` belongs in the tripwire, and execution time is
  the wrong axis for deciding that.** It costs 5-7 ms on an existing table —
  which is why it looked skippable — but it fired 3,479 times from `job_queue`
  and 2,360 from `monitoring/store`, and **every one of those was a PostgREST
  schema-cache reload**. Cost per statement is irrelevant when the damage is
  per invocation.

- Shipped: `tracking/threshold_review.ensure_schema` guarded (two tables, two
  probes); the tripwire rebuilt around all four statement kinds; a documented
  `DDL_GUARD_EXEMPT` map whose seven entries each cite a MEASURED call count,
  not an assumption; `test_the_exemption_list_stays_honest` so an entry cannot
  outlive the file it names; and `test_the_hot_modules_are_guarded_not_exempted`
  so the seven modules the outage was traced to cannot be exempted back out —
  an allowlist is easier to append to than a guard is to add, and that is the
  line that makes the shortcut fail.

- **Watched fail, not assumed:** reverting `threshold_review` to unguarded fails
  2; exempting it instead of guarding it fails 1; a new unguarded module fails 1;
  dropping the `db_setup` exemption fails 1; a bogus exemption fails 1. Suite
  **1,963 passed**.

- **Not done, deliberately:** `pregame_line_poller`'s `SELECT DISTINCT ON
  (o.game_id, o.market) ... FROM odds JOIN games` — 24-50 s on `IO/DataFileRead`
  during the incident — is now the largest remaining source of contention. Still
  needs its own index measurement.

**Session summary (2026-09-01, session 167 — the Stats page error was the whole API, and the cause was our own DDL):**

- Matt: *"Still having this error on the stats page and now no players are
  showing"* — two screenshots, NCAAF Pass Yards and MLB Hits, both showing
  `Connection error: [object Object]` above `No players`.

- **IT WAS NOT THE STATS PAGE, AND NOT THE DATA.** The RPCs behind the board are
  healthy: `player_recent_games_mlb(2026,'batter',5)` returns **3,197 rows in
  780 ms**, and it returns them as `anon`. `player_recent_games_ncaaf(2026,5)`
  returns 686. Grants are correct, the functions exist. The board was empty
  because **PostgREST was answering 503 to every request**. `edge_logs` for the
  minute in Matt's screenshot (10:11 UTC = 6:11 ET, the clock on the phone):
  **215 responses, all 503.** Every logged call to those two RPCs in 24h was a
  500 or a 503; not one succeeded.

- **THE CAUSE IS THIS REPO'S OWN write-time DDL.** Seven modules create their
  table at write time and re-ran the whole CREATE/ALTER/REVOKE block on **every
  call**, on the assumption that `IF NOT EXISTS` makes it free. Postgres
  disagrees on three counts: `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` takes a SHARE lock
  before it discovers the index exists, `ALTER TABLE ... ENABLE ROW LEVEL
  SECURITY` takes ACCESS EXCLUSIVE whether or not RLS is on, and **each one
  fires Supabase's `pgrst_ddl_watch` event trigger — PostgREST then returns 503
  to the entire app until its schema cache rebuilds.** From
  `pg_stat_statements` (measured, not estimated):

  | statement | calls | mean | total |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_api_call_ts` | 1,925 | 15,082 ms | 29,032 s |
  | `ALTER TABLE api_call_log ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` | 1,676 | 7,797 ms | 13,067 s |

  **11.6 hours of database time re-creating a table that already existed**, and
  ~3,600 forced cache reloads. `postgres_logs` shows the reload query itself —
  PostgREST's giant catalog `WITH base_types AS ...` — timing out **232 times**
  against the 8s `authenticator` limit, plus 108 timeouts of the `ALTER TABLE`
  and 30 each on `v_latest_odds_all_books` / `v_latest_dk_odds` reads starved
  behind it.

- **It is self-reinforcing, which is why it got worse rather than settling.**
  `monitoring/probe.py`'s writer drops its connection on ANY failure and clears
  its `ensured` flag, so it re-runs the ensure block on reconnect. The busier
  the database got, the more DDL it fired at it, the longer the cache stayed
  down.

- **Fixed: `data/ddl_guard.py`.** One indexed catalog SELECT answers "would
  every statement in this block be a no-op?" — table exists, has the columns,
  has the indexes, RLS on, `anon`/`authenticated` hold nothing. It **fails
  open**: SQLite, a shim connection, an unreadable catalog, any doubt at all
  returns False and the caller runs its DDL exactly as before, so it can only
  remove redundant work. Uses `%s` placeholders and a cursor fallback so it
  works on both `data/db.py`'s shim and a raw psycopg2 connection.

- **Applied to all seven sites, not one** (§1b: a fix that lands in one module
  and not the others is how this repo accumulates work): `monitoring/store.py`,
  `tracking/run_ledger.py`, `tracking/live_calibration.py` (this one ran on
  every live-loop calibration pass), `models/probability_calibration.py`,
  `tracking/job_queue.py`, `data/ingestors/dk_direct_feed.py` (ACCESS EXCLUSIVE
  on `odds`, the busiest table in the database), and
  `nfl/live_model/backtest/backup_snaps.py` — the last one found by the
  tripwire, not by reading.

- **`tests/test_ddl_guard.py`** — 21 cases: the guard's own truth table, one
  no-DDL case and one still-creates case per site, and a source tripwire that
  fails if any NEW module runs `ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` without the guard.
  **Watched fail, not assumed** (§1b): removing the short-circuit from
  `monitoring/store.ensure_table` fails 1; making the guard always return True
  fails 13; making it stop failing open fails 1.

- **Fixed: `[object Object]`.** supabase-js does not throw — it returns
  `{ data, error }`, and every helper re-throws that plain object. `String(e)`
  on a plain object is literally `"[object Object]"`, and **16 call sites across
  the app** did exactly that. New `mobile/src/lib/errors.ts::errorText` reads
  `message` and `code` first (so `PGRST002` / `57014` reach the user), falls
  back to a sentence, and can never render `[object Object]`. All 16 sites plus
  `authHelpers` / `discordHelpers` / `billing` now route through it.
  `mobile/scripts/verify_error_text.ts` pins it — and fails 6 checks when the
  object branch is removed.

- **Fixed: the empty state lied.** `No MLB Hits data for the last 5 games yet.`
  is what a total API outage looked like — the same copy an genuinely empty
  board gets, which sent the diagnosis at the data rather than the API. The
  board now says "Couldn't load players" with a Retry in the banner when the
  fetch failed, and keeps the "no data yet" copy for an actually empty result.

- **Verified:** `python -m pytest -q tests/` → **1,961 passed**. `npx tsc
  --noEmit` in `mobile/` → error set **byte-identical to `origin/master`** (57
  lines, all the documented pre-existing `queries.ts` casts, 0 in touched
  files), diffed against a `git worktree --detach origin/master`, not a stash.
  `npx tsx scripts/verify_error_text.ts` → all pass.

- **Found and NOT fixed, deliberately:** `SELECT DISTINCT ON (o.game_id,
  o.market) ... FROM odds JOIN games` (`data/ingestors/pregame_line_poller.py`)
  was observed running **24–50 s on `IO / DataFileRead`** during the incident,
  and timed out 20 times in 24h. It is real, and it is the reason the DDL storm
  was able to starve everything else rather than just being wasteful — but it is
  a separate piece of work needing its own index measurement, and fixing it
  means a migration, which is itself more DDL. Flagged for the next session.

- **Not done:** the fix ships when the worker redeploys — the DDL flood
  continues from the running container until then. Nothing was changed in
  production from this session.
